PIXEL CLICKER - GAME REFERENCE FOR AI READERS
FORMAT: plain text. Sections start with "=== NAME ===". Lines are dense facts; "->" = leads to / produces; "x" = multiplier; numbers in parentheses are code defaults (0 in an Inspector field usually means "use the coded default"). Code identifiers are kept verbatim so they can be searched (grep) in Assets/Scripts.
SCOPE: section 1 is a hand-written overview of how the game plays and fits together. Sections 2+ are GENERATED from the code (enums, block catalog, packs, devices, skills) and from every system's own notes (CLAUDE.md index, script header comments, docs/systems/*.md). Regenerate with: python3 tools/game-reference/build.py
NAMING: player-facing name "husk" = code name "old pixel" (OldPixelInfo, oldPixels, OldPixelDespawn). "Pixel type" = "tier" = "block type" (PixelClicker.PixelTier, enum PixelType). "Pure" pixels = the six basic types (White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue).

=== 1. OVERVIEW ===
ENGINE: Unity 6 (also guarded for older Unity), C#, TextMeshPro, URP. Single scene. All code in Assets/Scripts (~250 scripts, ~120k lines). Almost all UI and 3D models are built at runtime in code (no prefabs). PixelClicker.Awake adds most systems as components. Both input systems supported (legacy + new Input System) via PixelInput.
GENRE: 3D incremental clicker with skills (RuneScape-like), crafting, devices / light factory building, minigames, visitors / vendors and collectibles.

CORE LOOP
- One clicker CUBE in the middle of the screen shows a BLOCK of some pixel type. Left click = hit. Normal blocks break in 1 click; TOUGH blocks need clicksToCollect hits (only the last pays; PixelHit / PixelFinalHit events).
- Breaking a block pays CURRENCY of that block's own type (each pixel type is its own currency): amountPerClick (or a random payoutMin..payoutMax) x PayoutMultiplier (Ultra x Value x wish) x clickMultiplier. Click flow: CollectInternal -> AddCurrency -> PixelCollected event -> effects -> SpawnFallingCopy (the husk) -> RollTier (next block) -> Materialize.
- Next block = weighted random (spawnWeight) among UNLOCKED, spawn-enabled types of the current rotation (PickSpawnTier). Basic types unlock by thresholds of the previous type (TierUnlockMode.PreviousTierThreshold) or by shop purchase (ShopOnly).
- Every break drops a HUSK: a small physical cube (Rigidbody) of that type that falls to the floor. Husks despawn after a lifetime (oldPixelLifetime 15 s + Standard perk), are capped in number (OldPixelCap, base 50 + Standard perk), can be grabbed and thrown with the mouse (Pixel Grabbing, PixelGrab), and are the MATERIAL of the game.
- PIXEL RULES (docs/systems/PixelRules.md): a click gives the block's VALUE (currency); the HUSK is material and never pays currency by itself (PixelClicker.husksPayCurrency = off). Consumed husks give skill XP instead (GiveHuskXp -> PixelSkills.AddHuskXp, huskXpShare 0.5 of a click's XP; pure husks train Standard, a skill's husks train that skill). The only husk -> currency path is the RECYCLER device (10 husks, 2 s shake, pays 50% of what a click on that block pays now). Holding a UTILITY block gives CHARGES (e.g. Pesticide: 3 Hornet Nest charges that auto-clear hazards).
- AUTO CLICKER (shop) clicks on a timer; auto clicks give reduced skill XP (autoXpFactor 0.25) but Standard still gets its flat 1 XP.

BLOCK HOLD (right mouse button, PixelBlockHold)
- Hold right on the cube: the block is pulled out of the rotation and floats beside the cube while a meter drains (5 s, 10 s for hold blocks); the cube rolls a new block so you keep clicking. Letting go puts it back next in line (keeps its hits). Recharge 8 s; running dry = longer cooldown. Hazards and some specials can't be held.
- STAKE: holding one of the six pure blocks for >= 0.75 s stakes it: value 1 + 0.6 x seconds held + 0.1 per click during the hold; on release a win roll (60%) decides: it later pays that multiplier or nothing (revealed with a green / red aura when it comes round).
- Meter energy: every 5 own clicks +1% meter. Meter UI is an arc beside the cursor, shown only while hovering the cube or holding.
- HOLD BLOCKS (PixelBlockRules.HoldRule): data rules for bonuses while held (Whetstone = extra rapid clicks; Pesticide = charges; XP / payout multipliers). SPECIAL blocks = rare replacements of rolled blocks (Great Oak etc.).

SKILLS (PixelSkills + partial files; window PixelSkillsWindow)
- STANDARD: the always-on skill. Every collected block = flat 1 Standard XP (standardXpPerBlock), manual or auto, any skill, no multipliers. Perks: husks last +5 s per level, floor holds +5 husks per level from level 4. Level 2 popup opens the Skills window beside a highlighted Standard card.
- GATHERING SKILL PACKS: Woodcutting, Foraging, Fishing, Mining (= Prospector mode), Farming, Electrician. Choosing a pack (Skills window only, never Toggles) replaces the basic rotation with that pack's blocks; you train that skill. Packs have a FILLER block (useless, shrinks in share with level), HAZARD blocks (Pest, Gas Pocket, Hornet Nest, Toadstool...: tough, pay nothing, penalise XP; right click to clear), SPECIAL blocks and perks per level, a skill TREE (1 point per level), XP curve baseXp x xpGrowth.
- OFFLINE PROGRESSION skill: trains from every click while the chosen pixel pack still gets 50% XP.
- XP multipliers: minigames enabled add a global multiplier; bonus XP bank doubles gains while it lasts; hazards apply a temporary penalty; Mastery = progress of all skills together.
- VALUE CAP: a pixel type's Value upgrade level can't exceed the level of the skill that owns it (pure / non-pack types -> Standard) x valueLevelsPerSkillLevel (1).

ECONOMY / SHOP (PixelShop, PixelShopUI, PixelShopDefaults)
- Shop button appears once Black is unlocked. Tabs: Pixels (base pixels, toggleable single pixels, skill packs), Upgrades (sub-tabs Features = packs/upgrades, Value = per-type value levels, Operation = consumable stat upgrades), Consumables (potion / utility / seed cards), Minigames (packs that unlock minigames).
- Packs = ShopPack entries (BuiltInPacks table adds missing ones). Testing packs are hideInShop (Fire, Nuclear, Farmer's, Prospector's, Block Hold, Oak Trees test); the Dev Tools 'Shop unlocks' widget unlocks / relocks any pack.
- VALUE upgrades: per pixel type, paid in that type, cost valueBaseCost 25 x amountPerClick x 1.15^level; payout x (1 + level x valueBonusPerLevel) x milestone multiplier; capped by skill level (above). Hazards have no Value row.
- VENDORS sell / buy for pure currency: Lumberjack (Woodcutting) and Prospector (Mining) BUY skill blocks for pure pixels at a rarity-based rate (UnitValue = 1 / (spawnWeight x amountPerClick / clicks)) x sellRate 0.8 x luck, and sell devices / potions. Other visitors: Tinkerer, Wizard, Entrepreneur (trades), Farmer; rare cats Egg and Bean.
- CUBIE (PixelGuideVendor): the guide / shopkeeper. Visit 1 comes after 50 White pixel clicks and gives a Recycler ({recycler} page token); visit 2 holds time until the shop is opened (shop nudge + shop tutorial). Page texts are Inspector lists.

CONSUMABLES (PixelConsumables)
- POTIONS: one single potion per pixel type (all non-rare types): for N seconds only that type spawns. COMBO potions (crafted): 2-type for every pair of types; 3-type only among non-standalone types (pre-built at start). Potions can be auto-priced from expected return.
- DEVICES (DeviceKind): placed in the world by right-clicking them in the Inventory: Fan, Vacuum Device, Sorter, Magnet, Magnetized Sorter, Quantum Organizer, Storage Crate (10 slots), Sprinkler, Funnel, Wood Chipper, Recycler, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Bait Bucket, seeds, Prime Gauntlet... Some timed (countdown, shrink away), some permanent. Attuned devices (crafted with a potion) only affect husks of one type. Placed devices are saved (PlacedState: position, yaw, remaining, extra data).
- CARRY HANDLES (PixelCarryHandles): Storage Crate, Recycler and the Bait Bucket can be dragged by their handles (scroll / Q / E turns).
- CRAFTING (PixelCrafting): recipes, potion + pixel -> combo potion, device + potion -> attuned device.
- TESTER MACHINES (factory, not final): conveyor belts, kiln, splitter, elevator, belt sorter, trash can, tiny robot, connector.

MINIGAMES (PixelMinigame base; PixelMinigameLimits caps how many are on screen)
- Bought as packs (Minigames tab): Black Hole, Ghost, Meteor, Evil Tree, Snake, Sorting Race, Cube Dash, Red Light Green Light, Cube Evasion, Cube Blink, Breakout, Bomb Defusal, Ultra Holes. Passive: Stardust. Skill minigame: Weak Spots (Fishing catch). Some are TAKEOVER minigames (PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive pauses the normal game).
- Each enabled minigame raises the global XP multiplier.

COLLECTIBLES / RARE DROPS
- 7 Dragon Cubes (gather all -> wish), 3 God Cubes (~1 in 7 million, one of each), 6 Prime Cubes + the Wizard's Prime Gauntlet forging the Prime Cubelette. Rare drops sit at the END of the tier list; Collection Log book and a walkable gallery show them.

OTHER SYSTEMS
- PETS: one pet per block type (all except the rare collectible cubes), roam the floor, perks per type, outfits / names / Outfit Studio.
- FIRST PERSON: walk the floor, carry and stack husks, axe; out-of-bounds wall.
- CAMERA: base view, View window (V): orbital, top-down, first person, free cam, orthographic, zoom. Camera intro on start.
- WORLD LOOK: floor styles, sky styles, day / night, screen filters, bar edge styles, flat view.
- UI: Inventory (Currency groups, Consumables with Devices tab), Log (events, goals), Toggles window (after Auto Clicker), Pocket window + custom keybinds, Pixel Guide, Game Database, block tooltips, notices / tips (PixelNotice, PixelHints), status boxes, achievements, stats, title screen with music player, pause menu (settings, save / load, game guide, feedback, music player with shuffle).
- SAVES: JSON in persistentDataPath (PixelSaveGame); per-save settings sync (PlayerPrefs keys "PixelClicker.Setting.*"); autosave history with screenshots; offline progress on startup load. Inspector data upgrades: version ints (packDataVersion, looksVersion, ...) gate one-time fix-ups that RUN EVERY PLAY (play-mode changes are not saved to the scene), so removing them breaks the scene's old data.
- SHARED HELPERS: PixelCalm (InMenu / CameraBusy / ForTip), PixelPointerClaim (a world object owns the click), PixelHighlight (gold frame round any UI), PixelFind (find with version guard), PixelUIKit (UI builders), PixelFactoryKit (primitive builder), PixelBlockCatalog (one card per block type with TAGS), PixelStatusLines / PixelStatusBox, PixelWindows (one window at a time).
- DEV TOOLS (Editor / dev builds): Dev Hub (F9) with widgets, To-Do (Assets/Resources/Todo.txt), Changelog (Assets/Resources/Changelog.txt, never cleared), inspector, shapes, mockups, balance report, snapshot probe (F10 / F11).

PROJECT RULES (from CLAUDE.md)
- Every variable editable in Unity ([SerializeField] private + [Tooltip]); field initializers don't reach already-serialized components (use Awake / OnValidate fix-ups).
- Enum values are only appended. Copy the whole Assets/Scripts folder together. Compile check: tools/compile-check/check.sh.
- Save compatibility is not a concern during development. Skill packs are chosen only in the Skills window. Player-facing texts say "husk".
GENERATED: 2026-10-16 14:42 UTC

=== 2. DATA MODEL (from CLAUDE.md) ===
- PixelTier: name, colour/UIColor, PixelType enum (see PixelClicker.cs; values are only ever appended so saves stay valid), TierUnlockMode (PreviousTierThreshold / ShopOnly), spawnWeight, randomPayout + payoutMin/Max (a random whole amount per harvest, e.g. Electric 1-15; amountPerClick = the average for estimates; migrated by packDataVersion 3), clicksToCollect (tough pixels: only the last click pays; PixelClicker.PixelHit event for the others), startingAmount, translucent / materialOverride, glow + glowIntensity (emission on a runtime material copy + point light), vacuum flag, spawnSwitch (Auto = Glass, Luminescent and the IsSpecialType types get a spawn toggle in the Toggles window, see PixelTier.CanSwitchOff; spawnDisabled saved; CanSpawn used by PickSpawnTier), flyAway/flyDirection/flySpeed (old pixel streaks away in a straight camera-relative line with a smooth tapered trail instead of falling - Meteor; flyRandomDirection + flyAngleRange 10-170 deg = a random direction on the top half of the screen), count, totalCollected, unlocked.
- Every pixel type has a CARD in PixelBlockCatalog.cs (tags = what kind of block it is, Pixel Guide text, old-pixel text, borrowed sounds / effects): give a new pixel type its card there.
- Spawn: weighted random among unlocked tiers only (PickSpawnTier).
- Tough pixels raise PixelHit for hits 1..n-1 and PixelFinalHit (UI shows 'n/n', then the payout popup follows after toughPayoutDelay) on the breaking click. Click flow (CollectInternal(bool automatic)): AddCurrency → PixelCollected event → click effects → Vacuum() if vacuum tier → SpawnFallingCopy → roll next tier → Materialize.
- Events: CurrencyChanged, CurrencyGained, PixelCollected(tier, amount, automatic), PixelsVacuumed(vacuumTier, total, count), VacuumBreakdown(double[] perTier).
- Shop reward tiers are added to PixelClicker at runtime via EnsureTier (locked until bought). EnsureTier inserts new shop tiers before the Dragon Cubes (rare drops stay at the END of the tier list). EnsureTier and Awake must only raise Count, never wipe typed starting values.
- PixelShop.EnsureDefaultPacks() (called from Awake and editor-only OnValidate via delayCall) walks the BuiltInPacks table and inserts any missing built-in pack into the serialized list so it is editable in the Inspector. One toggle: addDefaultPacks. Context menu: "Add Missing Default Packs". Older saved data is upgraded by MigrateOldPackData(). It also removes a leftover "Pong" pack (name or minigame id pong) from the saved list and renumbers the other packs' requirements.

=== 4. BLOCK CATALOG (PixelBlockCatalog.cs: type | tags | Pixel Guide text) ===
White | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Gray | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Black | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Red | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Green | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Blue | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Glass | None | A see-through pixel. It is also the material for potions and the Pixel Bank's storage upgrade.
Vacuum | None | Clicking it sucks up every old pixel lying around: they swirl into it and their stored value is paid out at once. The old Vacuum pixel then keeps those pixels floating inside its glass body. While a Vacuum potion is running only every 10th click vacuums.
Obsidian | None | A Mining ore: black volcanic glass that takes 
Luminescent | None | A glowing pixel. Its old pixel stays bright as it falls and leaves a fading trail of light.
Singularity | None | Rare. Its old pixel becomes a gravity well: for 5 minutes it drags up to 20 nearby old pixels toward it, keeps them from despawning, and captures meteors that fly close. Wells packed close together starve each other, so space them out. Feeding one to a black hole counts toward the Event Horizon achievement.
Ghost | None | A gray, gelatinous blob. Clicking it is just a payout; its old pixel is the one that drifts away.
Meteor | None | A cratered rock that needs 3 hits; the lava cracks glow brighter with each one. Its old pixel shoots off across the screen, and every one that does has a chance of knocking a star (Stardust) down beside the cube.
Solar | None | A blazing sun. Every Solar click raises <b>Daylight</b> (it fades slowly): the higher it is, the faster seeds and Seed pets grow and the more hovering Solar pixels pay. Old Solar pixels hover and pay 8% of their value every 4 seconds; five touching ones never despawn and glow with a yellow aura.
Electric | None | Pays a random whole amount (
DragonCube1 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube2 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube3 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube4 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube5 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube6 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube7 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
Mirror | None | A chrome pixel. Clicking it pays its own value and then repeats the last payout of the previous different pixel you clicked.
Seed | Farm | A shell you dig open. Every hit throws dirt; the click that cracks it pays and plants three sprouts. Each sprout grows a random unlocked pixel over about 6 seconds (rarer types are likelier; never Vacuum or Singularity) and then pops off as an old pixel.
Water | Farm | A wobbly blue jelly. Its old pixel splashes the first time it lands, paying nothing, and instantly speeds up every growing sprout and planted Seed pet within about 5 pixel-widths (each only accepts water every 0.75 seconds).
Fire | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a flaming pixel. Clicking it blasts a ring of flames outward from the cube.
Copper | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Tin | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Iron | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Lead | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Zinc | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Nickel | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Treasure | Standalone | A chest that needs a random 1-20 hits, decided when it appears. Opening it pays the pixel's value x the hits x (1 + 0.15 x hits) and pours out a pile of random unlocked pixels at once: 2 plus one per hit, up to 24.
Nuclear | Standalone | A radioactive pixel with a toxic glow. Its old pixel ages every other old pixel within about 4 pixel-widths 40 times faster, so they disappear without paying - clear space before it lands.
Wood | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a solid log. A couple of chops and it pays a fair amount.
Rock | Standalone | Rock: Mining's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low mining level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the ores show up more.
Dirt | Farm+Standalone | Farming's filler block: it takes a few digs (shrinking each time, with digging noises) and pays almost nothing. At low farming level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the other farm blocks show up more. Old Dirt pixels lying near a growing sprout also make it grow faster.
Fertilizer | Farm+Standalone | Farming block. Clicking it gives every growing sprout and Seed pet on the field an instant jump in growth.
Pollen | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming block that floats. An old Pollen pixel close to a sprout when it pops doubles the pay of the pixel that sprout grows.
Wheat | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming block. It pays more for every sprout currently growing on the field (up to a cap).
Weevil | Farm+Standalone | Farming pest. While a Weevil sits on the cube it eats one growing sprout every few seconds until you click it away.
GoldenFruit | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming prize. A growing sprout has a 3% chance to produce one instead of a normal pixel; it pays a lot.
Wire | Standalone+Electrical | A copper wire, and a <b>conductor</b>: old Wire pixels carry the arcs that link devices and other conductors.
Battery | Standalone+Electrical | A conductor that takes 
Capacitor | Standalone+Electrical | Every other click you make stores one charge in it (up to 40). When you click the Capacitor it pays its value x (1 + 0.15 per stored charge), then the charge resets.
Resistor | Standalone+Electrical | A conductor that takes 
Transistor | Standalone+Electrical | Clicking it makes the NEXT pixel you collect pay double.
Fuse | Standalone+Electrical | Pays 3 times as much if you collect it within 3 seconds of it appearing, and only 0.3 times as much if you are slower.
Bulb | Standalone+Electrical | Pays 10% more for every old conductor pixel (Electric, Wire, Battery, Resistor) lying around, up to 30 of them.
Pest | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD. Never worth clicking: hit it 
GasPocket | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD. Never worth clicking: hit it 
Bark | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: rough bark. One chop, pays a little.
Sap | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a blob of sticky sap. It pays well for one chop, and an old Sap pixel does not bounce when it lands.
Leaf | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a flat green leaf with a pale midrib. One chop, pays little. An old Leaf pixel drifts down slowly instead of dropping.
Heartwood | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block, rare: the glowing red core of a tree. It takes 
Twig | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting filler block: a tangle of thin twigs. One click, almost no pay. Pinecone and Moss are its two siblings and share its spot: at low woodcutting level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the other wood blocks show up more.
HornetNest | Hazard+Standalone | The TERMITE HIVE, a HAZARD. Never worth clicking: break it (
Berry | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block: a juicy berry. One pick, pays a little. It squelches.
Mushroom | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block: a mushroom. It takes 
Herb | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block that is light and leafy. One pick, pays little. An old Herb pixel drifts down slowly instead of dropping.
Truffle | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block, rare: a buried treasure. It takes 
Weeds | Forage+Standalone | Foraging's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low foraging level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the good finds show up more.
Toadstool | Hazard+Standalone | A Foraging HAZARD. One left click sets it off: its spores make you dizzy and the cube jerks away from your cursor after every click for a while (and Foraging XP drops 10% for a minute). One RIGHT click clears it safely - be quick.
Silver | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Gold | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Bedrock | Ore+Standalone | The toughest rock: it takes a random 
Fish | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block, rare: a silvery fish. Clicking it starts a Weak Spots round (the camera zooms in and time slows): hit every spot to catch it and get paid, miss one and it gets away. The auto clicker leaves fish for you. Bait pellets on the floor (and fish and water in a Bait Bucket) make fish come up more often. With the Weak Spots minigame switched off in Toggles, a fish takes a random 7-25 clicks to reel in instead.
Seaweed | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block: see-through seaweed. One click, pays little. An old Seaweed pixel sinks slowly instead of dropping.
Shell | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block: a glossy shell. One click, pays a fair amount.
Pearl | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block, rare: a shining pearl. It takes 
Driftwood | Fish+Standalone | Fishing's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low fishing level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the good catches show up more.
Jellyfish | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD, and an electric one. Never worth clicking: hit it 
Bait | Fish+Standalone) | 
Bottle | Fish+Standalone | Fishing SPECIAL block, rare: a message in a bottle. It takes 
Whale | Fish+Standalone | Fishing SPECIAL block, very rare: a whale. Click it once and you hook it: it yanks the line and swims fast away from your mouse, dashing about the screen, while you chase it and click. It needs a random 
Mold | Hazard+Standalone | A Foraging HAZARD. One left click bursts it and the mold covers EVERY husk on the field; moldy husks die after a while unless you shake them clean (grab one and shake it). One RIGHT click clears it safely - be quick.
WoodChips | Standalone) | 
Tinder | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a tuft of dry grass and fluff. (Firemaking is a test skill: its mechanics are still to come.)
Kindling | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a bundle of thin, dry sticks.
Firewood | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a split log, ready for the fire. It takes 
Flint | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a hard striker stone that throws sparks.
Ember | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a glowing coal, still hot.
Ash | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking's filler block: grey ash. One click, almost no pay.
GoldenSnitch | Standalone | An extremely rare drop. It never stays on the cube: it sprouts wings and darts round the screen. Keep your mouse on it to charge the bar (it does not charge while your mouse is off it), then click when the closing white ring meets the gold one. Catch it for a huge reward - 400 clicks' worth of your best pixel and maybe an Ultra pixel. Miss three times, or wait too long, and it escapes.
Wildfire | Firemaking+Hazard+Standalone | A Firemaking HAZARD. Hit it 
GreatOak | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block, very rare: a mighty oak. It takes a random 
Whetstone | Standalone | Woodcutting block: a hard grey stone for sharpening. It takes 
Acorn | Standalone | Woodcutting block: an acorn. One click, pays a little. The trick is its OLD pixel: every acorn lying on the field raises the chance that a wood block turns into a Great Oak (they only have to be there - no gathering). With enough of them out the chance is far higher. Acorns despawn like other old pixels, so keep them coming.
Pesticide | Standalone | Woodcutting block: a canister of pesticide. It takes 
Charcoal | Firemaking+Standalone | Charcoal: made by the Kiln, and a Firemaking block.
GodSlifer | RareCollectible | One of the three Egyptian God Cubes: Slifer the Sky Dragon, a red cube held together by storm lightning. The rarest kind of drop in the game (roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one.
GodRa | RareCollectible | One of the three Egyptian God Cubes: Ra the Winged Dragon, a blazing golden cube wrapped in a sun's corona. The rarest kind of drop in the game (roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one.
GodObelisk | RareCollectible | One of the three Egyptian God Cubes: Obelisk the Tormentor, a deep blue stone cube that sheds dust. The rarest kind of drop in the game (roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one.
StoneSpace | RareCollectible+PrimeCube | One of the six Prime Cubes, an extremely rare drop (like the God Cubes, roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one of each. Hold all six and the Wizard will offer you a Gauntlet to bind them.
StoneMind | RareCollectible+PrimeCube | One of the six Prime Cubes, an extremely rare drop (like the God Cubes, roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one of each. Hold all six and the Wizard will offer you a Gauntlet to bind them.
StoneReality | RareCollectible+PrimeCube | One of the six Prime Cubes, an extremely rare drop (like the God Cubes, roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one of each. Hold all six and the Wizard will offer you a Gauntlet to bind them.

InfinityCubelette | RareCollectible | Forged with the Prime Gauntlet from all six Prime Cubes. It never drops and only one can exist.
Pinecone | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting filler block: a scaly pinecone. One click, almost no pay. It shares the filler's spot with Twig and Moss, so it comes up a lot at low woodcutting level and less as you level up.
Moss | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting filler block: a soft cushion of moss. One click, almost no pay. It shares the filler's spot with Twig and Pinecone, so it comes up a lot at low woodcutting level and less as you level up.

=== 6. SHOP PACKS (PixelShopDefaults.cs Create...Pack: method | first displayName | hidden in shop?) ===
CreateRgbPack | RGB Pack | hidden | Red, Green and Blue pixels in the random spawn pool. Cubie hands these over on his second visit.
CreateGlassPack | Glass Pixel | shown | Adds the see-through Glass pixel to the random spawn pool (switched on by default; you can switch it off in the Toggles window) and unlocks Crafting.
CreateVacuumPack | Vacuum Pixel | shown | Adds the rare Vacuum pixel. Clicking it sucks up every old pixel and collects them again.
CreateObsidianPack | Obsidian Pixel | shown | Adds the tough Obsidian pixel. It takes {clicks} clicks to collect, but pays more.
CreateLuminescentPack | Luminescent Pixel | shown | Adds the glowing Luminescent pixel, which pays more per click.
CreateGhostPack | Ghost Hunt | shown | A faint ghost cube floats across the screen now and then. Click it for a random pixel buff.
CreateGhostPixelPack | Ghost Pixel | shown | Adds the faint, see-through Ghost pixel to the spawn pool. Pays well.
CreateBombPack | Bomb Defusal | shown | A bomb with red, green and blue wires appears now and then. Cut the wire of the colour you hold the most of before the timer runs out, or it blows up your old pixels. Defusing earns bomb parts.
CreatePadPack | Ultra Pad | shown | Four round holes open in the floor, one near each corner of the screen. The marked hole (a spinning pixel floats in its centre) wants that pixel: drop it in for its value and a chance at an Ultra pixel. Anything else that falls into a hole is lost. The marker jumps to another hole after every correct drop.
CreateMeteorStrikePack | Meteor Strike | shown | A huge meteor drifts slowly across the screen now and then. Every click on it chips off a meteor chunk.
CreateEvilTreePack | Evil Tree | shown | Now and then an evil tree sprouts on the floor and grows into a giant. It flails about, so it is hard to hit. Chop through its health bar to fell it for Magic Wood and Woodcutting XP; if you take too long it sinks back into the ground.
CreateMeteorPixelPack | Meteor Pixel | shown | Adds the fiery Meteor pixel. When clicked it doesn't fall - it streaks away like a meteor.
CreateSortPack | Sorting Race | shown | Now and then some old pixels freeze in a jumbled grid. Click two pixels to swap them and group each type together before the timer runs out for a bonus payout.
CreateDashPack | Cube Dash | shown | Now and then the cube dashes around the screen, hanging in one spot before zipping to the next. The auto clicker switches off: click it as often as you can. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateTrafficPack | Red Light Green Light | shown | A traffic light appears beside the cube. Click on green to score, never on red (it costs you), and on yellow click slowly, in time with the circle that closes around the cube. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every point.
CreateEvadePack | Cube Evasion | shown | Now and then the cube runs away from your mouse, sliding along the edges and slipping out of corners. The auto clicker switches off: corner it and click. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateBlinkPack | Cube Blink | shown | Now and then the cube shrinks to nothing and teleports to a random spot, hides for a second or two, then pops back and stays a few seconds before teleporting again. The auto clicker switches off: click it whenever it shows. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateBreakoutPack | Breakout | shown | Now and then (with 30+ old pixels on screen) the old pixels become a wall of bricks. Bounce the ball with your paddle and break them: each one pays 10x its worth.
CreateSnakePack | Snake | shown | Now and then every old pixel stops despawning and freezes. Steer a snake with the arrow keys and eat them all without biting yourself for 10x their worth.
CreateSolarPixelPack | Solar Pixel | shown | Adds the blazing Solar pixel, forged from stardust. Meteor pixels sometimes knock a star out of the sky - collect them.
CreateElectricPixelPack | Electric Pixel | shown | Adds the Electric pixel: a cube held together by crackling lightning. Pays a random amount every harvest, overcharges the auto clicker when clicked and arcs to nearby devices to keep them running.
CreateElectricianPack | Electrician's Pack | shown | Unlocks the Electrician skill and its eight pixels: Electric, Wire, Battery, Capacitor, Resistor, Transistor, Fuse and Bulb. While the skill is in play (Skills window) they replace the basic cubes. Wire, Battery and Resistor carry your device links, a Battery also sets off an Overcharge, a Capacitor stores charge from every other click, a Transistor doubles your next click, a Fuse pays big if you click it fast and a Bulb shines brighter the more wired pixels lie around.
CreateSeedPixelPack | Seed Pixel | shown | Adds the Seed pixel: a brown shell you dig at with many clicks to crack. A sprout comes out, plants itself and grows a random old pixel that pops off.
CreateWaterPixelPack | Water Pixel | shown | Adds the Water pixel: a wobbling blue jelly cube. Old Water pixels splash when they hit the ground (and are used up); sprouts and the Seed pet near the splash grow and sow much faster for a while.
CreateFirePixelPack | Firemaker's Pack | hidden | TEST: Adds the Firemaking skill and its blocks: Tinder, Kindling, Firewood, Flint, Ember, Fire and Charcoal, with plain Ash as the filler and a Wildfire hazard. They only spawn while Firemaking is the pack in play (Skills window). Mechanics still to come.
CreateNuclearPixelPack | Nuclear Pixel | hidden | TEST: Adds the Nuclear pixel, a radioactive cube. Its old pixel is so radioactive that every old pixel lying near it despawns almost at once.
CreateTreasurePixelPack | Treasure Pixel | shown | Adds the Treasure pixel: a chest that takes a random 1 to 20 clicks to open. The more clicks it took, the bigger the reward, and when it bursts open a pile of other pixels pours out.
CreateProspectorPack | Prospector's Pack | shown | Adds the Mining skill and ten ore pixels (Copper, Tin, Iron, Lead, Zinc, Nickel, Silver, Gold, Obsidian and Bedrock): rocks that crack and shed chips as you hit them, break in a shower of nuggets and are sometimes a glittering rich vein that pays big. Obsidian and Bedrock take 15-20 clicks but pay a lot. The ores only spawn while Mining is the skill in play (Skills window), plus plain Stone as filler and a Gas Pocket to watch out for.
CreateWoodPixelPack | Lumberjack's Pack | shown | Adds the Woodcutting skill and the wood blocks: Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood (rare) and the fillers Twig, Pinecone and Moss, plus a Termite Hive to watch out for and a Whetstone (hold it with Block Hold and your clicks count for more). They only spawn while Woodcutting is the pack in play (Skills window). The fillers are useless and most of what you see at first; as your woodcutting level rises it gets rarer and the other blocks show up more.
CreateForagerPack | Forager's Pack | shown | Adds the Foraging skill and five foraged blocks: Berry, Mushroom, Herb, Truffle (rare) and Weeds, in a truly random rotation, plus two hazards (Mold and Toadstool: one right click clears them, one left click sets them off). They only spawn while Foraging is the pack in play (Skills window). Weeds are a useless filler that is most of what you see at first; as your foraging level rises they get rarer and the good finds show up more.
CreateAnglerPack | Angler's Pack | shown | Adds the Fishing skill: Water (very common), Seaweed, Shell, rare Pearl and rare Fish, plus a stinging Jellyfish to watch out for, and two special finds: a message in a bottle (a treasure map!) and a mighty whale. They only spawn while Fishing is the pack in play (Skills window). Clicking a Fish starts a Weak Spots round: hit every spot to catch it, miss and it gets away. Water is a plain filler that gets rarer as your fishing level rises.
CreateFarmerPack | Farmer's Pack | shown | Adds the Farming skill and eight farm blocks: Dirt, Seed, Water, Fertilizer, Pollen, Wheat, Weevil and Golden Fruit. They only spawn while Farming is the pack in play (Skills window) and are a farm in themselves: seeds grow sprouts, water, dirt and fertilizer speed them up, pollen doubles a sprout's pixel, wheat pays more the busier your field is, and weevils eat sprouts until you click them.
CreateMirrorPixelPack | Mirror Pixel | shown | Adds the Mirror pixel: polished chrome that reflects the last other pixel you collected, so clicking it pays that pixel's amount again.
CreateBlackholePack | Black Hole | shown | A black hole sometimes opens on the floor and swallows old pixels, feeding the singularity.
CreateSingularityPack | Singularity Pixel | shown | Adds the tough Singularity pixel, forged from what the black hole swallowed. Pays hugely.
CreateIntervalUpgradePack | Faster Clicking | shown | Auto clicker interval: {current}s  →  {next}s
CreateBankPack | Pixel Bank | shown | Store old pixels in a bank. Press B for a hose: right-click sucks a pixel up, left-click spits the selected one out, scroll to choose.
CreateBlockHoldPack | Block Hold | hidden | Hold the right mouse button on the cube's block to pull it out of the rotation: the cube gives you a new one while it stays held. Let go and it goes back. Holding drains a meter; if it runs dry the block is put back and the meter takes longer to recharge.
CreateOakTreesTestPack | Oak Trees (test) | hidden | TEST: Woodcutting tree mode. While Woodcutting is in play the cube rolls filler blocks (Twig, Leaf, Wood) and every few blocks an Oak tree arrives and plays out in order: leaves, a run of wood, then its special slots. Some trees are infested and end in a Termite Hive. Timber! when the last block is chopped.
CreateRobotPack | UFO Helper | shown | A little UFO hovers about the map. Click it, choose a consumable and place its ghost: every time that device runs out it beams a new one down there, as long as you have one to spare. It can also abduct loose pixels into your Pixel Bank.
CreateBankStoragePack | Bank Storage | shown | Pixel Bank capacity: {current}  →  {next}
CreateOldPixelCapPack | Old Pixel Capacity | shown | How many old pixels can lie around at once: {current}  →  {next}
CreateTimeStopPack | Time Stop | shown | Press T to stop time - everything freezes, even countdowns. Press T again to resume.
CreateTimeSlowPack | Time Slow | shown | Press S to slow time down (it shares the Time Stop energy meter). Fast things, like meteor pixels, slow to a crawl - slow enough to grab with Pixel Grabbing.
CreateComboPack | Combo Meter | shown | Click fast to build a combo. Reaching each combo milestone boosts your clicks; every level unlocks a higher tier. Top multiplier: x{current}  →  x{next}
CreateClicksUpgradePack | Multi-Click | shown | Clicks per auto click: {current}  →  {next}
CreateAutoClickerPack | Auto Clicker | shown | Clicks the cube for you every {interval} seconds.

=== 7. DEVICES (PixelConsumables.cs CreateDefault...: method | name | description) ===
CreateDefaultDevice | (set at runtime) | 
CreateDefaultFan | Fan | Place it and turn it. It gently blows old pixels along a cone up to {radius} units long for {duration} seconds.
CreateDefaultMagnet | Magnet | Place it near the cube. For {duration} seconds it pulls every loose old pixel within {radius} units into a heap around it, without collecting them.
CreateDefaultStorageCrate | Storage Crate | A sectioned crate with room for 10 pixels (2 rows of 5). It needs no switching on and stays until you remove it. Drag pixels onto it (Pixel Grabbing) and click the crate to store them in the next free sections; click a stored pixel to pick it up again.
CreateDefaultRecycler | Recycler | Drop up to 10 husks in, then press its red button: the lid closes and each one pays back 50% of its value.
CreateDefaultFunnel | Funnel | A wide glass funnel that hangs under the clicker cube and catches the pixels that fall off it. They are carried down a see-through tube and come out at an output on the floor - click where you want it. Point the output at a conveyor belt to feed it. Only one stands at a time: placing it again moves it.
CreateDefaultWoodChipper | Wood Chipper | A trailer-mounted wood chipper, bought from the Lumberjack. Wood pixels dropped into its hopper are shredded for Woodcutting XP and every few of them make a Wood Chips block that shoots out of its chute (a conveyor belt opening in front of the chute latches on). Anything that is not wood damages it, and enough of that breaks it for good.
CreateDefaultQuantumOrganizer | Quantum Organizer | A pad with walls. Switch it on, then drop a pixel onto the pad to bind that pixel type to it (up to three types). Every loose old pixel of a bound type is dematerialized with a glitch and rematerializes inside the box as storage (up to 60 pixels). Click the box to let some out again; everything comes out when its {duration} seconds run out.
CreateDefaultMagnetSorter | Magnetized Sorter | Craft it from a Sorter and a Magnet. Its magnet pulls loose old pixels from an arc in front of it into a box. Click an exit pipe to grab it and click again to put it down anywhere; scroll over a pipe to choose which stored pixel type it shoots out.
CreateDefaultMagicBeans | Magic Beans | Plant them (right-click in the Inventory, then click the floor). A tall beanstalk grows with a handful of random cubes along it, and one of them is a Dragon Cube you don't hold yet.
CreateDefaultSprinkler | Sprinkler | Place it near your seeds. For {duration} seconds it waters every growing sprout and Seed pet within {radius} units once a second, so they grow far faster.
CreateDefaultSorter | Sorter | Wraps around the cube. For {duration} seconds every pixel you collect is spat out of its pipe in a stream. Scroll to aim the pipe.
CreateDefaultComboFuel | Combo Fuel | Hold up to 3. When your combo bar would run out, one is used automatically: the combo is kept and the bar slowly refills instead. Click before it is full again to carry on.
CreateDefaultGhostBait | Ghost Bait | Right-click to use (needs Ghost Hunt). The next ghost appears sooner, and three ghosts float by, slower than usual. You can only hold one.
CreateDefaultBaitBucket | Bait Bucket | Right-click to set it out (needs Fishing as the skill in play). A bucket stands on the floor until you stop Fishing. Drag Fish and Water pixels into it (Pixel Grabbing): they are stored with their value instead of splashing or fading, and every stored fish makes more fish show up. Click the bucket to take a pixel out again. You can only hold one.
CreateDefaultGauntlet | Prime Gauntlet | Right-click to use. A golden cube with a socket on every face: set the six Prime Cubes in it and forge the Prime Cubelette. The Gauntlet and the cubes are used up. You can only hold one.
CreateDefaultConveyorBelt | Conveyor Belt Kit | Right-click to lay conveyor belts: click a point, then another, and a belt is built between them (short pieces make curves; scroll raises or lowers the end). Old pixels that land on a belt are carried along it. Right click ends a chain; hold the right button on a belt to remove it. The kit is not used up. You can only hold one.
CreateDefaultPetTreat | Pet Treat | Right-click to use. Your pets go hyper and glow for a minute, and the extra spawn chance they give is doubled. Hold up to 5.
CreateDefaultChargeBooster | Charge Booster | Place it. Electric pixels that link to it fill its meter and are used up. When the meter is full, click it for a few seconds of super-charged auto clicking. Dissolves after 100 auto clicks.
CreateDefaultLightningRod | Lightning Rod | Place it. Every few seconds lightning strikes it and turns every old pixel around it into an Electric pixel. Dissolves after 8 strikes.
CreateDefaultDragonSeed | Dragon Seed | A glowing seed. Plant it: it sprouts into a Dragon Cube you don't have yet.
CreateDefaultSeed | (set at runtime) | Right-click it in the inventory, then click the floor to plant it. It sprouts and grows one

=== 9. SCRIPT NOTES (the header comment of every script that has one) ===
--- Editor/PixelDefineSymbols.cs ---
Editor/PixelDefineSymbols.cs Editor-only ([InitializeOnLoad]): adds the scripting define PIXEL_URP to the project when the Universal Render Pipeline package is installed (and removes it when it is not). PixelFilters.cs uses #if PIXEL_URP for its post-processing volume (UNITY_PIPELINE_URP is only defined inside Unity's own packages, not in Assembly-CSharp). The first time it runs it changes Project Settings > Player > Scripting Define Symbols, which makes Unity recompile once. Menu: Pixel Clicker > Refresh Define Symbols (runs it again).
--- Editor/PixelShaderKeepAlive.cs ---
Editor/PixelShaderKeepAlive.cs Editor-only ([InitializeOnLoad], menu Pixel Clicker > Rebuild Shader Keep-Alive Materials): creates tiny materials in Assets/Resources/PixelShaderKeep with _EMISSION / transparent keywords switched on (URP Lit, Simple Lit, Standard). Needed because builds strip shader variants no saved material uses, which killed every glow / lava crack that is switched on from code with EnableKeyword (Luminescent, Solar, Meteor, bomb / pad lights). Any new runtime keyword toggle needs a matching keep-alive material. Because that alone did not fix builds, glow no longer relies on keywords only: PixelLooks.AddGlowShell draws a thin unlit coloured copy of the mesh (Sprites/Default, OverlayMaterial) over every glowing / emissive non-translucent pixel (plus a separate soft billboard halo AddGlowHalo, hidden with hideGlowHalo) (live cube UpdateGlowShell, falling copies, CreateDisplayPixel; disableGlowShell, glowShellExtra on PixelClicker - stored as "off" / "extra" so a scene serialized before the fields existed still gets the shell) and over the meteor rock (lava-crack overlay texture rockLavaOverlay). Face marks (Vacuum circles, colour-blind shapes) use PixelLooks.OverlayMaterial (render queue after the pixel).
--- Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs ---
Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs Editor-only menu for the game's system components (the list is PixelSystems.All): - Pixel Clicker > Add Missing Systems To Scene: every system that is not in the open scene yet is added to a 'Pixel Systems' object, on a child per group (UI, Core, Skills, Rares and Shows, Characters, Camera, World). Then they exist in EDIT mode, so their Inspector values can be changed and saved with the scene (before, most were only added when Play started, and every change was lost when it stopped). Systems already in the scene, wherever they are, are left alone. - Pixel Clicker > Move Systems Off The Clicker: every system found ANYWHERE in the scene outside 'Pixel Systems' (the clicker, its children, the camera...) is moved into its group with all its Inspector values (Copy Component + Paste Component As New, then the old one is removed); if the group already had a default copy (from Add Missing Systems), that default is removed so the hand-set values win. Asks first, listing them. Both work outside Play mode only, can be undone (Ctrl+Z) and mark the scene as changed - save the scene to keep the result.
--- PixelAchievements.cs ---
PixelAchievements.cs Modular achievements: Inspector list of Achievement (id, title, description, Kind, pixelType, target). New goal types = add a Kind value + a case in GetProgress; or Register(achievement, Func<double>) from code. One "{Pixel} Pet" achievement per pixel type (RegisterPetAchievements, ids pet_<Type>, single-tier custom, earned when PixelPets.IsOwned; addPetAchievements). Three more code- registered achievements (RegisterUsageAchievements / RegisterFeedAchievements: "Event Horizon" = feed a Singularity pixel to a black hole (PixelBlackholeMinigame.PixelSwallowed event -> PixelStats.SingularitiesFed); "Potion Taster" = every buyable potion drunk once, "Gadgeteer" = every device kind placed once; progress from PixelStats PotionsUsed / FansUsed / VacuumDevicesUsed / SortersUsed). Achievements have tiers (tiers[], earnedTiers); defaults per pixel type: tiered "Collect" (lifetime value, totalCollected: 10, 100, 1K, 10K, 100K, 1M) and tiered "Click" (PixelTier.timesCollected, saved: 10, 50, 250, 1K, 5K, 25K; Kind.ClickPixelType). The unlock POPUP sits centred above the combo meter by default (DefaultPopupPosition); hold the left mouse button on it (popupHoldSeconds 0.5, a ring fills round the cursor, PixelHold with useLeft) to pick it up, click to PIN it anywhere; the thin button on its far right collapses it to the icon box and expands it again with a shorter hold (popupCollapseHoldSeconds 0.3, saved); Settings > 'Reset achievement popup position' (ResetPopupPin) forgets the pin. The pinned position is the popup's LEFT edge; the pin is saved in (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.AchievementPopupX/Y, inside the black margins), right-click to put it back. Saved as "id:tiers". Unlocks quietly/with popup, ids saved by PixelSaveGame. Auto-added by PixelLog.
--- PixelAimCursor.cs ---
PixelAimCursor.cs The AIM CURSOR for edge-click aiming (PixelBurstPop): while the pointer is on the clickable cube (PixelClicker.TryGetBlockAt, isLive) the system cursor is hidden and a code-drawn cursor follows the pointer on its own top canvas: - near the middle of the cube (where husks scatter at random) a small spinning CUBE; - towards an edge (PixelClicker.TryGetAim sharpness > 0) an ARROW pointing the way the husk will fly, turning smoothly as the pointer moves round the edge; the cube fades into the arrow as the aim sharpens (the arrow grows with it), tinted by the current block's colour; - each click gives it a little punch; it pops in when the pointer reaches the cube. Anywhere else (off the cube, over UI, menus, first person, clicks blocked, edge aiming off) the normal cursor is back. Only shows the cursor it hid (Cursor.visible) - first person keeps its own. disableAimCursor switches it off. A system component (PixelSystems, UI group).
--- PixelArcGraphic.cs ---
PixelArcGraphic.cs A UI Graphic that draws a curved band (part of a ring) with ROUND caps at both ends and soft (feathered) edges, built as a mesh in OnPopulateMesh - no sprite, no radial fill. Angles are in degrees, 0 = east (right), counter-clockwise. radius / halfWidth are fractions of half the rect's smaller side. Set StartDegrees / EndDegrees to grow or shrink it (the Block Hold meter's track, glow, fill and ready pop).
--- PixelAttachment.cs ---
PixelAttachment.cs Attaching old pixels to each other (the first person BUILD controls, PixelFirstPerson): the snap maths, the glue and the prying off. * PixelAttachment.TrySnap(held, pool, ...): for the cube you hold, looks at the nearby old pixels and finds where it would sit if it were stuck to one of their faces. The held cube's orientation must be within maxAngle of an orientation ALIGNED with that pixel (one of the 24 cube rotations, NearestAligned); the spot is on one of the pixel's six faces (its centre pushed out by both half sizes) and slides along the face in half-cube steps (-0.5, 0, +0.5), so cubes can stack straight or like bricks. It must be within snapDistance of where you hold it. The result is the pose of the white ghost; clicking attaches there. * PixelAttachment.Attach(a, b): a FixedJoint from a to b (unbreakable, colliders ignore each other) and a PixelAttachment component on both. Attached pixels keep their lifetime frozen (OldPixelDespawn.Held is re-asserted every frame) so a build does not decay; they can't be picked up, only pried off: PixelAttachment.Detach(x) removes every joint to and from x (the first person player holds the RIGHT mouse button on it). Joints are not saved: loading a save leaves the cubes loose. * A whole build moves as one: Group(x) = every cube glued to x; the player carries it (PixelFirstPerson) after TakeApart removes the joints, and the links are glued back on release. * Foundations: TryGroundSnap lets a cube rest flat on the ground in any of the 24 orientations; AttachToWorld pins it with a joint that has no other body (pry it off like any cube).
--- PixelAudio.cs ---
PixelAudio.cs (UFO beam) The main-menu UFO's abduction plays ufo_beam (code-made PixelSynth.UfoBeam: a rising wobbling hum with a shimmer and a pop at the end, ~2.6 s; stopped if the abduction is aborted).
--- PixelAudioCues.cs ---
PixelAudioCues.cs Music CUES (part of partial class PixelAudio): the gameplay music changes at triggers and fades in / out. An Inspector list (Music Cues: name, trigger, param, priority, clip or playlist track number, volume, loop, fade in / out seconds). Triggers: Minigame (param = the minigame id: ghost, bomb, blackhole, eviltree, ...; empty = any takeover minigame; true while it has something on screen), Skill (param = the skill id in play), Night (the day / night cycle is in deep night), FirstPerson, UfoRide (riding the UFO), TitleScreen, Manual (code calls PixelAudio.SetMusicCue(name, on): the God Cube shows and the Dragon wish do). Every 0.25 s the highest-priority cue whose condition is true wins (the later one on a tie); a cue with neither a clip nor a track number is ignored. While a cue plays the normal playlist fades to silence and is PAUSED where it was (cueHoldsPlaylist) and fades back in, resuming, when no cue is wanted; a change from one cue to another fades the first out (its fade out), then the second in (its fade in). A cue that does not loop and ends by itself hands back to the playlist and is not restarted until its condition has gone false once. GameStart = the song you choose to play right after the clicker loads in (give the built-in "After load" cue a clip; Loop off = it plays once, then the playlist carries on; the param may be a number of seconds to cap it). Built-in cues (addDefaultCues: After load, Night, Bomb, Black hole, Evil tree, Ghost hunt, UFO ride, God find, Dragon wish) are added to the list by name when missing, WITHOUT clips: drop a clip (or a playlist track number) into the ones you want. disableCues turns the whole thing off. Mute / music volume apply to cues too.
--- PixelAutoClicker.cs ---
PixelAutoClicker.cs Timer that calls clicker.AutoCollect() (paused while a takeover minigame runs or the cube is shrunk away: PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive / PixelClicker.CubeHidden); Interval / ClicksPerTick properties; Activate() from shop; startRunning for testing.
--- PixelAutosaves.cs ---
PixelAutosaves.cs Autosave history (for testing) + save screenshots. THREE parts in this file: PixelAutosaves (static): every TIMER autosave (not manual saves, quit saves) is also copied into <persistentDataPath>/Autosaves as autosave_<ticks>.json + .meta (when / total pixels / play time, so the list needs no big file read) + .jpg (the screenshot); PixelSaveGame.Save calls Add. At most PixelSaveGame.autosaveHistorySize (100) are kept: Trim deletes the OLDEST when a newer one comes in. List() gives them newest first. PixelSaveShots (static + a hidden runner): a screenshot of the game window (ScreenCapture, shrunk to 320 wide, JPG) next to EVERY save: slot saves go to <save file>.jpg, autosave history to its own .jpg. A rolling copy is taken every few seconds while the game is visible and used when a save happens while paused (the Save / Load window shows the pause menu, not the game). Load(path) returns a cached Texture2D for the hover tooltips (slot rows in the Save / Load window, rows of the autosave window). PixelAutosaveWindow: the separate window opened by the small 'Autosaves' button in the Save / Load window (PixelPauseMenu.BuildSaveLoadPanel): a scrolling list (newest first) with a Load button per row and a hover tooltip with the screenshot; Load = PixelSaveGame.LoadFile (does not change the current slot) and then resumes the game.
--- PixelAxeSynth.cs ---
PixelAxeSynth.cs Code-made sounds for the first person axe and the Great Oak's bird nests (hooked into PixelAudio.SynthFor like PixelOceanSynth): axe_swing (a whoosh of filtered noise that sweeps up and fades), axe_chop (a wooden thock: a quick falling thump + a crack of noise, 3 variants), nest_fall (a soft rustle + a tiny chirp), nest_catch (two bright rising bird tweets). All ids are registered in PixelAudio.CreateDefaultSounds.
--- PixelBaitBucket.cs ---
PixelBaitBucket.cs Bait Bucket (DeviceKind.BaitBucket 14, a Supplies-category consumable; component added by PixelClicker.Awake; disableBaitBucket): right-click it in the Inventory (PixelConsumables.TryUseItem -> PixelBaitBucket.TryPlace, needs Fishing as the skill in play, one at a time) and a code-built red bucket grows in on the floor at a fixed screen spot (baitBucketScreenSpot, 0 = (0.89, 0.19), bucketScale 0 = 1.2 x the main pixel). It stays only while Fishing is the skill in play: switching skills (or Quit > Main menu) shrinks it away and everything inside pops out again as ordinary old pixels. Drag or throw loose old FISH pixels (up to fishCapacity 0 = 20) or WATER pixels (waterCapacity 0 = 40) over it (ScanPixels, like the woodchipper): they are stored with their payout instead of splashing / despawning (a Water pixel dropped in is saved from breaking). Stored fish are live bait: PixelBaitBucket.FishBonus (fishBonusEach 0 = 0.15 per fish, waterBonusEach 0 = 0.05 per water) is added to PixelBait.FishMultiplier, so Fish spawns more. Left-click the bucket (PixelPointerClaim / ClaimsPointer keep the cube and Pixel Grabbing out of it) takes the last stored pixel out into the mouse's hand. Not saved: a bucket is gone after a load (its contents are lost). Its bail is a carrying handle (shared PixelCarryHandles): drag it to move the bucket anywhere on the floor (scroll / Q / E turn it); the bucket's own click steps aside while the bail has the pointer.
--- PixelBalanceReport.cs ---
PixelBalanceReport.cs Dev tool (Dev Tools > Tools > 'Balance report'): writes <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots/balance_<time>.txt and copies it to the clipboard - one plain text / markdown report meant to be pasted into ANY AI. It starts with a READ ME block (what the game is, the balance goal, what every column means, what to answer), then the assumptions, a cross-skill comparison at each reference level (XP per click, minutes to reach each level at the manual and the auto clicking speed, pay per click, flagged outliers), a per-skill block table (share, clicks, pay, role) and the Inspector levers with their current values, and a CSV copy of the comparison. How the numbers are made: no formulas are copied. For every pack the report sets PixelSkills.PreviewPackId / PreviewLevel (read by PixelSkills.ActiveId and the filler / hazard share; PixelTier.CanSpawn counts the pack's own pixels as unlocked via PixelSkills.PreviewForces), so PixelClicker.SpawnShare gives the same shares the real spawn roll uses (filler fade, hazard share, Fishing's fixed table, special blocks, pets), then clears the preview again (try / finally). XP is per collected block (1, x autoXpFactor for auto clicks), as PixelSkills.OnCollected. NOT modelled: Value / pack upgrades and multipliers (base payouts only), minigame XP multiplier, bonus bank, Block Hold, skill-tree effects, the Fishing Weak Spots round, hazard defusing, guests, special blocks that are not unlocked in the save, Standard's rotation beyond what the save has unlocked. The reference speeds / levels are fields on PixelDevTools (Balance report section).
--- PixelBarEdgeFx.cs ---
PixelBarEdgeFx.cs BAR STYLES: what glows along the inner edge of the black bars (the HUD's top / bottom bars and the title screen's bar). PixelBarStyle.Current (Settings > Display 'Bar style', PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarStyle, the player's own like the other look settings - not per save): 0 Neon glow (the original: soft outer + inner gradient and a bright line, PixelBarGlow), 1 Pixel equalizer (columns of pixel cells rising from the edge to the music / sounds: AudioListener.GetSpectrumData, a quiet ambient wobble when nothing plays, falling peak dots), 2 Pixel wave (rolling heights, no audio), 3 Marquee (one row of cells with a light chasing along), 4 Scanner (a comet sweeping to and fro), 5 Thin line (just the line). PixelBarEdgeFx.Create(parent, edgeIsTop) builds one edge (all style parts, built lazily the first time a style needs them); the owner calls Tick(colour, strength) every frame (PixelHud for the two bars, PixelTitleScreen for the title bar's two edges). Cells are a tiled 12 x 12 sprite, columns snap to whole cells; the colour follows the glow colour (a slight hue shift along the row). To add a style: a name in PixelBarStyle.Names and a case in PixelBarEdgeFx.Tick.
--- PixelBeltSorter.cs ---
PixelBeltSorter.cs BELT SORTER (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Belt Sorter'): a low gantry that straddles a conveyor belt anywhere along it and kicks every pixel of ITS TYPE off the side. Placing: the ghost snaps to the belt under the mouse (TrySnap) and the chute is on whichever side of the belt the mouse is (the snapped yaw is turned round for the left side, the chute is always the model's +X). RIGHT CLICK (a quick tap; holding deletes it) turns the sorter round: it swaps to the other side of the belt and its chute / output swaps with it. FILTER SLOT: a tray on top of the beam holds ONE block, shown spinning above it (like a Storage Crate section): let go of a pixel you are dragging over the tray (or throw / drop one onto it: PixelGrab's 'recently held' tag) and it goes into the slot (a block already there is handed back); the slot's type is the filter. LEFT CLICK the shown block to take it out again (it jumps into your hand; PixelBeltSorter.ClaimsPointer makes PixelGrab step aside). With an empty slot the sorter lets everything pass. While a pixel of the filter type rides under the beam, PixelConveyorSystem asks PixelBeltSorter.TryDivert (called from its rider loop): the pixel leaves the belt (a paddle sweeps across), and is thrown out of the side - or put onto a belt that STARTS at the chute's mouth (within ~1.3 cubes), so a second belt can be fed from the side. Other types are untouched. The spot is stored as a distance along the belt (it follows the belt); saved with the tester save (spot, yaw, slot type + amount).
--- PixelBlackholeMinigame.cs ---
PixelBlackholeMinigame.cs Black hole minigame (Minigames tab, a pack with unlocksMinigame = "blackhole"): random-size swirling hole (runtime-drawn spiral textures) opens on the floor; old pixels inside are taken via PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel and spiral in (SpiralIn: eased start, angular speed grows as it falls, rotates to the path and stretches into a curved streak, darkens, fading trail, AbsorbFlash at the centre). While a hole is open it sets OldPixelDespawn.Frozen (old pixels stop ageing, time dilation; freezeOldPixels). A live counter (TextMeshPro, showHoleCounter) floats above each hole but is hidden until a pixel is consumed or the mouse hovers the hole (counterShowOnHover), stays counterVisibleSeconds then fades (counterFadeSeconds), and the first hole ever shows a tip box (PixelNotice, firstHoleMessage, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Hint.BlackHole). The shop row text is requirementLine ("Feed {1} pixels to black holes: {0} / {1}") and the Minigames-tab description is trackerHelp. Owns the singularity tracker (SingularityCount / SingularityThreshold, saved); extends PixelMinigame (id blackhole); the Singularity Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for blackhole. Shop shows the tracker row on the Minigames tab.
--- PixelBlinkMinigame.cs ---
PixelBlinkMinigame.cs Cube Blink minigame (id blink, pack unlocksMinigame = "blink", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 60K each; like Cube Dash: Takeover => true but the cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (30) starts: the cube SHRINKS to nothing (SetCubeShrink(0.02)), TELEPORTS while hidden (PickSpot inside the area* fractions, at least minHop away, SetCubeOffset), stays hidden hiddenMin..Max (1-2 s), grows back and stays visible visibleMin..Max (3-8 s), then vanishes and teleports again (Phase Vanish / Hidden / Appear / Visible / Return). Only manual clicks made while the cube shows count; at the end it pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click via AddCurrency (the clicks still pay normally); stats blink.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_blink, sounds blink_start/_vanish/_appear/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned), picture case in BuildMinigameModel. A success reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 15).
--- PixelBlockCatalog.cs ---
PixelBlockCatalog.cs THE BLOCK CATALOG: one entry ("card") per pixel type holding what used to be spread over several files (steps 1-2 of 'one definition per block'): - tags what KIND of block it is (PixelTag flags: Wood, Forage, Fish, Farm, Hazard, Ore, RetiredOre, Standalone, DragonCube, RareCollectible, PrimeCube, Plant, Electrical). PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock / IsHazard / IsStandalone / IsOre / IsFarm / IsForageBlock / IsFishBlock / IsRetiredOre / IsDragonCube / IsGodCube / IsInfinityStone / IsPlant / IsElectricalBlock read them (through PixelBlockTags.Has). - guide the Pixel Guide / block tooltip explanation (PixelGuideTexts.Describe; a lambda so it can read the tier's own numbers, e.g. t.clicksToCollect). - oldPixel what its old pixel does (PixelGuideTexts.OldPixel; null = the general text). - soundAs play another block's click / break sounds (PixelAudio; null = its own 'pixel_<name>' sounds). - fxAs use another block's break burst (PixelFarmFx.Break; null = its own). ADDING A BLOCK: give it one entry here (tags + guide at least). PixelGuideTexts keeps only the few texts that need its own helpers (the Bait numbers). The tags were generated from the old hand-written lists and the texts moved over word for word (checked to give the same answers).
--- PixelBlockHold.cs ---
PixelBlockHold.cs Block Hold (shop pack with unlocksBlockHold, Upgrades tab, cheap while testing; component added by PixelShop / PixelClicker.Awake, Activate / Deactivate like the Bank): HOLD the RIGHT mouse button on the block on the cube to pull it out of the rotation (PixelClicker.TryPullLive: the cube rolls a new block at once, so you can keep clicking while it stays held). Letting go puts it back IN LINE (PixelClicker.ReturnHeldTier: it comes AFTER the block that is on the cube now, with the hits it had - RollTier hands it out - so a hazard that came up while you held is dealt with first and holding / releasing can never reroll the cube). The hold drains a meter (holdSeconds x GripFactor: half of it at the start - startHoldShare 0.5 - plus 10% of that half at Standard level 3 and again at level 5, gripPerkBoost); letting go starts a short cooldown (releaseCooldownSeconds) before the meter recharges (rechargeSeconds for empty -> full). If the meter runs dry while holding, the block is put back anyway, the meter flashes red and the cooldown is longer (exhaustCooldownSeconds). Can't be held: hazards, Bottle / Whale / Fish / Treasure, or while a fish is being reeled in. A block with hits CAN be held and keeps them. Releasing a held block over a hazard moves the hazard to next in line (it is not removed). Filler can be held. The look (beam, aura, side slide, cursor arc meter) is described at the end of these notes. METER LOOK: the arc fill glides to the real value, goes blue -> amber -> red as it runs low (flickering faster the lower it gets), has a soft glow behind it, a bright pulsing tip riding the end of the fill, quarter-mark dots and a white "ready again" pop when a recharge finishes. All arc layers (track, glow, fill, ready pop) share ONE centre line (RingMid) and have round caps (PixelArcGraphic: a mesh band with round caps, no radial fill); the tip sits on that line and is about the bar's thickness. The meter only shows while the mouse is on the cube or a block is held (meterOnlyOnHover, 0.35 s grace while the next block materializes) and fades out over meterFadeSeconds (0.35) instead of vanishing. The hold lasts 5 seconds by default, 10 for a hold block (PixelBlockRules: Whetstone, Pesticide...). HOLD BLOCKS: what a held block gives is data (PixelBlockRules.HoldRule: rapid extra clicks / payout / XP multiplier + a label shown beside the meter); Whetstone (Woodcutting, 3 hits) = 2 extra rapid clicks (x3 at once). A partly broken block keeps its hits through the hold (TryPullLive -> ReturnHeldTier -> pendingHits), so a whetstone you only noticed after a click or two can still be grabbed. A right press within rightSpamGapSeconds (0.45) of another right press or of a hazard right click never starts a hold (spamming a hazard away must not grab the next block). STALL: PixelTier.stallsHold / stallSeconds (Great Oak): every click on such a block freezes the drain for stallSeconds and adds stallRefill (0.04) of the meter (PixelBlockHold.Stall), the meter turns icy while frozen. UNLOCKED BY THE STANDARD SKILL TREE (node 'hold', requireTreeNode on; 2026-10-16 - before that it was available from the start; requireShopPack off; the shop pack is no longer needed). Tree branches: 'lucky' (+10% stake win chance) -> 'highroller' (stakes grow 50% faster). STAKE (disableStake, stake* fields): holding one of the SIX BASIC blocks (IsStakeBlock: Black, Gray, White, Red, Green, Blue - nothing else) for at least stakeMinSeconds (0.75) puts a stake on it: the value grows 1 + stakeGrowthPerSecond (0.6) x seconds held; on release the outcome is rolled (stakeWinChance 0.6, +/- stakeWinChanceChangeAtFullHold over a full meter) - pays that multiplier or stakeLossValue (0 = nothing) - and travels with the block through the queue (PixelClicker.nextInLineStake -> liveStake, payout x liveStake). It is revealed when the block comes round again: its aura glows green / red (PixelClickerAura.StakeHighlight), a top status line says what it is worth and a good / bad sound plays. While holding, the label by the meter shows the value and the chance. CLICKS: every click during a stake hold adds stakePerClick (0.1) to the stake (stakeClickBonus, in StakeValue); every energyEveryClicks (5) clicks give the meter energyPerClicks (1%), holding or not (OnClick from PixelClicker.CollectInternal; autoClicksCount off = only your own clicks). A first-time tip (stake_intro) shows after 25 manual clicks. Skill XP is not changed by the stake. Not saved (the meter starts full); the pack itself is saved like any pack. No Toggles switch (right click does nothing else on normal blocks).
--- PixelBlockRules.cs ---
PixelBlockRules.cs The shared foundation for SPECIAL blocks and HOLD blocks (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; Instance; the two lists are [Serializable] data, defaults are added in Awake by name if missing, so a copy on a scene object keeps its edits). Docs: docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md. * SPECIAL BLOCK = a rare block that REPLACES a rolled block. SpecialRule: the block's tier definition (PixelTier: name, colour, clicks, pay...), the skill it belongs to (skillId; empty = any), the block types it can replace and the chance. PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier calls PixelBlockRules.TryReplace(clicker, pick) after every normal roll; the tier is created on first use (PixelClicker.EnsureSpecialTier, no spawn weight of its own). Defaults: Great Oak (Woodcutting, replaces any wood block, 1% rising to 25% with 12 Acorn old pixels lying on the field - SpecialRule.hasBoost). Fishing's Whale / Bottle / Pearl still use their own rolls (PixelFishingSpawns) - move them into this table when they are next touched (their chances feed the Pixel Guide's SpawnShare). * HOLD BLOCK = a base block of a skill that gives a bonus while it is held (Block Hold, the right button: PixelBlockHold). HoldRule: the block type, the skill, and what the bonus does: extraClicks (RAPID CLICKS like Overcharge: each manual click is followed by n extra full clicks, burstGapSeconds apart, via AutoCollect; PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockRules.OnManualClick), payoutMultiplier (every payout, CollectInternal) and xpMultiplier (skill XP of that skill, PixelSkills.AddXp) plus a label shown beside the hold meter. Defaults: Whetstone (Woodcutting, 2 extra rapid clicks = x3), Pesticide (Woodcutting, clearsHazard: a left click clears a Hornet Nest at once, PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> ClearHazardNow). HAZARD PENALTY (HazardHoldPenalty list): a hazard that GOES OFF (destroyed by clicks, PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) takes a fraction of the hold meter's current charge; default Hornet Nest 50%. Add a new special / hold block: give it a PixelType (append), a look and spawn data like any block (hold blocks are normal base blocks of their pack: shop pack reward tier + the skill's types list), then add ONE rule here. Behaviour beyond the numbers (the Great Oak's nests) still lives in its own script and listens to PixelClicker.PixelHit.
--- PixelBlockTags.cs ---
PixelBlockTags.cs The PixelTag flags (what KIND of block a pixel type is) and PixelBlockTags.Has / Of. The tags themselves are set on each block's card in PixelBlockCatalog.cs (this file is kept so a copy over an older Assets/Scripts folder does not leave a duplicate behind).
--- PixelBlockTextures.cs ---
Filler blocks / base block textures (PixelSkills.TryGetFiller, PixelClicker.FillerInfo, PixelBlockTextures.cs) Each skill pack can have a 'useless' FILLER block (SkillPack.fillerType = a pixel type name; Farming = Dirt, Mining = Stone, the old Rock block (enum PixelType.Rock kept for saves, now called Stone everywhere in the UI), which is now part of Mining's pixel list, unlocked with the first ore, IsOptIn is false for everything now (Wood is part of Woodcutting), and comes with the Prospector's Pack instead of its own pack (packDataVersion 13 migrates saved packs: drops the single-reward Rock pack, adds Stone to Prospector's, sets it to 1 click / pay 1)). While the pack is in play the filler makes up fillerStartShare (0 = 60%) of all spawns at level 1, fading evenly to fillerEndShare (0 = 10%) at fillerFadeLevel (0 = level 20): PickSpawnTier and SpawnShare (the Pixel Guide's percentages) give it the weight others x share / (1 - share) instead of its own spawn weight. Farming / Mining skill by level, so unlocked pixels come up more as you level. Wood, Dirt and Stone have crisp 64 px pixel-art surfaces (PixelLook.blockSurface Wood / Dirt / Stone, PixelBlockTextures.Get, look colour white; looksVersion 40).
--- PixelBlockTooltip.cs ---
PixelBlockTooltip.cs Block hover tooltip (added by PixelClicker.Awake; Settings > Interface 'Block tooltips', PixelBlockTooltip.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BlockTips, default on, per save; disableTooltip, hoverDelay 0 = 0.18, cardWidth 0 = 560): PixelClicker.TryGetBlockAt(screen, ...) finds the nearest of the clickable cube / an old pixel under the pointer; after the hover delay a card (canvas 655, glow + fancy window + accent strip in the pixel colour, real turning 3D model (PixelTierPicture), name, rarity tag from SpawnShare, worth, a bar for hits (GetLiveProgress) or old-pixel time left, PixelGuideTexts.Describe / OldPixel text, a sheen sweep) fades in beside the pointer. Hidden over UI, paused, title, first person, UFO cockpit, hose or god mode. THREE MODES (PixelBlockTooltip.Mode, Settings > Interface drop-down, PlayerPrefs BlockTipsMode, per save): 0 Full, 1 Minimal (the spinning 3D icon and the name only), 2 Off. ALT: a tap (released within altHoldSeconds 0.25) cycles Full -> Minimal -> Off -> Full and a small toast names the new mode; HOLDING Alt (longer than that) shows the other kind of the current mode while held (Full shows Minimal, Minimal shows Full, Off shows Full) and the release does not cycle. Grabbing a block keeps its tooltip open. The card keeps its spot until the pointer moves 70 units away from where it was placed, then glides there (so it no longer jitters with every mouse move). PINNED: My Window's Info button (PixelMyWindow.HoverDocked) moves the card into the window (UpdateDocked / Undock): it stops following the mouse, is scaled to the window's width, shows whatever is hovered (it keeps the last block while the mouse is over the window) and the window grows to fit it.
--- PixelBombMinigame.cs ---
