PixelBombMinigame.cs Bomb Defusal minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "bomb", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack): every 2-4 min a code-built rectangular bomb (casing, darker front panel, end blocks, corner screws, rails, blinking indicator light, an LCD timer (bezel + lit green screen, red when time is low) low on the front; the red/green/blue wires run straight across the top of the panel, each as two pieces hanging from pivots at its ends plus one wide invisible click box) appears beside the cube, kept fully in view (PixelUIKit.KeepOnScreen also keeps the hint / rising texts and the black hole counter on screen); cutting a wire only snaps that wire in two (CutWire: the pieces swing down); click the wire of the RGB pixel with the highest count at click time (CorrectWire) before timeLimit to defuse (+partsMin..partsMax bomb parts: bombParts = held (spent on Bank Storage via TrySpendParts), bombPartsEarned = ever earned = the tracker bar TrackerCount, like the Ultra bar; held parts are saved through PixelMinigame.ExtraValue / MinigameSave.extra); wrong wire or timeout = explosion flash + PixelClicker.ClearOldPixels(). Extends PixelMinigame (id bomb); sounds bomb_spawn/_click/_tick/_explode.
--- PixelBottleMap.cs ---
PixelBottleMap.cs The Message in a Bottle block (PixelType.Bottle 73, Fishing): PixelLook.bottleMap draws a clear, pale green GLASS CUBE (look: forceTranslucent, bevelled glass rim) with a small 3D treasure map floating inside - a parchment sheet, two rolled ends, a red ribbon and a red X (PixelBottleMap.Attach, called from PixelLooks.AddExtras so the cube, the old copies and the display models all get it). PixelBottleMapSpin turns it slowly and bobs it. looksVersion 51 replaced the saved Bottle look (the old painted-bottle texture, PixelBlockTextures.BottleGlass, is no longer used by it).
--- PixelBoundsSnapshot.cs ---
PixelBoundsSnapshot.cs Dev tool for tuning where old pixels may go (the view bounds + the floor). Dev Tools > Tools > 'Snapshot bounds (3 s)' waits boundsSnapshotDelay seconds (close the panel, set up the view you want) and then writes a text report to <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots/bounds_<time>.txt, copies it to the clipboard and saves a screenshot next to it. The report holds: screen size, bars, the camera that is on screen AND the 'home' camera that PixelViewBounds reads (pose, fov / ortho size, aspect, clip planes), the view kind / zoom / orbit state, the cube position and size, the floor (y, mesh bounds, own ground settings), PixelViewBounds' settings and its five planes, physics numbers, where the viewport edges (corners and edge middles, also inside the bars) land on the floor plane and on the cube's height, and every old pixel's position.
--- PixelBreakoutMinigame.cs ---
PixelBreakoutMinigame.cs Breakout minigame (id breakout, pack unlocksMinigame = "breakout", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; takeover minigame): every minInterval..maxInterval s with at least minPixels (30) old pixels around, up to maxBricks of them (random) become the bricks: frozen (kinematic, colliders off, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll), scaled to fit and glided (slideSeconds) into a wall in a court plane facing the camera (flat Sprites/Default blocks under a root, depthFraction of the way to the cube so it is in front of the old pixels; court = the whole screen between the black bars (manualCourtSize ticked: courtWidth/Height/CentreY), wallHeightFraction, brickColumns); all other old pixels shrink away and come back after the game (bystanderSeconds, same as Sorting Race) and the cube shrinks away (hideCube); the court's background is transparent by default (backOpacity 0..1, colour backColor). Mouse (projected onto the court plane) or Left/Right arrows move the paddle; the ball is a spinning CreateDisplayPixel of a random brick's type, speeds up per brick (speedUpPerBrick, maxBallSpeed); lives balls; every broken brick pays amount * brickMultiplier (10) immediately via AddCurrency and shrinks away (OldPixelDespawn.Begin); bricks left when the game ends (out of balls / timeLimit) go back to where they were and drop. Sounds breakout_start/_hit/_brick/_miss/_win/_lose, tip minigame_breakout.
--- PixelBurstPop.cs ---
PixelBurstPop.cs Where a clicked pixel's old copy lands (part of partial class PixelClicker, used by SpawnFallingCopyInner): when the ground is a real fall away (more than 2 pixel widths under the cube) the copy is not just nudged out at a random heading - TryBurstVelocity works out how long it will be in the air (gravity x gravityScale, no upward kick: burstUpSpeed 0 = -0.4..0, never above 0, and the copy appears at the cube's side popSideOffset 0 = 0.45 pixel widths out) and aims it at a random point on a ring round the cube (any direction, burstLandRadius 0 = 1.2..3.5 pixel widths), so a click throws pixels OUT in all directions and they land spread over the floor (usable as physics items) instead of dropping straight down in one stack. Short falls and disableBurstPop keep the old pop (popSpeedRange / popVerticalRange). EDGE-CLICK AIMING (edgeAiming, 2026-10-16): a MANUAL click near the cube's edge (on the screen: aimDeadZone 0.3 .. aimFullAt 0.85 of the way from its centre to its edge) throws the husk out of that side, within aimSpread (25 deg) either way; the middle stays random (spread 180) and it blends in between. Works for long falls (burst) and short ones (pop) alike. The auto clicker and first person always scatter. AimedDirection, set up by CollectInternal (aimThisCopy). Lets the player feed a Recycler / crate while clicking.
--- PixelButtonPulse.cs ---
PixelButtonPulse.cs Button pulse: every shop button (the shop's own CreateButton adds PixelButtonPulse) sends a clean neon outline out from under it when pressed - a thin glowing ring (a code-drawn 9-sliced rounded-rectangle outline sprite, RingSprite, the same on every button) is placed directly behind the button, centred on it whatever its anchors / pivot are (PixelButtonPulseRunner: grows 12 units beyond the edge on every side and fades over 0.45 s on unscaled time). The colour is the button's colour stretched to a neon (Neon); a button that turns from dark grey to bright when clicked (a tab) uses the bright colour one frame later. PixelButtonPulse.Disabled switches it off. Other windows' buttons (PixelUIKit.CreateButton) don't have it yet.
--- PixelCalm.cs ---
PixelCalm.cs The shared "what is going on right now" checks, so each script stops building its own long condition (a new blocker - a new full-screen window, a new intro - is added HERE once): - InMenu: the game is not in front of the player (pause menu, title screen or its reverse swing). Autosave screenshots, saving, the View window, and every place that used to check "pause menu OR title screen" by hand (Block Hold, tooltips, keys, minigame starts, vendors, the snitch...). Code that cares about only ONE of them (the pause menu itself, the title screen itself) still checks it directly. - CameraBusy: something else owns the camera (title screen / its swing, the camera intro, a takeover minigame). Orbital / free cam / zoom. - ForTip: a calm moment for a tip box or tutorial (in the game, no other tip or tutorial dim, Cubie not visiting or about to (a visit whose trigger is met: AboutToVisit, not the whole time before his second visit), the new-game intro over, no takeover minigame, time running). Perk popups, the shop / skills / toggles tutorials. Scripts with their own extra conditions keep them next to the shared check.
--- PixelCameraIntro.cs ---
PixelCameraIntro.cs Camera intro (added by PixelClicker.Awake): the camera holds a close-up side view of the cube (closeDistance (1.8 cubes), closeHeightInCubes, side, optional startFieldOfView) while the title screen shows - the blurred title picture is that view - then after startDelay it swings round the cube on an arc and settles into the scene's own camera pose over introSeconds (the end pose is whatever the camera has in the scene, nothing to set up; orthographic cameras zoom instead). A mouse click skips it (skipOnClick, off by default); clicks are locked during the zoom-out of a brand-new game (ClicksLocked, lockClicksOnNewGame). PlayReverse(onDone) plays the zoom backwards (reverseSeconds) for Quit > Main menu. Player switch: Settings > Camera intro (PixelCameraIntro.Enabled, applies next start).
--- PixelCarryHandles.cs ---
PixelCarryHandles.cs Shared carrying handles for placed things (first used by the Storage Crate, now also the Recycler): - BuildParts(part, halfWidth, depth, y, size, opacity): the model - on each short (+/-X) end two arms and a grip bar, all parts named "Handle", made through the caller's own 'part' callback (so each model keeps its own materials / PixelFactoryKit). 'size' = how far they stick out. - The component (AddComponent on the root, then Init): finds the child renderers named "Handle"; hovering one shows the open-hand cursor (PixelHandCursor), left-drag moves the root over the floor plane (kept on screen) with the fist cursor, scroll / Q / E turn it while dragging (PixelConsumables.TurnInput). Over(ray) / Dragging tell the owner the pointer is the handles'; static Hot (hovering or dragging any handles) is part of PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock and PixelGrab's guard. 'Blocked' (a Func the owner sets) pauses it, e.g. while the Recycler works. Moves go through a kinematic Rigidbody on the root (MovePosition / MoveRotation in FixedUpdate) so loose husks are pushed, not passed through; it moves at most maxCarrySpeed (7 u/s) and husks it shoves are slowed to pushedSpeedCap (2.5 u/s) so they slide aside instead of flying. The position / yaw are the root's own, so the saved PlacedState picks up where it was carried to.
--- PixelChargeBooster.cs ---
PixelChargeBooster.cs / PixelLightningRod.cs Electric devices (DeviceKind.ChargeBooster / LightningRod, defaults auto-added like the fan, cost Electric pixels; Device.uses / chargeCapacity / superChargeSeconds / superClicksPerSecond / strikeSeconds). Both last a number of uses, not time: PixelPlacedDevice.InitUses / SpendUse / UsesLabel (uses mode: no countdown, AddSeconds ignored, Remaining = uses left so saves store it; PixelConsumables.UsesKind). Charge Booster: spends one use per auto-clicker click (PixelClicker.PixelCollected, its own super-charge clicks excluded); PixelElectricLinks.ConsumeForBooster feeds each Electric pixel that links straight to it into AddCharge and dissipates the pixel (no +1 s, no chain through it); full meter (tall meter beside the body) = click it (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock while hovered) to start a super charge: superClicksPerSecond extra AutoCollect() calls for superChargeSeconds with crackling bolts and a light; charge is saved in PlacedState.aim. Lightning Rod: every strikeSeconds, if non-Electric old pixels lie within its radius, a bolt hits the tip and every one becomes an Electric pixel (same payout, ReleaseOldPixel + SpawnStoredPixel); one strike = one use. PixelBoltLayer (in PixelChargeBooster.cs) = shared unparented dynamic bolt mesh. Stats booster.supercharges, rod.strikes/converted.
--- PixelChipperDevice.cs ---
PixelChipperDevice.cs The placed Wood Chipper as a DEVICE (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.WoodChipper 15, sold by the Lumberjack): a thin PixelPlacedDevice added to the machine PixelWoodChipper.Place builds, so the chipper is bought, kept in the backpack, placed, saved / restored (position + turn; its health and chip count start fresh), and removed (hold the right button) like every other device. Permanent (no countdown, needs no switching on). All the machine's behaviour is in PixelWoodChipper.
--- PixelClickerAura.cs ---
PixelClickerAura.cs A soft glow BEHIND the clicker cube to make it easier to find without touching the pixels themselves (Settings > Interface > 'Clicker glow aura', PixelClickerAura.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.ClickerAura, default ON, saved per profile). A camera-facing quad with a radial fade (Sprites/Default, alpha blended) sits just behind the cube as seen from the camera, so the cube hides the middle and a halo shows round it from any view. Its colour is the colour of the pixel being clicked (washed towards white), fades smoothly when the tier changes and breathes slowly. The cube is CUT OUT of it: PixelDepthMask.shader (Resources) draws a copy of the cube's main mesh that writes only depth, in queue 3001 between the cube and the aura (3002), so a transparent cube doesn't show the glow through itself. Added by PixelClicker.Awake. Tunables are [SerializeField] fields.
--- PixelClickerHazards.cs ---
PixelClickerHazards.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: hazard blocks (Pest = Farming, Gas Pocket = Mining, Termite Hive = Woodcutting, and the other skills' hazards) - their settings (Pest bug swarm, Termite Hive), charging up and leaving by themselves (UpdateHazardCharges, UpdateHazardExpiry), defusing with right clicks (DefuseHazard, FinishDefuse, DefusePopup), clearing (ClearHazardNow), going off (TriggerHazard) and their per-skill stats (CountHazardForSkill). Which block is a hazard is a block tag (IsHazard, PixelBlockTags); penalties are in PixelSkills. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerHusks.cs ---
PixelClickerHusks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: everything about HUSKS (the pixels that drop off the cube; code calls them old pixels) - spawning the falling copy after a click (SpawnFallingCopy / SpawnFallingCopyInner, SpawnStoredPixel, SpawnStreamedPixel), how long they last (LifetimeFactor, AddDespawn), their amount (RollOldPixelAmount), the cap (CopyCap), the Vacuum pixel sucking them up (Vacuum, SuckRoutine), husk XP, saving / restoring them (OldPixelState), claiming / absorbing / releasing them for devices, their physics materials, trails and fly-away landing, and display copies (CreateDisplayPixel). The components ON a husk are in PixelHusks.cs. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerLookFixups.cs ---
PixelClickerLookFixups.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: RunLookFixups, called from Awake. The scene stores the Pixel Looks list (the 'looks' array in the Inspector) and a looksVersion number; each 'if (looksVersion < N)' step below adds or replaces the looks that changed in version N, so a scene saved before a look existed still gets it. They run in order on every Play until the scene is saved with the new number (the scene's data is old, so in practice they run each time). New look changes add the next step at the END with the next number. Split out of Awake in PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerLooks.cs ---
PixelClickerLooks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: how the block on the cube LOOKS - its mesh per Pixel Look (BaseMesh, MeshFor), colour / texture / surface (RenderColor, ApplyLookTexture, ApplyLookSurface, ApplyPixelColor, ApplyTierLook), damage cracks (SetLiveDamage), the glow shell / halo / point light for glowing tiers (UpdateGlowShell, AnimateGlowShell, UpdateGlowLight), materials (MaterialForTier, GetGlowMaterial, BuildTransparentMaterial, CreateVisualMaterial). Which look a type has lives in PixelLooks.cs; the saved list's fix-ups in PixelClickerLookFixups.cs. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerSkillBlocks.cs ---
PixelClickerSkillBlocks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: the core's side of the skill / special blocks - Solar clusters and seed boosts, plants and seed sprouts (RandomPlantPixelTier, RandomSeedPixelTier, PlantSeedSproutAt, SpawnSeedSprout), the farming blocks (Pollen, Weevil, Fertilize, Wheat / Golden Fruit settings), Water splash pieces, the Electrician's conductors and capacitor, the Treasure chest (OpenTreasure, TreasureFountain) and Fishing (its settings, Bottle / Whale / GiveFishingHaul). Most of each block's own behaviour lives in its own script (PixelFarmFx, PixelElectricFx, PixelTreasure, PixelFishingSpawns...). Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerTiers.cs ---
PixelClickerTiers.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: the tier list's helpers. Setting up the rare tiers at start (EnsureDragonCubes, EnsureGodCubes - God and Prime Cubes and the Cubelette -, EnsureGoldenSnitch, with goldenSnitchSpawnWeight), and the "Tier helpers" section: which tier is on the cube (GetClickTierIndex), spawn picking, unlocks, counts, the Block Hold hooks. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs ---
PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: a LOOK-ONLY preview of any pixel type on the cube, for the main menu (PixelTitleScreen cycles every type on the cube). ShowLookPreview(tierIndex) plays the materialize pop with that type's look WITHOUT changing the current block or raising PixelMaterialized (so the Golden Snitch, hazard hints, stakes etc. never react to it); EndLookPreview() brings the real current block back (a normal Materialize).
--- PixelClickerTypes.cs ---
PixelClickerTypes.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: the data shapes - the PixelType enum (every pixel type; values are only ever APPENDED so Inspector data stays valid), TierUnlockMode, PixelTier (one currency / block: colour, spawn weight, payout, toughness, glow, fly-away...) and the SpawnSwitch, plus the type checks right under the enum (IsWoodBlock, IsHazard, IsDragonCube, IsRareCube... - all read the block tags, PixelBlockTags). Split out of PixelClicker.cs.
--- PixelCollectionGallery.cs ---
PixelCollectionGallery.cs The Collection Log's GALLERY: the camera button on an entry's page (PixelCollectionLog) calls PixelCollectionGallery.Enter(entryIndex): you walk, in first person, round a PITCH BLACK room where every collectible floats and lights up its surroundings. Everything is built when you enter and DESTROYED when you leave (Esc / the Leave button): nothing waits in the background. The room is far away from the game (origin (-4000, -9000, -4000)), is seen only through its own camera drawing to a screen-sized RenderTexture that a full-screen canvas (sorting 900) shows, so no part of it can show in the normal view. * Room (settings on PixelCollectionLog, header Gallery): a roomSize (20) square floor drawn as the first person NEON WIREFRAME grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader: the lines glow only near the lit points = you and each cube) and a boundsSize (10) square you cannot leave (a faint wire wall + floor outline marks it). * Items: rows (Dragon Cubes, God Cubes, Prime Cubes, the Cubelette) of real models (PixelClicker.CreateDisplayPixel) floating, turning and bobbing, each with its own point light (only the nearest few lights are on at once). A cube you have not found is a dark '???' block with no light. The cube you look at shows its name and text at the bottom. * Darkness: the scene's directional lights are switched off while you are inside, the ambient light / reflections / fog are forced to black / off (restored on leaving), the camera clears to black. * Controls: mouse looks, W A S D / arrows walk, Shift runs, Esc leaves (PixelWindows entry); the game's own action keys are ignored meanwhile (PixelKeys.Pressed / Held check Active) and clicks on the cube are blocked.
--- PixelCollectionLog.cs ---
PixelCollectionLog.cs The Collection Log: a book of the rare collectibles (the seven Dragon Cubes, the three God Cubes, the six Prime Cubes and the Prime Cubelette). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake; opened by the small BOOK button at the top-left of the Log window (PixelLog.BuildUI -> PixelCollectionLog.Toggle()); Escape / the X close it (PixelWindows priority 70). A spread of two pages: a title page (with how many are found), then one entry per page. Click the right page to turn forward, the left page to turn back (the page squashes like a turning leaf). An entry is '???' (name, text and a black silhouette of its picture) until the cube is KNOWN = its tier's totalCollected > 0 (lifetime total, so spending it - the wish, the Gauntlet - never hides it). GALLERY: every entry page has a small CAMERA button (top right of the page) that walks you into PixelCollectionGallery (a pitch black first person room with all the cubes floating and lit, built on entering and destroyed on leaving); the settings are under the Gallery header here, and leaving brings the book back on the same page. Pictures are PixelTierPicture (a real model, turning). To add an entry: add a line to Entries (type + flavour text).
--- PixelCombo.cs ---
PixelCombo.cs Combo meter (shop upgrade "Combo Meter", UpgradeEffect.ComboMeter, 5 levels; level value = that tier's multiplier): quick manual clicks build a combo (breaks after 1.2 s idle). Tiered multiplier: tierCombos {10, 50, 200, 500, 1000} / tierMultipliers {1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5}; the highest tier whose combo count is reached (and that the upgrade level unlocked: UnlockedTiers = tiers whose multiplier <= maxMultiplier) sets Multiplier (x1 below tier 1; Tier); sets PixelClicker.ManualClickBonus every frame (manual payouts only); meter at the bottom-centre shows the next milestone (nextFormat). Fades out after hideAfterSeconds without manual clicks. It is the last thing to arrive after entering the game (UpdateEntrance: waits for the title screen, the camera intro and the sliding margins / buttons to finish plus entranceDelaySeconds (0 = 0.6 s), then fades in over entranceFadeSeconds (0 = 1.2 s); disableEntranceFade). Tier effects (by reached tier, cumulative, runtime-drawn UI sprites): 1 mild shake, 2 yellow flame aura (UpdateFlames, pooled sprites around the edges), 3 yellow glow, 4 violent shake (violentShakeFactor) + lightning bolts (UpdateLightning), 5 explosion (flash + sparks, repeats every explosionRepeatSeconds) + super-saiyan hair spikes (UpdateHair); when the combo breaks the effects wind down over breakSeconds (shake amplitude falls, glow / flames fade, the hair drops, the meter flashes white: breakFlash*), then the meter fades out as usual; tuned in the 'Combo Effects (by tier)' Inspector group. PixelShop finds/adds it and applies it via SetUpgrade.
--- PixelConnector.cs ---
PixelConnector.cs CONVEYOR CONNECTOR (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Conveyor Connector'): a short boxed channel for gaps in turns or for joining a belt to a machine. Two clicks, like a belt, but it is a single piece (no chaining): the first click snaps to a belt end / elevator top / splitter output / machine opening (IPixelPort: Kiln mouth, Storage Crate), the second click snaps to the other thing, and a box (floor, side walls, top ribs) forms between them. BEHAVIOUR: it FORCES old pixels along it. A belt that ends at the first point hands its pixels in (IPixelBeltAcceptor), and any loose old pixel (not held, not already carried / riding) that is inside the box volume is captured as well - its own path is thrown away and it is carried from where it entered to the far end (PixelPathCarrier, speedCubes cube widths per second, one gap behind the pixel ahead). At the far end the pixel goes onto a belt that STARTS there, or into a machine whose opening is there (PixelConveyorSystem.FindAcceptorAt, e.g. the Kiln or another connector), otherwise it is thrown on along the connector's direction. Width matches the belts (PixelConveyor.LaneCount). Saved with the tester save (the two points). HOSE ADAPTER (the same class, adapter = true; tester feature 'Hose Adapter'): a longer trough (3.2 cubes) that SNAPS onto a working Funnel's spout (PixelFunnel.SpoutEndFloor / SpoutYaw, TrySnapAdapter) and turns the spout's way. Its side walls only cover the entry part; the open part has teal port strips and two SIDE PORTS (SidePort(+1 right / -1 left)): a belt placed so its first point snaps to a port (PixelConveyor.Snap / PixelConnector.Snap know them) is fed from the side, else the far end is the exit (UpdateExit, every 0.25 s, picks a port that has a belt starting at it). The funnel's pixels simply fall into the trough's box and are carried to the chosen exit like any connector's.
--- PixelContainerHose.cs ---
PixelContainerHose.cs CONTAINER HOSE (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Container Hose', a single two-click line like the Conveyor Connector): a see-through hose like the Funnel's that joins one container to another. Click the SOURCE (a Storage Crate or a Quantum Organizer), then the TARGET (a Recycler, another Storage Crate, a Quantum Organizer or a Trash Can); both clicks magnetize to the nearest container's top (Snap: the container under the mouse, else the nearest top within 120 px). Clicked the other way round (target first) the hose runs from the crate / organizer anyway. Placed devices have NO colliders, so a container's place and top come from its MESH RENDERERS' bounds (Collect / Port; not its texts or the husks in it). An idle hose says why over its middle (IdleReason: no source / no target / waiting / source empty). The hose arches between their tops. Every pumpSeconds (0.5 s) it pulls one stored pixel out of the source (TakeOneForHose on PixelStorageCrate / PixelQuantumDevice), spawns it as a husk at the hose's start (SpawnStoredPixel) and carries it along the tube (PixelPathCarrier, can't be grabbed on the way). At the far end: a crate / trash can takes it directly (IPixelBeltAcceptor.AcceptFromBelt); for a Recycler / Quantum Organizer it is let fall into the bin / onto the pad (PixelRecycler.HoseDropPoint), marked Held for a moment so no other machine snatches it on the way down. It only pumps while the target has room (PixelRecycler.HoseCanTake + its count, the crate's free sections, the organizer's capacity) counting what is already in the tube or falling; otherwise pixels wait in the tube. The ends follow the containers when they are carried or turned (each frame: bounds of their mesh renderers -> top centre; the tube is rebuilt when an end moves). A container that is removed leaves its end where it was (the hose idles). Removing the hose lets what is inside it fall out. Saved with the tester save (both end points; on load each end latches onto the container found there).
--- PixelConveyor.cs ---
PixelConveyor.cs HEIGHT / RAMPS: belt ends (a, b) are full 3D points - the y is the belt's underside. While placing, the scroll wheel raises / lowers the point being placed (PixelFeatureTester.UpdateLine, steps of half a cube, ClampEnd keeps the slope under about 37 degrees); posts (BuildSupports) stand on the floor (FloorY) under a raised belt. Riders measure their place along the GROUND (Length = horizontal length), PointAt gives the sloped height, RunSpeed / LiftFactor adjust speed and rest height for the slope. Snap() matches belt ends and elevator tops by SCREEN distance, so raised ends can be snapped to; FindNext only chains belts whose heights match. The Conveyor Elevator (PixelElevator.cs) lifts pixels between heights. CORNERS: where belts chain at an angle the gap on the outside of the turn is filled automatically (PixelConveyorJoint.cs). WIDTH: PixelConveyor.LaneCount = 1 cube wide, 2 at Electrician level 10, 3 at level 20 (every belt, PixelConveyorSystem.Update rebuilds them when it changes). Each rider travels in a LANE (belt-local x, PixelConveyor.LaneOffset / NearestLane): a pixel is captured into the lane it landed in, riders keep their gap per lane, and a hand-on to the next belt picks the nearest lane there. CONVEYOR BELT (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Conveyor Belt'). Placing: click a point, then click another: a belt is built between the two (the second point starts the next belt, so a series of short straight pieces makes a curve; right click ends the chain, a second right click stops placing). Clicks near the end of an existing belt snap to it. RIGHT CLICK a placed belt (not while placing) to reverse its direction (PixelConveyorSystem). A belt is a low solid slab with side rails and moving chevrons that show the way. LOCKED RIDING (no physics): an old pixel that lands on a belt (not held, not glued into a build, not flying, and there is room: riders keep a gap of 1.1 cube widths) is CAPTURED by PixelConveyorSystem - made kinematic, centred on the line and carried along it at beltSpeedCubes (0 = 1.6 cube widths per second) by position, like the Storage Crate locks pixels in. At the end of a belt it is handed on to a belt that STARTS there (a chained piece, ends within 0.45 cube widths and not head-on); when there is no belt to hand it to, the end is detected: a machine whose opening is there (IPixelBeltAcceptor: the Kiln mouth, the Storage Crate) takes the pixel straight in, otherwise it is EJECTED: physics back on, thrown on a little along the belt's direction (so it can fly into a Kiln mouth or onto the floor). Riders queue when the one ahead is stopped. Flipping a belt flips its riders; grabbing a rider with Pixel Grabbing frees it. Things spat out of a machine or dropped by the player simply land on a belt and get captured. Belts are saved with the tester save (shape incl. heights, riders). Built with PixelFactoryKit. SPEED: each belt can have its own speed - in FIRST PERSON a right click on a belt opens a small window (PixelConveyorSystem speed editor: a number box in cube widths per second, Set / Reverse / Close; the mouse is freed while it is open); in the normal view a right click still reverses the belt.
--- PixelConveyorJoint.cs ---
PixelConveyorJoint.cs CORNER FILLER for chained conveyor belts: where a belt hands on to the belt that starts at its end (PixelConveyor.FindNext) and the two are not in a straight line, the square-ish gap on the OUTSIDE of the turn is filled with a slab mesh shaped by the angle (polygon: this belt's end, its outer corner, the point where the two outer edges meet, the next belt's outer corner, the next belt's start; with a sharp turn where the edges meet far away it is a simple wedge) plus matching outer rails. Built by PixelConveyorJoint.Refresh (called by each belt every 0.5 s and after it is rebuilt; it only rebuilds when the two belts' ends / heights / widths change) as a child of the belt, so it goes with the belt. Purely visual: pixels are handed from belt to belt as before. Straight chains and head-on ends get no joint.
--- PixelCrashLog.cs ---
PixelCrashLog.cs Local crash / error reports (added by PixelClicker.Awake). One text file per session in <persistentDataPath>/CrashReports/report_<date>.txt (header with versions + hardware, then every Error / Exception / Assert as it happens via Application.logMessageReceivedThreaded: message, stack trace, a cached game-state summary and the last recentLines log lines; repeated errors are counted; size and file count capped). A running.marker file is created at start and deleted on a clean quit; if it is still there at the next start the previous session crashed / froze, the new report says so and a notice box appears. The pause menu has a Report Folder button (PixelCrashLog.OpenFolder). Testers just send the .txt files. Also writes rendering_report.txt (GPU, render pipeline, quality levels, which shaders Shader.Find can see, glow setup via PixelClicker.DebugGlowReport) at start and on F10.
--- PixelCubeIcon.cs ---
PixelCubeIcon.cs UI MaskableGraphic drawing a tiny spinning shaded 3D cube (no camera/render texture) - achievement icons.
--- PixelCubiePeek.cs ---
PixelCubiePeek.cs Cubie peeks out from behind the Save / Load window (component added to the pause menu by PixelPauseMenu.BuildSaveLoadPanel, Setup(panelRect, isActive)): his head (a trimmed copy of the guide's drawing) is a sibling placed directly UNDER the window in the canvas order, so the box covers the rest of him; while the window is open and quiet (no rename / overwrite / result overlay) and there has been no mouse movement or click for idleSeconds (1, to be tuned later) plus a random extraDelay, he slides out of a random edge (left / right / top, random spot along it, showFraction of his head visible, slideSeconds), his pupils dart about (Glance) for stayRange seconds, then he ducks back; any mouse movement / click makes him duck at once; cooldownRange seconds before the next peek. Unscaled time. The slot rows in that window are now exactly as wide as the Save / Load buttons (the scroll bar sits in the margin). PixelCubiePeek.cs (more) The Cubie peek is now also on the Settings screen and the Key Bindings screen (PixelPauseMenu.AddCubiePeek(panel): one PixelCubiePeek component per panel, active while that panel is open).
--- PixelCustomKeys.cs ---
PixelCustomKeys.cs Custom keybinds: bind any item (a potion, a device, a seed, a supply...) or tool (the Pixel hose, the docked menu buttons) to a key of your own. Component added by PixelClicker.Awake; it fires the keys (Update: not while paused, on the title screen, typing or capturing a key) by doing what a My Window favourite does: item:<name> -> PixelConsumables.TryUseItem (drinks the potion, starts placing the device), tool:<id> -> PixelMyWindow.TryGetTool (the hose, or PixelMenuKeys.Click). One potion per key is fine (a black potion on one key, a white one on another). The bindings (target + KeyCode) are saved per save file as JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.CustomKeys (listed in PixelSettingsSync, ReloadFromPrefs). A key used by one of the normal actions (Settings > Key Bindings) is refused; a key used by another custom binding moves to the new one. Escape cancels a capture, Backspace / Delete clears the key. The window is the KEYBINDS TAB of My Window (PixelMyWindow builds a container and calls BuildView; Refresh / SetVisible keep it current): a drop-down of everything bindable + Add, then one row per binding (name, key box = click then press a key, X removes it).
--- PixelDashMinigame.cs ---
PixelDashMinigame.cs Cube Dash minigame (id dash, pack unlocksMinigame = "dash", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 50K each; NOT a TakeoverActive minigame - the cube must stay clickable - but Takeover => true so the limiter keeps every other minigame from starting while it runs): every minInterval..maxInterval s the cube dashes (PixelClicker.SetCubeOffset(world offset) / CubeBasePosition, applied in AnimatePixel) between random spots inside the area* screen fractions: dashSeconds dash, dwellMin..Max s hanging at the spot drifting in a Perlin wiggle (wiggleRadius), then the next spot (at least minHop away); after roundSeconds it zips home and pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click (amountPerClick x PayoutMultiplier x ClickMultiplier / clicksToCollect) via AddCurrency on top of the clicks' normal pay. Only manual clicks count (PixelCollected / PixelHit with automatic false); the auto clicker is off meanwhile (PixelMinigame.AutoClickerPaused, read by PixelAutoClicker). The cube stays the pixel it was (SetForcedSpawnTier, skipped when a potion already forces one; a Dragon Cube / meteor falls back to White). Stats dash.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_dash, sounds dash_start/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned).
--- PixelDatabase.cs ---
PixelDatabase.cs / PixelDatabaseWindow.cs Game Database (opened by the Database tab of the pause menu's Game Guide screen, PixelPauseMenu.BuildGuideScreen -> PixelDatabaseWindow.Toggle(); the window's canvas (520) sits above the pause menu and Escape closes it first, CloseIfOpen): a search engine over the live game data. PixelDatabase.Build() (run each time the window opens) makes one Entry per pixel (name + the Pixel Guide text + pay / clicks / weight), shop pack (PixelShop.AllPacks), skill (perks + tree nodes), consumable (PixelConsumables.ItemName/ItemDescription/ItemCosts), defined recipe (PixelCrafting.DefinedRecipes), minigame / vendor (PixelMinigame.All), achievement and tip (PixelHints.AllHints + the lines of Resources/HowToPlay.txt and Controls.txt), and TWO- WAY References between them (pack rewards / costs / requirements / unlocks, skill blocks / filler / hazard, recipe ingredients, achievement pixel, and 'mentioned in tip' by whole-word name match). PixelDatabase.Search(query, max): every word must appear in the entry's name / category / description / reference text; ranked name-exact > starts-with > contains > category > text. The window (canvas 108, PixelWindows 16, PixelPop): search box + results list on the left, the selected entry's description and its references on the right (click a reference to jump to it, '< Back' returns). Not covered yet: floors / skies / pets / vendors' wares. A new kind of game data should be added to PixelDatabase.Build. BROWSE / PICTURES / DISCOVERY: the window has category tabs under the search box (All, Pixels, Packs, Skills, Items, Recipes, Minigames, Awards, Tips; PixelDatabase.Search(query, max, category, showAll)), a turning 3D picture of the pixel (PixelTierPicture, Entry.tierIndex) beside a pixel's description, and a 'Show ???' switch. Entry.discovered: pixel = unlocked or collected, shop pack = bought or a reward pixel unlocked, skill = PixelSkills.IsAvailable, consumable = its required pixel unlocked; recipes, minigames, achievements and tips are always shown. An undiscovered entry is listed as '???' (name, text, picture and references hidden, searchable only by its category) unless 'Show ???' is on; other entries' references to it read 'Label: ???'. MORE ENTRIES: Pet (one per pixel type that can have a pet; discovered = owned; numbers from PixelPets.SpawnWeightFactor / DespawnSlowDown / FindChancePerClick), Floor and Sky (the looks of PixelFloor / PixelSkybox, IPixelLookSource) and vendors' wares (PixelVisitorMinigame.WareNames -> 'Sells' / 'Sold by' links on minigame + consumable entries). RULES / NODES: 'Block rule' entries (PixelBlockRules special-block and hold-block rules; text = PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes, links to the pixel, the blocks it replaces, the boost block, the hazard it clears and the skill) and 'Skill node' entries (every PixelSkills.TreeNode: description, cost, 'coming soon', links to its skill, the node it needs and the block toggle it unlocks); both are discovered when their pixel / skill is. PIN: Entry.pinType / pinKey (pixel = currency row, skill, achievement, listable consumable = favorite) drive the window's 'Pin to My Window' button (PixelMyWindow.CanPin / IsPinned / TogglePin). Known leak: a discovered entry's reference text still contains the hidden name, so searching for that exact name finds the discovered entry.
--- PixelDayNight.cs ---
PixelDayNight.cs Day / night cycle (added by PixelClicker.Awake; disableDayNight): TimeOfDay 0..1 (0 midnight, 0.5 noon; a new game starts at 0.38) advances with SCALED time (stops while paused / Time Stop) over LengthMinutes (Settings > Interface 'Length of a day': 4 / 8 / 15 / 30, default 8; switch 'Day / night cycle', default on; PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DayNight / DayNightLength, per save) and drives the scene's sun (RenderSettings.sun or the first directional light: it rises on one side of the scene, passes OVERHEAD at noon and sets on the other (an arc in the vertical plane turned by the scene sun's own yaw, sunLean 0 = 0.18 tilts it a little); the moon takes the same arc back, a bit lower; warm near the horizon, moonColor at night, intensity down to minSunIntensity 0.3), the ambient light (minAmbient 0.55) and the sky material's _Tint / _SkyTint (nightSkyColor, duskSkyColor); everything is restored when switched off. Night never goes fully dark. PixelDayNight.Level (0 night .. 1 day) is there for other systems (not used yet). The time of day is the saved stat counter daynight.time (x10000).
--- PixelDaylight.cs ---
PixelDaylight.cs Solar's daylight bar (moved out of PixelMeteorSolar.cs into its own file, because Unity can only save a component in the scene when its class lives in a file of the same name). Every Solar click raises daylight and it slowly fades; while it is up seeds and Seed pets grow faster and hovering Solar husks keep it up. A small sun + bar at the top right shows it. Not saved.
--- PixelDebug.cs ---
PixelDebug.cs PixelDebug.Info(msg, ctx): diagnostic logging that is compiled out of release builds (Editor/dev builds only). Use it instead of Debug.Log for non-error messages. Also: PixelUIKit.SetText(label, text) skips identical strings; open windows (shop, Inventory, Log, stats) refresh on a timer (rowRefreshSeconds, refreshSeconds), not every frame; each class with static state has a [RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod] ResetStatics() (Enter Play Mode without domain reload) - add one when adding static state. PixelHints warns (editor only) once per unknown Trigger id.
--- PixelDevDocs.cs ---
PixelDevDocs.cs The 'Docs' tab of the Dev Hub (PixelDevHub): the DOCUMENTATION section with three permanent sub-tabs: To-Do (0), Changelog (1) and Notes (2). * To-Do and Changelog: PixelDevDocs only provides their content roots (PixelDevDocs.Root(0) / Root(1)); PixelDevTools fills them (Resources/Todo.txt, Resources/Changelog.txt) and rebuilds them when PixelDevDocs.Shown(sub) fires (the Docs tab is visible and that sub-tab is showing). * Notes: your own notes, typed in-game. A list of notes on the left ('+ New note'), the selected note's title and text on the right (multi-line, scrolls), Delete (a second click confirms). Saved automatically a moment after every change, when the Docs tab is hidden and when the game closes, to <persistentDataPath>/DevNotes.json (not part of the game save). The selected sub-tab is remembered (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.DocsSub).
--- PixelDevHub.cs ---
PixelDevHub.cs The DEV HUB: ONE window for every dev tool (opens with PixelAction.DevTools, F9, the pause menu's Dev Tools button; PixelDevTools.OpenPanel / Close delegate here). It replaces the old Dev Tools panel, the Inspector window and the Mock up window. * The window itself is a PixelWindowChrome window: drag by the header (kept between the black bars), minimize and dock to the left / right screen edge. * FILING-CABINET TABS along the top: Main (PixelDevTools' widgets), To-Do, Changelog, Inspector (PixelDevInspector), Shapes (PixelDevShapes), UI Mock (PixelDevMockup). Drag a tab sideways to REARRANGE the tabs; drag it down out of the strip to POP IT OUT into its own floating window (drag that window by its header, '-' minimizes it); the 'Dock' button or dropping the floating window on the hub puts it back as a tab. Order, active tab, floating tabs + their places and the hub's open state are saved (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.Hub.*). * Tools get their content root with PixelDevHub.TabRoot(id) (a top-left anchored RectTransform of the tab's design size, 700 x 780 or 500 x 780 for the mockups) and learn when they are on screen through PixelDevHub.SetTabCallback(id, visible => ...): visible = the hub is open and the tab is the active docked tab (or a floating, un-minimized window). * PixelWidgetBoard (also here) = draggable WIDGETS: each Main tab row sits on a card with a grip on its left; drag the grip to move the row, the others make room; the order is saved.
--- PixelDevInspector.cs ---
PixelDevInspector.cs 'Inspector' (Dev Tools > Tools tick box, Editor / dev builds like the rest of Dev Tools): ONE draggable, minimizable window to change values while playing. * World tab: the whole simulation. Time (timeScale, fixedDeltaTime, targetFrameRate, vSync), Physics (gravity incl. a quick 'Gravity Y', solver iterations, bounce / sleep thresholds, contact offset...), live counts (FPS, old pixels, rigidbodies, awake ones) and a drop-down of every game system in the scene (PixelShop, PixelFloor, PixelPets...) with a Select button that opens that system in the Selected tab. * Selected tab: 'Pick' mode (while on, a left click on anything with a collider selects it instead of clicking the cube: PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock), Parent / Active / Destroy buttons, a filter box and a foldable list of the object's components. Game scripts show EVERY field of a supported type (numbers, bool, text, enum, Vector2/3, Color; private runtime state included), Unity components show a fixed list (Transform, Rigidbody mass / drag / velocity..., Collider bounciness / friction, Light, Camera, Renderer, AudioSource). An old pixel also shows its tier's data (PixelTier: spawn weight, payout...). * All rows are built by reflection (Member = a getter + setter), values refresh a few times a second (not while you type), enum / bool rows are buttons. 'Copy edits' puts every change you made as 'Owner.field = value' lines on the clipboard so a good value can be put into the code. Nothing here is saved in the game save (only open / tab / minimized / position in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.Insp*). Edits last until the object or the session ends.
--- PixelDevMockWindow.cs ---
PixelDevMockWindow.cs Just a doorway now. The Mock up mode WINDOW is gone: the Shape placer (PixelDevShapes) and the UI mockup builder (PixelDevMockup) are tabs of the Dev Hub (PixelDevHub: 'Shapes' and 'UI Mock'). They still ask this class for their content roots (TabRoot 0 = Shapes, 1 = UI pieces) and for the height they may use (ContentHeight); the hub tells them when their tab is on screen (PixelDevShapes.SetActive / PixelDevMockup.SetActive) and whether the hub is open (PixelDevMockup.SetWindowOpen).
--- PixelDevProbe.cs ---
PixelDevProbe.cs Dev snapshot tools that hand Claude what it cannot see (all driven by PixelDevTools: keys PixelAction.MarkMoment F10 / InspectHover F11 and Tools tab buttons; Editor / development builds only). Reports go to <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots (Save) and the clipboard. MarkMoment: screenshot + a text report of this instant (screen, view, camera, the crash log's game-state summary and last log lines). UiLayoutDump: every active overlay canvas as a tree (name, rect in the 1920 x 1080 canvas space with the origin top-left like the mockup builder, text + font size, colours, button / toggle / slider flags, raycast targets) - the UI is built in code, so this is the only way to see it as text. Inspect: what is under the mouse right now - every UI object the pointer hits (topmost first, with its rect and look) and every collider along the ray (name, scripts, old pixel tier / amount), plus where the cursor meets the floor plane. Copies to the clipboard and shows a short notice. PixelBoundsSnapshot (the floor / view-bounds numbers) writes through the same Save.
--- PixelDevShapes.cs ---
PixelDevShapes.cs / PixelDevMockup.cs Layout dev tools ('Mock up mode' tick box in the Dev Tools Tools tab = PixelDevMockWindow, ONE draggable window with a Shapes tab and a UI pieces tab and a minimize button; both components are created by PixelDevTools at start (Boot) so saved layouts reappear, the host window turns the tabs on with SetActive; their controls are built into PixelDevMockWindow.TabRoot; state in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.*, not in the game save). Shape placer (tab 0); click the floor to drop the brush shape (ShapeKind Cube / Sphere / Cylinder / Capsule / Ramp (runtime wedge mesh) / Wall / Disc, sizes in main-pixel widths), click a shape to select + drag it on the floor plane, Q / E (PixelAction.RotateLeft/Right) turn, the wheel turns smoothly and Ctrl + wheel resizes (the hovered / dragged shape, else the brush), a see-through GHOST of the brush follows the mouse over the floor (UpdateGhost) and the shape is dropped with the brush's size / turn, Delete removes; ShapeMode Solid (static collider) / Dynamic (rigidbody) / Ghost (no collider); colour cycle button; every shape has a billboard number label, the panel lists id / kind / mode / x z y / rotation / size and 'Copy layout' puts the text on the clipboard; 'RESET ALL SHAPES' deletes everything (one click). While open PixelClicker.DevClickBlock (part of ExternalClickBlock) keeps the cube from taking the clicks. UI mockup builder (tab 1); right-click on a piece deletes it; resize grips are a drawn corner grip (PixelUIKit.GripSprite, also on My Window); add Window / Box / Button / Text / Image pieces (own canvas 540, 1920 x 1080 units, origin top-left, no UI-scale division) - drag to move, yellow corner handle to resize, number badge, exact X / Y / W / H fields, text box, font size, opacity slider, hex colour, layer Front / Up / Down / Back, duplicate, delete; Image pieces show PNG / JPG files from <persistentDataPath>/Mockups (Folder button opens it, Refresh rescans; a checker placeholder otherwise); 'Show when closed' keeps the pieces visible after the panel is closed, 'Hide' hides them; 'Copy layout' lists every piece back to front; 'RESET ALL' deletes all.
--- PixelDevTextEdit.cs ---
PixelDevTextEdit.cs Custom text for the Dev Hub (component added by PixelDevHub.Awake): a quick RIGHT CLICK (not a hold: holding is the widget favorite, PixelRightTap tells them apart) on any label in the hub - widget titles and control labels, tab names, tester cards, buttons - opens a small box to type your own text (Save / Reset / Cancel, Enter / Esc). A label is found by its place in the hierarchy (the names of its GameObjects up to the hub canvas), so it needs no registration; labels inside drop-downs / input fields and the search results are left alone. Edits are kept per label path in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.CustomText (JSON), which PixelSettingsSync stores in every save profile (and PixelDevTextEdit.ReloadFromPrefs re-reads). Every 2 s while the hub is open (and when it opens) the labels are scanned and the saved texts put on them; each frame the edited ones are kept at their text (code that rewrites a label's text, like 'Clear (3)', cannot undo your edit). Reset brings back the text the label had before it was edited.
--- PixelDidYouKnow.cs ---
PixelDidYouKnow.cs "Did you know?" tips (added by PixelClicker.Awake): every few minutes (firstDelaySeconds 180, then a random intervalSeconds 150-300) a random general hint from Assets/Resources/DidYouKnow.txt (one tip per line, '//' lines are notes, {key:Action} placeholders become the player's keys via PixelKeys.Replace, a line starting 'shop:' only shows once the Shop button is available) pops up - only when the game is NOT BUSY (Busy: PixelCalm.ForTip plus no open window, not in first person); a busy moment just waits and retries. Tips come from a shuffled bag, so none repeats before all were seen. Settings > Gameplay 'Did you know tips' (Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DidYouKnow, per save via PixelSettingsSync). TWO PLACES (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DidYouKnowDock / X / Y, the player's machine, not per save): - DOCKED (default: right edge, near the top) at the height where it was dropped: the box hides just past the side edge (dockY = its centre, canvas units from the middle, saved). When it is docked it slides into the side; when a tip triggers it SLIDES OUT, stays showSeconds (12 s, paused while hovered; a click sends it back at once) and slides back in. No button is left on screen. - FLOATING anywhere: the box pops in at its pinned spot and fades out the same way. MOVING it works like the achievement popup: hold the left mouse button on the box (holdSeconds 0.5, PixelHold ring) and it follows the mouse; a left click puts it down - within dockSnap of the left / right screen edge it DOCKS to that edge at that height, anywhere else it floats there; a right click puts it back to the default (docked right, top), as does Settings > Accessibility 'Reset tips location' (ResetLocation). A quick click (no hold) just dismisses the tip. Kept inside the black bars.
--- PixelDisplaySettings.cs ---
PixelDisplaySettings.cs Player display / accessibility settings (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.*, applied before the first scene): graphics quality preset (QualitySettings), VSync, fullscreen + resolution, UI scale (SavedUIScale takes effect on the next start; UIScale = the value in use: PixelUIKit.CreateCanvas divides the reference resolution by it; the black bars' canvas is not scaled, so PixelHud.BarHeight is in scaled-canvas units and RawBarHeight in bar units), and colour-blind marks (ColorBlind / ColorBlindChanged: PixelLook.colorBlindSides draws a triangle / square / circle on the faces of the red / green / blue pixels, PixelBombMinigame adds R/G/B letters under the wires).
--- PixelDragonFind.cs ---
PixelDragonFind.cs Dragon Cube find show (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; PixelDragonFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos), queued): called from PixelClicker.CollectInternal (a click, manual or auto) and SeedSprout.PopOff (a grown Dragon Seed) - never from offline progress (AddCurrency alone does not play it). A canvas (690, blocks clicks) darkens the screen (darkness), the cube is drawn by a hidden studio camera into a RenderTexture on a RawImage (so nothing in the scene can dim it), zooms up from where it was found (zoomInSeconds), dances about (danceSeconds, danceRange, golden UI sparkle pool sparklesPerSecond), then hops into the backpack icon (PixelGhostMinigame.BuildBackpackSprite, same place as the Ghost's; stashSeconds) with a sparkle burst and a bump, then the screen brightens (fadeOutSeconds). Escape skips the show (a PixelWindows entry, priority 165, so it never opens the pause menu: it jumps to the backpack landing + fade and drops queued shows); heading foundFormat; sounds dragon_find / dragon_stash (code-made PixelSynth.DragonFind/DragonStash); disableShow. Under Infinite resources the Dragon Seed (always held) grows any of the seven even if you hold it (PixelClicker.PickAnyDragonCube).
--- PixelElectricFx.cs ---
PixelElectricFx.cs Looks / effects of the Electrician pixels: PixelLook.electricFx (ElectricAmbient) attaches per-type ambient particles (Wire copper sparks along the edges, Battery rising green bubbles, Capacitor crackling blue static, Resistor red heat embers, Transistor cold blips, Fuse a spark fountain on top, Bulb warm motes), Hit / Break (called from CollectInternal for IsElectricalBlock types, disableFarmFx) throw sparks plus coloured arcs (FractureFx.Play(..., core, glow); smoke for Resistor / Fuse), and each pixel has its own synthesized click sound (PixelSynth.Elec(kind, seed): twang, thunk + hum, charge whine + snap, buzz, digital blip, hiss + pop, glassy ting; used while the pixel_<type> clip list is empty); looksVersion 34.
--- PixelElevator.cs ---
PixelElevator.cs CONVEYOR ELEVATOR (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Conveyor Elevator'): a square tower that lifts pixels from the END of a belt up to a higher belt. PLACING (two stages): 1. the ghost follows the mouse and MAGNETIZES to a belt end that is near it (PixelElevator.TrySnap: it turns to the belt's direction, the belt's end meets its entry face, and its base takes that belt's height); Q / E turn it when it is not latched; click to fix the base. 2. Move the mouse up or down to set the HEIGHT (the mouse ray is cut with a vertical plane through the base, quantised to heightStepCubes) and click to confirm; right click goes back to stage 1. The tower's posts run down to the floor when the base is raised. A belt end that points at the entry hands its pixels straight in (IPixelBeltAcceptor): they are drawn to the shaft, carried up by a PixelPathCarrier and put out of the top (exit face, +Z) onto a belt that STARTS at OutputPoint (PixelConveyor.Snap offers that point, so a new belt snaps to the top of the tower, at its height) or flung forward. Belts can also be raised and lowered while placing (scroll wheel, PixelFeatureTester) so ramps and bridges are possible. Saved with the tester save (position, turn, height). Built with PixelFactoryKit.
--- PixelEvadeMinigame.cs ---
PixelEvadeMinigame.cs Cube Evasion minigame (id evade, pack unlocksMinigame = "evade", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 70K each; like Cube Dash / Blink: Takeover => true, cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (25) starts and the cube RUNS FROM THE MOUSE (Steer, in viewport space measured in screen heights): inside fleeRadius (0.22) it accelerates (agility) directly away from the pointer at up to fleeSpeed (0.5 screen heights per second, faster the closer the mouse is), slides along the wall when it runs into the area's edge (edgeMargin, area* fractions) and, cornered on both axes, breaks back across the middle; away from the mouse it just coasts to a stop. Clicks (manual only) count; at the end it returns home and pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click via AddCurrency; stats evade.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_evade, sounds evade_start/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned), picture case in BuildMinigameModel; a success reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 15).
--- PixelEvilTreeMinigame.cs ---
PixelEvilTreeMinigame.cs Evil Tree minigame (id eviltree, pack unlocksMinigame = "eviltree", Minigames tab, needs the Lumberjack's Pack; extends PixelMinigame): now and then (firstDelay 60 s, then minInterval 150 .. maxInterval 300 s, PixelMinigameLimits.AllowAnother) a sapling sprouts on the floor near the cube (PixelBait.FindGround, kept inside the view) and GROWS over growSeconds (14) into a giant tree (heightInCubes 5 cubes tall) with glowing eyes and a jagged mouth. It FLAILS so it is hard to click: the whole tree leans and jerks to new spots (the click colliders move with it), its arms whip about, and it flails harder as it grows. It has a HEALTH BAR (health 40, each manual click on it does damagePerClick 1; the auto clicker never hits it): at 0 it topples over and pays a random minWood..maxWood MAGIC WOOD (the minigame's tracker / material: PixelMinigame.MaterialName, shown in the Inventory's Materials tab and Crafting, saved like any minigame count) and Woodcutting XP only (SkillXpTarget = Woodcutting, default 30 XP, whatever skill is in training; needs the Woodcutting skill bought). If it is not felled within stayAfterGrownSeconds (30) of being fully grown it sinks back into the ground. Everything is built from primitives at run time (no assets). Magic Wood has no use yet (crafting later).
--- PixelEyedropper.cs ---
PixelEyedropper.cs Eyedropper (created on demand by PixelEyedropper.Begin(callback)): hides the pause menu (PixelPauseMenu.SetMenuVisible(false)), waits for the end of the frame, grabs a screenshot (ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture), shows it frozen full-screen on its own canvas (950) with a small loupe (colour swatch + hex code) following the pointer; left-click hands that colour to the callback, right-click / Escape (PixelWindows 175) cancels, then the menu comes back. Used by the small eyedropper button next to the 'Neon bar glow' tick box in Settings > Display (PixelPauseMenu.AddEyedropperButton, PixelUIKit.EyedropperSprite(); the 'Bar glow colour' hue slider is gone): PixelHud.SetGlowFromColor(colour) stores an exact colour (PixelHud.GlowCustomColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.GlowColor, hex, per save via PixelSettingsSync), sets the hue slider to its hue and switches 'Cycle glow colour' OFF; the neon bar glow of the game's black margins (PixelHud.Update) and the main menu's bar (PixelTitleScreen) read PixelHud.GlowColourNow(...) = the picked colour while static, else the hue (drifting while cycling). Moving the hue slider clears the picked colour; switching Cycle on drifts on from the picked colour's hue.
--- PixelFactoryKit.cs ---
PixelFactoryKit.cs Small shared helper for building machines out of primitives in code (conveyor belt, kiln): materials copied from the cube's material (PixelClicker.CreateVisualMaterial, emission off unless asked), boxes / cylinders / balls, see-through ghost versions (alpha) with no colliders. The Wood Chipper still has its own private copy of this (its Kit class): move it onto this helper when it is next touched.
--- PixelFanDevice.cs ---
PixelFanDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Runtime behaviour of a placed fan (device kind = Fan): pushes old pixels inside a floor wedge (cone angle/length/height) along its forward, spinning blades, timer. Placed like the vacuum, turned with scroll wheel or Q/E while placing.
--- PixelFarmFx.cs ---
PixelFarmFx.cs Farming pixel particles: PixelFarmFx.Attach (ambient loop per PixelLook.farmFx: Pollen, Dew, Crumbs, Spores, Chaff, Sparkle, Bugs; attached in PixelLooks.AddExtras), Hit (non-final hit) and Break (per-type burst, gold ring for Golden Fruit) called from CollectInternal (disableFarmFx); sounds are PixelSynth.Farm(kind, seed) (pixel_dirt/fertilizer/pollen/wheat/weevil/goldenfruit, farm_hit, farm_gleam); looksVersion 29. Seed / Water are no longer in IsSpecialType (no Toggles spawn switch). PixelSkills.Update unlocks every Farming type once the first is unlocked.
--- PixelFeatureTester.cs ---
PixelFeatureTester.cs The 'Tester' tab of the Dev Hub: a place to spawn and try things that are not part of the game yet (a consumable, a device, a machine...). Each testable thing is a Feature registered in Awake (Register(name, description, ghostBuilder, placeBuilder)): the tab lists them as cards with a 'Place' button. Place = a see-through GHOST follows the mouse over the floor (PixelConsumables.TryGetFloorPoint); left click puts a real one down (and keeps placing), Q / E turn it, right click or Escape stops (PixelWindows entry); 'Clear' removes the ones placed. While placing, clicks on the cube are blocked (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock). Placed test objects ARE saved (see SAVING below). To add something to test: write a builder (see PixelWoodChipper: CreateGhost / Place) and add one Register line here. Two-point features (RegisterLine, a conveyor belt) use a marker ghost, then a preview piece between the first point and the mouse; every click builds a piece and the end starts the next. DELETE: hold the right button on any placed tester object (not while placing) to remove it (PixelHold ring; a quick right click still flips a belt / re-links a panel: PixelRightTap). BELT SELECTION card: 'Select belts' mode toggles belts by click or box-drag (teal glow, PixelConveyor.SetSelected) and sets speed / reverses them together. Placing snaps to a grid by default; SHIFT = free placement. (It used to be: SHIFT while placing snaps to a grid and angle steps (PixelSnap). SAVING: every placed object is saved with the save file (PixelFeatureTester.ExportAll / ImportAll, TesterSave = feature name + JSON from the feature's ExportState / ImportState; hooked into PixelSaveGame after the placed devices are restored). LINKING (Solar Panel): after placing, linkPanel mode - click a device inside the ring; a right click on a placed panel starts it again. The Conveyor Belt is also a real game item (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.ConveyorBelt, on sale at Electrician level 5): its right click calls PixelFeatureTester.StartFeature("Conveyor Belt"), which places without the Dev Hub. Features now: Vacuum Cleaner (PixelVacuumCleaner.cs: drive with W A S D / arrows, the front intake sucks husks up and the hose spits them out where the mouse aims), Container Hose (PixelContainerHose.cs: a single line from a crate / organizer to a Recycler / crate / organizer / trash can), Wood Chipper (PixelWoodChipper.cs), Conveyor Belt (PixelConveyor.cs, PixelFactoryKit.cs), Kiln (PixelKiln.cs; makes Charcoal old pixels), Solar Panel (PixelSolarPanel.cs).
--- PixelFeedback.cs ---
PixelFeedback.cs Give feedback (for testers): the pause menu's long green Give feedback button (right above Quit; no longer in Settings) or the rebindable PixelAction.Feedback key (default F8; PixelKeys enum value 14, label 'Give feedback') opens a 1400x960 window (own canvas 800, above the pause menu, PixelPop): a multi-line text box, Take screenshot (CaptureRoutine: hides this window's canvas and the pause menu's (PixelPauseMenu.SetMenuVisible(false)) for a frame, WaitForEndOfFrame, ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture = the game without any menu; up to maxShots 4, selected with small numbered buttons in the drawing area), a pencil (8 colours, fixed width pencilWidthFraction of the picture; strokes are drawn straight into the texture's pixel array by PixelFeedbackCanvas pointer events), Undo (removes the last stroke and replays the rest over the clean screenshot, maxStrokes), Remove shot, Open feedback folder, Cancel and Save feedback: everything is appended to ONE text file <persistentDataPath>/Feedback/feedback.txt (a block per entry: date / time, game version, Unity version, platform, session minutes, the text, the screenshot file names) and each (drawn) screenshot is saved next to it as feedback_<timestamp>_<n>.png. While it is open the game is paused (restored on close; the pause menu already pauses) and PixelKeys.Typing reads true so the game's keys do nothing; Escape closes it (PixelFeedback.Open / ClosedThisFrame keep the pause menu from also stepping back). Added to the pause menu's object by PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI.
--- PixelFilters.cs ---
PixelFilters.cs Screen filters (Settings > Display > 'Screen filter' drop-down + 'Filter strength' slider; saved per profile through PixelSettingsSync: PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.Filter / FilterStrength; component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelFilters.Instance). A filter is a Preset (Inspector list `presets`, index 0 = Off; defaults CreateDefaults: CRT TV, VHS, Wild West, Noir, Night Vision, Neon Dream) with TWO parts, both scaled by the strength slider: 1) URP post-processing (only with the Universal RP package, #if PIXEL_URP and a URP pipeline asset): a runtime global Volume with ColorAdjustments, WhiteBalance, Vignette, FilmGrain, ChromaticAberration, LensDistortion and Bloom, blended from neutral to the preset by the strength; the cameras get renderPostProcessing switched on (re-checked every cameraCheckSeconds). Without URP this part is skipped and only the overlay works. 2) A screen-space overlay canvas (sortingOrder overlaySortingOrder, above the UI so it also covers windows like a real screen): colour tint, vignette, CRT rounded corners (mask), scanlines (tiled 4 px texture, rolling), animated static noise, a VHS tracking band, film dust / scratches. All textures are generated in code once. 3) THE SCREEN WARP (presets with warp > 0, shader Assets/Resources/PixelCrtWarp.shader 'Pixel Clicker/CRT Warp'): at the end of every frame the finished picture (world AND all UI) is copied (ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotIntoRenderTexture) and drawn back through the shader: barrel warp, rounded black glass corners, scanlines, colour fringing, vignette. The game's real input is untouched (everything still reads the real pointer = a point of the unwarped picture), so the OS cursor is hidden and a software cursor is drawn by the shader at the warped position of that point (InverseWarp) - the cursor always sits on what you click. If the picture comes out upside down, tick `flipVertical`. Off = the volume is disabled, the overlay canvas hidden and the warp loop stopped, so it costs nothing.
--- PixelFind.cs ---
PixelFind.cs PixelFind.First<T>() / All<T>() / Count<T>(): the only place with the FindFirstObjectByType / FindObjectOfType version check. Use it instead of calling Unity's Find methods directly. PixelFind.Cached<T>(): the same as First, but it remembers the answer (searches again only when the remembered object was destroyed, e.g. after Settings > Restart reloads the scene, or when none was found yet). Use it for the game's single components (PixelShop, PixelStats, PixelSkills...) anywhere that runs often; First does a full scene search on every call.
--- PixelFirstPersonGrid.cs ---
PixelFirstPersonGrid.cs The first person floor: while PixelFirstPerson is active the normal floor is hidden (PixelFloor.FloorOff includes PixelFirstPerson.Active, the ground collider stays) and this draws a bright neon grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader, world-space lines) on a quad just above the ground, only within a radius of lit points: the player, the clicker cube and old pixels resting on the ground (up to 64, nearest to the player first; collected every 0.1 s). Settings live on PixelFirstPerson (header "Neon floor"). Added/removed by PixelFirstPerson.Enter / Exit.
--- PixelFirstPersonHotbar.cs ---
PixelFirstPersonHotbar.cs The first person HOTBAR (added by PixelFirstPerson.Enter, removed on Exit): five slots along the bottom, picked with the mouse wheel (when you are not holding a cube - then the wheel moves the cube) or the number keys 1-5. Slot 1 holds the AXE; the other slots are empty hands (pick things up, click the cube). While the axe is picked it is drawn in your hand (a viewmodel built from primitives, child of the first person camera) and StartSwing(onImpact) plays the swing: wind-up (0 - 0.3), strike (0.3 - 0.5, onImpact fires at 0.5 = the moment it connects), recover. The swing sound is played here; the hit sound and the rewards are PixelFirstPerson's (ChopOldPixel / the axe bonus on PixelCollected). Tunables are serialized on PixelFirstPerson ("Axe") and passed into Setup.
--- PixelFirstPersonWall.cs ---
PixelFirstPersonWall.cs Out-of-bounds protection for first person (added/removed by PixelFirstPerson.Enter / Exit; settings under its "Out of bounds" header). The play area is the same circle old pixels are kept in (PixelFloor.TryGetEdgeCircle: where the floor fades out). Constrain() runs after every move: the player is pushed back inside the circle (by the controller's radius), and if they ever fall far below the ground (through a gap, out of the world) they are put back at the spot they entered at. VISIBLE WALL: a neon wireframe cylinder drawn at the circle - a grid of thin quads (vertical lines + rings, SpriteDefault vertex colours) built as a small PATCH of columns round the player's angle, rebuilt every frame (about a hundred quads) and faded by distance, so it only appears when the player is within wallVisibleCubes of the edge and the lines stay put on the wall as the patch follows.
--- PixelFishingSpawns.cs ---
PixelFishingSpawns.cs Fishing's FIXED spawn table (part of partial class PixelClicker; used by PickSpawnTier and SpawnShare while Fishing is the skill in play, `disableFishingTable` = the old filler / hazard weights): Water 80.5%, Driftwood 5%, Seaweed 7.5%, Jellyfish 3%, Shell 3%, Fish 1% (`fishWaterShare` ... `fishFishShare`, 0 = these defaults; they are weights, so they are normalised if one block is missing). Everything else comes from a replacement roll: a rolled Fish becomes a WHALE `whaleChance` (0 = 5%) of the time, a rolled Seaweed or Shell becomes a message in a BOTTLE `bottleChance` (0 = 2%), a rolled Shell can instead become a PEARL `pearlChance` (0 = 6%), and collecting a Water pixel makes the NEXT pixel a Bait block with `baitChance` (0 = 10%, QueueBaitAfter). Bait pellets still raise Fish's weight (PixelBait.FishMultiplier) and active pets their type's (PixelPets.SpawnMultiplier). SpawnShare (the Pixel Guide) works out the same numbers, bait included.
--- PixelFloorFade.cs ---
PixelFloorFade.cs FADE INTO THE SKY (fadeIntoSky, on by default): a painted floor uses Resources/PixelFadedFloor.shader (unlit, alpha) that fades the floor itself to fully see-through past the fade circle (_Center / _FadeStart / _FadeEnd set by UpdateGroundShader, _Light follows the day / night cycle), so the solid sky-coloured ring (below) is not needed and the skybox shows all the way down; only the wireframe rim stays. PixelFloor.SelfFading = ground shader or faded picture. Floor edge fade (partial class PixelFloor, UpdateEdgeFade from LateUpdate; disableEdgeFade): the floor fades out in a circle round the player (the cube, or the first person player via PixelFirstPerson.TryGetPlayerPosition) so its edges / seams are never seen: a flat annulus mesh with vertex alpha (BuildEdgeFade: see-through inside r0, solid from r1 and far past the floor's edges, coloured like the sky's horizon (EdgeFadeColor: TryGetHorizonColor, else the scene sky tint / background / the floor's average), Sprites/Default, a hair above the floor top) plus a digital wireframe in the rim (RebuildWire: a world-aligned grid every wireSpacingCubes (0 = 1.25 pixel widths) and three rings, clipped to the rim, brightness bell-shaped across it, wireColor (alpha 0 = cyan), wireStrength 0 = 0.9; rebuilt when the centre moves). fadeStartCubes / fadeWidthCubes 0 = automatic from the floor's size (start about half the way to the nearest edge) - BUT with a base view (PixelOrbitCamera) every floor uses the STANDARD area instead (StandardRadii: fade starts at standardFadeStart 0.9 of the screen's half width on the floor at 16:9, solid at standardFadeEnd 1.3, so the floor fills the screen and only the sides / corners show the wireframe; stretched one-copy pictures are enlarged to cover it, EffectiveTileSize). The ring is solid only OVER the floor: past the floor's own edge its alpha melts away over ringSoft (UpdateRingAlpha, per-vertex, redone when the centre moves), so the sky below the horizon shows instead of a flat horizon-coloured band. Scaled by the floor's own fade-in visibility; hidden with the floor / FloorOff.
--- PixelFloorLive.cs ---
PixelFloorLive.cs The fifth batch of floors (part of partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeWorlds' fall-through -> ShadeLive; styles from LiveStyles) and the LIVE OVERLAY (PixelFloorOverlay: real 3D objects on the floor plane, updated every frame, so they can follow the mouse without redrawing a texture). GRASS (big still pictures like Lush Grass: 1024 px, stretchToFloor over 40 world units, drawn in WORLD units on worker threads, never repeated or stretched; every blade is its own tapered stroke from a 3 x 3 cell search - BladeLayer - bent by a noise wind field): Wild Meadow (hue patches, wind-bent blades, wildflower clusters, seed heads), Clover Lawn (curving mower stripes, three-leaf clover patches, daisies, worn spots, a fairy ring of mushrooms), Mossy Glade (speckled moss, ferns, mossy stones, twigs, puddles), Prairie Grass (tall golden wind-swept blades, green patches, purple flowers, rocks). SEE-THROUGH LASERS (FloorStyle.hideBase: the floor picture is hidden - ApplyVisibility / the edge fade read HideBaseNow - but the collider stays): Neon Lasers (overlay Lasers: beams drifting / turning / pulsing over nothing, neon colours from the style), Laser Tracker (overlay LaserTracker: emitters round the cube shoot beams that all converge on the mouse's point on the floor, with a pulsing ring there). The beams are flat LineRenderer ribbons (TransformZ alignment, the transform turned Euler(90,0,0) so they face the camera from above). The style's still picture (ShadeLaserPicture, 160 px) is never on the floor; it is the menu icon and the title screen's background picture. PICNIC BLANKET: a gingham blanket with a woven texture, fringe, folds and crumbs on meadow grass, plus overlay Ant: a small 3D ant (built from primitives, no colliders) that walks to the mouse's point on the floor with a tripod leg gait and wiggling antennae, and wanders when the mouse is off the floor. The overlay fades with the floor (Display floor / the menu fade) and follows FloorStyle.overlay.
--- PixelFloorModes.cs ---
PixelFloorModes.cs What the floor does while 'Display floor' is OFF (Settings > Display, 'When floor is off': PixelFloor.OffMode, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FloorOffMode, global): 0 Invisible (only the sky, the default), 1 Flat plane (PixelFloor.UpdatePlane: a flat opaque lit sheet in the floor style's average colour just under the floor top, receives shadows; the edge fade melts its rim into the sky), 2 Landing pulse (PixelFloorPulse.Landed / LandedAt, called from OldPixelImpact - OnCollisionEnter plus a fall check in its FixedUpdate for pixels that stop after a fast fall without a collision event: a soft rounded-square ripple, tinted by the pixel's colour, lies on the surface the pixel landed on - turned to the contact normal and the pixel's heading, sized by the pixel and the impact - grows and fades in about 0.7 s; at most 24 at once, so it also works on slopes / shapes / any other solid surface).
--- PixelFocus.cs ---
PixelFocus.cs FOCUS POTIONS (component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelFocus.Instance; one potion per skill, the list is an Inspector list: displayName, skillId, durationSeconds). While one is active, the HAZARD block of its skill stops spawning (PixelSkills.TryGetHazard returns false, so SpecialInfo gives the hazard no weight and the rest of the rotation renormalises; the pre-rolled Next-up block is dropped too, PixelClicker.UpcomingValid). A hazard that is already on the cube is NOT removed. The filler is untouched (the shuffle bonus still works). Only the skill named in skillId is affected, and only while that skill is in play; the timer keeps running if the player switches away. First potion: Lumberjack's Focus (woodcutting, 60 s, holds off the Termite Hive, the Hornet Nest block's new name). NOT sold anywhere yet: for now it is consumed from Dev Tools (the 'Focus potions' widget: drop-down + Drink + Cancel) through Drink(index) / Cancel(). A shop / crafting recipe from the skill's own materials is the intended source later. Runs on game time (pauses with the game), one potion at a time (drinking another replaces it), not saved. The countdown shows as a PixelStatusLines line (key 'focus').
--- PixelFreeCam.cs ---
PixelFreeCam.cs FREE CAM (View window tick box 'Free cam', PixelViewKind.FreeCam): the scene camera is detached and flies about by hand while the game carries on and stays clickable. W / S move forward / back along the view, A / D strafe, Space up, C down, Shift = fast; look round with the middle mouse button dragged (or the arrow keys); Ctrl + mouse wheel changes the flying speed. Like the orbit camera it keeps a hidden, disabled copy of the camera at its normal pose (PixelFreeCam.Home, read through PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera) so the view bounds and fitted floors do not follow the wandering camera, and leaving it (another view tick, a takeover minigame, the title screen, the camera intro) snaps the camera back to that pose. It is never saved as the player's view (PixelViewWindow.SaveKind skips it): the saved view (Orbital / Top down) is what comes back. The flat (orthographic) view is turned off while it is on (PixelOrthoView), like in first person.
--- PixelFunnel.cs ---
PixelFunnel.cs The FUNNEL (consumable device, PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Funnel 16, one at a time; the placed device is the thin PixelChipperDevice stub on the same object). A wide glass cone hangs just UNDER the clicker cube (mouth radius mouthRadius cube widths, its mouth mouthDrop below the cube's underside) and catches every loose old pixel that falls into it: the pixel is locked (kinematic, moved by position by the shared PixelPathCarrier), drawn to the throat and carried down a see-through TUBE (PixelTube, a smooth curve from the throat to the output) at tubeSpeed cube widths per second, one gap apart. The output sits on the floor where the player clicked (the object's own position; the spout points away from the cube, Scroll / Q / E turns it a bit, a conveyor belt opening in front of the spout makes it latch on like the Wood Chipper); at the end the pixel is put back as an ordinary old pixel (physics again) with a small push, or dropped onto the belt. While placing, the output can only be put within maxReach cube widths of the cube. Placing a second funnel MOVES the first (no extra one is used up). Ghost / display (shop picture) models are built by the same code (Mode); the cone and tube are pinned to the WORLD (the object's yaw only turns the spout). Riders in the tube are plain HOLD (backpressure + physics pile): the cone has a SOLID surface (a hidden two-sided mesh collider: the sloped wall and a floor closing the throat), so caught pixels do not vanish into the tube at once: they land in the cone and PILE UP with real physics. One by one (holdFeedSeconds), while the tube has room, the lowest pixel in the cone is locked and carried down the tube.
