At the spout the tube waits (PixelPathCarrier canExit = ExitHasRoom) when a belt starts there and has no room for another pixel (PixelConveyorSystem.StartHasRoom) or a connector there is full, so a flooding hose queues up the tube and then fills the cone (the pile can heap up over the rim) instead of overflowing the belt. disableHold = the old direct catch (no collider).
--- PixelFunnelDevice.cs ---
PixelFunnelDevice.cs The placed Funnel as a DEVICE (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Funnel 16): the same permanent stub as the Wood Chipper (PixelChipperDevice) but only the OUTPUT parts (base plate and collar) count when the game asks "is the pointer over this device" (removing it with the right button): the big cone, rim, legs and tube hang round the cube and must not catch clicks meant for it. All the behaviour is in PixelFunnel.
--- PixelGelFx.cs ---
PixelGelFx.cs The Ghost pixel's look (looksVersion 39 replaced the saved look): a gray, translucent, gelatinous blob - PixelLook.blob swaps the cube for a very rounded one (PixelGelFx.BlobMesh, PixelEgg.RoundedCubeMesh(0.32) scaled 1.1x so it doesn't look smaller than the other pixels, via PixelClicker.MeshFor; hit box still a cube), PixelLook.gelFx adds a darker nucleus that drifts, turns and breathes inside (PixelGelCore, unlit vertex-coloured rounded cube) and a few tiny bubbles rising through it; stronger jelly wobble (wobbleAmount 0.2, speed 2.8); colour gray (0.62, 0.66, 0.71, alpha 0.4), glossy 0.92. Old behaviour unchanged (bounces slowly, floats off screen). Sounds (code-synthesized while their clip lists are empty): click pixel_ghost = PixelSynth.GhostClick (a wet gloopy bloop with a second bubble, a squelch and a breathy tail, 3 variants), landing pixel_bounce_ghost = GhostBounce (soft squishy splut), and ghost_float (played by OldPixelFloat.StartFloating) = GhostFloat (a hollow breathy rising 'whoo').
--- PixelGhostMinigame.cs ---
PixelGhostMinigame.cs Ghost minigame (bought in the shop's Minigames tab via a pack with unlocksMinigame = "ghost"): a faint wobbling cube floats across the screen at random intervals; the ghost carries a small glass cube with the chosen potion's pixel cube inside (PickRandomBuff at spawn, BuildPotion); clicking it drops the potion (DropPotion: rigidbody, shatters on first hit or after dropMaxSeconds, glass shards like the glass pixel, sound glass_shatter) and the free buff starts then (PixelConsumables.ApplyBuff; GrantRandomBuff is the fallback when no potion could be picked); counts toward GhostsCaught (saved); extends PixelMinigame (id ghost); the Ghost Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for ghost. PixelShop finds/adds this component. Backpack (needs Pixel Grabbing switched on in Toggles - it is unlocked from the start, no purchase, PixelGrab.CanGrab): the falling potion has air drag (grabFallDrag) and can be grabbed with the mouse (potionGrabRadius, kinematic follow, throw on release); a semi-transparent pixel-art backpack icon (bottom right above the bar, backpackSize/Margin/Alpha) shows while it falls; releasing over the icon keeps the potion (KeepPotion, PixelConsumables.AddKept, no shatter, no buff) and, if that potion is already at its limit, raises that potion's bonusCap by one (permanent, saved as PotionSave.bonus; ItemCapacity(item) = maxPotionsHeld + bonus drives ItemRoom/ClampHeld(index, n)/the shop's Full text), so e.g. 16 held can be used down to 6 and re-bought to 16.
--- PixelGodFind.cs ---
PixelGodFind.cs The finds of the three Egyptian God Cubes (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; PixelGodFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos), queued; called from PixelClicker.CollectInternal when a God Cube pays out). The cubes (PixelType.GodSlifer / GodRa / GodObelisk, PixelClicker.IsGodCube, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes, spawn weight godCubeSpawnWeight = 0.000001, one of each: CanSpawn is false and AddCurrency ignores a second one) have their OWN show each, all drawn in UI space on a canvas that blocks clicks (sorting 691, unscaled time, Escape skips to the backpack like the Dragon Cube show): SLIFER - a storm: dark clouds, three lightning strikes (flash + jagged bolts), a long red lightning serpent that flies across the screen in a figure-eight with crackling arcs, then coils into the cube in a final blast. RA - a golden sphere rises and grows, bursts into a sun (rays, whiteout), then golden wings (two fans of feathers) beat around the cube to the rhythm of the sound while feathers fall, and fold in with a golden shockwave. OBELISK - the screen shakes harder and harder as rubble falls, a giant blue fist lowers into view, cocks back and SLAMS the screen: flash, crack lines radiate, shockwaves, debris; the cube rises from the crater. Then the cube hops into the backpack like the Dragon Cube's. Sounds god_slifer / god_ra / god_obelisk (code-made PixelGodSynth, timed to the shows). Settings: disableShow, showSpeed, darkness, cubeSize. The cube picture is a hidden studio camera -> RenderTexture (a trimmed copy of PixelDragonFind's studio; the two could share one helper).
--- PixelGodSynth.cs ---
PixelGodSynth.cs Code-made sounds of the three Egyptian God Cube find shows (PixelAudio.SynthFor -> PixelGodSynth.For; put your own clips in the Sounds list to replace them). Each clip is one long piece that is timed to its show in PixelGodFind (start of the show = start of the clip): god_slifer (storm rumble, three thunder cracks at 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.4 s, the dragon's roar from 1.5 s, a final blast at 4.9 s), stone_space / stone_mind / stone_reality / stone_power / stone_time / stone_soul (the Prime Cubes, 5.5 s, each one's own voice, the climax at 3.2 s) and stone_cubelette (six rising notes, the snap at 3.4 s, a chime at 5.0 s), god_ra (a rising shimmering choir, a swell at 1.2 s, wing beats from 1.6 s, a golden chime at 4.0 s) and god_obelisk (a growing rumble, the fist rising, the slam at 2.55 s, falling stone).
--- PixelGoldenSnitch.cs ---
PixelGoldenSnitch.cs The GOLDEN SNITCH (PixelType.GoldenSnitch 101): a very rare drop (a rareDrop tier like the Dragon Cubes, PixelClicker.EnsureGoldenSnitch, spawnWeight goldenSnitchSpawnWeight 0.00003) that is never clicked off the cube (CollectInternal ignores it, Block Hold can't grab it). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake. 0. ON THE CUBE: it lands (PixelClicker.PixelMaterialized) and already LOOKS like the snitch: the detailed golden cube model - engraved dark edges, a middle band, bright corner studs and face gems, gold wing knobs, a soft cube glow shell, a gold point light, sparkles - sits over the clicker cube (onCubeCover 1.03 x its size) and follows its turn / pops every frame (LateUpdate -> FollowCube), wings folded away. It waits for a MANUAL click (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> CubeClicked; the auto clicker leaves it alone). Nobody clicks it for onCubeSeconds (120) and it slips away (escaped). 1. SPROUT: the click makes that same cube sprout two silver feathered wings right where it sits (sproutSeconds 1.1, fluttering faster and faster) while it eases off the cube towards the camera and turns to face it. The cube rolls a fresh block behind it (PixelClicker.ReplaceCurrentPixel). 2. CHASE: it darts round the screen fast (viewport steering inside the black bars: quick darts to far points, little hops, feints, a flutter), leaving a gold trail, its wings buzzing (snitch_buzz). Keep the mouse ON it (hoverScale x its size on screen) and a bar above it charges (chargeSeconds 3.2 of hovering); off it the bar does NOT charge (drainPerSecond 0 = it just waits). It flies faster and more wildly the fuller the bar. A click while it is still charging costs earlyPenalty (25%) of the bar ('Too early!'). 3. CAPTURE: with the bar full a white ring closes in on it towards a gold target ring (ringSeconds 1.1); CLICK while the mouse is on it AND the rings meet (captureTolerance 0.2) to catch it. Too early / too late = a miss: the bar drops to missKeep (35%), it bolts away; maxMisses (3) misses or its lifetime (lifetimeSeconds 30) and it escapes off the screen. CAUGHT: it freezes, folds its wings and bursts into gold sparkles: it counts as collected (the Golden Snitch tier +1) and pays rewardClicks (400) x what a click on your best unlocked pixel pays now, plus an ultraChance (50%) Ultra pixel; minigame-style skill XP (skillXp 40 to the skill in training). Status lines (key 'snitch'), event log lines, code-made sounds (PixelSnitchSynth via PixelAudio: snitch_appear / buzz / ready / catch / miss), Stats (snitch.seen / caught / escaped, Best snitch.fastest). In first person the crosshair is the pointer. Pauses with the pause menu; not saved (one in flight is simply lost on quit).
--- PixelGrab.cs ---
PixelGrab.cs Pixel Grabbing (unlocked from the start - Awake sets grabbingActive; the shop pack was removed by MigrateOldPackData; it keeps its on / off switch in Toggles > Upgrades, on by default): hold left mouse on an old pixel (sphere-cast, grabRadius) to drag it on the camera-facing plane through it (rigidbody velocity follows the mouse, so it still collides), release to throw (throwStrength, maxThrowSpeed); a held pixel's OldPixelDespawn.Held freezes its lifetime (a held glass pixel can't shatter; releasing slower than gentleReleaseSpeed = a soft set-down: no break, speed damped, gentleLifetimeBonus seconds added via OldPixelDespawn.AddLifetime) and its ScaledGravity is paused. While time is slowed a still-flying meteor pixel (OldPixelFlight, trigger collider) can be grabbed too: PixelClicker.LandFlyingPixel turns it into an ordinary old pixel (solid, gravity, view bounds, normal lifetime); grabbing / throwing divide / multiply velocities by PixelTimeStop.SlowFactor so it still follows the mouse normally. Gentle collisions: a carried pixel is light (carriedMassFactor 0 = 0.2 of its mass, stays light lightSecondsAfterThrow 1.5 s after the throw) and every old pixel within ~1.7 widths of a light one has its speed capped to hitReactionSpeedLimit (0 = 5) for 0.7 s (LimitReactions, MakeLight), so piles react less and nothing flies far; physics still applies (disableGentleHits). Sets PixelClicker.GrabEnabled so clicks on old pixels no longer pass through to the cube. Sounds grab/drop. Steady carrying (2026-10-16, held husks used to jitter / bounce against the floor and piles): while held a husk is interpolated, uses continuous collisions, can't tumble (freezeSpinWhileHeld), is pushed out of things gently (heldDepenetrationSpeed 2) and is lifted heldAutoLift (0.35) widths off what it rested on; all of it is put back on release. PixelShop finds/adds it. Tags: OldPixelInfo.heldByPlayer / lastHeldTime / RecentlyHeld(seconds) = 'recently held by the player' (held now, or let go within recentlyHeldSeconds 0 = 6, Inspector); the Storage Crate stores a tagged pixel that lands in its footprint from any height. Accessibility 'Drop guide for held pixels' (Settings, PixelDisplaySettings.DropGuide, on by default): while holding a pixel LateUpdate / UpdateDropGuide draws a faint line straight down from it to the first surface a sphere of its size would land on (ignores itself, triggers and the live cube) and a ring on that surface = where it drops if let go standing still.
--- PixelGreatOak.cs ---
PixelGreatOak.cs The GREAT OAK (PixelType.GreatOak 77, PixelBlockRules special rule): a huge Woodcutting block that takes a random 50-100 clicks. While Woodcutting is the skill in play every rolled wood block (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig) has a 5% chance of becoming one (edit it in PixelBlockRules; no spawn weight of its own). While you hit it, BIRD NESTS fall out of the tree (this component, added by PixelClicker.Awake, listens to PixelClicker.PixelHit): each hit has a nestChance (0 = 40%) to drop one, a nest (a woven ring with eggs) lands on the ground near the cube and stays for nestSeconds (0 = 9, it blinks away in the last 2 s). Click it (the pointer, or the crosshair in first person - with any item selected) before it vanishes for nestXp (0 = 30) Woodcutting XP, a chirp and a puff of feathers. At most maxNests (0 = 10) lie about at once; each nest follows a short scripted arc out of a random SIDE of the cube (no physics and no collider at all, so it cannot bounce away), lands close to the cube (up to scatterCubes, 0 = 3.2 cube widths), makes one small hop and stays.
--- PixelGuideVendor.cs ---
PixelGuideVendor.cs Cubie's intro visits only start when nothing else is open (Calm: no window in PixelWindows, no tip box, no minigame...) and only while 'Show first-time tips' (PixelHints.TipsEnabled) is on: with tips off the visits are skipped, their progress is recorded and the starter gift is handed over quietly once the shop exists (Update); Replay tutorial still plays them. Cubie, the suspicious cube guide / shopkeeper (added by PixelClicker.Awake; code-drawn teal cube with darting sideways eyes, hat, smirk; slides in like the visitors, time stops, a paged speech box, Next button or Escape = next page). Visit 1 (new game, after the intro tips, calmSeconds (1.5) of calm): a shady dealer's pitch - pixels are money 'because that's what we're all told' (a fiat-currency joke), clicking is the monotonous grind you endure until the good stuff opens up - then he leaves and you click pixels (4 pages; visit 2 has 7 and ends on the same theme: the clicking never stops, it just pays better). Visit 2 (RGB UNLOCK, 2026-10-16: when visitTwoAfterBlack (100) Black pixels have been EARNED - Visit2Due, BlackUntilVisitTwo for the guide line / Log goal): he says the basics are done, GIVES THE RGB PACK for free (PixelShop.GrantPackRewarding; the pack is hidden from the shop, so everything that needs it still works) plus a taste of Red / Green / Blue (rgbGiftMin..rgbGiftMax each, AddBonusCurrency - a gift is not 'earned') when the page containing {gift} shows (a gift box appears in his hand); the shop opens with it (PixelShop.requiredTier Red); he points at the Auto Clicker as the first buy and at a skill (Big Tim) next; with Cubie off (disableGuide) RGB unlocks quietly at the same point; says more vendors will come and can be switched off in the Toggles menu, mentions the Wizard. Progress = stat counters guide.stage1/2 (saved with the game); a save that already has the shop gets no visits and no gift (stages set silently). PixelGuideVendor.Pending/Visiting hold back the shop tutorial; Settings > Replay tutorial calls PixelGuideVendor.Replay() (clears the stage counters, both visits play again, no second gift). The vendors are free now: the Wizard / Tinkerer / Pixel Entrepreneur packs were removed from the shop (MigrateOldPackData drops them from saved lists); the guide starts them (StartVendors: Wizard right after visit 2 with a first visit in wizardFirstVisitMin..Max s, Farmer at farmerAtTiers (5), Tinkerer at tinkererAtTiers (6) unlocked pixel types, Entrepreneur at entrepreneurAtTiers (8); a vendor the player switched off in Toggles is never restarted; PixelVisitorMinigame.ScheduleFirstVisit). All texts / numbers are Inspector fields (disableGuide turns it all off). The page lists (visitOnePages / visitTwoPages) are filled with the built-in lines when empty (EnsureDefaultPages in Awake and the editor's OnValidate, like PixelHints), so Cubie's lines are edited in the Inspector. Visit 1 waits until visitOneAfterWhiteClicks (100) White pixels have been clicked (the White tier's timesCollected). A visit-1 page containing {recycler} hands over a Recycler (PixelConsumables.GiveDevice; text recyclerGiftText); a page list without the token still gets it when the visit ends.
--- PixelHazardTell.cs ---
PixelHazardTell.cs TELLS that the block on the cube is a HAZARD (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; applies to every hazard block - PixelClicker.IsHazard: Hornet Nest, Pest, Gas Pocket, Jellyfish, Mold - while it sits on the cube): a pulsing warning-orange glow shell round the cube, a bobbing red warning triangle with a '!' floating above it (a billboard), and a warning sound (hazard_warn, a two-tone warble) the moment the hazard appears, plus a dry skittering chitter (termite_chitter) for the Termite Hive. Everything pulses FASTER as the hazard's time on the cube runs out (it leaves by itself after hazardSeconds), so the pulse also tells how long is left. Switch off with disableTells; colours / sizes are serialized. Sounds (code-made): PixelHazardSynth, hooked into PixelAudio.SynthFor.
--- PixelHighlight.cs ---
PixelHighlight.cs Shared pulsing gold frame round ANY UI element (the Shop button's highlight, made reusable): PixelHighlight.Set(owner, rect, colour) frames 'rect' on its own canvas (sorting order 615: above the windows, below the tip boxes at 620), Clear(owner) fades it out (only the owner that set it can clear it). One frame at a time; it follows the rect every frame (windows popping in / moving) and hides while the rect is inactive. Used by PixelShopNudge (the Shop button while the shop hold is on) and PixelSkills' perk popups (the skill's card, e.g. Standard level 2). Created on demand, no scene setup.
--- PixelHintBox.cs ---
PixelHintBox.cs A bottom-of-screen instruction line inside a window-style box (same look as PixelStatusBox, which does the same job at the top). PixelHintBox.Create(font, canvasTransform, fontSize, bottomOffset) returns the TMP label: set its .text as before (PixelUIKit.SetText also works). The box grows / shrinks with the text (wraps at MaxWidth) and is invisible while the text is empty, so callers need no show / hide code. Used by the first person hint line (PixelFirstPerson). Look values are consts at the top (no component of its own in the scene).
--- PixelHold.cs ---
PixelHold.cs LOOK (PixelHoldMeter): the meter sits to the RIGHT of the pointer (left when there is no room), bigger (ring 104, bar 300 x 30): dark plate, thick ring with a bright spark at its end and the percentage in the middle, a pulsing glow that strengthens as it fills, a pop-in; the bar version has a glossy fill, a rim and a spark. First person pry puts it beside the crosshair. PixelHold.Update(caller, target, seconds, label, colour, circle, buildSound, useLeft): hold-the-right (or, useLeft, the LEFT) mouse-button helper with a filling bar next to the pointer (PixelHoldMeter, built lazily) or, with circle = true, ONLY a ring that fills round the cursor (no text) and a rising 'buildup' sound that stops when the hold ends (PixelHold.ProgressOf(target) lets the owner react, e.g. the removed device glows red); true when the hold completes. Used by PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval (hold on a placed device -> PixelPlacedDevice.RemoveNow(), no refund; hit-test = renderer bounds, ignoring the range disc / blow area) and PixelUI.UpdateActiveHud (hold on the active-potion line -> PixelConsumables.CancelActive). Settings (removeHoldSeconds, texts, colour) are in PixelConsumables; sound device_remove.
--- PixelHud.cs ---
PixelHud.cs Screen frame: black bar along the top and bottom (barHeight) and the docked buttons. PixelHud.Ensure(gameObject) then hud.Dock(buttonRect, anchor(0|1,0|1), keepOutWhile) gives a button the standard ButtonSize and makes it slide off screen (only a sliver shows) until the mouse is near (PixelDockedButton); it stays out while its window is open. Windows that open from a docked button use hud.SideShownInset / hud.WindowOffset for position (both equal the gap the docked buttons keep from the black margin, ShownInset, so windows and buttons line up with the same gap on every side); top/bottom-anchored overlays add PixelHud.Instance.BarHeight. Used by Inventory, Shop, Log and Crafting buttons. Menu buttons setting (PixelHud.ButtonMode, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.ButtonMode, per save, Settings > Interface 'Menu buttons'): 0 peeking (sliver shows, default), 1 always showing (SlideButtons false), 2 hidden (Sliver 0 until the mouse is near). While 'always showing' (mode 1) each docked button gets its own nested canvas with overrideSorting at alwaysOutSortingOrder (0 = 90, PixelDockedButton.UpdateSorting, plus a GraphicRaycaster), below every window canvas (100+), so windows overlay the buttons; other modes use the window's own canvas order again. Bar colour: Settings > Display 'Custom bar colour' tick box + an eyedropper button beside it (PixelHud.BarColorOn / BarColorPicked / SetBarColorFromColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarColorOn + BarColor (hex); the player's own, NOT per save - see the note in PixelSettingsSync.Known_); the game's top / bottom margins (PixelHud.barImages) and the main menu's middle bar (PixelTitleScreen.bar) repaint when PixelHud.BarColorVersion changes, using PixelHud.BarTint(base) (the picked colour with the bar's own opacity; the menu bar stays 92% opaque). The docked buttons sit towards the screen edge, not centred in the bar (buttonEdgeBias, 0 = 0.55), to stay clear of the neon bar glow. The pause menu adds this component if missing.
--- PixelHusks.cs ---
PixelHusks.cs Small helper components that ride on HUSKS (the pixels that drop off the cube; code calls them old pixels), split out of PixelClicker.cs: ScaledGravity (gravity scale per body), OldPixelImpact (landing sound / pulse), OldPixelPopIn (pop-in animation), OldPixelFlight (fly-away marker), OldPixelDespawn (lifetime, swell-and-shrink despawn, Frozen / HoldAll for black holes and takeovers), OldPixelInfo (what tier a husk is, its amount, held-by-player tag). PixelClicker adds them in SpawnFallingCopy.
--- PixelInfinity.cs ---
PixelInfinity.cs The Prime Cubes, the Gauntlet and the Prime Cubelette (a homage; names are work in progress). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake. * Six stones (PixelType.StoneSpace..StoneSoul 85-90, PixelClicker.IsInfinityStone, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes) are rare drops with exactly the God Cubes' rules (IsGodCube is true for them: one of each, same spawn weight godCubeSpawnWeight, CanSpawn false once held, backpack stash) and each has its OWN find show (PixelGodFind.DrawStone: Space tesseract, Mind thought-bolts, Reality tearing shapes, Power detonation, Time's backwards clock, Soul wisps) and code-made sound (PixelGodSynth.Stone). Looks: PixelLooks.StoneLook, looksVersion 60. * When the player holds all six (and has no Cubelette and no Gauntlet) the WIZARD offers the Prime Gauntlet (PixelWizardMinigame.ModifyWares + aside line): DeviceKind.Gauntlet 17, a golden cube consumable (vendorOnly, hold 1; price = the Device's costs, editable in the Consumables Inspector). * Right-click the Gauntlet in the inventory (PixelConsumables.TryUseItem) -> this window: a golden cube laid out flat (six faces). Pick a stone from the tray, click a face to place it (click a placed one to take it back). With all six in place the Forge button makes the Prime Cubelette: the Gauntlet and the six stones are used up, the Cubelette tier (PixelType.InfinityCubelette 91, never rolled) is added and its show plays (PixelGodFind.DrawCubelette). Closing the window just puts the stones back (nothing is spent until the forge). The Cubelette is a trophy for now (it has no use yet).
--- PixelInput.cs ---
PixelInput.cs Static input helpers for both input systems (PointerPosition, LeftPressed, RightPressed, PointerOverUI); minigames/consumables use using static PixelInput;.
--- PixelInputCaret.cs ---
PixelInputCaret.cs A blinking text cursor drawn by us for an input field (the built-in one did not show in the dev tools' fields). Add it to a TMP_InputField's object: it hides the built-in caret and draws a white bar at the caret position while the field has focus; works for single-line and multi-line fields. Used by the Inspector's fields and the Dev Hub's Notes.
--- PixelKeys.cs ---
PixelKeys.cs Rebindable keys: PixelAction enum (TimeStop, Hose, RotateLeft, RotateRight, EventLog, TimeSlow, FirstPerson), defaults + the player's choices (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Key.*), PixelKeys.Pressed/Held(action) (both input systems; KeyCode <-> Key mapping), TryCapture (rebinding screen), Name, Replace(text) (fills {key:TimeStop} style placeholders in Controls.txt, tip boxes, device placing messages; also rewrites old literal "Q / E", "Press T to freeze time", "press B for the hose"). A key already used by another action swaps. Other scripts must call PixelKeys instead of checking fixed keys. While a text box has the keyboard (PixelKeys.Typing, once per frame) every Pressed / Held / KeyPressed / KeyHeld returns false, so typing in the recipe search or other input fields never triggers Time Stop, the hose, first person and so on. Escape (pause / close window) is not rebindable.
--- PixelKiln.cs ---
PixelKiln.cs The KILN (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Kiln'): a brick kiln with a glowing mouth on its +X side, a chimney that smokes and a chute at the back. ANYTHING that goes into the mouth from the side burns: an old pixel (not held, not glued into a build) inside the mouth zone (a box just in front of the door, mouthCenter / mouthHalf in kiln units) is taken out of the game (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no payout), drawn in while it shrinks and flares, and a CHARCOAL old pixel (PixelType.Charcoal 81, PixelClicker.EnsureCharcoalTier, standalone, despawns like any old pixel) is queued: charcoalPerPixel of them come out of the back chute charcoalDelaySeconds after the burn, one every charcoalGapSeconds. A conveyor belt (PixelConveyor) can lead into the mouth: a belt END at the mouth hands its pixels straight in (IPixelBeltAcceptor), and another can carry the charcoal away. The kiln roars while it burns (light, ember sparks, thicker smoke). Not saved.
--- PixelLogoView.cs ---
PixelLogoView.cs The title screen's 'PIXEL CLICKER' logo as a grid of little bevelled cells (one UI image per lit pixel of the 5x7 letters, plus a shadow image and a glow image each), so every cell can move, scale, spin and change colour every frame. PixelTitleScreen.BuildLogo adds this component to the 'Logo' object and calls Init. * STYLES: a table (Styles) of 20 looks, each a small static function (Cell + time -> offset / scale / rotation / colour / glow, 'S') plus an INTRO kind that plays every time the style is shown (Drop, Fly, Zoom, Wipe, Spiral, Stack, Flip): Classic, Rainbow Wave, Neon, Shatter, Matrix, Inferno, Frost, Glitch, Jelly, Flip, Blocks, Plasma, Synthwave, Gold, Ghost, Storm, Shockwave, Disco, Ocean, Hologram. To add one: write a function, add a line to Styles. A style may also scale a whole LETTER (S.lsx / lsy, about its base / centre). * FAVORITE: a small star under each arrow (fades in with it) makes the style showing now the favourite of the save (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.LogoFavorite = index + 1, in PixelSettingsSync, so it is per save); that style is shown instead of a random one, and ReloadFavorite switches to it when a save loads. Click the star again to clear it. * ARROWS: two invisible buttons at the far left / right of the logo fade in while the mouse is near them and step to the previous / next style (wraps); the style's name shows under the logo for a moment. A random style is picked at every launch (never the same as last time; PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.LogoStyle remembers the last one only for that). * PixelTitleScreen only positions / bobs / fades (SetAlpha) the container. Runs on unscaled time (the game is frozen while the title shows). Cost: ~200 cells x 3 images, updated each frame only while the title screen is up.
--- PixelLookSource.cs ---
PixelLookSource.cs / PixelNoise.cs IPixelLookSource = anything with looks to step through (Usable, Current, StyleName, PreviewTexture/Color/Rect, Step); the pause menu's AddLookRow and the title screen's BuildLookPicker only use this. PixelNoise = shared tileable noise (Hash, TileNoise, Fbm incl. separate u/v periods, Voronoi) used by floor textures.
--- PixelLumberjackVendor.cs ---
PixelLumberjackVendor.cs The Lumberjack (id lumberjack, 'Big Tim the Lumberjack'; a cube like Cubie in a red plaid shirt with a big beard, a green beanie and an axe): BUY / SELL TABS (PixelVisitorMinigame.BuySellTabs). BUY tab = his wares: the WOOD CHIPPER (a placed device, PixelConsumables DeviceKind.WoodChipper, costs wood and bark) and plain POTIONS of the Lumberjack pack's pixels (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig, Acorn, Whetstone, Pesticide). SELL tab = he BUYS those pack pixels for pure currency (RollTrades: offerCount offers, sellRate 0 = 0.8, luck, 'Sell' buttons; the Great Oak is not bought). Only visits while Woodcutting is the skill in training (CanVisit); started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors as soon as Woodcutting is available; counter lumberjack.bought.
--- PixelMagnetDevice.cs ---
PixelMagnetDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Magnet (DeviceKind.Magnet 10, auto-added like the fan by EnsureDefaultPotions, Utilities card; needs Electric unlocked, 20 Electric pixels, durationSeconds 30, radius 5, pullAcceleration 24, absorbDistance 0.9 = the 'settle' distance): like the Vacuum Device it pulls loose old pixels in radius towards a point in the gap of its horseshoe (BuildMagnetBody: red arch + legs, silver tips, base plate), but never collects them: pixels inside the settle distance just get their speed damped, so they sit in a heap (held / flying / vanishing / kinematic pixels are skipped, ageing is unchanged); placed like the vacuum (starts disarmed, click to switch on), robot-placeable (IsPlaceableKind), tip device_Magnet, stat device.placed.Magnet, not part of the Operation tab yet.
--- PixelMagnetSorterDevice.cs ---
PixelMagnetSorterDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Magnetized Sorter (DeviceKind.MagnetSorter 11, Device.craftOnly = never sold: PixelConsumables.ItemNotSold is checked by the shop cards / search / TryBuyItems and the visitors' wares; made in Crafting from a Sorter + Magnet: ItemKind.Device ingredients (Ingredient.device, Item.device, listed among the materials only for device kinds some recipe uses, gold-rimmed cube icon), default recipe added by EnsureDefaultRecipes; settings arcDegrees 110, radius (arc length), boxCapacity 60, shootInterval, pipeReach, sorterExitSpeed, durationSeconds 180). Placed like the fan (turn with scroll / Q / E, starts disarmed). A grey box with a red horseshoe magnet on a short tube at the front (+Z): loose old pixels inside the floor arc in front (BuildMagnetSorterBody, translucent fan named 'Range') are pulled to the magnet mouth and, within absorbDistance, swallowed (ReleaseOldPixel(body,false), fly-in) and stored per type with their payout (Stock: count + total amount; shot pixels carry the average). Three exit pipes (cubic-Bezier PixelTube from ports on the box's back to a nozzle on the floor, Rebuild): hover one (screen distance to its points), left-click grabs it, it follows the mouse on the floor plane (clamped to pipeReach, never inside the box), left-click puts it down, right-click puts it back; the mouse wheel over a pipe cycles which stored type it carries (floating model + 'xN'); every shootInterval a pipe with stock fires one pixel of its type from the nozzle (PixelClicker.SpawnStreamedPixel = SpawnStoredPixel with the Sorter's no-collision OldPixelStream); shot pixels are ignored by the magnet for 6 s. PixelPointerClaim (hovering / grabbing a pipe) blocks cube clicks via ExternalClickBlock. Saved in PlacedState.extra (ExportState/ImportState). NOTE: it must not define its own Update / LateUpdate (they would hide the base's); per-frame work runs in OnTick.
--- PixelMenuFx.cs ---
PixelMenuFx.cs The FANCY PAUSE MENU (Settings > Interface > 'Fancy pause menu', PixelMenuFx.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FancyMenu, default ON, saved per profile). It is a layer on top of the classic menu: nothing of the classic menu's code or layout was changed, so switching the setting off (live, no restart) goes back to exactly the old menu - that is the backup. What it adds (all components check Enabled every frame and hide / reset themselves when it is off): * PixelFancyButton on every main-menu button (PixelPauseMenu.LayoutMainButtons -> PixelMenuFx.Decorate): hover grows + brightens the button and slides its label, a coloured accent stripe on the left widens, a light shine sweeps across (on hover and every few seconds, offset per button), press squishes it, a small pixel icon (Floppy / Cog / Door) sits on the left, and every time the menu opens the buttons slide in one after the other (entranceStagger). * PixelMenuBackdrop (PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI -> BuildBackdrop): soft pixel motes drifting up behind the panel + a darkening vignette over the dimmer. * PixelMenuPanelGlow: a rounded glow behind the main panel that slowly cycles colour and breathes; PixelMenuTitleLine: a light segment sliding along a line under the title. * The SETTINGS and STATS screens get the lighter button animation (PixelMenuFx.DecorateAll: brighten, shine, press; no scaling inside scroll lists). * The docked HUD buttons also get the light animation WITH the idle shine (DecorateHud), and the SHOP gets the same treatment (its buttons, a glow behind the panel and the sliding title line; PixelShop.BuildPanel / CreateButton). * PixelQuitFx: the Quit to... box darkens the screen and pours flickering red neon light round the window (PixelPauseMenu.BuildQuitPanel). Everything runs on unscaled time (the game is paused while the menu shows).
--- PixelMenuKeys.cs ---
PixelMenuKeys.cs Blank-by-default keyboard shortcuts for the docked menu buttons: PixelAction.Inventory/Shop/LogWindow/Crafting/Toggles/Bank/Skills (default KeyCode.None, shown as '-', Backspace / Delete in the binding screen clears a key; PixelKeys.Pressed/Held return false for None and Set never swaps None). Each window calls PixelMenuKeys.Register(action, button) after making its dock button; the component clicks that button when its key is pressed (only if the button is active and the game isn't paused / on the title screen / typing). A new docked menu should register too.
--- PixelMeteorMinigame.cs ---
PixelMeteorMinigame.cs Meteor minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "meteor", Minigames tab): a large glowing meteor (runtime-built: lumpy noise-displaced rock mesh lumpiness, lava-crack albedo/emission textures lavaCracks, a camera-facing glow sprite haloOpacity, a two-layer tail incl. a white-hot hotCoreTail, a particle-system ember stream embersPerSecond, flickering light) drifts slowly across the screen; each click gives chunksPerClick meteor chunks but never destroys it. Tracker "Meteor Chunks" (saved generically); the Meteor Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for meteor. Extends PixelMinigame (id meteor). It speeds up gradually across the screen (disableSpeedUp / speedUpAmount, same total time), a rumble loop (meteor_rumble) plays while it crosses, every click plays meteor_hit and throws mining effects (sparks, rock chips, dust: BuildImpactFx / EmitImpact, disableImpactFx, impactChips / impactSparks) and the glow flashes; a chunk plays meteor_click. The sounds are code-made (PixelMeteorSynth) and replaceable in the PixelAudio Sounds list.
--- PixelMeteorSynth.cs ---
PixelMeteorSynth.cs Code-made sounds of the Meteor minigame (PixelAudio.SynthFor -> PixelMeteorSynth.For; put your own clips in the Sounds list to replace them): meteor_spawn (a fiery whoosh with a deep boom as it enters), meteor_rumble (a seamless low rumble loop while it crosses, PixelAudio.StartLoop / StopLoop), meteor_hit (a dull pick-on-rock thud with gravel, every click) and meteor_click (a bright pick clink with chips, when the click gives a chunk).
--- PixelMinigame.cs ---
PixelMinigame.cs NeedsNormalCamera (default = Takeover): true while the minigame needs the camera at its normal pose (the orbit camera glides home, the zoom snaps back); Weak Spots overrides it to false. Abstract base for minigames (registry PixelMinigame.All/Find(id), Id, Running, Activate/Deactivate, optional goal tracker: HasTracker, TrackerTitle/Description/Count/Goal, RequirementFormat, GoalReached). Skill XP bonus: every Report(MinigameEvent.Clicked) (each success: ghost caught, meteor click, star collected, bomb defused, Ultra Hole drop, Snake / Sorting / Breakout win, Dash round) calls GrantSkillXp(), which gives bonus XP to the skill in training (none while Standard is trained) via PixelSkills.AddXp (so the XP popup shows too); defaults in DefaultSkillXp by id (meteor 1, black hole 1 per swallowed pixel, pad 6, stardust 8, ghost 12, dash 15, bomb 20, snake / sort / breakout 25, others 5); per minigame skillXpReward (0 = default) and disableSkillXp in the Inspector. A minigame can name its own skill with SkillXpTarget (meteor = Mining, evil tree = Woodcutting): its XP then always goes there, whatever is in training (nothing if that skill is not bought). Bought minigames get an on/off row in the Toggles window (PixelMinigame.UserDisabled, saved per minigame; the shop never auto-starts a disabled one). Shop packs refer to minigames by id (unlocksMinigame; goals via a PackRequirement of kind MinigameGoal); the shop shows tracker rows and the save system stores counts generically - a new minigame needs no changes to PixelShop or PixelSaveGame. For the dev tools' Despawn button a minigame must call Track(go) on everything it puts in the scene (roots, messages, effects) and override OnDespawned() to reset its busy flag / spawn timer (DespawnNow() stops its coroutines and destroys the tracked objects). TakeoverActive, AutoClickerPaused and the floor area claims are cleared on every scene unload (Settings > Restart).
--- PixelMinigameLimits.cs ---
PixelMinigameLimits.cs Limits how many minigames have something on screen together (minAtOnce 2 / maxAtOnce 3, limitAtOnce, quietSecondsAfterPlay 30 (nothing spawns that soon after Play; PixelTitleScreen.PlayTime), retrySeconds; added by PixelClicker.Awake). Each minigame overrides PixelMinigame.Busy and asks PixelMinigameLimits.AllowAnother(this) before its timer spawns something (by default only ONE minigame / visitor may be busy at a time; the allowOverlap tick box brings back the min..max numbers, a number between them is rolled each time); SpawnNow (dev tools) ignores the limit. A new minigame must do the same. Minigames that take over the floor's old pixels (Snake, Sorting Race) override PixelMinigame.Takeover and set PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive; AllowAnother refuses every minigame (takeover or not, and the Stardust stars) while a takeover round is Busy, and Pixel Grabbing / cube clicks are off meanwhile. The Ultra Pad and Black Hole never spawn on top of each other: PixelMinigame.ClaimArea / AreaFree keep a list of the round floor areas in use, and each one's TryPickSpot rejects spots that overlap the other's. BREATHERS: when the last busy minigame / visit finishes, Update starts a breather (breatherMin 40 .. Max 90 s at random, x takeoverBreatherFactor 1.5 after a takeover round) during which AllowAnother says no, so minigames bought together no longer follow one another straight away; RetrySeconds is randomised so waiting minigames do not all poll on the same frame (disableBreathers switches it off).
--- PixelMold.cs ---
PixelMold.cs Foraging's Mold block (PixelType.Mold 75, a hazard-style block that replaced the Toadstool in the Foraging pack; the Toadstool still exists but is switched off for now) and the glowing Foraging finds. Component added by PixelClicker.Awake (PixelMold.Instance, Inspector fields for every number). MOLD (Foraging hazard, 1 left click sets it off, 1 right click clears it): with wholeMap (on) setting it off molds EVERY husk on the field at once; zoneHoldSeconds 0 = 30 and killSeconds 0 = 22 (longer than before). Old notes: every left click on the Mold block on the cube calls PixelMold.Spread (PixelClicker.CollectInternal / TriggerHazard) = the mold zone around the cube grows by spreadWidths old-pixel widths (a faint green disc on the floor, up to maxWidths). Right click (DefuseHazard) / the block leaving only STOPS the spreading - the zone stays and then fades by itself after zoneHoldSeconds + zoneFadeSeconds. Every infectInterval s the zone infects the old pixels lying inside it (PixelMoldy: a green shell that darkens; the pixel dies after killSeconds - the timer waits while it is held). SHAKE a held moldy pixel (PixelMoldy.FixedUpdate adds up the change of velocity of the dragged body) to shake the mold off: shakeNeeded cleans it and gives cleanImmuneSeconds (OldPixelInfo.moldImmuneUntil) so it is not re-infected at once, and gives cleanXp (3) Foraging XP (GiveCleanXp). The shell is a pale blue-green fuzz that turns whiter (moldRipeColor) as the pixel nears death - never dark, so moldy husks don't look like Truffles. Not saved: a loaded game has no zone / mold. GLOW: PixelMold.TryAttachGlow (called from PixelClicker.SpawnFallingCopy) gives a fresh Berry / Mushroom / Herb / Truffle old pixel a glowGlowChance chance to be a glowing variant; it only starts to glow (PixelForageGlow, shell fades in and pulses) once OldPixelDespawn.AgeFraction reaches glowStartFraction (0.5 = halfway through its lifespan). The glow is a look only for now (no extra pay). Also not saved.
--- PixelMyWindow.cs ---
PixelMyWindow.cs (the window is called POCKET now; the class and the saved keys keep the old name) POCKET'S PINNED TAB IS A BOARD OF WIDGETS (like the Dev Hub's): each pinned thing is a card (BuildCard) with a header you DRAG by (PixelPocketGrip: the cards slide round each other, the order is saved in the entries), a '-' that folds it up to its header (Entry.min, saved), and a small x you HOLD with the left button (0.6 s, a ring round the cursor, the card turns red: UpdateRemoveHold) to remove it. Under the last card a BLANK card with a + opens a search (OpenPicker / FillPicker: every currency, skill, goal, tool, item, achievement and a note, filtered as you type); picking one pins it and a new blank card follows. With no card left only the blank one shows. There is no Edit mode any more. (Old text below that talks about Edit mode / rows describes the earlier list.) My Window (the player's own window; component added by PixelClicker.Awake): a docked 'My Window' button on the right edge under the Pixel Guide (shows once two pixel types are unlocked, showFromStart) opens ONE free window (canvas 107, not registered with PixelWindows, not closed by other windows) that is dragged by its header, resized by the corner handle (position / size / open state are global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.MyWindow.*) and holds a vertical LIST of rows chosen in Edit mode (an add bar: type + target dropdowns + Add; each row gets ^ v x buttons, currencies a '/min' toggle). Row types (Entry.type): currency (one pixel type, optional per-minute rate sampled over 60 s), skill (a chosen skill, 'training' = follows the skill in training, 'all' = one row per available skill), goal ('next', 'all' = every row of the Log's Goals tab via PixelLog.CurrentGoals(), or mg:<id> a minigame tracker), achievement (PixelAchievements id), note (an input field) and fav (a button: item:<name> uses a consumable through PixelConsumables.TryUseItem, tool:<id> = the Pixel hose (PixelBank.ToggleHose) or opens a window via PixelMenuKeys.Click). The rows are saved per save file (JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.MyWindow, listed in PixelSettingsSync.Known_, reloaded by PixelMyWindow.ReloadFromPrefs). Values refresh every refreshSeconds (0 = 0.4); rows are rebuilt when what an 'all' row expands to changes. Layout (current): opens as a tall window (defaultSize 0 = 450 x 660) at the right side just above the bottom black bar (layout version 2 ignores older saved geometry). It can only be dragged INSIDE the playfield between the black bars and the screen sides (ClampToScreen). MINIMIZE button in the header: only the header remains (still draggable); the full height is remembered. LOG button in the header (PixelMyWindow.LogDocked, kept on this machine): docks the EVENT LOG into the window as a tab (Pinned | Event log; the log lines of PixelHints.TryGetHistory, newest at the bottom, a click shows the full tip); while docked and the window is open the bottom-left log overlay stays away and the event log key opens the tab. INFO button in the header (PixelMyWindow.HoverDocked, kept on this machine): pins the block HOVER TOOLTIP into the window: while it is on and the window is open (Pinned tab, not minimized) the tooltip card no longer follows the mouse - PixelBlockTooltip moves its card into the window's hover slot (TryGetHoverSlot / SetHoverHeight), scaled to the window's width, and it shows whatever the mouse hovers (and stays on the last one while the mouse is over the window); a hint shows until something was hovered. TABS (always shown under the header): Pinned | Event log (only while the log is docked) | Keybinds (PixelCustomKeys.BuildView: bind items / tools to your own keys). The header buttons are laid out side by side from the right (LayoutHeaderButtons) so they never overlap. DOCKING: let go of a drag with the window against the left or right screen edge (edgeSnap px) and it docks there at that height (dockSide -1 / +1, dockY): only a sliver (dockSliver) stays on screen and it slides out while the mouse is near / over it or while editing (UpdateDock); an UNDOCK arrow button appears in the header while docked (it pulls the window back into the screen); a pale strip at the edge shows while a drag is close enough to dock. Saved globally: PixelClicker.MyWindow.Min / Dock / DockY next to X / Y / W / H.
--- PixelNextUp.cs ---
PixelNextUp.cs The window has a header (title + '-' button) and is a PixelWindowChrome window: drag it by the header, minimize it to the header, dock it against the left / right screen side (slides out on hover); remembered in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.NextUp.Window.*. The 'Next up' window (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; shown while the Block Hold upgrade is owned and Settings > Interface > 'Next-up window' is on, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.NextUp, default on): a slim vertical window at the left edge with THREE spinning cubes - the block coming NEXT on top, the block on the cube NOW in the middle (bigger) and the block BEFORE it at the bottom (dim). When the block changes the cubes slide down one slot (the new next slides in from above, the old 'before' slides out below); any other change (a Block Hold swap) pops the changed cubes in and out. Data: PixelClicker.PeekNextTier() (a block put back by Block Hold that waits in line, else the pre-rolled 'upcoming' tier: PixelClicker.RollTier hands out that same tier, so the preview is true; it is re-rolled if a potion / skill / switch makes it invalid; nothing is pre-rolled while this window is hidden, so normal play rolls exactly as before), CurrentClickTierIndex and PreviousTierIndex. The cubes are the blocks' REAL models (PixelTierPicture: a hidden studio camera per block rendering into a RenderTexture, shown by a RawImage), not flat coloured cubes.
--- PixelNuclearPixel.cs ---
PixelNuclearPixel.cs Nuclear pixel (PixelType.Nuclear 33, IsStandalone (no seeds, no 3-type combo potions), spawn switch in Toggles; testing pack 'Nuclear Pixel': 10 White, no requirements, spawn weight 0.3, pays 8; looksVersion 27): glowing toxic-green look with PixelLook.radiation (PixelRadiationFx.Attach: drifting green motes). Its OLD pixel gets OldPixelRadiation (added in SpawnFallingCopy; nuclearRadiusPixels 0 = 4 main-pixel widths, nuclearAgeRate 0 = 40 age-seconds per real second, disableNuclearFx): every 0.15 s it calls OldPixelDespawn.Hasten(seconds) on every other old pixel in range (not other Nuclear pixels; held pixels, black holes, takeover minigames and the dev no-despawn switch still protect; gravity-well keep-alive does NOT), so they vanish (no payout) within a second or two; a pulsing green ring (PixelFxRing) and light show the reach.
--- PixelObsidianFx.cs ---
PixelObsidianFx.cs Obsidian flair: natural black glass (dark neutral bevel rim, no emission / violet; looksVersion 31) with PixelLook.obsidianFx = a few pale glints (Attach, from AddExtras), Hit (shards + glints, stronger with progress) and Break (glass spray + glints) called from CollectInternal (disableFarmFx), plus FractureFx (jagged white crack lines racing outward in the camera plane, flickering and fading; more / longer with progress; replaces the old rings) and shine. Obsidian's hit / break no longer use the white FractureFx lines: they throw black / grey dust that poofs sideways (PixelObsidianFx.Dust: a flat ring of swelling, fading puffs plus a few drifting up) along with the glass shards. The Obsidian sheen (PixelLook.shine) follows the jagged mesh (ShineMesh(size, jagged, seed) displaces its grid with PixelRockMesh.Displace) so it never sticks out past the chipped edges. Obsidian clicks play PixelSynth.ObsidianPing (while pixel_obsidian has no clips) with a pitch that rises per hit (PixelAudio.OnHit 0.6 -> 1.45, break 1.6).
--- PixelOfflineProgress.cs ---
PixelOfflineProgress.cs Offline progress: PixelSaveGame stores savedAtTicks (UTC) on every save and, on the startup load only, calls Grant(lastSaved). Rare drops (Dragon Cubes) are not averaged but rolled with a Poisson count of pixels x weight / total weight (RollPoisson). If the auto clicker is running it pays the average earnings for the (capped, scaled) time away - clicks = time x efficiency / interval x clicks-per-tick, split across unlocked tiers by spawn weight, tough pixels needing several clicks - and shows a "Welcome back" window (held back until the title screen is gone, Update + pendingGains; any later load - another slot - Dismiss()es it). Settings: max hours, efficiency, minimum away time.
--- PixelOperation.cs ---
PixelOperation.cs / PixelShopOperation.cs Upgrades > Operation (the third sub-tab of the shop's Upgrades tab, operationSubTabText; upgradesSubTab 2): one row per consumable (Vacuum Device, Fan, Sorter, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Combo Fuel, Ghost Bait, Pet Treat - listed once the pixel type it needs is unlocked or you hold one, PixelConsumables.OperationListed) with a rotating 3D picture on the left (a hidden studio per consumable far below the scene OperationStudioHeight, a camera rendering into a RenderTexture shown by a RawImage, re-rendered each frame only for visible rows, UpdateOperationPictures; the model comes from PixelConsumables.CreateDisplayModel = the real device builders with displayOnly (no timer / range) or simple item models), an orange '!' box with the consumable's description and an orange upgrade arrow that opens the shared upgrades window (OpenOperationWindow, openOperation) listing that consumable's upgrades as plain rows (name + level, cost coloured by affordability, Upgrade button) whose orange '!' tooltip shows the statistics (OperationStatText: now / next level / at max, from PixelConsumables.OperationBase). Upgrades are PixelOperationUpgrade entries (PixelOperation.cs: id, name, description, device, stat (PixelOperationStat), maxLevel, perLevel, baseCost x costGrowth^level paid in the pixel the consumable itself needs, unit) in PixelShop.operationUpgrades (the built-ins are added by id, EnsureDefaultOperationUpgrades); the level is the stat counter op.<id> (PixelStats.Best, saved generically). Effects: PixelConsumables.EffectiveDevice(d) (a clone with Duration / Radius / Pull / Blow / Sorter max force / Uses / Charge needed / Super charge / Strike interval applied, used for placing, previewing and descriptions), Storage in ItemCapacity, Pet Treat length (PixelPets.TryUseTreat) and extra Ghost Bait ghosts (PixelGhostMinigame.UseBait).
--- PixelOrbitCamera.cs ---
PixelOrbitCamera.cs BASE VIEW: at start (Start -> ApplyBaseView, useBaseView) the scene camera is moved to baseDistance 0 = 6 units from the cube at baseTilt 0 = 89 degrees, baseTurn 180 (the game's original orientation, +Z at the top of the screen): that pose is the game's normal view and the 1.0 of the zoom slider (Enter takes it as the home pose, so the camera intro settles into it, PixelViewBounds reads it through HomeCamera and takeover minigames glide back to it). The View window keeps the camera in Top down (this camera, pitch locked) whenever nothing else uses it. Orbital camera (added by PixelClicker.Awake; chosen in the View window (PixelViewWindow, key PixelAction.ViewWindow) as 'Orbital', or 'Top down' = Enter(topDown: true): the same camera with the pitch locked at 89 degrees; disableOrbit): the REAL camera swings round the cube (Enter captures the home pose; yaw / pitch / distance round CubeBasePosition, rotation = look-at-cube x aimOffset so the cube keeps its on-screen spot): middle-mouse drag (PixelInput.MiddleHeld, dragDegreesPerPixel 0 = 0.25) or arrow keys (keyDegreesPerSecond 0 = 90) turn / tilt (pitch min minPitch 0 = 6 so it never looks from under the floor), Ctrl + wheel and the View window's zoom slider (DistanceFraction, MinFraction 0.35 .. MaxFraction 2) zoom, the window's Auto orbit tick box (AutoOrbit, AutoSpeed = autoOrbitDegreesPerSecond 0 = 10) turns the camera slowly round the cube by itself, and its hold buttons (HoldTurn / HoldTilt) add to the arrow keys; dragging / arrow keys still work, followSharpness smoothing. Its controls are shown as a hover tooltip in the View window (no hint line on screen). Clicks need no change (raycasts go through TargetCamera). While PixelOrbitCamera.Active, PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera (a hidden disabled copy at the home pose) is what PixelViewBounds and PixelFloor.FitToView read, so old pixels stay inside the NORMAL view and fitted floors don't re-fit. Leaving glides back over returnSeconds (0 = 0.7); the title screen, the camera intro and takeover / zooming minigames (any Busy + Takeover one) end it or block it.
--- PixelPathCarrier.cs ---
PixelPathCarrier.cs Shared helper for machines that CARRY old pixels along a path (users: the Funnel and the Conveyor Elevator; the belts have their own rider system). Add(rb) locks a loose old pixel (kinematic, no velocity); Step(...) each FixedUpdate draws new ones to the path's start (approachSeconds), then moves every pixel along the polyline at 'speed' keeping 'gap' behind the one ahead, keeps them from despawning, lets go of ones that were grabbed or destroyed, and calls onEnd(rb) when one reaches the end (optional canExit gate: while it returns false the pixel waits at the end and the ones behind queue up) (the caller then puts it back into the world with ReleaseAt). ReleaseAll() lets everything go (the machine was removed).
--- PixelPauseMenu.cs ---
PixelPauseMenu.cs Pause menu (main menu = mainColumns-column button grid LayoutMainButtons, mainPanelWidth wide; fitBetweenBars keeps it and the text screens between the black bars via AvailableHeight/FitViewHeight) (Esc; the on-screen Pause button is off by default - showPauseButtonOnScreen): sets Time.timeScale = 0, Resume (alone) / Save-Load + Stats / Settings + Game Guide (Controls, How To Play, Database tabs) / Give feedback (alone) / Quit (alone; opens a 'Quit to...' box with Desktop and Main menu - Main menu saves, then PixelTitleScreen.ReturnToMenu()) (MenuEntry.fullRow) (Restart now lives on the Stats screen, Report Folder moved to the Dev Tools panel; the Stats screen shows one total "Potions used" row instead of the potions drop-down; a search box over the Stats list filters rows by name / value and shows a section when its heading or any row matches, StatRowsShown) (Settings: tick boxes for cube rotation, pulsing, "Abbreviate numbers" and "Hide purchased shop items" (static PixelShop.HidePurchased, PlayerPrefs; HiddenAsPurchased in the shop list keeps a bought pack listed while it still has upgrades to buy) -> PixelClicker.AllowRotation/AllowPulsing and static PixelClicker.AbbreviateNumbers (read by FormatNumber, PixelUI, PixelLog and the stats), stored in PlayerPrefs), PixelPauseMenu.IsPaused. PixelClicker ignores clicks while Time.timeScale <= 0.
--- PixelPestSwarm.cs ---
PixelPestSwarm.cs (Pest rework) The Pest block (PixelLook.blockSurface Pest, PixelBlockTextures.PestHide(level,max): black carapace plates, hazard stripes round every face, red eyes that multiply and glow with hits; jagged mesh + twitching wobble, bugs crawl over it via farmFx Bugs; looksVersion 43). Every non-final hit spills a few bugs (PixelPestSwarm.Scatter); when it GOES OFF PixelPestSwarm.Release sends a swarm of code-built bugs (body, head, red eyes, legs, feelers; max 80 at once) over the floor to every edible planted sprout (SeedSprout.GetEdible/IsEdible), they climb it and gnaw (Gnaw shakes it, more bugs = faster, pestChewSeconds 4), then SeedSprout.Devour() kills it (pixel lost) and the bugs run off; with no sprouts they just scatter. Tuning on PixelClicker: pestBugsPerSprout (0 = 6), pestBugsMin (8), pestBugSpeed (3.5), pestChewSeconds (4), disablePestSwarm (old instant KillAll). Dirt now takes 3 digs (packDataVersion 15): each hit plays seed_dig + SeedDigFx and shrinks the live cube towards old-pixel size (digShrinkTarget, reset in Materialize). Seedlings from a cracked Seed shell grow seedlingGrowSeconds (0 = 40 s; the sprout's despawn timer is extended to match). Migration 15 also adds any missing farm block (Seed, Water...) to a saved Farmer's Pack, and PixelSkills.EnsureDefaults makes sure Farming's types list has every farm block (a missing Seed was why it never spawned); migrations 13 and 14 now run in order.
--- PixelPetsStudio.cs ---
PixelPetsStudio.cs Part of partial class PixelPets: the Outfit Studio (button 'Design outfits' in the Customize Pet window, own canvas customizeSortingOrder + 20, PixelWindows 170). The player builds custom outfits from basic shapes (CPiece: cube / sphere / cylinder / capsule / cone, position, size, turn, colour (16 StudioColors), opacity, optional mirror = a second copy on the other side) on a dummy: a plain grey cube or the real-look model of ANY pet they own (studioDummy; some pets look different), in a hidden studio (StudioStageHeight) shown by a RawImage (drag to turn, wheel to zoom; a yellow marker shows the selected shape's centre). Outfits (COutfit: name, forType = one pet type or -1 any) are edited live (every change saved) with a piece list, nine sliders, swatches, duplicate / delete, New / Delete outfit, and 'Wear this outfit'. Outfit numbers 10000 + n are custom outfits (CustomBase; BuildOutfit handles them, so live pets, title-screen pets and previews all use them; deleting one renumbers / clears pets wearing it); the outfit arrows cycle OutfitChoices(type) = built-ins then the custom ones that fit that pet. Saved as JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.PetOutfits (per save file via PixelSettingsSync, reloaded by PixelPets.ReloadOutfitsFromPrefs).
--- PixelPixelGuide.cs ---
PixelPixelGuide.cs / PixelGuideTexts.cs Pixel Guide (added by PixelClicker.Awake): a docked 'Pixel Guide' button on the right edge directly under Toggles (PixelHud.Dock with a negative sideOffsetY; shows once two pixel types are unlocked, hideUntilSecondPixel) that slides out a window like Toggles (same spot, docks beside the open shop with the Currency panel's width and the shop's height via FitBesideShop; opening one closes the other: PixelToggles.CloseWindowIfOpen / PixelPixelGuide.CloseWindowIfOpen; Escape closes it, PixelWindows 15). It lists every pixel IN THE ROTATION (PixelClicker.InRotation(i) = can spawn now and not left out by the skill pack in play; found Dragon Cubes too; SpawnShare(i) = its share of spawns) as a row: a turning real-look 3D picture (a hidden studio per tier, CreateDisplayPixel, a camera rendering into a RenderTexture, re-rendered only for rows in view), the name in its colour, a line 'Pays N - clicks - X% of pixels' and an orange '!' box whose tooltip is PixelGuideTexts.Describe(type, tier) (the exact mechanics + what its old pixel does). Special / hold blocks (PixelBlockRules) are tagged in the sub line and get their role sentences from PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes automatically. A new pixel type needs a case in PixelGuideTexts.Mechanics (and OldPixel if its old pixel behaves differently).
--- PixelPlacedDevice.cs ---
PixelPlacedDevice.cs SetRemoveGlow(0..1): while the right mouse button is held on the device (PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval + PixelHold ring) it tints red, glows and lights up red as the ring fills. Abstract base of placed devices: countdown, timer text facing the camera, shrink-away (InitCommon, OnTick, IsDying). Armed / disarmed: every device you place starts DISARMED (Disarm() right after SpawnDevice; grey via per-renderer property blocks - ApplyTint / ClearTint, a desaturated dull version of each material's colour, emission off; the countdown does not run, OnTick is not called, the timer text shows DisarmedText 'Click to start'; subclasses must check Armed in FixedUpdate / event handlers: fan, vacuum, sorter TryRoute, booster OnCollected / AddCharge, PixelElectricLinks ignores disarmed devices) until you LEFT-CLICK it once (PixelConsumables.UpdateArming: DeviceUnderPointer(true) using the virtual HitTest(ray, out distance) - the sorter overrides it with its ring + pipe so clicking the cube inside doesn't arm it; while the mouse is over a disarmed device PixelConsumables.HoveringDisarmed makes PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock true) -> Arm() fades the colour in over ArmFadeSeconds (armFadeSeconds 0 = 0.7) and starts the countdown (sound device_arm). Saved as PlacedState.disarmed.
--- PixelPointerClaim.cs ---
PixelPointerClaim.cs One shared "this click belongs to something in the world, not the cube" flag. A device / visitor that has the pointer over a part it reacts to calls PixelPointerClaim.Claim() every frame it has it (sorter handles, Magnetized Sorter pipes, Quantum Organizer, Storage Crate, Recycler button, carry handles, Bait Bucket, the Egg, the Beanstalk's fruit, a Treasure Map spot); PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock reads Claimed (this frame or the last, so script order does not matter). Replaces the separate HandleHot / Hot flag each of them used to have (and the matching list in ExternalClickBlock): a new device only calls Claim(). PixelRecycler.ButtonHot and PixelCarryHandles.Hot stay as their own flags because Pixel Grabbing steps aside for exactly those.
--- PixelPop.cs ---
PixelPop.cs Open / close animation for windows and tip boxes (component on the Inventory box, the Log panel, the Got-it box, the Shop panel, the Crafting window, the Toggles window and every pause-menu screen: PixelPauseMenu.SetView pops the screen in, resuming shrinks the open one): it grows from smallScale to full size on enable (growSeconds, small overshoot, unscaled time, scales around the rect's pivot) and PixelPop.Hide(go[, deactivate]) shrinks it (shrinkSeconds) before deactivating; PixelPop.Show(go) also cancels a shrink under way; PixelPop.IsOpen(go) = shown and not closing. Open / close such windows through Show / Hide / IsOpen, not SetActive / activeSelf. Fit to screen: a fixed-size window (not stretched in x / y) that is bigger than the canvas between the black bars is scaled down by PixelPop.ComputeFit (UI scale 130% makes the canvas only ~830 units tall, so the 880-tall shop would be cut off).
--- PixelPowerClick.cs ---
PixelPowerClick.cs POWER CLICK (TEST - may be removed or parked). Unlocked by the Standard skill tree node 'power' (requireTreeNode); branches 'quick' (charge 40% faster) -> 'aftershock' (cooldown a third shorter). Hold the LEFT mouse button on the cube: the press is a normal click as always, and if you keep holding for holdDelay (0.25 s) a charge builds (chargeSeconds 1.2): a ring fills round the cube, the cube trembles harder and harder, and at full charge the ring turns gold and pulses. Let go at full charge and it fires: one click worth clicksWorth (25) clicks (PixelClicker.PowerClick - payout x25, tough blocks take 25 hits, the combo grows by 25), a white flash, a shockwave ring, a kick of the cube and a big 'POWER CLICK x25' popup with a boom. Letting go early just cancels (no cost). Then a cooldown (cooldownSeconds 45) - a thin grey ring round the cube while you hover it shows what's left. Normal fast clicking never charges (each press is shorter than holdDelay). Not on: rare drops, hazards, Fish (Weak Spots) and the Golden Snitch; never while a husk is grabbed, a block is held (right mouse), a takeover minigame runs, in first person, in a menu or while time is stopped. Switch: disablePowerClick. Stats: power.clicks. Component in PixelSystems (group Core); its overlay canvas sits under the windows.
--- PixelPrefs.cs ---
PixelPrefs.cs The one door to PlayerPrefs: every script calls PixelPrefs.GetInt / SetInt / GetFloat / SetFloat / GetString / SetString / GetBool / SetBool / HasKey / DeleteKey / Save instead of PlayerPrefs directly (same behaviour, nothing is saved to disk until Save, like before). Why: PlayerPrefs can't tell what TYPE a key holds, so reading a float key with GetInt quietly gives 0 - the per-save settings list once did exactly that and wiped the picked glow colour, the hues and the skill pack on every load. In the Editor / dev builds PixelPrefs remembers the type each key is written or declared with and logs a warning the first time a key is used as another type. Per-save settings: PixelSettingsSync copies the keys declared with PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave(key, kind) into each save file (its own built-in list declares itself the same way); a new per-save setting declares its key once, from its class's ResetStatics or Awake, next to the code that reads it, with the right kind - instead of being added by hand to a list in another file.
--- PixelProspectorVendor.cs ---
PixelProspectorVendor.cs The Prospector (id prospector, 'Pip the Prospector'; a visitor drawn as a CUBE like Cubie - orange earth tones, hard hat with a lamp, pickaxe, grin - the test for turning vendors into cube characters): he BUYS mining goods for pure currency (RollTrades: up to offerCount 3 different ores you hold >= minHeld, each asks minSellFraction..maxSellFraction of your stock and pays a random unlocked pure pixel (White..Blue) at the rarity-based rate (UnitValue) x sellRate (0 = 0.8) x luck; the base trade rows say 'Sell' via TradePayWord / TradeText). He only visits while Mining is the skill in training (CanVisit = PixelSkills.TrainingId == MiningId; a failed check just waits for the next visit timer) and is started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors as soon as Mining is available (first visit after about otherFirstVisitSeconds x 0.5-1). Counter prospector.bought. Mining's whole 'sink' for now is selling to him; the Sell tab / skill upgrades are on the to-do.
--- PixelRecycler.cs ---
PixelRecycler.cs RECYCLER (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Recycler 19, Utilities card - listed FIRST there - and Cubie's gift on visit 1 via the {recycler} page token): a permanent open-topped bin (PixelChipperDevice stub for removal / saving its spot; built here by Place / CreateGhost / CreateDisplay with PixelFactoryKit: plinth + feet, green walls with a dark liner, brushed-metal corner pillars and rim, a glowing band round the body and a status lamp - green idle, amber while working - recycling arrows on the front, the shared carry handles and a big red button). Drop old pixels (husks) into it - Pixel Grabbing, or anything that lands inside: a husk that comes to rest inside the bin's walls is CAPTURED (PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel without credit, so it leaves the floor's old-pixel list: no despawn count, no cap, no vacuum, no termites; its OldPixelDespawn is Held so it never shrinks away). The husks stay in the bin as real physical pixels until it is closed. Up to 'capacity' (10) can be inside; a counter above the bin shows 'n / 10'. Husks inside never despawn (their OldPixelDespawn.Held is re-set every tick, so poking one with Pixel Grabbing cannot start its timer; one taken out of the bin is let go again; with the bin full, extra husks lying in it get KeepAlive). Click the red BUTTON on its front: a slatted LID extends out of the inner back edge and STRETCHES over the opening (Lid Pivot z scale 0.02 -> 1, lidSeconds), the bin SHAKES harder and harder for processSeconds (2 s, shakeStrength) while the band pulses amber, then every husk inside is recycled into payoutShare (50%) of what a click on that block pays NOW (CurrentValue: base payout x the current Value / Ultra / wish multiplier, so Value upgrades bought after the husk dropped count) as currency of its own type; the shake plays recycler_shake (PixelSynth.RecyclerShake: motor wind-up, faster rattles, clunk) (pixel rules: the Recycler is the one machine that turns husks back into money, at a loss), the husks are destroyed and the lid pulls back. Nothing is captured while it works, and the shut lid (it has colliders) keeps husks out: ones dropped on it fall in once it opens. ButtonHot (the pointer is over a button) is part of PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock and PixelGrab's guard, so pressing it neither clicks the cube nor grabs a pixel. Captured husks that lie still for 0.3 s are frozen in place (kinematic + parented to the bin: Settle / Freeze), so the pile cannot jitter or bounce and the husks scale with the bin when the floor's props fade (main menu, after a load: while the bin is scaled everything inside is frozen into it and nothing is captured or let go). Grabbing one takes it OUT (TakeOut: unfrozen, unparented, back on the old-pixel list, so it is a normal husk again and is captured anew if dropped back in). Husks above or in the opening get KeepAlive every check, and PixelGrab calls KeepIfOver on letting go, so an old husk's timer cannot run out in the moment before the bin captures it (that used to make a dropped husk vanish uncounted). Loaded husks are put straight into the bin, frozen (ImportState). SAVED: the husks inside go into PlacedState.extra ('Type,amount;', ExportState / ImportState via PixelConsumables) and are spawned back into the bin on load. HANDLES: the Storage Crate's grip handles on both short ends (shared PixelCarryHandles: BuildParts in Build, the component added by Place): drag one to carry the bin over the floor (scroll / Q / E turn it); everything inside is frozen into the bin while it is carried; not while processing. BACK HATCH: a small door in the back wall (the wall is built in four pieces round the hole, metal frame, slatted door with a handle). In first person, click it with the crosshair (within hatchReachHusks) and it slides up into the hole's top edge the same way the lid stretches (Hatch Pivot y scale 1 = shut, 0.03 = open; aim at the hole to shut it again; the door and frame glow hatchHighlightColor while the crosshair is on it), so husks can be tossed in by hand. It shuts while the bin works. MOVING: the carry handles move the bin through a kinematic Rigidbody (PixelCarryHandles), so loose husks are pushed instead of glitching through; while carried every husk inside is frozen into the bin each frame and any that still ends up outside is let go and uncounted at once (KeepContentsWhileCarried). A husk that leaves the bin is handed back to the floor's husk list (AdoptOldPixel), never left orphaned. Pressing the button freezes every counted husk into the bin first (a still-settling one could be jostled out by the shake, let go and caught again after the cycle) and tips any UNCOUNTED husk lying in the bin out over the front / back rim (EjectUncounted), also again before paying. AUTO RECYCLING (autoRecycle, on by default since 2026-10-16 - the session reports showed nobody stopped clicking to fill the bin): every husk that is captured is recycled on its own recycleDelay (0.5 s) later - no button, no filling up (RecycleDue from Capture, RecycleOne). Each pays payoutShare of its value as BONUS currency (AddBonusCurrency: it does not count towards the next tier's unlock) AND skill XP (xpPerHusk 1 x the STREAK multiplier, PixelSkills.AddHuskXp: the husk's own skill, pure husks train Standard). The streak grows by streakStep (0.2) for each husk of the SAME type as the one before, up to streakMaxMultiplier (5; was 20 - a long all-White streak made the Recycler out-earn clicking), and ends after streakTimeout (6 s) without a recycled husk; a different type keeps streakKeepOnSwitch (half) of it; the counter above the bin shows 'Gray streak x1.4 XP', the band glows warmer and the click sound climbs with it. Stats recycler.husks / paid / xp, Best recycler.streak. The idea is that husks reach it WITHOUT the player stopping (bin placed where husks land; edge-click aiming is planned). The button / capacity / lid cycle below is the old manual mode (autoRecycle off) and still works in auto mode for anything left inside. A FULL bin makes its button flash and pulse (UpdateButtonFlash, flashColor). Removing the Recycler destroys what is inside. Not a belt acceptor yet.
--- PixelRemoveTint.cs ---
PixelRemoveTint.cs The "being cancelled" look: while the player holds the right button to remove something, it turns RED (colour towards red, a red glow in its emission, a red point light) as the hold ring fills, exactly like a placed device (PixelPlacedDevice.SetRemoveGlow). PixelRemoveTint.Set(gameObject, 0..1) does it for ANY object (added to it on demand; 0 puts everything back and removes itself): used by PixelFeatureTester.UpdateDelete (conveyor belts, kilns, splitters, elevators, solar panels, the tester's chipper). Placed devices keep their own SetRemoveGlow (it also dulls them when disarmed). The running potion line (PixelUI.UpdateActiveHud) uses Shake() / RedText() from here: the text reddens and trembles very slightly as the hold fills.
--- PixelRightTap.cs ---
PixelRightTap.cs A quick right CLICK on a target, told apart from a right HOLD on it (holding is used to delete tester objects, PixelHold). One instance per user: call Update(whatIsUnderThePointer) every frame the right button could be involved (when RightPressed, RightHeld or Pending); it returns true on the frame the button is let go on the same target after at most maxSeconds. Used by the belt flip (PixelConveyorSystem) and the Solar Panel's re-link (PixelSolarPanel), which used to fire on the press.
--- PixelRockMesh.cs ---
PixelRockMesh.cs Jagged rocky pixels: PixelLook.jagged (Obsidian, Meteor, Rock, the six ores; looksVersion 36 replaced their saved looks and removed the Obsidian bevel rim - rocky pixels have no outline) draws a chipped cube mesh (PixelRockMesh.Get(baseCube, type): 8x8 quads per face, flat shaded, only edge / corner vertices pulled inwards by a position hash so shared vertices match; faces stay flat, never bigger than the cube, same per-face UVs). PixelClicker.MeshFor(look) / BaseMesh() pick the mesh: the live cube swaps its MeshFilter mesh in ApplyTierLook, old pixel copies, display pixels and their extras / glow shells use MeshFor of their own tier (never srcFilter.sharedMesh, which may be another type's jagged mesh). The hit box is still a cube.
--- PixelSessionRecorder.cs ---
PixelSessionRecorder.cs Session recorder (dev / playtest tool, never shown to players): records how a real play session goes, so balance numbers can be set from the player's actual pace instead of guesses. Component in PixelSystems (group Core). - Dev Hub widget 'Session recorder' (PixelDevTools): Start, then Stop & save -> a text report in <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots + clipboard (PixelDevProbe.Save), meant to be pasted to Claude. - Tester builds (no Dev Tools): tick recordEveryPlay on the component. Recording starts by itself when the title screen goes away and the report is written into <persistentDataPath>/<testerFolder> (default 'Feedback', the same folder as the Give feedback notes, so a tester sends one folder) every autosaveMinutes and when the game quits. What it records: only time in the game counts (not the pause menu / title screen). Clicks per second (manual and auto, per minute and the best 10 s), blocks broken, income per pixel type per minute, Standard and training-skill XP per minute, a TIMELINE of milestones found by polling once a second (pixel types unlocked, shop packs bought / levelled, skill level-ups, Cubie's visits, the shop window first opened), Block Hold and stakes (hold.count, stake.placed, stake.won / lost), husk counts (blocks broken = husks dropped; despawned unused 'old.despawned', removed by the floor cap 'old.capped', grabbed, vacuumed, recycled, shredded, banked - PixelStats counters), and the state at the start and end.
--- PixelShaders.cs ---
PixelShaders.cs PixelShaders.SpriteDefault(): the only place that looks up the Sprites/Default shader (unlit, alpha-blended, vertex colours - trails, glow shells, face marks, arrows, stars, hole trails). Shader.Find only finds shaders that are in the build, and a URP project often leaves Sprites/Default out, so it falls back to the shader of Resources/PixelShaderKeep/Sprites_Default (made by the Editor keep-alive script). Use it instead of Shader.Find("Sprites/Default").
--- PixelShopConsumables.cs ---
PixelShopConsumables.cs Third-and-a-half part of partial class PixelShop: the Consumables tab = two purchase cards (Potions, Utilities/devices) side by side, each with an item drop-down (locked items shown in grey text), a count box with -/+ and a Buy button; TryBuyItems(item, count). Hides the scrolling list while that tab is open. Craft-only combo potions are never listed. Settings are in PixelShop.cs.
--- PixelShopNudge.cs ---
PixelShopNudge.cs (+ PixelGuideVendor.ShopHold) Shop hold after Cubie's second visit (added by PixelClicker.Awake): if the shop tutorial is still due (PixelShopTutorial.DoneKey, tips on) the visit does NOT unfreeze time when he leaves: PixelGuideVendor.ShopHold keeps Time.timeScale 0 (UpdateHold re-freezes after a pause-menu resume) until the shop window opens (PixelShop.WindowOpen), then time resumes and PixelShopTutorial.RequestStart() starts the tutorial without its start delay (leaves the hold on the title screen / tips off). While held the Shop dock button stays slid out (ShopHold in its keep-out lambda), the tier guide line (PixelUI.UpdateGuide, forced to shopGuideText) pulses gold and the Shop button (PixelShop.ShopButtonRect) gets the shared pulsing gold frame (PixelHighlight.Set, glowColor); disableNudge.
--- PixelShopPictures.cs ---
PixelShopPictures.cs Part of partial class PixelShop: rotating 3D pictures on shop rows/cards (AddRowPicture, AddCardPicture, AddPackPicture; pixel packs cycle through their reward tiers every pictureCycleSeconds, Value rows, Consumables cards follow the drop-down, minigame packs use primitive models BuildMinigameModel for ghost/meteor/blackhole/bomb/pad/snake/sort/breakout - a new minigame needs a case + its id in ModelledMinigames). One hidden studio per distinct picture key (tier:<Type>, item:<name>, game:<id>; y -3900), drawn only while on screen (UpdatePictures); hidePictures, pictureResolution. Shop cost lines no longer print '(you have n)' (NoHave strips it from old saved format strings).
--- PixelShopTutorial.cs ---
PixelShopTutorial.cs Shop tutorial (added by PixelShop). ONLY the shop's blue '?' hold button plays it now (autoStart off: when the shop becomes available it is just marked done, DoneKey, so the tutorials waiting for it carry on; Cubie's shop hold no longer starts it). With autoStart on (old behaviour): the first time the Shop button is available (PixelShop.ShopAvailable) and the screen is calm (title screen over, PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide false, no other tip, no takeover minigame, not paused; startDelay 2 s) a series of small "Got it" boxes (PixelNotice.Show(small: true, beside: shop.PanelRect), 440 wide, just right of the shop window) walks through the shop, each step opening its tab via PixelShop.TutorialOpen(tab, subTab): the currency list + overview, Pixels, Upgrades > Features, Upgrades > Value, Operation, Consumables, Minigames, closing hint.
