Before the first box the screen fades darker and the game pauses (BeginRun: a click-blocking black overlay on canvas 600, below the tip boxes, dimAmount 0 = 0.4 / dimSeconds 0 = 0.7; time is restored and the overlay fades out at the end) and PixelShopTutorial.Active makes the Shop button (and its key) ignore clicks until the tutorial is over. Steps are an Inspector list (steps, empty = the built-in ones, enableTutorial); Cubie narrates it (PixelGuideVendor.BeginNarration/EndNarration: he slides in at the bottom right at 0.85 scale, stands still and points at the shop window with a stick (pointer: arm + stick + gold tip turned towards the shop's on-screen centre every frame, ShopPointTarget; his left arm hides meanwhile) and taps it on each new step (NarratorReact), no speech box, and leaves when the tutorial ends); every box has one height (UniformHeight, from the longest step, min boxHeight) so Got it never moves; the shop window has a blue '?' hold button left of its X (the pause menu's PixelHoldButton with a fill bar, helpHoldSeconds 0.7 on PixelShop) that calls Replay(); remembered as hint id shop_tutorial (PixelHints.IsTipSeen/SetTipSeen; Settings > Replay tutorial clears it, so it replays after the intro if the shop is unlocked).
--- PixelShuffleBonus.cs ---
PixelShuffleBonus.cs SHUFFLE BONUS (a test; component added by PixelClicker.Awake; needs Block Hold, it builds on its quick tap): TAP the right button on the FILLER block of the skill in play (Driftwood in Fishing, Twig in Woodcutting... = PixelSkills.TryGetFiller) and Block Hold sends it behind the next block as usual (a tap = released within tapSeconds, 0.35). The first shuffle starts a combo: a 'SHUFFLE x1' box with a bar that runs down (barSeconds, 6) shows under the top bar. The combo only GROWS when the block it was shuffled behind has been broken (waiting = true until a PixelCollected arrives); when the filler comes back, shuffle it again before the bar runs out for x2, x3... (no cap yet). BREAKING the filler instead cashes the combo in: bonus XP = xpPerShuffle (3) x the combo, on top of the normal XP (PixelSkills.AddShuffleXp). If the bar runs out first the filler breaks by itself (dropped from the queue, or auto-collected if it is on the cube) and a smaller consolation bonus (expireFactor 0.35 of the cash-out) is paid. The combo block on the cube (the shuffled filler that came back from the queue, not a freshly rolled one) lights up gold: PixelClickerAura.ComboHighlight forces the cube's glow aura on in gold and makes it pulse. A skill switch (other filler) or the title screen ends the combo. Fields are Inspector values on this component (0 = the coded default); disableShuffleBonus switches the whole thing off. Not saved.
--- PixelSkillSwitchFx.cs ---
PixelSkillSwitchFx.cs The spin-and-flash of the 'skill switch' test block (PixelClicker.ShowSkillSwitchBlock, called by PixelSkills.SetActive when another skill is chosen): added to the cube's pixel for about a second - an extra fast spin (spinDegreesPerSecond, easing out) and a white point-light flash (flashIntensity, fading) so the swap reads as an event. Unscaled time; it removes itself (and its light) when done, and adding it again restarts it. The scale pop comes from the normal materialize.
--- PixelSkillsBonus.cs ---
PixelSkillsBonus.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the bonus XP bank. Per skill, stat counter skill.<id>.bonus (starts 0, floored at 0). PixelSkills.OldPixelConsumed(tierIndex) is called when an old pixel is CONSUMED (PixelClicker.Vacuum and AbsorbOldPixel = Vacuum pixel / Vacuum Device, the black hole swallowing, the Pet Vacuum; pixel rules: they also give husk XP, see PixelSkills.AddHuskXp) and works on the pack in play (none for Standard): a GOOD pixel (IsGood: Mining = ores, Farming = plants, Electrician = conductors; goodTypeOverrides 'packId:Type,Type') adds goodBonusXp (0 = 3) x a rarity factor (1..4, from 1/sqrt(spawn weight)); every other pixel (junk AND neutral) takes badBonusXp (0 = 1) off. In AddXp, DrawBonus DOUBLES each gain while the bank holds anything - the extra comes out of the bank (after the hazard penalty). Shown as a gold 'Bonus N' after the XP on the skill tracker; disableBonusXp. Not on the Skills window yet; other consumers (magnet sorter, quantum organizer, bank, UFO) don't count.
--- PixelSkillsMastery.cs ---
PixelSkillsMastery.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: Mastery, one bar for the progress of ALL skills together, along the bottom of the Skills window (BuildMasteryBar, RefreshMastery, gold fill, 'Mastery 12.5% - x / y XP', tooltip). Every skill has a level cap (SkillPack.levelCap, 0 = defaultLevelCap on the component, 0 = 30; the caps only measure mastery for now - a skill keeps earning XP past its cap) worth CapXp(pack) = the XP from level 1 to the cap (baseXp x xpGrowth^n summed); MasteryProgress = sum over EVERY pack except Standard (Mining, Farming, Electrician, Offline Progression and any pack added later; skills not unlocked yet count as 0) of min(skill XP, its cap XP) / sum of all cap XP, so it only reaches 100% when every skill is at its cap. An event-log line at every 10% (counter mastery.step, saved) and 'Mastery complete!' at 100%. Not shown on the pinned tracker yet.
--- PixelSkillsMinigames.cs ---
PixelSkillsMinigames.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the minigame XP multiplier. AddXp multiplies every gain by MinigameXpMultiplier = 1 + EnabledMinigames x xpPerMinigame (0 = 0.1, i.e. +10% each), capped by maxMinigameXpBonus (0 = no cap), disableMinigameXpBonus. EnabledMinigames counts PixelMinigames that are Running and not UserDisabled (Toggles > Minigames); visitor vendors don't count. Recomputed once a second. Shown in the Skills window header (right side, gold 'Minigames: x1.3 XP (3 on)').
--- PixelSkillsPopups.cs ---
PixelSkillsPopups.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: '+n <Skill> XP' popups while a skill trains (AddXp calls QueueXpPopup; XP is gathered xpPopupGatherSeconds (0 = 0.6 s) and shown as whole numbers, fractions of auto clicks carry over; UpdateXpPopups runs from the window's Update; XP from the auto clicker is gathered separately and appears beside the cube instead (xpFromAuto set by OnCollected, FlushAutoXpPopup, CubeScreenPoint, rises beside the cube and follows it); text in the pack's accent colour, hangs just under the mouse cursor and follows it (drifting a little further down as it fades), on its own canvas 106 so it never mixes with the other +n texts; hideXpPopups, xpPopupSize).
--- PixelSkillsStandard.cs ---
PixelSkillsStandard.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: Standard is a real skill. Every collected block gives it a FLAT standardXpPerBlock (1) XP - manual or auto, any block, whatever skill / Offline is trained, no multipliers (AddXpFlat); popups only when Standard is the one trained and the Skills tab exists; level-up log lines only once AnyTrainable). Its perks apply in EVERY rotation: Lingering pixels (StandardLifetimeBonus = (level-1) x lifetimePerLevel 5 s (old pixels last oldPixelLifetime 15 s by default, so Standard level 2 = 20 s; the field was renamed from fallingCopyLifetime so the old saved 6 s is dropped), added in PixelClicker.FallingCopyLifetime, which all old-pixel despawn timers read) and Roomy floor (StandardCapacityBonus = (level-3) x capacityPerLevel 5 from level 4, added to PixelClicker.OldPixelCap), Steady grip (level 3) and Firm grip (level 5): Block Hold's meter starts at half length and each adds 10% of that (PixelBlockHold.GripFactor reads the Standard level); 'Sharper clicks' (level 7) is still 'coming soon'. Standard shows level / XP bars in the Skills window and the tracker like any skill, has no bonus bank, filler or hazard and does not count towards Mastery. SKILL TREE (2026-10-16, DefaultNodes(StandardId), PixelSkills.HasStandardNode): the first point (level 2) goes on 'Block Hold' (hold; Block Hold is no longer available from the start - PixelBlockHold.requireTreeNode) or 'Power Click' (power, PixelPowerClick.requireTreeNode); branches hold -> lucky (+10% stake win) -> highroller (cost 2, stakes grow 50% faster) and power -> quick (charge 40% faster) -> aftershock (cost 2, cooldown a third shorter). The level 2 popup now points the player to the skill point and the tree. Minigame bonus XP still skips Standard. Standard's XP popups and level-up log lines show from the very FIRST click (no longer only once a skill pack exists). Its level 2 perk carries the intro popup (StandardLevel2Notice, the perk's noticeText: 'you have been training a skill all along...'), shown once through the perk notices. The level 2 popup opens the Skills window on Standard, sits small to its right (PixelNotice beside the window, time frozen until Got it) and frames the Standard card with the shared pulsing gold frame (PixelHighlight) until Got it (Perk.noticeInWindow, any perk can use it; perkWindowVersion 1 switched it on). The Skills window has a '?' button next to the X that plays Cubie's skills intro again (StartTutorial).
--- PixelSkillsTracker.cs ---
PixelSkillsTracker.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the pinned skill tracker. A pin button at the top left of the Skills window (BuildPinButton, green while pinned) closes the window and shows a small draggable window (own canvas sortingOrder - 1 (below the Skills window and its tooltip), dragged by PixelPopoutDrag, which now has a locked flag) with the training skill's name, level and an XP bar (RefreshTracker: TrainingId, LevelOf). Hovering its XP bar shows the XP box (PixelSkills.XpTip: 'Skill: lv/lv', Current XP, Next level, Remainder), like the Skills window's header bar and each card's bar. Left / right arrows either side of the skill name (trackerPrev/Next, CycleTrainedSkill) switch the skill in training through Standard and every available pack. Its buttons: a padlock stick (trackerStuck: can't be dragged), downsize ('-' / '+': a one-line small version) and X (closes it; the Skills menu is NOT reopened). DOUBLE CLICKING the name (small or full; PixelDoubleClick) opens the Skills window on the skill in training with its tree beside it (never the expanded tree). Open / small / stuck / position are PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Skills.Tracker* (global, not per save); hidden on the title screen and until the Skills button exists (SkillsTabUnlocked: Standard level 2), so Standard alone can be pinned.
--- PixelSkillsTutorial.cs ---
PixelSkillsTutorial.cs / PixelTutorialDim.cs Skills tutorial (part of partial class PixelSkills, started from the window's Update by UpdateTutorialStart the first time a skill pack is available and the screen is calm: not while the shop tutorial is still due, no tip / visitor / pause / takeover; enableTutorial, tutorialStartDelay 0 = 2 s, tutorialSteps empty = the built-ins): same format as the shop tutorial - PixelTutorialDim.Begin fades the screen darker and pauses the game (a click-blocking overlay on canvas 600; PixelTutorialDim.Active is true meanwhile and the Skills tab / Shop button ignore clicks), Cubie narrates (PixelGuideVendor.BeginNarration) and his stick points at the part each step describes (PixelGuideVendor.PointOverride, TutorialFocus Window / Header / Cards / Train / Tree / Mastery / Pin), each step is a small Got it box beside the Skills window (PixelNotice beside: windowRect); the Train / Tree steps look at the first real pack with a tree and the player's own selection comes back at the end. Remembered per save as stat guide.skillsTutorial (TutorialDoneKey, cleared by Settings > Replay tutorial). It replaced the old one-box introText / skills_intro tip. PixelTutorialDim is shared with the shop tutorial (dimAmount / dimSeconds still live on PixelShopTutorial).
--- PixelSkillsWindow.cs ---
Skill tree window (PixelSkillsWindow.cs, PixelScrollFadeX.cs) The tree's nodes sit in a SIDEWAYS scroll view (nodeView + nodeScroll, wheel / drag; RefreshTree widens nodeArea when the tree is wider than the view) whose left / right edges fade out while there is more to see (PixelScrollFadeX: a CanvasGroup on every node and connector line, alpha by distance to the edge). A button at the tree window's TOP LEFT (treeExpandButton, arrow glyph) makes the tree cover the Skills window (treeExpanded: UpdateTreeLayout starts the tree at the window's left edge); pressing again, closing the tree or the window gives the window back.
--- PixelSnakeMinigame.cs ---
PixelSnakeMinigame.cs Snake minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "snake", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; every minInterval..maxInterval s when at least minPixels old pixels lie around and AllowAnother): snapshots the old pixels on the floor (not flying / vanishing / held; up to maxPixels), hides the cube (SetCubeShrink), shrinks away every old pixel that is not part of the round (they grow back afterwards) and freezes the chosen ones (isKinematic, gravity-well / float behaviours off, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll so nothing despawns; the area is cut to the part of the floor that is in view and any pixel outside it or in the air is moved onto the floor inside it) and spawns a code-built head (green cube with eyes) that glides at speed pixel-widths/s on the floor plane (camera-relative arrow directions, no 180° turns, the area's edges just stop it); arrow keys (both input systems) or the on-screen arrow buttons steer. Touching a pixel (eatDistance) appends it to the body, laid along the head's trail (PlaceBody, segmentSpacing); biting the body past safeSegments or running out of timeLimit ends the round (pixels drop and age again); eating all pays rewardMultiplier (10) x each eaten pixel's OldPixelInfo.amount via PixelClicker.AddCurrency and shrinks them away. While PixelSnakeMinigame.Active cube clicks (ExternalClickBlock) and Pixel Grabbing are off. Sounds snake_start/_eat/_win/_lose, tip minigame_snake. Extends PixelMinigame (id snake; dev Spawn/Despawn work, Despawn ends the round cleanly).
--- PixelSnap.cs ---
PixelSnap.cs Placing SNAPS BY DEFAULT (Active = Shift NOT held); hold SHIFT for FREE placement (consumables: PixelConsumables placing; Dev Hub tester machines and belts: PixelFeatureTester; first person carried blocks). Snapping puts the spot to a GRID (gridCubes old-pixel widths, the same spacing as the first person neon floor) and the turn to ANGLE steps (angleDegrees, default 15 so 45 and 90 are hit exactly). The numbers are set on PixelConsumables (Inspector: "Shift snapping") and pushed here; until then the defaults apply. First person: PixelFirstPerson.SnapPoseToGrid snaps a carried block's resting spot (on the ground, not when it sticks to another cube) to cells of the same grid and its turn to the angle steps. Point() only snaps x / z (the height stays); Yaw() rounds a turn. Callers keep the un-snapped turn themselves so Q / E can still turn smoothly while Shift is held.
--- PixelSolarPanel.cs ---
PixelSolarPanel.cs The SOLAR PANEL (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Solar Panel'): a flat panel of blue cells on the ground with an indicator post. It is POWERED while Solar old pixels (PixelType.Solar) lie on it (its pad, a little bigger than the panel). It links to ONE placed device (PixelPlacedDevice: vacuum device, fan, sorter...): right after placing, the tester shows a ring of linkRadiusCubes (0 = 8) cube widths round the panel and you click a device inside it to connect; a right click on a placed panel (not while placing) starts the linking again. A straight BLUE LINE (with a bead running along it while powered) joins the panel to the device; while the panel is powered it calls the device's HoldPower() every frame, so the device's countdown stops. SOLAR USE: a Solar pixel on the pad that is NOT part of a complete group (5+ touching, the clicker's UpdateSolarClusters keeps those alive: OldPixelDespawn.IsKept) is slowly USED UP - one every consumeSeconds (0 = 8) - while a group of 5 on the pad is never used and powers the panel for good. The indicator is blue (powered), blue-white (powered by a complete group, lasts) or dim red (no solar). Saved with the Tester objects (PixelFeatureTester Export / Import: position, turn, link position).
--- PixelSortMinigame.cs ---
PixelSortMinigame.cs Sorting Race minigame (id sort, pack unlocksMinigame = "sort", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; takeover minigame like Snake): every minInterval..maxInterval s, with at least minPixels old pixels of 2+ types lying around, up to maxPixels of them (types taken in turn so groups are similar in size) are frozen (kinematic, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll), shuffled and slide into a grid centred on the screen's floor (shrunk to fit the view); the old pixels that are not in the race shrink away and come back afterwards (shrinkOthers, bystanderSeconds); the cube shrinks out of the way during the race (cubeShrinkScale, PixelClicker.SetCubeShrink) and the grid never takes more than maxScreenFraction of the screen; a timer (baseSeconds + secondsPerPixel * n) starts once they are in place; click one pixel then another (screen-distance pick, clickRadiusPixels) to swap them (swapSeconds); sorted = every type forms exactly ONE unbroken run in reading order (Runs() == distinct types) -> each pixel pays amount * (rewardMultiplier + speedBonusMultiplier * timeLeftFraction) via PixelClicker.AddCurrency and shrinks away; timeout = they drop and age again. Sounds sort_start/_ready/_pick/_swap/_win/_lose, tip minigame_sort.
--- PixelSplitter.cs ---
PixelSplitter.cs SPLITTER BLOCK (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Splitter'): a low square block that SNAPS to the end of a conveyor belt (PixelSplitter.TrySnap: bring the ghost near a belt end and it latches on, turned the belt's way, its entry face meeting the belt's end, at the belt's height) and shares what the belt brings out of the OTHER THREE sides (front +Z, right +X, left -X). A new belt snaps to any of the three output points (PixelConveyor.Snap offers SplitterOutputs). It is an IPixelBeltAcceptor: a pixel handed in is sent to the next output in turn (round robin), but only among the sides that have a belt starting there (output points within ~1 cube of a belt start); if no side has a belt, all three are used and the pixels are flung out. Each side has its own PixelPathCarrier (entry -> centre -> out), so a side that is full (maxQueuedPerSide) is skipped and, if all are full, the belt keeps (ejects) the pixel. Raised splitters get posts to the floor. Saved with the tester save.
--- PixelSprinklerDevice.cs ---
PixelSprinklerDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Sprinkler (DeviceKind.Sprinkler 9, auto-added like the fan by EnsureDefaultPotions, Utilities card; needs Water unlocked, 40 Water pixels, durationSeconds 45, radius 4.5, Device.sprayInterval 1): BuildSprinklerBody (base, post, spinning 'Spinner' head with 'Nozzle 0/1'), droplet jets (PixelFx.Make per nozzle) and every sprayInterval s PixelClicker.WaterSplashAt(point, reachOverride) - the same instant growth jump a splashing Water pixel gives every sprout / planted Seed pet in reach (each target still accepts water only every seedWaterCooldown); placed like the vacuum (starts disarmed, click to switch on), robot-placeable (IsPlaceableKind), tip device_Sprinkler, not part of the Operation tab yet.
--- PixelStardustMinigame.cs ---
PixelStardustMinigame.cs Stardust minigame (id stardust, passive: Running = the Meteor pixel is unlocked, no pack of its own; added by PixelShop like the others): every meteor pixel that shoots off (PixelClicker.FlyAwayLaunched static event) has starChance of knocking a star down; after fallDelaySeconds a runtime-built five-pointed star (vertex-coloured mesh, Sprites/Default, trail, point light) falls beside the cube onto the old-pixel floor (rigidbody, gravityScale), waits starLifetime s (blinks then shrinks away) and is collected by clicking within clickRadiusPixels of it (+stardustPerStar stardust, rising text). Tracker "Stardust" (saved generically, goal stardustGoal); the Solar Pixel pack (PixelType.Solar, Pixels tab, needs the Meteor Pixel pack + the stardust MinigameGoal) is gated by it. Tip minigame_stardust, sounds stardust_fall/_collect (code-synthesized while their clip lists are empty: PixelSynth.StarFall a falling glassy whistle with tinkling sparkles, StarChime a rising three-note chime; + the automatic stardust_spawn/_click). Magic dust (a world-space PixelFx particle system, gold / lavender sparkles, ~60 per second while the star falls or moves, none once it lies still) trails the star, and a white twinkle (a four-point sprite that pulses every ~2.4 s) shows while it is still (Star.dust / Star.twinkle). Has a Toggles-window switch and dev Spawn/Despawn like every minigame.
--- PixelStatics.cs ---
PixelStatics.cs One registry for "clear this static game state when the scene is reloaded". Statics outlive Settings > Restart (it reloads the scene), so game state kept in a static (a status line, a held penalty, a takeover flag...) carried into the new game - the old 'Oak 3 / 9' tree text came back after a Restart that way. A class with static GAME state registers its reset once, from its [RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod] ResetStatics: PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart(OnRestart); and puts the clearing in that method. Registering the same method twice is ignored. Keep DEV switches (Dev Tools tick boxes) out of the restart reset; they are meant to survive it. References to the new scene's objects are re-found by the new scene's Awake, so clear only state, not something the new scene may already have set.
--- PixelStats.cs ---
PixelStats.cs Play stats. Typed fields (manual/auto clicks, play time, pixels spent, ghosts/meteors clicked, meteors/black holes spawned, fans/vacuum devices/sorters placed, singularities fed, potions used per name, highest combo) plus generic counters: any script calls static PixelStats.Count("key", n) / Best("key", v) (keeps the max) / Fastest("key", v) (keeps the min); they are saved in ExtraData.counterKeys/counterValues and need no other wiring. Counters in use: shop.purchases/items, snake/sort/breakout.win/loss, snake.length, sort.fastest, bomb.defused/exploded, ultra.<minigame id> (PixelMinigame.UltraRoll + the Pad), ghost.kept, combo.tier, old.vacuumed/banked/grabbed/shattered, crafts, holes.fed, sessions, session.longest, rate.best (best pixels per minute, sampled each second), timestop/timeslow.seconds/uses. GetLines(number) builds the Stats screen's rows (headings General / Pixels / Clicks per pixel / Husks (+ Recycler cycles and payout) / Block Hold (holds, stakes won / lost - counted when shown - best stake) / Skills (level + hazards set off / cleared per skill: hazards.triggered.<id>, hazards.defused.<id>) / Combo and time / Crafting and items / Devices placed (every DeviceKind) / Rare finds (Dragon x/7, God x/3, Prime x/6) / Pets / Minigames (Ultra sources from PixelMinigame.All); per-pixel-type earned rows; a minigame's rows only once it runs or has data; Ultra earned, stardust, bomb parts and achievements are read from their owners); the pause menu's Stats screen just renders that list (RebuildStatRows, rebuilt only when the row list changes). To add a stat: record it with PixelStats.Count/Best/Fastest and add a row in GetLines. Saved by PixelSaveGame (ExtraData).
--- PixelStatusBox.cs ---
PixelStatusBox.cs The shared top-of-screen status box: potion timer, device placing line and the takeover minigames' status lines (Blink, Breakout, Dash, Evade, Snake, Sorting, Traffic, Weak Spots, Ultra Holes). PixelStatusBox.Create(font, canvasTransform, fontSize) returns the TMP label (use it as before: PixelUIKit.SetText, .color); the label sits in a rounded window-style box that SLIDES OUT FROM UNDER THE BLACK BAR (a RectMask2D strip starts at the bar's inner edge, so the box is clipped until it has slid below it and is drawn OVER the bar's edge effect). It slides back under the bar when the text is empty or Show(label, false). Create(..., stackBelow: true) hangs the box under the other boxes (PixelStatusLines uses it). Show(label, bool) / IsShown(label). Box size follows the text (boxPadding, maxWidth). A thin accent line along the bottom takes the label's colour. Fixed look values live in the consts at the top (not Inspector fields: the box has no component of its own in the scene).
--- PixelStatusLines.cs ---
PixelStatusLines.cs Keyed text lines for the top status box (PixelStatusBox, hanging under the potion / minigame status boxes): PixelStatusLines.Set(key, text, colour, seconds) shows or replaces the line for that key (seconds > 0 = it goes away by itself, 0 = stays until Clear(key)); Clear(key) removes it. Several lines stack in one box (first line's colour = the accent). The lines are cleared when a scene unloads (Restart reloads the scene; old lines must not come back). Used for the Black Hole counter, the Bomb timer / result, the Ghost / Meteor / Evil Tree messages that used to float in the world.
--- PixelStorageCrate.cs ---
PixelStorageCrate.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Storage Crate (DeviceKind.StorageCrate 13, auto-added like the fan, Utilities card; 300 Gray, hold 3, bodyDiameter 2.5 = width, bodyHeight 0.3; model PixelConsumables.BuildCrateBody: a wooden tray with two rows of five compartments): permanent and never disarmed (no countdown: duration 1e9, timer text hidden; PlaceDevice / RestorePlacedDevice skip Disarm). Drag old pixels over it with Pixel Grabbing and left-click the crate: loose old pixels lying within its footprint (FindLoose, nearest first) are stored in the next free section (row 1 left to right, then row 2; ReleaseOldPixel + FlyIn, their payout is kept, a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model sits in the compartment). Left-click a stored pixel (PickSection, screen distance) to take it out: SpawnStoredPixel at the compartment and PixelGrab.BeginHold puts it in the mouse's hand. A pixel you hold and let go of above the crate is stored at once (any pixel tagged 'recently held by the player' - OldPixelInfo.RecentlyHeld, PixelGrab.recentlyHeldSeconds - that ends up in the footprint is stored too, from ANY height: CaptureRecentlyHeld) (TryStoreReleased: PixelGrab.HeldBody was a pixel last frame and is not now, and it is inside the footprint and below ~3 units above it). Two grip handles on the short (+/-X) ends (the shared PixelCarryHandles: parts by PixelCarryHandles.BuildParts from BuildCrateBody, behaviour by the component): hovering one shows the open-hand cursor (PixelHandCursor: code-drawn open hand / fist textures set with Cursor.SetCursor, requested per frame, normal cursor returns when nobody asks), left-drag moves the crate over the floor plane (DragTo, kept on screen) with the fist cursor, and while dragging scroll / Q / E turn it (PixelConsumables.TurnInput; also when placing it; the yaw is saved in PlacedState). PixelStorageCrate.ClaimsPointer() makes PixelGrab step aside when the click belongs to the crate; PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks. Removing it (Ended) lets everything out. Saved in PlacedState.extra ('S,slot,tier,amount;'). Tip device_StorageCrate.
--- PixelSystems.cs ---
PixelSystems.cs ONE list of the game's system components (Awake used to add them one line at a time). Each entry = a component type + the group it belongs to (UI, Core, Skills, Rares and Shows, Characters, Camera, World), in the order they must start. - At run time PixelClicker.Awake calls AddMissing: a system that is NOT in the scene yet is added to the clicker object (as before), and in the Editor a warning names them, so they can be put in the scene for editing. - In the Editor, the menu 'Pixel Clicker > Add Missing Systems To Scene' (Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs) puts every missing system on a tidy 'Pixel Systems' object with one child per group, so their Inspector values can be edited and SAVED in edit mode (a component only added in Play mode loses every change when Play stops). 'Pixel Clicker > Move Systems Off The Clicker' moves the ones sitting on the clicker object into those groups, values included. A new system goes in this list (with its group); every system script must sit in a file of the same name and find PixelClicker with PixelFind (not only GetComponent), so it works on any object.
--- PixelTalk.cs ---
PixelTalk.cs Talking noises for dialogue boxes: while a box types its text out (PixelNotice tip / tutorial boxes, Cubie's speech panel) PixelTalk.Typed plays a short code-made blip (sound id talk_blip, six voiced squeaks from PixelSynth.TalkBlip) for every second letter, its pitch following the letter (vowels a little higher) so it mumbles like speech without being words. Both boxes have a small speaker button next to their back button (PixelTalk.AddMuteButton) that mutes / unmutes the blips (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TalkMuted, kept per save profile by PixelSettingsSync). The speaker icon is a pixel-art sprite drawn from text rows (SpeakerSprite).
--- PixelTermiteSwarm.cs ---
PixelTermiteSwarm.cs The Termite Hive's termites (Woodcutting's hazard, PixelType.HornetNest keeps its old enum name so saves stay valid). Every non-final hit on the hive spills a few termites that run off (Scatter); when it GOES OFF (PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) Release sends one termite for EVERY old pixel lying about (up to PixelClicker.TermiteMax, 0 = 60): they spill out of the cube, scuttle over the floor to their pixel and start eating it - a small bar over the pixel counts down (PixelClicker.TermiteEatSeconds, 0 = 5). CLICK the old pixel (or grab it) before the bar runs out and the termite gives up and runs off; if the bar empties the pixel is eaten: removed with no payout. A pixel that vanishes some other way (despawn, vacuum, collected) just frees its termite. Cosmetic code-built termites (body, head, legs, feelers; at most 80 at once), nothing is saved. Tuning on PixelClicker: termiteSpeed (0 = 3.8 cube widths / s), termiteEatSeconds, termiteMax, disableTermites (the hive then just costs the usual XP penalty).
--- PixelTierPicture.cs ---
PixelTierPicture.cs Shared 'real model' pictures of pixel types: PixelTierPicture.Get(clicker, tier) returns a RenderTexture of the block drawn with its REAL look (PixelClicker.CreateDisplayPixel in a hidden studio far below the scene with its own camera and light), PixelTierPicture.Render(tier, yaw) turns it and re-renders it (call it only for pictures that are on screen). First user: the Next-up window (PixelNextUp). The Pixel Guide, the shop pictures, the Log skin studio and the shop operation studio each still have their own copy of this studio code: move them onto this helper when they are next touched (one place, one set of heights).
--- PixelTimeStop.cs ---
PixelTimeStop.cs Time Stop (shop pack with unlocksTimeStop, Upgrades tab): press T to set Time.timeScale = 0 (everything incl. countdowns) and again to resume. Has an energy meter (maxStopSeconds, refillSeconds, minStartFraction, hideDelaySeconds): drains (unscaled time) only while stopped, auto-resumes at 0, refills immediately after resuming, shown at the top only while stopped / refilling / briefly after full. Static PixelTimeStop.IsStopped: PixelClicker still takes cube clicks while it is true, animates the cube with AnimDelta (unscaled), and uses timeStopStockpileMax instead of maxFallingCopies, so spawned old pixels hang at the cube (no physics step) and burst out on resume (payouts are immediate). Switch in the Toggles window (UserDisabled, saved). A screen haze (radial tint + vignette, showHaze, hazeColor/hazeEdgeColor, fades in/out, canvas below other UI) shows while stopped. Sounds time_stop/time_resume/time_denied plus the looping hum time_stop_loop (loopSoundId; started/stopped via PixelAudio.StartLoop/StopLoop). PixelShop finds/adds it. Time Slow (shop pack unlocksTimeSlow, Upgrades tab, needs Time Stop; ActivateSlow/DeactivateSlow): press S (PixelAction.TimeSlow) to set Time.timeScale = slowTimeScale (0.15; Time.fixedDeltaTime scaled too) using the same energy meter at slowDrainFactor of the stop drain, lighter haze (slowHazeStrength), sounds time_slow/_end/_loop, tips timeslow / timestop_slow. Static PixelTimeStop.IsSlowed / SlowFactor / EndAll() (called before returning to the main menu); PixelClicker.AnimDelta is unscaled while slowed so the cube still feels normal.
--- PixelTinyRobot.cs ---
PixelTinyRobot.cs TINY ROBOT (a Dev Hub > Tester feature for now, PixelFeatureTester 'Tiny Robot'; meant to become an Electrician-unlocked consumable): pick a SPOT ON A CONVEYOR BELT (the ghost snaps to the belt under the mouse, PixelTinyRobot.TrySnap; a click that is not on a belt does nothing) and a little hover robot appears over it. For robotSeconds (default 60) it keeps fetching the NEAREST loose old pixel to that spot (within reachCubes, default 18): it flies over, grabs it (the pixel is made kinematic and hangs under the robot), flies back and puts it down on the belt at the spot (it waits a moment while another pixel is still in the way), where the belt's normal capture takes over and carries it off. Pixels that are held by the player, flying, attached, already riding a belt / carried by a machine or standing on any belt are left alone; a pixel the player grabs while the robot has it is simply let go. When its time is up it drops what it carries and flies away. The spot follows the belt (it is stored as a distance along the belt). Saved with the tester save (spot + seconds left).
--- PixelTitleCubeShow.cs ---
PixelTitleCubeShow.cs Part of partial class PixelTitleScreen: while the main menu shows, the cube behind it changes to a random pixel type every cubeShowSeconds (1 s) - every type in the game (locked ones too; retired ores left out) - through PixelClicker.ShowLookPreview (look only: the game does not change). When Play is pressed (after a Continue has loaded the save) the menu cube POPS OFF like a husk - a snapshot of its mesh / materials thrown out sideways and up that spins and shrinks away within huskPopSeconds (0.6 s), on unscaled time, no physics, never a real husk - and the player's own block materializes in its place (EndLookPreview); then the usual camera intro plays. cubeShow off = the menu shows the real block as before.
--- PixelTitleIntro.cs ---
PixelTitleIntro.cs Part of partial class PixelTitleScreen: the main menu's INTRO when the game launches (Open(false), not when coming back from the game, not when a New game auto-starts). The screen starts fully black; once the blurred picture behind is ready (and introHoldSeconds more) the black FOLDS DOWN from the top and bottom into the middle bar (introCollapseSeconds, easing in and out, its colour turning into the bar's), the bar gives a small thump, then everything else comes in: the bar's glow fades up, the logo drops in from above, the Play / Load / Settings / Quit buttons and the other widgets pop in one after another with a little overshoot (introStagger apart), the rest of the foreground fades in. Buttons work once it is done. Any key or click skips straight to the end. playIntro off = the old instant screen.
--- PixelTitleMusic.cs ---
PixelTitleMusic.cs (The list always shows what is audible now: a cue's clip that is not one of the tracks is named in the caption: PixelAudio.PlayingClip.) The main menu's background music selector (created by PixelTitleScreen.Build under its foreground, showMusicPicker): a small bottom-centre box with a MUSIC caption, previous / next arrows and a drop-down listing every track (PixelAudio.MusicTrackCount / MusicTrackName / CurrentMusicTrack / SelectMusicTrack; the track names are the clips' names). It is SHOWN when the title screen opens and fades away after hideSeconds (musicPickerHideSeconds, 0 = 5) without activity (the mouse over it, a click, the list open); it fades back in while the mouse is over the spot. SHARED by the pause menu (PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI: same bottom-centre spot, no caption, hidden after pauseMusicHideSeconds 0 = 1.5 s and fading out in 0.15 s instead of 0.8 s; open = paused and not on the title screen): Create(parent, font, hideSeconds, showCaption, fadeOutSeconds, isOpen) - it shows when its screen opens (isOpen turns true) and while hovered. A SHUFFLE button right of the speaker toggles PixelAudio.MusicShuffle (per save file; lit = on, dim = in order). A speaker button at its right end mutes / unmutes the music (right click opens Settings > Audio directly: PixelPauseMenu.OpenAudioSettingsFromTitle) (PixelAudio.MusicMuted, per save file, also a Settings > Audio tick box); the chosen song and the playlist are per save too. Starts the chosen track at once; the usual shuffle / order carries on after it. Hidden entirely when there are no tracks.
--- PixelToadstoolDaze.cs ---
PixelToadstoolDaze.cs The Toadstool's spores (Foraging hazard, set off by one left click): a temporary status effect. For 'seconds' (12) every one of the player's own clicks on the cube makes the cube JERK AWAY from the mouse cursor (a jump of jumpSize cube widths straight away from the pointer on the screen, kept within maxDistance of home), then it drifts slowly back towards its home spot. Uses PixelClicker.SetCubeOffset (like Cube Dash) and gives way to a takeover minigame. A top status line counts the time down. Component created on demand (PixelToadstoolDaze.Begin from TriggerHazard).
--- PixelTogglesTutorial.cs ---
PixelTogglesTutorial.cs Toggles tutorial (added by PixelToggles.Start; part of the "Show first-time tips" intro together with the skills tutorial): when the Auto Clicker is bought with tips on, PixelShop.ApplyPurchase calls PixelTogglesTutorial.Request() (stat guide.togglesDue, saved) INSTEAD OF the plain hint box beside the Toggles tab (with tips off the old hint box still shows). At the next calm moment (title screen over, no Cubie visit, no other tip / window / tutorial, shop closed, not paused, startDelay 2 s) the screen dims and pauses (PixelTutorialDim) and Cubie narrates like the shop tutorial (BeginNarration, stick at the part each step describes via PixelGuideVendor.PointOverride, small Got it boxes that follow the Toggles window - PixelNotice nearScreen): the Toggles tab, the Upgrades group (where the Auto Clicker is ticked on), the other groups. (The old last step, a hint at skills and the Lumberjack's Pack, was removed: the Skills tab now appears with Standard's level-2 popup.) Steps are an Inspector list (empty = the built-ins). Done = stat guide.togglesTutorial (Settings > Replay tutorial clears it and, when the Toggles tab is there, asks for it again). Skipped entirely when TipsEnabled is off.
--- PixelTrafficMinigame.cs ---
PixelTrafficMinigame.cs / PixelTrafficSynth.cs Red Light Green Light minigame (id traffic, pack unlocksMinigame = "traffic", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 80K each; like Cube Dash: Takeover => true, cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (30) starts. A code-built traffic light (dark housing, red / yellow / green lamps with halos, unlit Sprites/Default materials, billboarded to the camera each frame, lightOffsetCubes 1.9 cube sizes to the right of the cube) cycles Green (greenMin..Max 2-5 s) -> Yellow (3-5 s) -> Red (1.5-3 s) -> Green...; Judge() scores every manual click (PixelCollected / PixelHit with automatic false): green +1, red -1; yellow: a click within hitWindow (0.22 s) of the moment the closing ring meets the cube is +1 (once per beat, lastGoodBeat), any other click -1. During yellow a LineRenderer circle closes round the cube once every beatSeconds (0.9, starts ringStartScale 3.2 cube sizes out, brightens / turns white as it nears; a tick sounds on the beat). The status line is coloured like the light and flashes white on a mistake. At the end it pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth x max(0, points) via AddCurrency (the clicks still pay normally); stats traffic.rounds / traffic.best, tip minigame_traffic; a round with points > 0 reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 20). Sounds are code-made (PixelTrafficSynth, filled in by PixelAudio.SynthFor while the clip lists are empty): traffic_start/_green/_yellow/_red/_tick/_good/_bad/_win/_lose.
--- PixelTrashCan.cs ---
PixelTrashCan.cs TRASH CAN (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Trash Can'): a plain cylinder you put on the floor. Within suctionRadiusCubes (default 2.5 cube widths) of it every loose old pixel - things thrown out of a Funnel hose, a machine or the end of a conveyor belt, or simply lying there - is pulled towards its mouth and, once it gets there, shrinks away and is DELETED (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no credit). Belts and connectors that end at the can hand their pixels in directly (IPixelBeltAcceptor). Pixels the player holds, ones riding a belt / carried by a machine (kinematic), flying pixels and attached ones are left alone. Saved with the tester save (position).
--- PixelTreasure.cs ---
PixelTreasure.cs Treasure pixel (PixelType.Treasure 32, IsStandalone, spawn switch in Toggles; pack 'Treasure Pixel': 50K White + 25K Black, spawn weight 0.1, clicksToCollect 10 = the average for estimates): a tough pixel with a RANDOM click count - PixelTier.randomClicks / randomClicksMin..Max (1-20), rolled per fresh pixel in Materialize (liveNeed, NeededClicks(tier) replaces tier.clicksToCollect in CollectInternal, SetLiveDamage and the live texture). The hit popup shows only the hit number (PixelHit gets needed = -1; the final hit shows n/n). Payout = amountPerClick x need x (1 + need x treasureRewardGrowth (0 = 0.15)) (TreasureMultiplier). Opening (PixelClicker.OpenTreasure): TreasureFx.Open (coins, glitter, splinters, gold ring + flash, sound chest_open) and a pile of treasureBasePixels (2) + need (max treasureMaxPixels 24) pixels of random unlocked types (not rare drops / fly-aways / Singularity / Treasure) is PAID at once (RollOldPixelAmount x (1 + need/10), one AddCurrency per type) while the same number of old pixels fountain out for show with amount 0 (TreasureFountain); stats treasure.opened, treasure.most_clicks; disableTreasureFx. Each non- final hit: TreasureFx.Hit (splinters + sparks, chest_hit). Look: PixelLook.chestTexture + damageCracks (PixelLooks.ChestTexture(step 0-8): 64 px planks, gold straps / frame / lock plate / rivets, a lid seam that leaks more light per step; looksVersion 26).
--- PixelTreeMode.cs ---
PixelTreeMode.cs TREE MODE (step 1 of docs/systems/WoodcuttingProgression.md; tested in Unity 2026-10-16 - it plays well, timings / numbers still being tuned; component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelTreeMode.Instance). While the skill named in skillId (woodcutting) is in play AND a shop pack with unlocksTreeMode is owned (the hidden 'Oak Trees (test)' pack: unlock it with the Dev Tools 'Shop unlocks' widget), the cube stops rolling the pack's blocks by weight and alternates between: - the FILLER rotation: random picks from 'fillers' (Twig, Leaf, Wood; Moss and Sap from level 2). At least a minimum of them, then a growing chance per block until a tree is certain at a maximum. The run SHRINKS with the level: 12-30 at level 1 (minFillerAtLevel1 / maxFillerAtLevel1) down to 3-10 at fillerLevelSpan (20) and above (minFillerAtTop / maxFillerAtTop), so trees are a treat early on; - a TREE: a species picked by weight among the unlocked ones (Oak only for now) plays out its slot list in order (Fixed / Run / Wildcard / Optional / Hazard). An INFESTED tree fills its Hazard slot (the Termite Hive, always last): chance infestedChance at infestFromLevel rising to infestedChanceAt20 at level 20, 0 while a Focus potion holds hazards off; it is announced on the tree bar from its first block. PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier asks TryPick first (tree picks skip the filler family swap and the special-block replacement). Every pick leaves a TAG (TakeTag: 1 = tree block, 2 = the tree's last block, 4 = the tree's first block) that PixelClicker keeps with the pre-rolled block and the live block, and RollTier reports each change of the live block (OnLiveChanged): the tree bar counts the live blocks, and 'Timber!' fires when the last block leaves the cube (bonus XP = timberXpShare of the tree's blocks, a status line, timberSound, stat woodcutting.trees_felled). The tree bar is a PixelStatusLines line (key 'tree'): 'Oak 3 / 9', plus '- Termite Hive at the end - Pesticide: n' on an infested tree (the infested colour says it is infested). Not saved: a reload starts in the filler rotation. Not built yet (see the design doc): other species, the Great Oak as a species, the infested look and sound, lookahead past one block, Pesticide charges, Shuffle switched off inside a tree. A block pulled out with Block Hold loses its tree tag (pulling the tree's last block fires Timber early).
--- PixelTube.cs ---
PixelTube.cs Static PixelTube.Build(points, radius, sides, mesh): smooth round tube mesh along any 3D path (rotation-minimising frames, no caps). Used by the bank hose.
--- PixelVacuumCleaner.cs ---
PixelVacuumCleaner.cs VACUUM CLEANER (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Vacuum Cleaner'; meant to become a consumable): a big canister vacuum on wheels that SUCKS husks up through the wide intake at its front and SPITS them back out of its hose, wherever the mouse points - a way to move husks to a specific place. - Left-click its body to DRIVE it (a teal ring under it; Driving); click it again or press Escape (PixelWindows entry) to stop. - While driving: W A S D or the arrow keys move it over the floor relative to the camera, diagonals included (normalized, driveSpeedCubes 5 model units per second; it turns to face where it goes). The keys are claimed (PixelKeys.ClaimDriveKeys), so S does not slow time and the arrows do not turn the orbital camera. - THE MOUSE ONLY AIMS: the hose's nozzle swings out towards the mouse's point on the floor (at most hoseReachCubes from the body) and points at it. - Every loose husk that comes within intakeRadiusCubes of the intake is sucked in (ReleaseOldPixel without credit: type + value kept in the tank, capacity 25, counter over the body) and AUTOMATICALLY spat out of the nozzle again, one every spitInterval, in an arc that lands on the mouse's point (SpawnStoredPixel). A husk it just spat out is ignored by the intake for a moment (ignoreSpatSeconds). - Sucking and spitting only happen while it is driven (a parked one just stands there). Removing it lets what is in the tank out. The model is built at modelScale (2.2) x a husk's size = one 'model unit'. Moves through a kinematic Rigidbody (MovePosition), so husks in the way are pushed. Saved with the tester save (position, yaw, tank contents).
--- PixelVacuumDevice.cs ---
PixelVacuumDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Runtime behaviour of a placed vacuum device (lathe-turned body PixelConsumables.BuildVacuumBody / VacuumLatheMesh: flared base, waist, funnel mouth with a lip, two glowing bands, a glowing core and a small spinning turbine 'Spinner' turned in OnTick; it lasts only vacuumDeviceSeconds (0 = 5 s), DeviceSeconds(d)): pulls old pixels in its radius, PixelClicker.AbsorbOldPixel, timer text above it, shrinks away at 0. Built by PixelConsumables.
--- PixelVacuumFx.cs ---
PixelVacuumFx.cs The Vacuum pixel's look (looksVersion 38 replaced the saved look; thick solid violet bevel outline (outlineThickness 0.055, strength 1) so it never reads as the plain Glass pixel; the old six-faced vortex marks are no longer used by it, faceCircles / faceRing still exist for other looks): PixelLook.vacuumContainer draws a clear lavender glass container - a hollow box with thin walls (PixelVacuumMesh: outer skin, inner surfaces facing the hollow, a round opening (HoleRadius 0.21 of the edge, Wall 0.07) in each of the FOUR side faces (+/-X, +/-Z) with a tube through each wall; the top and bottom are plain walls; PixelClicker.MeshFor swaps the mesh like the jagged rock mesh) and PixelLook.windFx adds a glowing lavender lip round every opening (overlay ring) and a suction wind at each one (rate ~26 per second per opening) (PixelVacuumFx.Attach: a local-space ConeVolume particle system (narrow 26 degree cone, 1.05 cube-widths long) whose +Z points out of the opening: stretched streaks with a RADIAL velocity towards the emitter origin (the opening's centre) so they funnel into the hole, a slight orbital curl, shrinking away as they arrive, ~46 per second). Both live cube and old pixels get them (old pixels keep the sucked-up pixels floating inside the glass via PixelVacuumContents).
--- PixelViewBounds.cs ---
PixelViewBounds.cs Keeps old pixels inside the camera's view with no scene objects: each FixedUpdate builds planes through the camera along the left/right/top/bottom screen edges (top/bottom inside the black bars) plus a near limit; a pixel past a plane is pushed back and its velocity reflected (bounciness, friction). The border follows each pixel's real size: the distance to a plane is compared with the pixel's reach towards it (box support function: the sum of |normal . axis| x half size along the pixel's own axes, from its BoxCollider and lossyScale), plus extraEdgeMargin (default 0), so a pixel's outer surface - not its centre - meets the screen edge (the old fixed 0.15 margin let big pixels poke off screen and kept small ones too far in). Skips kinematic pixels and fly-away tiers (Meteor). CIRCLE MODE (circleAroundFloor, default on): instead of the four screen-edge planes pixels are kept inside a vertical cylinder round the floor's middle whose radius is where the floor fades out (PixelFloor.TryGetEdgeCircle), by the pixel's own reach; the near-camera limit stays. Added by PixelClicker.Awake if missing. (An earlier version with big invisible wall slabs was reverted: the slabs tinted the screen and acted as a false floor.)
--- PixelViewWindow.cs ---
PixelViewWindow.cs FREE CAM (4th tick box, PixelFreeCam.cs): a detached camera flown by hand (WASD, Space / C, Shift, middle drag to look); never saved as the player's view, no zoom slider, no flat view. The View window (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; opens ONLY with its key, PixelAction.ViewWindow, default V; closes with the key, its X or Escape (PixelWindows 130); a small draggable window that does not pause the game; disableViewWindow). It replaces the old F (view) and X (zoom) keys, which are retired: View tick boxes - First person, Orbital, Top down (the normal view) - where ticking one unticks the others (PixelViewWindow.SetView: leaves the current view, enters the new one; an orbital <-> top down switch just re-aims the same camera, PixelOrbitCamera.SetTopDown); an 'Orthographic (flat) view' tick box (PixelOrthoView.Enabled, the same setting as Settings > Flat view; greyed out in first person, which is always 3D); a Zoom slider (the normal view's PixelZoom.SetTarget, or the orbit / top down camera's DistanceFraction; not in first person); and, in Orbital / Top down, the orbit controls: Auto orbit tick box + speed slider, and hold buttons for turning (and, in Orbital only, tilting). Each view row shows its CONTROLS as a vertical hover tooltip (AddViewTip; the old hint lines at the bottom of the screen for first person and orbit are gone). While the window is open first person gives the mouse back (PixelFirstPerson checks PixelViewWindow.IsOpen). The choices are saved with the save file's settings (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.ViewKind / ViewBaseZoom / ViewAutoOrbit / ViewAutoSpeed / ViewScrollInvert, listed in PixelSettingsSync): ApplySaved puts the view back once the title screen and camera intro are over. There is no separate Default view any more: TOP DOWN is the normal view (Default only means 'no view in use for a moment': the intro, a takeover minigame, first person ending) - Update puts the player's view back (ApplySaved every 0.4 s while Default; nothing saved = startView Top down at startZoom 1). Zoom 1.0 is the BASE view (PixelOrbitCamera: the scene camera is put 6 units from the cube at 89 degrees at start, useBaseView / baseDistance / baseTilt / baseTurn); the slider and the wheel zoom between 0.35x and 2x of that and the zoom is saved (ViewBaseZoom; the wheel too). 'Invert zoom scroll' tick (orbit section, PlayerPrefs ViewScrollInvert, default ON) flips the wheel direction in PixelOrbitCamera (scroll up = zoom in). (first person is never restored by itself; orthographic is the existing FlatView setting).
--- PixelWeakspotMinigame.cs ---
PixelWeakspotMinigame.cs Weak Spots (id weakspot, the skills' minigame; no shop pack, Running = any skill pack other than Standard is the pixel pack in play (startRunning for testing), Toggles > Minigames switch like the others; Takeover => true so nothing else starts meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval (120-240 s, first after firstRoundDelay 60 s) a round zooms a SECOND camera (CopyFrom of the main one, which draws nothing meanwhile, like first person mode) in on the cube (zoomDistance 2.6 cube widths, zoomSeconds), drops Time.timeScale to slowScale (0.1, fixedDeltaTime scaled) and 3-5 glowing weak spots (code-textured camera-facing quads with a shrinking timer ring, found by raycasting the zoom camera onto the cube's hit box, spaced spotGap apart) appear; the cube ignores ordinary clicks (ExternalClickBlock, PixelClicker.HoldSpin stops its spin, auto clicker paused). Variations per spot (RollKind, disableVariants): still (red/orange), drifting (blue, bounces round the cube face in viewport space, re-raycast each frame, driftChance 0 = 0.3, driftSpeed), pulsing (green, shrinks to pulseMin and swells every pulseSeconds, its click radius shrinks with it, pulseChance 0 = 0.3) and flickering (yellow, shows flickerOn s then vanishes flickerOff s, can't be hit while gone, flickerChance 0 = 0.2). Click all of them within huntSeconds (4 real seconds) for baseXp + speedXp x time left of XP for the skill in training via GrantSkillXp; running out of time pays nothing. Time Stop / Time Slow / the title screen end the round at once. The zoom camera starts / ends at the REAL camera's live pose every frame (PlaceCamera) and NeedsNormalCamera is false, so the player's orbit / zoom view is left alone and nothing pops when the round ends. Each round logs one line about the cube, its mesh and the spot collider (DescribeCube; the F10 'mark this moment' report carries it). Stats weakspot.rounds/wins, tip minigame_weakspot, sounds weakspot_start/_hit/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned).
--- PixelWindowChrome.cs ---
PixelWindowChrome.cs Reusable window behaviour for a code-built window (first user: the Dev Tools panel; My Window, the Mock up window and the Inspector each still carry their own copy of this logic - folding them into this one is the obvious cleanup). PixelWindowChrome.Attach(windowRect, font, prefKey, headerHeight, firstButtonRight, buttonTop, buttonSize, ...) on a window whose RectTransform is anchored + pivoted at the TOP LEFT of a full-screen canvas gives it: * DRAG by its header strip (a transparent handle behind the header's buttons), kept inside the playfield: between the black bars (PixelHud.BarHeight) and the screen sides. * MINIMIZE (button '-'): everything except the header, the handle and the chrome buttons is hidden and the window shrinks to the header (restores exactly what was showing). * DOCK to the left / right edge: drag it against a side (a pale strip shows where) and it docks at that height with only a sliver showing; it slides out while the mouse is near it or you are typing in it; an undock arrow puts it back. Dragging a docked window by its header also undocks it and carries it off. Optional: defaultPlacer (first-time position). Users: Dev Hub, the Next up window. RESIZE: EnableResize() adds a grip in the bottom-right corner that SCALES the window (0.55x - 1.6x, saved as prefKey + Scale; the Dev Hub uses it). Saved in PlayerPrefs prefKey + Min / Dock / DockY / X / Y (not part of the game save).
--- PixelWindows.cs ---
PixelWindows.cs CloseAllExcept(owner) closes every open window with priority < 100 (Crafting calls it when it opens, and closes itself when AnyOpenExcept(this) turns true; the event log fades out whenever any registered window opens, see PixelHints.Update). Static registry of closable windows (placing a device 100, shop upgrades window 40, shop 30, log 20, inventory 10). Escape closes the topmost one; the pause menu only opens on Escape when none is open. New windows should PixelWindows.Register(this, priority, isOpen, close) and Unregister(this) in OnDestroy. Entries whose owner was destroyed are dropped on every scene unload (Restart).
--- PixelWoodChipper.cs ---
PixelWoodChipper.cs The WOOD CHIPPER: the first thing built for the Dev Hub's Tester tab (PixelFeatureTester). A detailed machine built from primitives in code (PixelWoodChipper.Build(ghost)): a trailer chassis with two tyres, hubs, fenders, a hitch with a jack stand and coupler; an orange cutter housing with a hatch, bolts, a flywheel guard with a spinning pulley, warning stripes and a danger plate; a big tapered FEED HOPPER (double-sided walls, dark liner, yellow / black hazard rim) over two ribbed feed rollers; a discharge chute with a swivel hood; an engine with fins, valve cover, air filter, fuel tank, muffler and exhaust stack; a control panel with levers, a gauge and an e-stop; an amber beacon; reflectors. Build(true) makes the see-through placement ghost (no colliders). DISCHARGE ARROW + BELT LATCH: while placing, the ghost shows a flat arrow from the chute outwards (the chips shoot out along it). If the opening (start) of a conveyor belt lies in the arrow's area, the chipper latches on (PixelWoodChipper.TrySnapToBelt, used by PixelFeatureTester's placement snap): it turns so the arrow runs the way the belt runs and the chute sits just over the belt's opening; the arrow turns green. A placed chipper drops its Wood Chips blocks straight onto a belt opening in that area (BeltAtOutlet). BEHAVIOUR (the placed machine): any old pixel inside the hopper (or just above its mouth, suction) is pulled to the rollers and SHREDDED: PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel takes it out of the list (no payout), it is drawn through the rollers while it squashes, and a burst of pixel-coloured chips plus wood chips comes out of the chute. While it works the machine shakes, the rollers and flywheel speed up, the beacon blinks and the exhaust puffs more. Pixels you are holding are left alone. WOOD CHIPS: every woodPerChips (5) wood pixels consumed (woodConsumed / chipProgress) the chute spits out one 'Wood Chips' old pixel (PixelType.WoodChips, PixelClicker.EnsureWoodChipsTier, never spawns on the cube; it despawns like any old pixel until later tools turn it into currency). A BLUE bar under the health bar shows the progress to the next block. GAME RULES: a WOOD pixel (PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock) gives Woodcutting XP (woodXp, PixelSkills.AddXp on the Woodcutting pack, with the usual popup). Any other pixel DAMAGES it (damagePerOtherPixel off 'maxHealth'): the paint darkens, sparks fly, the exhaust turns black and thick; a health bar floats over it. At 0 it is BROKEN for good (stops, no beacon, smoke, 'BROKEN' label): clear it in the Tester tab and place a new one. Tunables are [SerializeField] fields (editable live with the Dev Hub's Inspector).
--- PixelZoom.cs ---
PixelZoom.cs Zoom of the normal view (added by PixelClicker.Awake; set from the View window's zoom slider with PixelZoom.SetTarget - the old X key is gone; disableZoom). Old note: PRESS it to step through the zoom levels (zoomLevels, empty = 1 / 0.6 / 0.35 = fractions of the normal camera distance; the step happens when the key is let go, and only if the wheel was not used meanwhile), HOLD it and use the mouse wheel to zoom smoothly anywhere between the closest and the furthest level. The real camera slides along the line from the cube to its normal position (the cube keeps its spot on screen; orthographic cameras scale their size); `followSharpness` smooths it. While zoomed PixelZoom.HomeCamera (a hidden copy at the normal pose, also returned by PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera) is what PixelViewBounds / PixelFloor.FitToView read, so old pixels stay inside the NORMAL view. The zoom snaps back to normal (ResetNow) when the title screen, the camera intro, first person, the orbit camera or a takeover minigame needs the camera.

=== 10. SYSTEM DOCS (docs/systems/*.md) ===
--- docs/systems/CombatDesign.md ---
Combat (design, not built yet)

> An enemy is a tree that fights back.

Status: idea, written up 2026-10-16. Nothing built. Parked for AFTER 1.0, maybe (2026-10-16) - see docs/GameDesign.md section 9b. It is built on Woodcutting's tree mode (PixelTreeMode.cs, the "bag" of
blocks played out in a designed order), which the developer has tested in Unity and likes (2026-10-16; only timings and numbers left to tune), so the base is proven.
Overall game design: docs/GameDesign.md. Gear (weapons are gear): GearDesign.md.

How to use it: open questions first (C1, C2...), each with a suggestion. Answered ones move into the design below.

---

Open questions

Shape

| # | Question | Suggestion |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | Is Combat its own skill pack (chosen in the Skills window), or does it happen alongside every skill? | Its own pack, "Combat", like the gathering skills: it matches the existing structure and keeps each skill's rotation clean. Occasional "intruders" (C9) can visit other skills later. |
| C2 | One Combat skill, or several (RuneScape's Attack / Strength / Defence)? | One skill. Splitting it triples the grind without adding new actions; the depth comes from gear and enemies instead. |
| C3 | What does "getting hit" cost, with no player health? | Like hazards today: the enemy steals a little currency, scatters your floor husks, or drains the Block Hold meter, plus a short XP penalty. It hurts; nothing dies. |
| C4 | Is there player health at all (and food to heal)? | No health bar. Food is a buff you eat before or during a fight (less damage, faster attacks), so Fishing / Farming still feed Combat the RuneScape way without a fail state. |
| C5 | What does the auto clicker do in combat? | It fights weak enemies on its own; elites and bosses wait for your own clicks, so "many ways to play" holds and active play stays better. |

Content

| # | Question | Suggestion |
|---|---|---|
| C6 | What are the enemies - fantasy monsters or everyday nuisances? | Everyday nuisances, to fit the real-world, mundane concept: pests, stray machines, rival vendors, tax inspectors - and Cubie's debt collectors as his bill grows (prestige). |
| C7 | What do enemies drop? | Husks of materials (scrap, bones, hides, paperwork...), combat XP, a rare drop per enemy (a trophy for the Collection Log), and cosmetics from bosses. |
| C8 | Are there bosses? | Yes, rare: a long slot list with phases (armour, then weak spots, then a frenzy of attack blocks), announced like an infested tree, with a cosmetic drop. |
| C9 | Do enemies ever turn up outside Combat? | Later, optionally: an "intruder" can wander into another skill's rotation now and then (like a visitor), so other skills get a taste of it. |
| C10 | Where do weapons come from? | Crafted from other skills' materials (Mining metals, Woodcutting handles), bought from a vendor, or dropped by bosses. |

Fit

| # | Question | Suggestion |
|---|---|---|
| C11 | Does it clash with the "no story, mundane grind" concept? | Not if the enemies stay ordinary (C6). Combat becomes one more job in the grind - pest control, debt collection - not a heroic quest. |
| C12 | Is combat in 1.0 or an update? | An update after 1.0, once tree mode, gear and the skill polish are done. It is a big new skill, the kind the 1.0 plan says comes later. |

---

The idea in one paragraph

While Combat is in play the cube alternates between a wandering rotation (weak critters and scraps, like Woodcutting's filler) and
an enemy arriving. Each enemy is a designed slot list played out on the cube, exactly like a tree species: armour blocks,
weak-spot blocks, flesh blocks and attack blocks. A health bar counts it down; breaking the last block defeats it and drops loot.
Attack blocks are the enemy's turn: clear them in time or they hit you.

How it reuses tree mode

Tree mode already has every piece combat needs (PixelTreeMode.cs header):

| Tree mode today | Combat version |
|---|---|
| Filler rotation (Twig, Leaf, Wood...), a tree arrives after minFiller..maxFiller blocks | Wandering rotation (critters, scraps), an enemy arrives the same way |
| A species picked by weight among the unlocked ones | An enemy picked by weight among the unlocked ones (level-gated) |
| Slot kinds: Fixed, Run, Wildcard, Optional, Hazard | Fixed = set body parts, Run = several armour plates, Wildcard = random body part, Optional = a weak spot that may or may not show, Hazard = an attack block |
| Infested trees, warned from the first block | Elite enemies, warned on the health bar from the first block |
| Tree bar: "Oak 3 / 9" (a PixelStatusLines line) | Health bar: "Rat King 3 / 9" |
| "Timber!" + bonus XP when the last block leaves | "Defeated!" + loot + bonus combat XP |
| Pick tags (first / last block) | the same tags: the first block triggers the entrance, the last the defeat |

So a new enemy is data, not code: one more entry in a list of slot lists, like a new tree species. The shared part should be
generalised once ("encounter mode") so Woodcutting, Combat and later Mining veins / Fishing spots all use it (the Woodcutting doc
already plans this: "The same system for other skills").

The blocks of a fight

| Block | What it does |
|---|---|
| Flesh | a normal block; one hit, a little damage |
| Armour | a tough block (several hits, like tough pixels today); a better weapon breaks it faster |
| Weak spot | a rare, high-damage block; can start a Weak Spots round for a critical hit |
| Attack | the enemy winds up (a visible charge, like a hazard's tell); clear it in time or take a hit (C3) |
| Finisher | the last slot; breaking it defeats the enemy |

Player actions (all existing mechanics)

- Left click: hit, as always.
- Right-click clear: the existing hazard clearing works on attack blocks.
- Block Hold as a parry: holding an attack block blocks it (no hit) and stores a counter-attack - a new use for Block Hold.
- Weak Spots minigame: crits on weak-spot blocks, like catching fish.
- Consumables: food buffs (C4), potions; a "smoke bomb" to skip an enemy.
- Gear: the weapon in your one gear slot (below).

Gear and other skills

- Weapons are gear. With one slot ("you're a cube"), wearing a sword means not wearing the Tractor Glove or the Prime Gauntlet:
  exactly the choice the one-slot rule is for. Weapon types change the fight, not just the numbers: a hammer smashes armour, a dagger
  hits weak spots more often, a shield-like item parries for free.
- Skills feed Combat (pillar 3): Mining metals and Woodcutting handles -> crafted weapons (C10); Fishing / Farming -> food buffs (C4);
  Electrician -> powered weapons later.
- Combat feeds skills: monster husks (scrap, bones, hides) become crafting materials and machine-repair parts; trophies fill the
  Collection Log; bosses drop cosmetics (the late-game rewards in GameDesign.md).

Tone

Enemies are ordinary nuisances, not fantasy monsters (C6), so combat fits the game's concept - the real-world grind with a simulated
feel. Combat is just another job: pest control, clearing out broken machines, and seeing off Cubie's debt collectors when his bill gets
silly. No heroes, no quest; the humour is how mundane it all is.

Build order (when it is time)

1. ~~Prove tree mode in Unity (Woodcutting).~~ Done 2026-10-16: it feels good; tuning remains.
2. Generalise it into a shared "encounter mode" (data lists of slot lists, used by Woodcutting first).
3. Build the gear slot (GearDesign.md), so weapons have somewhere to go.
4. Combat pack: the wandering rotation, 3-4 enemies, attack blocks with the "getting hit" cost (C3), the health bar, loot.
5. Then: elites, a boss, weapons from crafting, food buffs, the Block Hold parry.

Fits the game / could be better

- Reuses: tree mode (the whole encounter loop), hazards (attack blocks and their clearing), tough blocks (armour), the Weak Spots
  minigame (crits), Block Hold (parry), gear (weapons), PixelStatusLines (health bar), the Collection Log and crafting.
- New pieces: the Combat skill pack, the "getting hit" penalty, loot tables per enemy, weapons as gear items, food buffs.
- Do first: generalise tree mode into encounter mode before writing combat, so the two never become two copies of the same system.
--- docs/systems/ConnectionMap.md ---
Connection map

What every skill, machine, consumable, minigame and special pixel gives, needs and answers, so it is easy to see where the game already links up and where it does not yet.
Rules this map follows: PixelRules.md (a click gives currency and drops a husk; husks are material, never currency by themselves; holding a utility block gives charges).

State legend: Built = in the game now · Designed = agreed in a design doc, not built · Idea = suggested, not agreed · ? = intent not written down yet (fill it in).

Keep this map current: when a system gets a new link (or loses one), change its row here. The pruning test (end of this page) uses it.

1. The big picture

            click (currency + husk)
                   |
   skills ---------+--------- machines / consumables
 (what spawns,     |          (turn husks into XP, charges,
  hazards, perks,  |           storage, a little currency)
  unlocks for      |
  other skills)    v
             Pure pixels -> shop (machines, consumables, skill packs)

- Clicking is the staple source of currency.
- Skills decide what spawns, bring their hazard, and unlock blocks / tools for *other* skills.
- Machines and consumables turn husks into something useful; they answer the game's pressures (despawn, space, hazards, one skill at a time, the rotation).
- Vendors buy skill currency for Pure pixels; the shop sells machines, consumables and skill packs for Pure pixels.

2. Skills

| Skill | Core blocks | Hazard | Gives others | Needs from others | Machines / tools that belong to it | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Standard | the six basic pixels (+ Glass, Luminescent) | - | Pure pixels (the shop currency) | - | the shop | Built |
| Woodcutting | Wood, Leaf, Twig, Sap, Bark, Acorn, Heartwood, Pinecone, Moss, Pesticide; Great Oak | Termite Hive | wood husks (Chipper XP, Kiln Charcoal, crafting); hives -> Focus potions | Whetstone (Mining, Designed); Pesticide charges (Farming, Designed) | Wood Chipper (Built), Pesticide Sprayer (Designed), Lumberjack (Built) | Built; tree rework Designed (WoodcuttingProgression.md) |
| Mining | Rock (filler), Copper, Tin, Iron, Silver, Gold, Obsidian, Bedrock | Gas Pocket | Whetstone -> Woodcutting (Designed); metals -> Electrician? (Idea) | ? | Prospector (Built) | Built (perks mostly text) |
| Farming | Dirt (filler), Seed, Water, Fertilizer, Pollen, Wheat, Weevil, Golden Fruit | Pest | Pesticide charges -> Woodcutting (Designed); Saplings -> better trees (Idea) | water / sunlight: Solar daylight speeds growth (Built); Fishing water? (Idea) | Sprinkler (Built), Seeds (Built), Farmer visitor (Built) | Built |
| Foraging | Weeds (filler), Berry, Mushroom, Herb, Truffle, Treasure | Mold | ? | ? | ? | Built (perks text only) |
| Fishing | Water (filler), Fish, Seaweed, Shell, Pearl, Bait, Bottle, Whale | Jellyfish | ? (water / driftwood -> Farming? Idea) | ? | Bait Bucket (Built), Weak Spots minigame (Built) | Built |
| Electrician | Electric, Wire, Battery, Capacitor, Resistor, Transistor, Fuse, Bulb | - | power for machines (Built: Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, device links); the conveyor belt at level 5 (Built), wider belts at 10 / 20 (Built); a chainsaw for Woodcutting (Idea); Recycler speed (Designed) | metals from Mining? (Idea) | Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Conveyor Belt (Built); the factory machines below belong here (?) | Built |
| Offline Progression | the assigned pack's blocks | - | earnings while away | - | - | Built |

Gaps: Foraging gives nothing to other skills and needs nothing yet; Fishing has no outgoing link; Mining has no incoming link.

3. Machines and consumables

Placed devices and consumables (the shop / vendors)

| Item | Takes in | Gives out | Pressure it answers | Skill home | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vacuum Device | loose old pixels | husk XP (pixel rules); later maybe husks to a Recycler / storage | despawn | ? | Built |
| Fan | - | moves old pixels | space | ? | Built |
| Sorter (ring) | collected pixels | streams them out of a pipe | space | ? | Built |
| Magnet | loose old pixels | holds them in a heap | despawn, space | ? | Built |
| Magnetized Sorter (crafted) | old pixels | chosen types out of three pipes | space | Crafting | Built |
| Quantum Organizer | bound types | stores them in its box | despawn, space | ? | Built |
| Storage Crate | old pixels | stores them, click to take out | despawn | ? | Built |
| Funnel | falling old pixels | a queue down a tube to a belt (with backpressure) | space, despawn | Electrician? | Built |
| Wood Chipper | wood husks | Woodcutting XP + Wood Chips | despawn | Woodcutting | Built |
| Sprinkler | - | waters sprouts | - | Farming | Built |
| Charge Booster | Electric pixels | a super charge | - | Electrician | Built |
| Lightning Rod | old pixels around it | turns them into Electric pixels | - | Electrician | Built |
| Bait Bucket | Fish / Water husks | more fish spawn | - | Fishing | Built |
| Seeds | - | grow a pixel type | - | Farming | Built |
| Potions (per pixel type) | Glass + pixels (crafting) | that type's effect for a while | the rotation | Crafting | Built |
| Combo Fuel | - | keeps the combo | - | Combo | Built |
| Ghost Bait / Pet Treat | - | ghosts sooner / hyper pets | - | Ghost minigame / Pets | Built |
| Gauntlet | the six Prime Cubes | the Prime Cubelette | - | collectibles (Wizard) | Built |
| Lumberjack's Focus | Termite Hives (Designed) | trees without hives for a while | hazards | Woodcutting | Built as a dev widget; hive recipe Designed |
| Recycler | husks (fill the bin, press its button) | currency at 50% of each husk's value | despawn | early game (Cubie's gift); Electrician speed later (Idea) | Built |
| Pesticide Sprayer | Pesticide husks | clears hives automatically | hazards | Woodcutting / Farming | Designed |

Factory machines (Dev Hub tester features today)

| Machine | Takes in | Gives out | Pressure | Skill home | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Conveyor Belt (+ corner joints) | husks | moves them | space | Electrician (level 5) | Built (a real item at Electrician 5) |
| Splitter | a belt's husks | shares them over three outputs | space | Electrician? | Built (tester) |
| Conveyor Elevator | a belt's husks | lifts them to a higher belt | space | Electrician? | Built (tester) |
| Conveyor Connector / Hose Adapter | husks in its box / from a Funnel | onto the next belt or machine | space | Electrician? | Built (tester) |
| Belt Sorter | a belt's husks + one filter block | that type off the side | space, the rotation | Electrician? | Built (tester) |
| Kiln | husks | Charcoal husks | despawn | ? | Built (tester) |
| Trash Can | loose husks | deletes them | space | ? | Built (tester) |
| Tiny Robot | loose husks | puts them on a belt | space | Electrician (planned consumable) | Built (tester) |

Hands (first person)

| Action | Takes in | Gives out | Pressure | Later replaced / kept by | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Break husks by hand | loose husks | skill XP (a fraction of the click's XP) | despawn | machines (Chipper, Recycler) | Built |
| Pick up and carry husks | loose husks | husks fed to a machine by hand | despawn, space | belts, Funnel, connectors, Tiny Robot | Built |
| Repair a broken machine | husks from other skills (Mining metals, Electrician Wire) | the machine runs again | machine breakdowns | stays a hands-only job | Designed (PixelRules.md) |

Machines can break from the wrong input (main cause) or gentle wear (optional); a broken machine stops and keeps its contents until it is repaired in first person (PixelRules.md, "Machine breakdowns and repair").

Gaps: Charcoal (Kiln) and Wood Chips (Chipper) have no use yet; most factory machines have no decided skill home (Electrician is the natural one); several shop devices have no skill home (fine for early-game tools, but say so).

4. Pressures and what answers them

| Pressure | Answered by |
|---|---|
| Despawn (husks vanish) | first person by hand (the first answer), Wood Chipper, Storage Crate, Magnet, Quantum Organizer, Funnel, Recycler (Designed), Kiln |
| Machine breakdowns (wrong input, wear) | sorting before the machine (Belt Sorter, Splitter), repairs in first person (Designed) |
| Space (the floor circle is small) | belts, Splitter, Elevator, Connector, Fan, Sorter, Trash Can, free cam; a bigger floor as a reward (Idea, the dev toggle exists) |
| Hazards | right-click clearing, Pesticide charges / Sprayer (Designed), Focus potions, Infestation dial (open), sap bait (Designed), Belt Sorter |
| One skill at a time | auto clicker + machines working while you focus elsewhere; Offline Progression |
| The rotation (cannot choose the next block) | Block Hold, Shuffle, potions, lookahead perks (Designed) |

5. Minigames and visitors

| Minigame / visitor | Gives | Connects to | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| All skill-affecting minigames | currency / XP; the minigame XP multiplier boosts skill XP (PixelSkillsMinigames) | skills | Built |
| Evil Tree | Magic Wood + Woodcutting XP | Woodcutting (Magic Wood has no use yet) | Built |
| Weak Spots | catching fish | Fishing | Built |
| Stardust | stardust while the Meteor pixel is unlocked | ? | Built |
| Black Hole, Ghost, Meteor | currency, ghost / meteor events | ? | Built |
| Snake, Sorting Race, Cube Dash, Red Light Green Light, Cube Evasion, Cube Blink, Breakout, Bomb Defusal, Ultra Holes | currency (RGB-pack minigames) | ? | Built |
| Cubie | guide, shop, tutorials | the whole early game | Built |
| Tinkerer, Wizard, Entrepreneur, Farmer | trades; Wizard -> Gauntlet; Farmer -> Dragon Seeds | collectibles, Farming | Built |
| Prospector, Lumberjack | buy skill currency for Pure pixels; sell skill tools | Mining, Woodcutting | Built |
| Egg, Bean (cats) | rare visits | ? | Built |

Gaps: most minigames only pay currency; they could each feed one skill (e.g. Bomb Defusal -> Mining, Snake -> Foraging) the way the Evil Tree feeds Woodcutting.

6. Special pixels

| Pixel | What it does | Connects to | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| Glass | potion and Pixel Bank material | Crafting, Bank | Built |
| Luminescent | glowing trail | ? | Built |
| Vacuum | sucks up old pixels for husk XP | husks, skills (XP) | Built |
| Obsidian | a tough Mining ore | Mining | Built |
| Singularity | gravity well holding old pixels | despawn, space | Built |
| Ghost | drifting old pixel, Ghost minigame | Ghost minigame | Built |
| Meteor | flying old pixel, Stardust | Stardust minigame | Built |
| Solar | daylight that speeds growth; a hovering Solar husk keeps daylight up | Farming (growth) | Built |
| Electric | Overcharge, device power | Electrician, auto clicker | Built |
| Mirror | repeats the last payout | the rotation | Built |
| Fire | ring of flames | ? (Kiln fuel? Idea) | Built |
| Treasure | random-hit chest, pours out pixels | ? | Built |
| Nuclear | ages nearby old pixels fast | a hazard-like pressure | Built |

7. Collectibles and meta systems

| System | Connects to | State |
|---|---|---|
| Dragon Cubes (wish), God Cubes, Prime Cubes + Gauntlet | Collection Log, gallery, Wizard | Built |
| Pets | pixel types (a pet per type), Pet Treats | Built |
| Crafting | potions, attuned devices, Magnetized Sorter | Built |
