| Pixel Bank | husk storage for later use (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician), one shared capacity; stored husks carry no payout (Designed) | Built; storage-only rework Designed |
| Achievements, Collection Log | everything (goals) | Built |
| Combo meter | clicking | Built |
| Time Stop | clicking, devices | Built |

8. Open gaps to decide (from the tables above)

1. Foraging: what does it give other skills, and what does it need? (e.g. Herbs -> potions, Mushrooms -> Farming fertilizer)
2. Fishing: an outgoing link (e.g. Water -> Farming, Pearls -> crafting).
3. Mining: an incoming link (e.g. Electrician powers a drill; Charcoal fuels a smelter). Its metal husks as machine repair material (Designed) give it an outgoing link.
4. Electrician as the factory skill: confirm it is the home of belts, splitters, elevators, connectors, sorters and the Tiny Robot.
5. Dead-end outputs: Charcoal, Wood Chips and Magic Wood need a use.
6. Minigames: give each one a skill it feeds.
7. Pixel rules rework: step 1 built (husks stop paying; Vacuum gives husk XP, Solar keeps daylight up, Bank is storage). Open: should the husk minigames (Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout) keep paying currency per husk?
8. Shop relics: most shop pixels and devices are left over from early development. Decide which consumables stay shop-only from the start and which move to unlocks later (and from which skill).
9. Breakdowns: which machines can break, which inputs break each one, whether wear exists, and which husks repair which machines.

The pruning test

A skill, block, machine or feature earns its place by passing at least two of these:

1. Unique action: it asks the player to do or decide something new.
2. Connected: it gives to, or needs from, at least two other things on this map.
3. Pressure: it creates or answers one of the pressures in section 4.

Anything that fails two of the three: merge it into something else, or cut it.
--- docs/systems/GearDesign.md ---
Gear, first person unlock and the alien vendor (design, not built yet)

Status: ideas agreed in chat (2026-10-15/16), nothing built. Parked for AFTER 1.0, maybe (2026-10-16) - see docs/GameDesign.md section 9b. The to-do list has one line per step (Assets/Resources/Todo.txt, "Gear system" onwards).
This doc collects what was agreed and, more importantly, the blank spots: the decisions nobody has made yet. Each blank spot has a
suggested default (marked Suggestion) so building can start without waiting, but the user decides.

The four pieces and their order

1. Gear system: one gear slot (you're a cube), free swapping, later more faces.
2. First person as a mid-game unlock: the Cube Visor, the first gear piece.
3. The alien vendor: only reachable in first person; sells relics and face sockets.
4. Legendary gear: the Prime Gauntlet's powers, God Cube relics, the Dragon Radar, the Seeker's Charm.

Each step needs the one before it. Building out of order means hand-wiring things that should come from the shared gear system.

Goals

- Gear changes HOW you play, not just your numbers. Each piece is a new action or a new rule, not "+10% pay". The game already has
  many % layers (potions, Focus, pets, perks, combo, stakes, upgrades, Operation).
- Choices matter. One slot means wearing one thing means not wearing another.
- The super rares finally pay off. Today the God Cubes and Prime Cubes mostly sit in the Collection Log, and the Cubelette is a trophy.
- First person becomes a place with things to do, and reaching it is a "wow" moment instead of a key you press on day one.

---

1. Gear system

Agreed
- One slot, on the top face, worn like a hat. Cubie's line: *"Where would you even put a second one? You don't have arms."*
- Free, instant swapping, with a quick-swap key, so gear is picked for the moment.
- Later, more faces: "face sockets" unlock the other faces one at a time, up to six; the bottom face comes last ("you're sitting on it").
  The alien sells sockets as its priciest item.
- Data-driven: one entry per gear item (name, slot, where it comes from, its effect); effects plug in through shared hooks the way
  PixelBlockRules works, never checks scattered through PixelClicker.
- Lives in Pocket (PixelMyWindow) as a new Gear tab. Saved per save file; a Database category; Stats rows.

Blank spots
- Is gear a new kind of item, or a consumables Device? The Prime Gauntlet is already a Device (DeviceKind.Gauntlet), and devices have
  owned counts, the inventory, prices and the shop.
  Suggestion: gear items are Devices with a new "equippable" flag, so the shop, inventory, crafting and saving all work already;
  the equipped item is one saved field.
- Can you own duplicates? Suggestion: no, gear is one of each (like the God Cubes).
- What does a second face DO when gear is worn there - the same as the top face? Or does each face have a theme (top = active power,
  sides = passive, bottom = ???)? Suggestion: all faces equal at first; themes only if it feels flat.
- How many face sockets, at what price, and where else do they come from (only the alien, or also skill levels / Mastery)?
- Swap cooldown? Free swapping could let a player cycle gear every second to stack effects.
  Suggestion: no cooldown, but an item's power cooldown keeps running while it is off, so cycling gains nothing.
- Does gear show on the cube? A tiny hat / glove / visor on the top face would be charming, but every item then needs a model.
  Suggestion: yes, small and simple, built from primitives like the vendors are.
- Does the auto clicker trigger gear powers, or only your own clicks? Suggestion: only your own clicks.

---

2. First person as a mid-game unlock

Agreed
- Today first person is available from the start (its key only checks for menus / takeovers: PixelFirstPerson.CanEnter).
- It becomes a gift: the Cube Visor, the first gear piece, given at a mid-game milestone.
- First use plays a camera dive down into the floor.
- Hidden until then: the View window's First person tick box and the first person key.
- Dev Hub: the Unlock widget can give it for testing.

Blank spots
- Which milestone? Candidates: buying a second skill pack, Standard level 5, a Mastery %, Cubie's third visit, owning N devices.
  Suggestion: a Cubie visit triggered by Standard level 5, so it fits his existing visit system and the Standard skill.
- Who gives it? Cubie (the guide) or the alien (but the alien needs first person to be reached at all).
  Suggestion: Cubie gives the Visor; the alien's ship is what you discover with it.
- Does the Visor have to be WORN to use first person? If yes, first person costs your one gear slot, which clashes with the alien's
  relics (also first-person gear). Suggestion: no, once received, first person is unlocked for good and the Visor is a free bonus item
  (or it doesn't take the slot). This needs a decision before building.
- The Collection Log gallery is walked in first person. Should it stay usable before the unlock? Suggestion: yes, the gallery is its
  own room; it doesn't spoil the main floor reveal.
- What does the dive look like, and is there a line from Cubie after it?

---

3. The alien vendor

Agreed
- A 3D alien whose UFO lands at the edge of the floor, a spot never seen from the normal top view. You walk over in first person.
- Ties the game's UFO (the UFO Helper, the main menu UFO) into the world.
- Built on the shared vendor base PixelVisitorMinigame (wares, prices, dialogue), adding only the 3D body and the walk-up trigger -
  not a hand-wired fourth vendor.
- Sells relics (first person gear) and face sockets.

Blank spots
- Is the ship always there once unlocked, or does it visit on a timer like Cubie and the other vendors?
  Suggestion: it visits (lands, stays a few minutes, leaves), so walking out is a small event, and the first landing is scripted to
  happen right after the Visor.
- What does it charge? Ordinary pixels, a new currency, husks, or something only first person gives (phase pixels - see relics)?
  Suggestion: phase pixels, so first person feeds its own shop.
- Name and personality. Every vendor has one (Cubie, Pip the Prospector, Big Tim the Lumberjack). Also: does it talk in text, or in
  bleeps with subtitles?
- What if the player never goes into first person? Does anything nudge them (a Cubie line, a beam of light visible from the top view)?
  Suggestion: a faint light beam at the floor edge that you can just see from the normal view.
- How does "walk up and talk" work? Look at it and press a key, click it, or just walk close? Suggestion: walk close, a prompt
  appears in the bottom hint box (PixelHintBox), press the interact key.
- Does it ever appear on the main menu with the other wandering vendors?

---

4. Alien relics (first person gear)

Agreed (the ideas)
| Relic | What it does |
|---|---|
| Grav Boots | Low gravity and high jumps; reach husks stacked up high |
| Tractor Glove | Pull husks to you from across the floor, then throw them (e.g. into the Recycler) |
| Xeno Lens | Shows hidden phase pixels that only exist in first person and pay well |
| Blink Core | A short teleport dash |
| Probe Drone | A little bot that follows you and clicks the cube while you are away from it |

- Relics are first-person-only on purpose, so first person needs enough to do there.

Blank spots
- Phase pixels need their own design: what they look like, where they appear, how often, what they pay, whether they are a currency.
  They are the backbone of first person's reason to exist, so they deserve their own short doc before the relics are built.
- Does first person have enough to do with these five? Today you can walk, click the cube and grab husks. The relics add movement and
  husk handling; is that enough to make it a real way to play, or does it need a goal (e.g. the machine repair idea already on the to-do)?
- Is the Probe Drone too strong? It is basically an auto clicker that works while you're away. Suggestion: it clicks slower than the
  Auto Clicker and only while you're in first person.
- Tractor Glove vs Pixel Grabbing: grabbing already exists. What makes the glove different enough? (Range? Several at once? Throwing?)
- Do relics do anything at all outside first person? Agreed: no, on purpose. Confirm, since it means top-view players never use them.

---

5. Legendary gear (the super rares)

Agreed (the ideas)
- The Prime Gauntlet becomes gear instead of being used up by forging. Each Prime Cube set in it unlocks a power with a cooldown:

| Stone | Power |
|---|---|
| Space | Pull every floor husk into the Recycler or crate at once |
| Time | Rewind the last 10 s of clicks, or a free long Time Stop |
| Power | One punch breaks any tough block at 50x pay |
| Reality | Turn the current block into any block you pick |
| Mind | See the next 5 blocks and choose which one comes up |
| Soul | Pets much stronger for a minute |

  All six = the Snap: half the husks on screen vanish and pay out; long cooldown.
- The Cubelette becomes what the Gauntlet turns into after so many Snaps.
- Dragon Radar charm: a hint when a rare is about to roll. The wish stays as it is.
- God Cube relics: Slifer = every 20th click chains lightning to nearby husks; Ra = a sun phase, a golden hour for a minute;
  Obelisk = tough blocks take double hits.
- Seeker's Charm from the Golden Snitch: later snitches are easier to catch and pay more.

Blank spots
- Super rares are 1 in 70,000 to 1 in 7 million. Most players will never own legendary gear. Is that fine (bragging rights), or should
  there be a slow guaranteed path too (e.g. the Wizard sells a lesser copy)?
- How do you GET a God Cube relic? Is the God Cube itself the relic (wear the cube), or does finding it unlock a crafted relic?
  Suggestion: the find unlocks a relic you can equip; the cube stays in the Collection Log.
- "Rewind the last 10 s" needs the game to record recent clicks and undo them (currency, husks, the cube's block). That is a big piece
  of work on its own. Suggestion: keep only the "free long Time Stop" half for the first version.
- Mind and Reality could break the skills' designed rhythms (Woodcutting's tree shapes, Fishing's spawn table). Do they work during
  trees / special blocks, or only in the normal rotation?
- How many Snaps make the Cubelette, and what does the Cubelette do once it exists (still a trophy, or the strongest gear piece)?
- Gauntlet with only some stones: usable from 1 stone, or only once all six are set? Suggestion: each stone's power works as soon as
  it is set; the Snap needs all six.
- The Dragon Radar's hint: a sound, a glow on the cube, a line in the status box? And how early before the rare roll?

---

Fits the game / could be better

- Reuses: the Devices list (owning, prices, saving), Pocket for the tab, the vendor base for the alien, PixelBlockRules-style hooks
  for effects, the Gauntlet window for setting stones, the first person system and its neon floor, PixelHintBox for prompts.
- Needs first: the rare set table cleanup already on the to-do (one entry per super-rare family), so legendary gear reads its data
  from one place instead of enum ranges.
- New pieces: the gear entry list + effect hooks, the equipped-item save field, phase pixels, the alien's 3D body and landing.
- Watch for: each gear effect becoming another scattered check. Effects should ask the gear system ("is the Tractor Glove worn?")
  through one call, the way PixelCalm answers "is the game in a menu?".
--- docs/systems/MainMenuFlow.md ---
MainMenuFlow

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Main menu camera / floor / welcome-back flow

The camera swing is smoother (PixelCameraIntro.Apply turns against an 'up' that blends from world up to the scene camera's own up (upRef), so a TOP-DOWN scene camera no longer makes the picture flip 180 degrees at the very end of the swing: yaw / pitch / distance interpolation instead of a slerp between the two offsets, one continuous look-at blend, quintic Smoother ease, frame time clamped to 0.05 s). The floor-fade switches are keepFloorOnTitle (false = fading on) and floorFadeSeconds (0 = 1): a serialized bool that defaults to true / a float that defaults to 1 would read false / 0 on a scene saved before the field existed - new serialized defaults must be 'off' / 0 = default. Margins slide: PixelHud.Update slides the top / bottom black bars (and PixelDockedButton.Apply pushes every docked button outward by the same amount, PixelHud.RevealEased, revealSeconds 0 = 0.9) away while PixelTitleScreen.Showing or PixelTitleScreen.Reversing (the camera swinging back) and in as the camera moves on Play. Props fade: old pixels and placed devices shrink away / back with PixelFloor.propsK (ApplyProps, PropsFadeIn() called after a save load, PixelClicker.CollectOldPixelTransforms): on Quit > Main menu they go first, then the floor, then the camera; on arrival the floor comes first, then they grow back. Blur background OFF draws the live scene unblurred at full size (PixelTitleScreen.UpdateSharp, a full-resolution camera render in strongImage). While the textured floor is hidden / fading a flat floor-coloured copy of the floor mesh sits just under it (PixelFloor.UpdateFlatBase, AverageColor, queue 1990), so the menu background is the floor's colour and the textured floor fades in from it. Live background ON (PixelTitleWanderers.LiveBackground): the main menu and the camera intro show only the sky - no floor: PixelFloor.LateUpdate fades the floor with FloorVisibility (0..1 over fadeSeconds 1, hideFloorOnTitle): hidden while the title screen shows or the camera is moving (PixelCameraIntro.Moving), so after Play the camera swings as normal and the floor fades in once it has settled; the fade draws see-through copies of the floor's materials (PixelClicker.BuildTransparentMaterial, EnsureProxies / DropProxies, tiling copied by ApplyTiling), at 0 Renderer.forceRenderingOff hides it (the renderer stays enabled), and the old pixels on the floor are hidden below 0.6 (PixelClicker.SetOldPixelsHidden; pets / devices are not). Quit > Main menu: PixelTitleScreen.BeginReturn first calls PixelFloor.FadeOutThen(ReverseCamera) (the floor fades out), THEN PixelCameraIntro.PlayReverse zooms the camera back. Live background OFF: no camera intro at all (PixelCameraIntro.LateUpdate keeps the camera at its normal pose, PlayReverse returns false) - the title shows the blurred floor picture and Play just fades it into the default view; the floor is never hidden. The 'Welcome back' offline-progress window now waits for the title screen AND the camera intro (PixelOfflineProgress.Update checks PixelCameraIntro.Moving).
--- docs/systems/PixelAudio.md ---
PixelAudio

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelAudio.cs

Sound system. Alt-tab keeps the music track: OnApplicationFocus pauses the music source on focus loss and resumes it (same position) on return (musicHeldByFocus); UpdateMusic also remembers the playing clip / time every frame (musicLastClip / musicLastTime) and, when the source has stopped mid-track while the app is focused, resumes that clip at that time instead of picking a new random track (and never picks a track while !Application.isFocused). Named Sound list (id, clips[] picked at random, volume, pitch range, cooldown) filled in the Inspector - an empty/unknown sound is silent; music tracks (shuffle/loop); Master/Effects/Music volume + mute (saved in PlayerPrefs, sliders in the pause menu's Settings via PixelUIKit.CreateSlider); a voice pool; looping sounds via static PixelAudio.StartLoop(id)/StopLoop(id) (first clip of that sound, fades by loopFadeSeconds). Plays by itself from existing events (clicks click/auto_click/pixel_<type>, hit, vacuum, pixel_land (from OldPixelImpact -> static PixelClicker.OldPixelLanded(intensity); volume scales with impact speed), combo (pitch rises), purchase, craft, drink, device_place, achievement, <minigame id>_spawn/_click, ui_click from every PixelUIKit.CreateButton (the shop adds its own), ui_scroll from PixelScrollSound, a pixel type's own bounce pixel_bounce_<type> (e.g. pixel_bounce_electric: OldPixelImpact plays it, volume by impact speed, instead of pixel_land when that sound has clips; Electric's ships as a code-synthesized crackling zap, PixelSynth.Zap, used only while the sound's clip list is empty - assign your own clips to replace it) which is added to every ScrollRect the game builds); other code calls PixelAudio.Play("id"). PixelAudio.Stop("id") cuts off every voice still playing that sound (used by Time Stop / Time Slow when time resumes). The older per-script sound slots (purchaseSound, drinkSound, craftSound, caughtSound, hitSound) still exist. The pause menu adds this component if missing.

Track choice: MusicTrackCount / MusicTrackName(i) / CurrentMusicTrack / SelectMusicTrack(i) (starts the track now, shuffle / order carries on after it); used by the main menu music selector (PixelTitleMusic.cs).

Shuffle / playlist: the FIRST song after the game loads is always random (then shuffleMusic decides: random or in order). Settings > Audio > "Custom playlist" (shown with 2+ tracks) opens a checklist screen (PixelPauseMenu.BuildPlaylistPanel): only ticked songs play (IsTrackEnabled / SetTrackEnabled / EnableAllTracks, PlayerPrefs PixelAudio.PlaylistOff = unticked clip names, so new songs start ticked); the last ticked song cannot be unticked. The main menu selector can still start any song.

Per save (PixelSettingsSync): MusicMuted (the speaker button next to the main menu music selector + Settings > Audio "Mute music"), the custom playlist (PixelClicker.Setting.PlaylistOff) and the chosen song (PixelClicker.Setting.Song, set by SelectMusicTrack; on startup / save load that song plays instead of a random first pick). Named PixelClicker.Setting.* so a new game resets them. Old pixel vanish: OldPixelDespawn plays pixel_vanish (code-made PixelSynth.Bloop: a small high upward chirp, quiet, 0.04 s cooldown, pitch 0.88-1.3) when the shrink ends.

Playlist preview / speaker right click: each song row of the custom playlist has a small play button left of its tick box (PreviewTrack(i) plays ~8 s starting 30% into the song on its own AudioSource, the music goes quiet meanwhile, again = stop, closing the checklist stops it). Right clicking the main menu speaker opens Settings > Audio directly (PixelPauseMenu.OpenAudioSettingsFromTitle: Audio section unfolded and scrolled into view).

Music cues (PixelAudioCues.cs, partial class PixelAudio): the gameplay music changes at triggers and fades in / out - an Inspector list Music Cues (name, trigger Minigame / Skill / Night / FirstPerson / UfoRide / TitleScreen / Manual, param, priority, clip or playlist track number, volume, loop, fade in / out). The highest-priority true cue plays on its own AudioSource while the playlist fades down and is PAUSED where it was, then resumes when no cue is wanted. Built-in cues (Night, Ghost hunt, Bomb, Black hole, Evil tree, UFO ride, God find, Dragon wish) are added by name WITHOUT music - assign clips to the ones you want. PixelAudio.SetMusicCue(name, on) switches a Manual cue (the God Cube shows and the Dragon wish do). disableCues, addDefaultCues.
--- docs/systems/PixelBank.md ---
PixelBank

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Intended job (design, not built yet): the Bank collects husks for their EFFECT, never to cash in later. It is where you keep husks you cannot use yet (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician is unlocked) and spit them out when a machine or use wants them. Under the pixel rules (PixelRules.md) a stored husk carries no payout (today PixelBank.Entry.value keeps it and a spat-out pixel pays it again; that goes, with a save migration). Capacity stays one shared total across all types (IsFull = total >= capacity, already the case); the window keeps listing counts per type.

PixelBank.cs

Pixel Bank (shop pack with unlocksBank, Upgrades tab): a Bank tab docked to the middle of the left edge (opposite Toggles) opens a window listing stored pixels per type (click a row to select it); the Bank tab is half width (bankTabWidthFraction 0.5) and a square hose button with the drawn hose icon (DrawHoseIcon, same picture as the window's hose button) sits on its right, so it is the part that slides out first (PixelHud.DockInGroup(..., leftOffset, groupWidth): both buttons slide as one group; PixelDockedButton.PointerNear/SlideAmount); hovering either hose button shows a tip (hoseTipText, "click here or press {0}") and, like the B key, the button equips the hose. While the Bank window is open both tabs are tucked away and invisible and the window takes their place at the screen edge; when it closes they fade back in already tucked away (FadeTab, buttonFadeSeconds 1.2, same as the Toggles tab); a hand-drawn hose icon button at the top left of the window (BuildWindowHoseButton) switches the hose on / off (green while on). Hose = cubic-Bezier tube (PixelTube.Build) hanging from the ceiling to a nozzle above the mouse spot. While equipped (PixelBank.HoseOn, sets PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock so the cube ignores clicks; Pixel Grabbing and device removal step aside): mouse wheel cycles stocked pixel types, left-click spits one (a spat-out vacuum-tier pixel runs PixelClicker.Vacuum(tier, target, exclude) like clicking one, but the old pixels swirl into the SPAT pixel (it keeps flying, they chase it) instead of the cube; PixelClicker.SpawnStoredPixel: a real old pixel with its own tier look and its stored payout), hold right-click (areaHoldSeconds) = area suction: a ring (areaRadius) around the nozzle pulls in old pixels of the selected type at areaRate per second (nothing selected -> the type of the pixel nearest the nozzle; stops when the bank is full); a plain right-click sucks up the old pixel nearest the mouse (within suckRadius, any type except fly-away) into the bank (capacity; ReleaseOldPixel + a fly-in coroutine). The hose hangs from the CEILING and the nozzle always points straight down (rewritten: the flick / screen-edge entry point and the X shot arc were dropped as 'wonky'): the mouse ray's first hit (UpdateHose, ignoring the main cube) gives a spot, the nozzle hangs nozzleHeightCubes (0 = 2.5) main-pixel widths above it (lowered so it stays below the top of the screen), the hose is drawn UNLIT with vertex colours (HoseVertexColor: hoseColor x a hand-made light from RenderSettings.sun, alpha fading to 0 from hoseFadeStart (0 = 3) over hoseFadeLength (0 = 4) world units above the nozzle - PixelTube.Build's optional colorOf), so its top fades out instead of being clipped by the camera; the cubic hose runs from hoseOffscreenExtra (0 = 14) units above the nozzle straight down into the swivel ball at the nozzle's back (its top swayTop and middle control trail behind, followSharpness, so it swings). A thin red laser beam (FindBeam: a Raycast straight down from the mouth + a forgiving SphereCast of radius suckRadius / 2 that snaps the dot onto a pixel it brushes; UpdateAimLine, hideAimLine, aimLineAlpha 0 = 0.55, aimLineWidth 0 = 0.025) ends in a pulsing red dot (beamPoint); beamTarget = the old pixel it is on. Left-click spits the selected pixel straight down the beam (spitSpeed) onto the dot, right-click sucks the pixel the beam is on, hold right-click = area suction in a flat ring round the dot (areaRadius; no selection -> the type under the dot). The nozzle is a modelled assembly, scaled as a whole by nozzleScale (0 = 1.6; the hose end and the hang height use the scaled length) (BuildNozzle, PlaceNozzle: hose collar + orange ring, metal body, grip ring, narrowing front with a flared lip and dark opening, built along -Y from the mouth; +X = screen-up side): a glass cube (displaySize) on a mount on its upper side holds a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model of the selected type (real look), and a dark counter plate on the camera-facing side of the body carries the stored count as 3D text (counterText; a floating text only shows 'Bank full' / 'Bank empty'). The Bank Storage shop upgrade (leveled, UpgradeEffect.BankCapacity, requires the Pixel Bank pack; 5 levels -> capacity 100/160/250/400/600, paid in glass + vacuum pixels + bomb parts) calls PixelBank.SetCapacityOverride. A PackCost can now spend a minigame counter via minigameCurrency (a minigame id; PixelMinigame.TrySpendTracker; shop helpers CanAffordCost / SpendCost / CostName). Stored counts and total value per tier are saved (PixelBank.Entry, SaveData.bank). Sounds hose_toggle, bank_suck/_spit/_select/_full/_empty. PixelShop finds/adds it.
--- docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md ---
Special blocks and hold blocks (PixelBlockRules.cs)

One data-driven place for two kinds of skill block. A component (added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelBlockRules.Instance) holds two [Serializable] lists; built-in rules are added by name at start if missing (context menu "Add Missing Default Rules").

Special blocks - rare replacements
SpecialRule: tier (the block's PixelTier: name, colour, clicks, pay), skillId (empty = any skill in play), replaces (block types it can take the place of), chance.
PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier ends with PixelBlockRules.TryReplace(clicker, pick); the tier is created on first use by PixelClicker.EnsureSpecialTier (no spawn weight, unlocked so the cube shows it).
Default: Great Oak (Woodcutting, replaces Wood / Bark / Sap / Leaf / Heartwood / Twig, 1% rising to 25% as Acorn old pixels lie on the field (hasBoost, boostBlock, boostFullCount 12, maxChance), 50-100 clicks, pays 150; nests: PixelGreatOak.cs).
Not migrated yet: Fishing's Whale / Bottle / Pearl (PixelFishingSpawns, their chances feed the Pixel Guide's SpawnShare) - move them here when next touched.

Hold blocks - bonus while held
HoldRule: type, skillId, extraClicks, burstGapSeconds, payoutMultiplier, xpMultiplier, clearsHazard + hazardType, label. Held = Block Hold (right button on the cube, PixelBlockHold). While held:
- extraClicks: RAPID CLICKS like Overcharge - every manual click is followed by n extra full clicks, burstGapSeconds apart (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockRules.OnManualClick -> AutoCollect; the burst never starts another burst).
- payoutMultiplier: every payout (CollectInternal).
- xpMultiplier: that skill's XP (PixelSkills.AddXp).
- label: shown beside the hold meter (e.g. "Clicks x3").
- clearsHazard / hazardType: a manual left click on that hazard block clears it at once, safely (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> ClearHazardNow; no explosion, no XP penalty, no payout).
Defaults: Pesticide (Woodcutting base block, 3 hits, clears Termite Hives (enum HornetNest)) and Whetstone (Woodcutting base block, 3 hits, 2 extra rapid clicks = 3 at once).

Adding one
1. Append a PixelType, give it a look (PixelLooks default + a looksVersion migration) and spawn data.
2. Hold block: make it a normal base block of its skill pack (shop pack reward tier + a packDataVersion migration for saved packs + the skill's types list). Special block: nothing else (the tier is made by the rule).
3. Add ONE rule to the list. Behaviour beyond numbers stays in its own script (listen to PixelClicker.PixelHit / PixelCollected).

On the Pixel Guide
Special and hold blocks appear on the guide by themselves: PixelBlockRules.EnsureActiveSpecialTiers makes the tiers of the skill in play when the guide refreshes (so the Great Oak is listed from the start of Woodcutting), the row's sub line is tagged "SPECIAL, 5% replaces a block" / "HOLD block", and PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes adds the role sentences to the '!' tooltip (which blocks it replaces and the chance; what holding it does). Spawn shares include the replacements (PixelClicker.SpawnShare = AdjustShare(SpawnShareBase)): a special block shows chance x the share of what it replaces, and those blocks show a little less. Each new block still needs its own flavour case in PixelGuideTexts.Mechanics.

Stall (a trait on any block)
PixelTier.stallsHold (+ stallSeconds, 0 = 1.5): every click on such a block freezes the Block Hold meter's drain for that long and adds stallRefill (0 = 4%) of the meter (each click restarts it; PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockHold.Stall). The meter turns icy white-blue while frozen. Independent of hit count and of the special / hold roles. Tagged "STALL" on the Pixel Guide with its own sentence. Default: the Great Oak (so a held Whetstone / Pesticide keeps its bonus while you work through an oak).

Hazards that drain the hold meter
HazardHoldPenalty list: when a hazard GOES OFF (destroyed by clicking it, PixelClicker.TriggerHazard -> PixelBlockRules.OnHazardWentOff) the Block Hold meter loses loseFraction of its CURRENT charge (PixelBlockHold.LoseFraction; the meter flashes red briefly). Clearing the hazard safely (right clicks, a held Pesticide) does not count. Default: Hornet Nest, 50%.

Charge blocks (HoldRule.chargesWhenHeld)

A hold rule with chargesWhenHeld > 0 turns its block into a CHARGE block: while it is held (Block Hold) it shrinks to nothing over chargeSeconds and hands out its charges one at a time, then it shrinks to husk size and pops off as a husk worth nothing (PixelBlockHold.UpdateCharging / UseUp, PixelClicker.SpawnStoredPixel with amount 0; it does not go back in line). Letting go after the first charge also uses it up, so tapping cannot farm charges. Charges are stat counters charges.<Hazard> (saved with the stats): PixelBlockRules.Charges / AddCharges / UseCharge. When that hazard is on the cube for PixelClicker.chargeClearDelay (0.35 s), one charge clears it automatically and safely (PixelClicker.UpdateHazardCharges -> ClearHazardNow, stat charges.used.<Hazard>).
Default: Pesticide = 3 charges over 1 second against the Termite Hive (HornetNest); it no longer clears a hive with a click while held (its old clearsHazard rule is switched off at start). The tree bar shows the charge count on an infested tree.
--- docs/systems/PixelConsumables.md ---
PixelConsumables

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Attuned devices (PixelConsumables.AppendAttunedDevices, PixelCrafting.FindAttuneRecipe)

Crafting a Vacuum Device, Fan or Magnet with a plain (single-type) potion (ResultKind.Attuned, any box order, uses up one of each) makes an attuned copy that only affects old pixels of the potion's pixel type. For every potion type a craft-only Device clone (attuned, attuneType, name 'Attuned <device> (<Type>)', colour tinted by the pixel) is appended at runtime AFTER all normal devices (so indexes never shift; FindDeviceOfKind skips them; ItemAttuned, FindAttunedDevice); the saves keep only the ones you hold. PixelPlacedDevice.AttuneTier / Accepts(body) (set in SpawnDevice) is checked by the Vacuum / Fan / Magnet FixedUpdate. The crafting materials list always shows those three device kinds (when held).

PixelConsumables.cs

Potions (one per pixel type, editable list: name, type, costs, duration, owned) and placeable devices (Vacuum Device: right-click in inventory → cylinder follows mouse → left-click floor to place; right-click cancels). Unified Item* accessors (potions first, then devices) used by shop/inventory/save. Drinking one calls PixelClicker.SetForcedSpawnTier for its duration. Instant-use items (the Supplies category: IsSupplyItem, CategoryOf 0 potion / 1 device / 2 seed / 3 supply; a Supplies card in the shop's Consumables tab (suppliesCardTitle) and a 4th Inventory category) are devices of kind ComboFuel / GhostBait / PetTreat (not placed; Device.maxHeld = hold limit, 0 = unlimited, drives ItemCapacity / ItemRoom / ClampDeviceHeld and the shop's Full text; defaults auto-added to saved scenes like the fan): Combo Fuel (hold 3) is used automatically by PixelCombo when the timer bar would run out (TryUseAutoItem): the combo is kept, the bar rebuilds over fuelRefillSeconds in fuelBarColor, a click ends the refill, otherwise it then runs down normally. Ghost Bait (hold 1; right-click in the inventory = TryUseItem -> PixelGhostMinigame.UseBait, needs the minigame running): the next ghost comes within baitFirstDelay and baitGhosts (3) ghosts follow baitGap apart, each crossing baitSlowFactor times slower. Pet Treat (hold 5; right-click -> PixelPets.TryUseTreat, needs a pet out): for treatSeconds (60) pets are hyper (hop treatHyperFactor x as often, a bit harder; the Ghost pet drifts faster), glow (a point light + halo child "Treat Glow") and the extra spawn chance they give is multiplied by treatExtraFactor (2). Seeds are devices of kind Seed (Device.seedType, one per growable pixel type, auto-added after the other devices by EnsureDefaultPotions; not Meteor / Seed / Dragon Cubes; IsSeedItem / SeedTypeOf): price worked out in ItemCosts from the pixel's payout (seedPriceFactor 3 x amountPerClick x Value multiplier x (1 / spawn weight)^seedRarityPricePower 0.5, min seedMinPrice 3, paid in that same pixel, so low tiers are cheap and rare ones dear), hold limit seedMaxHeld 200; right-click in the Inventory (Consumables > Seeds category) = placement mode like devices (a sprout preview follows the mouse; each left click on the floor plants one via PixelClicker.PlantSeedSproutAt(point, tier) -> a SeedSprout that grows exactly that pixel type (SeedSprout.ForceTier), keeps planting while seeds remain, right-click / Escape stops; stat seeds.planted); excluded from the Tinkerer and the Gadgeteer achievement. PixelShop sells them (Consumables tab); PixelShop auto-adds this component if missing.
--- docs/systems/PixelCrafting.md ---
PixelCrafting

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelCrafting.cs

Crafting (shop pack with unlocksCrafting, Upgrades tab): bottom-right "Crafting" button opens a window laid out like the SHOP: the window takes the shop window's size (PixelShop.PanelSize, capped to the space between the black bars) and a Materials panel hangs off its left exactly where the shop's Currency panel is (BuildMaterialsPanel, width / gap = PixelShop.CurrencyPanelWidth/Gap): a scrolling list of one row per owned item formatted like the Currency rows (spinning cube - or a glass cube with a smaller pixel cube inside for potions - on the left, small name on top, bold amount underneath; drag a row into a box or click it). The window itself holds the two drop boxes, a recipe/cost readout and a Craft button in a Body that FitWindow centres a little in the free height. Drag (or click) grid items into the boxes; Recipe list in the Inspector (two ingredients, pixel currency or potions, any order -> a potion or device); defaults = each pixel + Glass -> that pixel's potion; plus automatic combo recipes: a potion + ANY pixel -> a 2-type combo potion, and a 2-type combo potion + one more pixel -> a 3-type combo potion (Vacuum and Dragon Cubes can't be part of a combo) (FindComboRecipe, ResultKind.Combo / Triple; PixelConsumables craft-only potions that spawn their 2-3 types evenly via PixelClicker.SetForcedSpawnTiers(a, b, c?); ALL pair and triple potions (~1100) are generated once at start by AppendComboPotions (flagged craftOnly, hasThird / thirdType, looked up by a bit mask comboLookup, FindComboPotion(a, b[, c])) so item indexes never shift; hidden from the shop; the save stores only the ones you hold, PotionSave.second/third, activePotionThird; the Inventory makes a potion's row only when first held, CreatePotionRow). Combo ingredients are Ingredient.combo/second/hasThird/third (runtime) and Item.hasThird/third. Easier crafting: clicking a row in the Materials list puts that item in the first free box (QuickPlace); clicking a KNOWN recipe in the Recipe Book fills both boxes with its ingredients (FillBoxes / Decompositions; auto-filled boxes are pinned so they stay even if you lack the item); with one box filled, every Materials row that makes nothing with it is greyed out and can't be clicked / dragged (Cell.disabled); Craft x1 / x5 / Max buttons above Craft (craftAmount, MaxCraftable, the Craft button reads 'Craft x N'; recipe stats are counted per canonical key recipe.c.<sorted types>); the Recipe Book lists every Recipes entry + every 2-type combo (??? until crafted) + only the 3-type combos you made, has a search box (known recipes only, bookSearch) and its known rows are clickable (fill the boxes). Right-click a box to empty it. The boxes are sunken frames with a glowing rim and a breathing '+' while empty (AnimateSlots), and the how-to text is gone: hovering a box shows slotTipText and hovering a material materialTipText in a small tooltip that follows the mouse (craftTip, UpdateTip; hidden while dragging). A hand-drawn book button (top-left of the window) toggles the Recipe Book (BuildBook): a panel to the RIGHT of the window, as tall as it and as wide as the Materials panel (like the shop tutorial's 'Got it' boxes), recipes as uniform two-line rows (BookLine: the result in bold, what it is made of dimmer underneath); Escape closes it first: AllRecipes() = the Recipes list + every automatic combo recipe; a recipe is shown once it was crafted (PixelStats counter RecipeKey(r) = recipe.<kind>.<potion>.<second>.<device>, set in Craft; owning the result also counts) and as ??? otherwise. Crafting level (placeholder, nothing uses it yet): every craft gives XP (XpPerCraft: potion xpPotion 5, 2-type combo xpCombo 12, 3-type xpTriple 25, device xpDevice 30, per craft made, plus xpDiscovery 20 the first time a recipe is crafted; 0 = default) into the stat counter crafting.xp (saved generically); CraftingLevel / LevelOf (level 2 needs xpFirstLevel 40, each next xpGrowth 1.35 x more); a level bar with 'Crafting level N - x / y XP' runs along the bottom of the window (BuildLevelBar, RefreshLevelBar), a level-up posts an event-log line, and every craft shows its own cyan '+n XP' that rises over the bar (ShowXpPopup, separate from the cursor / cube +n popups). Spends via PixelClicker.TrySpend / PixelConsumables.TryRemoveItem, adds via AddItem. PixelShop finds/adds it.

Crafting update (current). Every pixel except the Dragon Cubes has a single potion now (pixel + Glass; IsStandalone types too: ores, skill blocks, Fire...). Combo potions (2 / 3 types) still use only the non-standalone pixels (AppendComboPotions, FindComboRecipe: standalone pixels would make tens of thousands of combos and their enum values are past the 64-bit Bit mask). The Recipe Book is EMPTY at the start: RecipeKnown is only true once the recipe was crafted (stat RecipeKey), no more ??? rows and owning the result no longer counts. The Materials panel has a search box (materialSearch, filters by name, ignores folded categories while searching) and foldable category headers Pixels / Potions / Devices (materialsCollapsed, MaterialHeader pool, RefreshList lays headers and the pooled cells out by y).
--- docs/systems/PixelDevTools.md ---
PixelDevTools

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelDevTools Changelog tab

The Changelog moved from the pause menu to the Dev Tools panel as a third tab (BuildChangelogView / RebuildChangelog, changelogResource 'Changelog', newest first); the right half of the tab row is shared by the To-Do and Changelog buttons. The pause menu's Changelog button / screen are no longer built (the old fields stay unused).

PixelDevTools.cs

Dev tools (Editor/dev builds only unless allowInFullBuild is ticked on the component; removes itself otherwise): a glowing "divine" slide-out Dev Tools button docked at the bottom-centre of the screen (BuildDockButton, PixelDevButtonFx: pulsing golden halo, floating halo ring, sparkles; PixelHud.Dock now supports anchor x = 0.5 = centred; no longer in the pause menu) opens a panel (registered in PixelWindows, Escape closes it): pixel dropdown + amount field + Add, add-to-all (both also give the same number of Ultra pixels via GiveAmount, alsoAddUltra), minigame dropdown + Spawn (PixelMinigame.SpawnNow/DisplayName), "Skip intro" (PixelShop.DevSkipIntro: unlocks Gray + Black, buys the RGB pack and Auto Clicker free), Unlock all shop items (PixelShop.DevUnlockAll), tick box for Q = PixelClicker.ClearOldPixels, Close is an X button at the top right of the panel (no bottom Close button). Layout (current): pixel drop-down | amount field | green plus button (Add) | yellow paw button (Give pet: the selected pixel's pet, or every pet quietly for All pixels; there is no separate give-all button) - both drawn in code by DevIcons (PlusSprite/PawSprite/FileSprite); 'Skip intro', 'Give next Dragon Cube', 'Unlock all shop items', 'Reset all counts to 0'; a file-icon button at the TOP LEFT of the panel opens the crash report folder; the god mode row reads 'God Pixel Mode' with its controls as a hover tooltip (AddTip / PixelDevTip, PixelHoverTip). The panel uses PixelUIKit.StyleWindow like every window. Input row "Clicks per click" (clickCount, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.ClickCount, synced per save): sets static PixelClicker.DevClickCount N - one click counts as N clicks everywhere: payout x N (on top of any multiplier), tough pixels take N hits per click (extra hits carry over), timesCollected += breaks, and the combo grows by N (PixelCombo.AddClick). tick box "Infinite resources" (PixelClicker.InfiniteResources: TrySpend/CanAfford/Ultra boosts cost nothing, and consumables are unlimited too: PixelConsumables.ItemOwned reports 99999 of every item, using / placing / planting / crafting never decrements and the shop never says Full; the Pixel Bank too: it never fills up (TakeIntoBank skips the capacity check, window shows 'Stored: N / Infinite'), spitting never lowers the stored counts and area suction runs 10x faster; the hose can spit every unlocked ordinary pixel type even if none is stored - PixelBank.Has - paying RollOldPixelAmount, and the nozzle plate shows an infinity sign). Dev tool settings (tick boxes, amount, drop-down choices) are remembered in PlayerPrefs (PixelClicker.Dev.*, rememberSettings). The pixel drop-down's first entry is "All pixels" (replaces the old add-to-all button). "Skip intro" / "Unlock all" leave everything with a Toggles switch (auto clicker, grabbing, time stop, combo, minigames, spawn switches) unlocked but OFF. Tick box "Disable old pixel despawns" (OldPixelDespawn.DevNoDespawn). Tick box "Disable old pixel limit" (disableOldPixelLimit, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.NoLimit, PixelClicker.DevNoOldPixelLimit: CopyCap returns 0 = no cap on how many old pixels lie around, the Old Pixel Capacity upgrade is ignored). Tick box "God pixel mode" (PixelClicker.GodMode blocks cube clicks / grabbing / device removal): small display next to the cursor, scroll = pick a pixel, left = SpawnStoredPixel at the cursor, right = destroy old pixels near it. Tick box "On-screen spawn selector" (spawnSelectorEnabled; also holds the minigame drop-down + Spawn button): a drop-down row at the top of the screen (with the minigame drop-down + Spawn + Despawn buttons; Despawn = PixelMinigame.DespawnNow) (normal / Monochrome pack = White+Gray+Black / RGB pack = Red+Green+Blue / every pixel type incl. locked ones) that calls PixelClicker.SetDevSpawnTiers so only those types spawn. Middle-click swap: a third drop-down at the end of the on-screen selector row ('Middle click: <pixel>', every type incl. locked, swapDropdown, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.SelSwap); with the selector on, a middle click (PixelInput.MiddlePressed, not over UI) calls PixelClicker.DevSwapCurrentPixel(type) which replaces the pixel on the cube at once (devSwapped keeps even a locked type until it is clicked; the next pixel rolls normally). Uses PixelUIKit.CreateDropdown/CreateInputField. Escape closes the panel first. The on-screen spawn selector fades out after selectorHideSeconds without use and the Dev Tools dock button after the longer dockHideSeconds (PixelFadeOnIdle: a CanvasGroup that returns when the mouse is over the row / near the dock spot, a drop-down is open or the panel is open; faded UI can't be clicked). The window is capped at panelHeightLimit (760): the pixel dropdown row, the tabs and the Close button stay fixed and the other rows scroll (a drop-down can't live inside a scroll list). To-Do tab (Tools | To-Do buttons at the top of the panel; BuildTodoView, RebuildTodo, todoResource = Assets/Resources/Todo.txt): a scrolling list of that file's lines ([ ] open, [x] done, # Heading, // hidden note). Button "Reset all counts to 0" (second click confirms; PixelClicker.ResetAllCounts + PixelBank.SetState(null)): zeroes every pixel count, Log lifetime total and click count and the Bank, keeps unlocks, Ultra pixels, shop packs and achievements.

Keys and the dock button (current). The Dev Tools window opens / closes with PixelAction.DevTools (default F9, rebindable in Settings > Key bindings; the three dev keys are hidden outside the Editor / development builds). The glowing dock button and its tick box were REMOVED (the Dev Hub window made them obsolete; so were PixelDevButtonFx / PixelFadeOnIdle and the "Skip intro" button / PixelShop.DevSkipIntro). The old "Unlock All" button is gone: the Unlock widget's 'All shop items' does PixelShop.DevUnlockAll (every shop item and ordinary pixel type, switchable things left off) and the Max XP widget fills skills (see "Unlock and Max XP widgets"). PixelAction.MarkMoment (F10) and PixelAction.InspectHover (F11) call PixelDevProbe.MarkMoment() / Inspect() at once (screenshot + state report; what is under the mouse). Tools buttons 'Snapshot bounds', 'Mark this moment' and 'UI layout dump' wait boundsSnapshotDelay seconds first (so the panel can be closed). Reports land in <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots and on the clipboard (PixelDevProbe.Save). Mock up mode (one tick box) = PixelDevMockWindow: one draggable window with a Shapes tab (PixelDevShapes) and a UI pieces tab (PixelDevMockup) and a minimize button that leaves a thin header; replaces the two separate 'Shape placer' / 'UI mockup builder' tick boxes (old ShapesOn / MockupOn prefs are ignored).

PixelDevInspector.cs

Tools tab tick box 'Inspector' opens a draggable, minimizable window (header notes in PixelDevInspector.cs): World tab (time scale, fixed step, gravity / Gravity Y, solver iterations, thresholds, live counts, a drop-down of every game system) and Selected tab (Pick mode = click any collider, Parent / Active / Destroy, filter, foldable components; game scripts list every supported field by reflection, Unity components a fixed list; old pixels also show their PixelTier). 'Copy edits' copies every change as Owner.field = value lines.

Dev Tools window (PixelWindowChrome)

The Dev Tools panel is no longer a modal dialog: no dimmer, so the game stays clickable. PixelWindowChrome (header notes in the script) gives it drag by the title (kept between the black bars), Minimize ('-'), and docking to the left / right edge (drag against a side; slides out on hover or while typing; undock arrow). Prefs PixelClicker.Dev.Window.*. My Window, the Mock up window and the Inspector still have their own copies of this logic.

Dev Hub (PixelDevHub.cs) - replaces the Dev Tools panel, the Inspector window and the Mock up window

One window for every dev tool: drag, minimize, dock to a screen side (PixelWindowChrome), filing-cabinet tabs (Main, To-Do, Changelog, Inspector, Shapes, UI Mock) that can be reordered by dragging and popped out into floating windows (drag a tab down; 'Dock' button or dropping the window on the hub puts it back). The Main tab is PixelDevTools' rows as WIDGETS: every row is a card with a grip (PixelWidgetBoard, order saved as PixelClicker.Dev.WidgetOrder). The 'Inspector' and 'Mock up mode' tick boxes are gone (they are tabs now). Tools get their content with PixelDevHub.TabRoot(id) and PixelDevHub.SetTabCallback(id, visible => ...); PixelDevMockWindow is only a doorway to the hub's tab roots. The Inspector applies an edited value while you type and again on Enter, reads it back and reports the result in its status line (so a value the game overwrites is visible).

Hub tabs: icons, Docs, Notes

Hub tabs are small: each shows only a pixel-art icon, the active tab also shows its name (PixelDevHub.LayoutTabs, icons drawn from text in TabIcon). Tabs now: Main, Docs, Inspector, Shapes, UI Mock. Docs (PixelDevDocs.cs) has three permanent sub-tabs: To-Do, Changelog and Notes (your own notes typed in-game: list on the left, title + multi-line text on the right, auto-saved to <persistentDataPath>/DevNotes.json, Delete confirms with a second click). PixelInputCaret draws the text cursor in the Inspector / Notes fields.

Spawn widgets (replaced the on-screen spawn selector)

The 'On-screen spawn selector' tick box and the row at the top of the screen are gone. The Main tab now has a PINNED widget board above the scrolling one (its own saved order, PixelClicker.Dev.PinnedOrder; pinned because a drop-down's list would be clipped inside a scroll view): Add pixels (the old pixel drop-down | amount | Add | Give pet row), Spawned pixel (drop-down: normal / Monochrome / RGB / every pixel; Spawn = only that pixel spawns (SetDevSpawnTiers) and a single exact pixel replaces the one on the cube now, the button is green while active; Despawn = back to normal), Minigames (drop-down, Spawn = SpawnNow, Despawn = DespawnNow of the chosen one or every busy one) and Middle click (drop-down, first entry 'off': a middle click swaps the pixel on the cube, DevSwapCurrentPixel). Built by PixelDevTools.BuildSpawnWidgets; saved: Selector (override active), SelPixel, SelMinigame, SelSwap.

Feature Tester tab + Wood Chipper

The hub's Tester tab (PixelFeatureTester.cs) lists things that are not in the game yet as cards with Place / Clear. Place = a see-through ghost follows the mouse over the floor, left click puts a real one down (keeps placing), Q / E turn it, right click / Esc stops; placed test objects are not saved. Add a feature = one Register(...) line in PixelFeatureTester.Awake. First feature: Wood Chipper (PixelWoodChipper.cs): a detailed machine built from primitives (trailer, tyres, hopper, rollers, chute, engine, controls, beacon...) that pulls old pixels in and shreds them (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no payout) with chips, shaking, blinking beacon and exhaust smoke.

Wood Chipper rules: a wood pixel (PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock) gives Woodcutting XP (woodXp, 3, via PixelSkills.AddXp with the usual popup); any other pixel does damagePerOtherPixel (14) of maxHealth (100): the paint darkens, sparks fly, the exhaust gets thicker and blacker, a health bar floats over it; at 0 it is broken for good (stops, no beacon, 'BROKEN' label) and has to be cleared and replaced.

Hub resize, widget headers
- The Dev Hub window has a grip in its bottom-right corner (PixelWindowChrome.EnableResize): dragging it SCALES the whole hub (0.55x - 1.6x, remembered as PixelClicker.Dev.Hub.Window.Scale). It is a scale, not a re-layout: the tabs keep the layout they were built with. A real re-layout would need every tab to build against a variable size.
- Every widget card (PixelWidgetBoard) has a header strip with its title and a '-' button: it folds the card up to just the header (hidden controls are restored on '+'); remembered per board (<board prefKey>.Min). The pinned cards at the top push the scrolling list down / up as they fold.
- Button labels inside widgets shrink to fit (PixelUIKit.FitOneLine), so long labels like DESPAWN no longer spill out of their button.

One widget board, favorites
- The Main tab is ONE scrolling PixelWidgetBoard (saved order key Dev.WidgetOrder2): the old fixed "pinned" top half is gone. Drag a card by its whole header strip (title bar, easy to hit); the small grip icon is part of it.
- HOLD the right button on a card (0.7 s, a ring fills round the cursor, PixelWidgetBoardDriver -> PixelHold) to make it a FAVORITE: it snaps to the top, is tinted gold, and stays above the non-favorites (Partition); hold again to let it go back to the top of the others. Saved as <board key>.Fav.
- Drop-downs inside the scrolling board: PixelUIKit.LetListEscapeMasks moves an opened list to the root canvas so the scroll view's mask cannot clip it (untested in Unity: if a list still looks cut off, this is the place).

Search bar, custom text, docked drag
- The Dev Hub header has a SEARCH field (PixelDevHub.BuildSearch / RegisterSearch(label, tabId, reveal, keywords)): it finds tabs, Main-tab widgets (PixelWidgetBoard.SearchTab registers every card) and tester features; picking a result (or Enter for the first) opens its tab, unfolds / scrolls to the card (PixelUIKit.ScrollTo) and flashes it. Other tabs (Docs, Inspector...) are not searched inside.
- RIGHT CLICK any label in the hub (a quick click; a hold is the widget favorite) to type your own text (PixelDevTextEdit: Save / Reset / Cancel). Labels are found by hierarchy path; edits live in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.CustomText and are stored per save profile by PixelSettingsSync. Drop-down captions, typed text and search results are left alone.
- Docked windows (PixelWindowChrome, My Window) can be dragged off the edge by their header: the drag undocks them (PixelPopoutDrag.onBegin).
- A 'Collapse all' button above the Main tab's widgets folds every card up to its header (it reads 'Expand all' while everything is folded; PixelWidgetBoard.SetAllMinimized).

Unlock and Max XP widgets

Main tab widget Unlock (shopunlock): a drop-down with 'All shop items' and then every shop pack (marked [not in shop] for hideInShop packs and (unlocked) / (level n) for owned ones), an Unlock button (free, as if bought; upgrades go to their top level) and a Relock button (takes the pack back, locks its reward pixels, upgrades to level 0). 'All shop items' + Unlock = PixelShop.DevUnlockAll (every pack AND every ordinary pixel type, switchable things left off; it no longer touches skills); + Relock = every pack relocked. The old separate Unlock everything button is gone. Main tab widget Max XP (maxxp): a drop-down with 'All skills' and then every skill with its level, a Max XP button (that skill / every skill, Standard included, to its level cap: PixelSkills.DevSetSkillXp(pack, true)) and Reset XP (back to level 1). Texts shopUnlockText, shopRelockText, shopAllText, maxXpText, resetXpText, allSkillsText; the shop pick is remembered (SelShopPack).

Super Rares widget

Main tab widget Super Rares (superrares) replaced the three separate 'Give next Dragon Cube', 'Give next God Cube' and 'Play next God show' widgets: a drop-down of every rare collectible tier in the game's order (the seven Dragon Cubes, the three God Cubes, the six Prime Cubes, the Prime Cubelette, the Golden Snitch; (held) marks ones you have), a Spawn button that puts the picked one on the clicker cube in place of the current block (PixelClicker.DevSwapCurrentPixel; click it to collect it like a real find, the God / Prime shows play then; the Golden Snitch waits for your click and flies) and a Gauntlet button that adds one Prime Gauntlet (DeviceKind.Gauntlet) to the inventory. A second row holds the "Farmer always has dragon seeds" tick box (moved here from its own widget; PixelFarmerMinigame.DevAlwaysDragonSeeds). Widget cards can now have several rows (Widget(..., rows), WidgetRowGap). Texts spawnRareText, giveGauntletText; the pick is remembered (SelRare). Every remembered Dev Tools choice is kept PER SAVE: the keys are listed once in PixelDevTools.IntPrefs / StringPrefs and declared with PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave; a new remembered choice goes in that list.

Dev reports widget

Main tab widget Dev reports (devreports, 3 rows of two buttons) replaced the six separate report widgets: Open crash report folder (greyed out when crash reports are off) | Snapshot bounds; Mark this moment | UI layout dump; Balance report | Session recorder (Start, then Stop & save with the time recorded on the button; PixelSessionRecorder). Old widget ids report, bounds, moment, uidump, balance, session are gone.
--- docs/systems/PixelDragonWish.md ---
PixelDragonWish

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelDragonWish.cs

Dragon Cubes + the wish. Seven rare-drop tiers (PixelType.DragonCube1..7, PixelTier.rareDrop, helpers PixelClicker.IsDragonCube/DragonCubeStars; created by PixelClicker.EnsureDragonCubes, switch disableDragonCubes, weight dragonCubeSpawnWeight (0 = default 0.0001 each, ~1 in 70,000 blocks each)) spawn with no unlock - since 2026-10-16 every rare drop rolls ON ITS OWN before the normal pick at a FIXED chance per block = weight / rareReferenceWeight (7), so the odds no longer depend on how many pixel types are unlocked (PixelClicker.TryPickRare; before, with only White unlocked, a Dragon Cube was ~1 in 1,400) (CanSpawn / GetClickTierIndex honour rareDrop), pay 1 of their own currency, show in the Inventory / currency panel only once held, and are kept out of the unlock chain, Value upgrades, potions, crafting (recipes, crafting grid, combos), pets and achievements (IsDragonCube / rareDrop filters). Look (PixelLook.starCubes 1-7 + starColor, PixelLooks.StarCubesMesh): glassy orange translucent cube with a thin outline and 1-7 dark orange small cubes inside laid out like dragon-ball stars (unlit vertex-coloured mesh via OverlayMaterial, so they show through the body; looks added by looksVersion 10). First find of each: event-log line + tip dragon_first; holding all seven (PixelClicker.HasAllDragonCubes) shows a pulsing "Summon the Dragon" button under the top bar. Summon (SummonRoutine, all unscaled time): uses up the seven cubes, Time.timeScale 0, sky dims, seven block-drawn cubes rise into a ring, orbit faster with lightning flickers, spiral together in a white flash, then a follow-the-leader cube dragon (BuildDragon / AnimateDragon: body segments sit where the head was lag ago; head with mane, horns, eye, whiskers) bursts out; it offers three random wishes (PickWishes): Fortune (fortuneSeconds of current income at once), Golden Hour (PixelClicker.WishMultiplier x3 payouts for 10 min), Ultra Blessing (5 Ultra on random unlocked types), Potion Stock (3 random potions), A New Friend (an unowned pet via PixelPets.Award(type, false)); the first of the three wishes is always 'Dragon Friend' (GiveDragonPet: a Dragon Cube pet - DragonCube1..7 are valid pet types - you don't own yet, offered until all seven are owned) and the other two are random. Escape does nothing meanwhile (registered window). Sound dragon_summon (code-synthesized PixelSynth.Summon while its clip list is empty); stats dragon.summons/wishes. Added by PixelClicker.Awake.
--- docs/systems/PixelEggMinigame.md ---
PixelEggMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelEggMinigame.cs / PixelBeanMinigame.cs

Two very rare cat visitors (ids egg, bean; the FIRST visit ever (FirstVisitNow, counter <id>.met, TracksMeeting) gives the special gift below, later visits bring a random present of pixels (GivePixelGift: giftTypes 3 random unlocked types, each worth giftClicksMin..Max (30-120) normal clicks of that pixel); started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors at 4 / 6 unlocked pixel types, first slot 10-25 min later; PixelVisitorMinigame got the hooks IsGift (accepting runs OnGift() after the optional second speech box instead of opening a wares window), TracksMeeting (the first real visit sets <id>.met; dev Spawn doesn't count), VisitChance (rolled once each time the visit timer runs out; a failed roll waits for the next timer) and CanVisit, plus AcceptLabel / RejectLabel). Egg = an orange cat; gives an egg (PixelEgg: a white cube with rounded corners built by PixelEgg.RoundedCubeMesh, stands in for the cube (it takes the cube's place and size, the cube shrinks away via SetCubeShrink so the auto clicker pauses, and returns when the egg hatches), clicked via a screen-distance test, PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks; 1-50 clicks rolled when given (clicksMin/Max), thin dark crack slivers appear as it takes hits; the last click starts the hatching show (PixelEggHatch.cs, component added by PixelClicker.Awake, disableShow; the egg hides and stays until it ends): a dark canvas (691) over the screen, the egg and pet drawn by a hidden studio camera into a full-canvas RenderTexture (1 studio unit = 100 canvas units; egg = a shell + a detachable lid of rounded cubes with glowing crack slivers): zoom to the middle, shake harder (shakeSeconds) with chips and cracks lighting up, a flash as the lid flies off, the pet peeks out of the shell, hops out and jumps about with sparkles (jumpSeconds), then hops into the backpack icon like a Dragon Cube; Escape skips (PixelWindows 165). The pet is PixelPets.Awarded when it ends (FinishHatch, quietly - the show replaces the found popup; if the show can't play the old shell burst + popup are used) (PetCandidates: not rare drops / standalone); state in counters egg.pending/need/clicks, restored after a load). Bean = a grey tabby cat; gives Magic Beans (a seed-kind device with Device.beans, craft-only, hold 1, PixelConsumables.BeansItem(); planted like seeds from the Inventory's Seeds category: PlantSeed -> PixelBeanstalk.Plant, needs a Dragon Cube you don't hold, PickMissingDragonCube): PixelBeanstalk grows a twisting stalk over 7 s with leaves and 5-7 random pixel cubes hanging on stems, one of them the Dragon Cube; click a cube to pick it (ordinary = a paid old pixel at 3x, Dragon Cube = AddCurrency + PixelDragonFind.Play, PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks); left alone it withers after 15 min and still hands over the Dragon Cube; the pending cube is kept in bean.pending (tier + 1) and the stalk regrows after a load.
--- docs/systems/PixelFactory.md ---
Machines in the Tester: conveyor belt and kiln

Both are Dev Hub > Tester features (PixelFeatureTester), built from primitives in code with PixelFactoryKit (shared materials / boxes / cylinders / ghost versions). Not saved.

Conveyor belt (PixelConveyor.cs)
- Place: click a point, click another: a belt between them; the second point starts the next piece (short pieces = a curve); right click ends the chain, right click again (or Esc) stops. Clicks near a belt end snap to it (PixelConveyor.Snap). Two-point features are RegisterLine in the tester (marker ghost, then a preview piece).
- Right click a placed belt (not while placing): reverse it (PixelConveyorSystem.Update).
- PixelConveyorSystem.FixedUpdate LOCKS every old pixel that lands on a belt (not held, not kinematic, not glued into a build, not flying, room for it) onto the belt and carries it along by position at beltSpeedCubes (0 = 1.6 cubes/s), no physics (like the Storage Crate). At the end it is handed to a belt that starts there, otherwise (an end is detected) it is ejected with physics back on, flying on in the belt direction. Riders keep a gap (queue when blocked). Flipping a belt flips its riders; grabbing one frees it.

Kiln (PixelKiln.cs)
- Anything inside the mouth zone (in front of the +X door) burns: ClaimOldPixel, drawn in while it shrinks, then charcoalPerPixel Charcoal old pixels (PixelType.Charcoal 81, EnsureCharcoalTier, standalone, look migration looksVersion 58) leave the back chute after charcoalDelaySeconds, one per charcoalGapSeconds. Fire light, embers, smoke (thicker while it burns).
- A belt can lead into the mouth; another can carry the charcoal away.

Wood Chipper discharge arrow and belt latch
While placing, the ghost chipper shows a flat arrow from its chute outwards (PixelWoodChipper.BuildArrow; the chips shoot out along it). If the opening (start) of a belt lies in the arrow's area, PixelWoodChipper.TrySnapToBelt (the tester's placeSnap hook) turns the chipper so the arrow runs the way the belt runs and puts the chute over the belt's opening; the arrow turns green. A placed chipper drops its Wood Chips blocks straight onto a belt opening in that area (BeltAtOutlet).

Belt speed (first person)
Right click a belt in first person: a small window (PixelConveyorSystem speed editor) with a number box (cube widths per second, 0 stops it), Set, Reverse and Close; Esc closes it; the mouse is freed while it is open (PixelFirstPerson playable). In the normal view a right click still reverses the belt.

Belt ends feed machines (IPixelBeltAcceptor)
At a belt end with no belt after it, PixelConveyorSystem asks the registered acceptors (IPixelBeltAcceptor: AcceptsAt(world) / AcceptFromBelt(rb); machines call PixelConveyorSystem.Register) whether one has its opening there. The Kiln (its mouth zone, roomier) burns the pixel; the Storage Crate (over its footprint, a free section needed) stores it. Otherwise the pixel is ejected as before.

Lumberjack: Buy / Sell tabs, Wood Chipper device, potions
PixelVisitorMinigame got BuySellTabs (a vendor that sells wares AND buys pixels: Buy tab = wares, Sell tab = trades). The Lumberjack sells the Wood Chipper (DeviceKind.WoodChipper 15, Device.vendorOnly = not listed in the shop or sold by the Tinkerer; costs wood + bark; placed like the Fan with the turn input, its chute arrow latches onto a belt opening; thin PixelChipperDevice : PixelPlacedDevice on the machine PixelWoodChipper.Place builds, so it is saved / restored / removed like any device) and plain potions of the Lumberjack pack's pixels; and buys those pixels (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig, Acorn, Whetstone, Pesticide - not the Great Oak) for pure currency.

Solar Panel + saving the tester machines
- PixelSolarPanel.cs (Dev Hub tester feature): a flat panel powered while Solar pixels lie on it; it calls HoldPower() on its ONE linked device (blue line). Solar pixels on it are slowly used up unless they form a complete group of 5 (the existing Solar cluster keep-alive). After placing, click a device inside the blue ring to link; right click a placed panel to re-link (PixelFeatureTester.BeginLink).
- All tester machines save with the save file: SaveData.testerObjects = PixelFeatureTester.TesterSave[] (feature name + JSON from each feature's ExportState/ImportState). Imported after the placed devices (panels re-link to the nearest restored device by position). Belt riders are saved with their belt (PixelConveyorSystem.ExportRiders/ImportRider) and skipped by the normal loose old-pixel save.

Heights, ramps and the Conveyor Elevator
- Belt ends are 3D points (y = underside). Scroll while placing a belt raises / lowers the point (posts appear), so belts can slope; Snap finds belt ends / elevator tops by screen distance. Riders move by ground distance (Length), with PointAt / RunSpeed / LiftFactor handling the slope.
- PixelElevator (tester feature): magnetizes to a belt end, click for the base, mouse height, click to confirm. Entry on its -Z face (an IPixelBeltAcceptor), exit on the +Z face at OutputPoint, where a new belt can start. Uses PixelPathCarrier.

Conveyor Connector
PixelConnector.cs (tester feature, two clicks, one piece): see its header comment. Machines with an opening implement IPixelPort (Kiln, Storage Crate) so the connector can magnetize to them; PixelConveyorSystem.FindAcceptorAt / ForEachPort expose the registered acceptors.

Corner joints
PixelConveyorJoint.cs: belts chained at an angle get a corner filler (slab + outer rails) on the outside of the turn, built automatically by each belt every 0.5 s (see the file header).
--- docs/systems/PixelFirePixel.md ---
PixelFirePixel

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelFirePixel.cs / PixelProspector.cs

Fire pixel + Prospector's Pack (PixelType.Fire 25, ores Copper..Nickel 26-31; PixelClicker.IsOre / IsStandalone = no integration: these types get no potions, seeds, crafting recipes / combos, pets, achievements, Value upgrades, Entrepreneur trades or guide gifts - the filters are at each of those sites; they still spawn, pay, show in the Inventory / Log / currency panel). Fire (testing pixel, pack 'Fire Pixel': 5 White, no requirements; look = glowing orange + PixelLook.flames (PixelFlames.Attach: a stream of flame squares rising off the cube) + burn; tier glow): its click calls FireBurstFx.Play (a horizontal ring of flame squares flying outwards from the cube, sparks, an expanding PixelFxRing shock ring and a light flash, sound fire_burst); its old pixel gets OldPixelBurn (falls normally while it slowly shrinks away over fireBurnSeconds (0 = 6) shedding embers and crackling (fire_crackle), doesn't age, then is released with no payout); disableFireFx. Prospector's Pack (10 White, no requirements): six ore tiers, tough (clicksToCollect 2-5) with PixelLook.oreTexture (PixelLooks.OreTexture: 64 px pixel-art grey rock with veins / flecks of oreColor metal, dark cracks spreading per hit via damageCracks); every non-final hit shows OreFx.Hit (rock chips + metal flecks, sound ore_chip), the break OreFx.Break (rocks, nuggets, dust; the ore's own pixel_<ore> click sound is a synthesized break); rich vein: a fresh ore is a rich vein with richVeinChance (0 = 8%) - it glitters (OreFx.Glitter) and pays richVeinMultiplier (0 = 5) x with a gold burst, shock ring and ore_gleam (first one per session is logged); ore old pixels clink when they land (pixel_bounce_<ore>); disableProspectorFx. Prospector mode (chosen as the Mining skill in the Skills window, no longer a Toggles row; PixelProspector.Setting, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.Prospector, per save via PixelSettingsSync; shown once an ore is unlocked): the ores are NOT in the random spawns unless the mode is on (PixelProspector.LeftOut(type, prospecting) in PickSpawnTier: mode off = ores never spawn; mode on = White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue, Glass and Luminescent (IsBase) are left out and the ores spawn instead), PixelClicker.RefreshSpawnTier() re-rolls at once. All sounds are code-synthesized while their clip list is empty (PixelAudio.SynthFor: PixelSynth.FireBurst/FireCrackle/OreChip/OreBreak/OreGleam/OreClink). Shared particle helper PixelFx.Make(parent, name, Spec) (little squares, Sprites/Default).
--- docs/systems/PixelFirstPerson.md ---
PixelFirstPerson

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelFirstPerson.cs

First person mode (added by PixelClicker.Awake; rebindable key PixelAction.FirstPerson, default F; disableFirstPerson): shrinks the player to heightFraction (0 = 1.5, three times the old 0.5; walking and jumping scale with it via SizeScale) of an old pixel's edge and drops a CharacterController player on the floor in front of the cube (WASD / arrows, Shift run runFactor, Space jump jumpHeight, mouse look lookSensitivity, walkSpeed in old-pixel widths per second; unscaled time so it works during Time Stop; PixelFirstPersonPush nudges old pixels you run into: each contact only brings the pixel up towards pushSpeed (0 = 3 old-pixel widths per second, a fraction of the gap per contact), along your direction, no lift, spin damped, never a shove while you stand on it - nothing flies). Carrying: right-click a cube under the crosshair (FindPickup: nearest old pixel within carryReach (0 = 3.5 player heights) and a few degrees of the view) picks it up (PickUp: kinematic, colliders off, OldPixelDespawn.Held, gravity script off), up to maxCarried (0 = 3) stacked just in front of the chest (PlaceCarried, follows the view); right-click with nothing aimed at drops the top one (DropTop), left-click (not on the main cube) throws it (ThrowTop, throwSpeed 0 = 7 widths/s); leaving first person drops them (DropAll). PixelFirstPerson.TryGetPlayerPosition gives the player's spot (the floor edge fade centres on it). The normal camera stays where it is but its culling mask is set to 0 (so PixelViewBounds, fog, floor fitting and Camera.main users are unaffected) and a second camera (CopyFrom of it, near clip 6% of the player's height) at the eyes draws instead. Left-click while the crosshair is on the cube, at any distance (AimingAtCube: angle to the cube vs its apparent radius + aimAssistDegrees 3, occlusion ignored), calls PixelClicker.ManualClickFromAfar() (same rules as a mouse click: paused / intro lock / minClickInterval). PixelFirstPerson.Active makes PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock read true (cube clicks, grabbing, device removal step aside), suppresses Time Slow's S key and the hose key, and a registered window (priority 120) makes Escape leave it; it can't start (or ends) during the title screen, pause, popups, visits, takeover minigames or while the hose is on. A crosshair (green when aiming at the cube) + a hint line are the HUD; cursor locked while playing and freed while paused. Windows are closed on entering (the cursor is locked, so UI can't be used inside).

Update: the F key is retired. First person is now the 'First person' tick box of the View window (PixelViewWindow, key V): PixelFirstPerson.TryEnter() / Leave(), Escape still leaves, and while the View window is open the mouse is free (the player doesn't look / move). First person is always the 3D view: PixelOrthoView forces perspective while it is active (ForcePerspectiveNow on entering).

Build controls (attaching cubes) - replaces the old right-click carrying

LEFT click now grabs: aiming at a free old pixel (FindPickup(false), within reach) picks it up, otherwise a left click aimed at the clicker still clicks it. You hold ONE cube straight ahead along the view (PlaceCarried). Mouse wheel moves it closer / farther (holdDistance, wheelStep), holding the right button and moving the mouse turns the cube instead of the view (Look -> heldRotation, turnSensitivity). When one of its faces is near another cube's face (PixelAttachment.TrySnap: within attachAngle of a grid-aligned orientation and attachDistance of a spot on the face, sliding along it in half-cube steps) a white pulsing ghost cube shows where it would stick. Left click then attaches it there (AttachHeld: a FixedJoint to the other cube, PixelAttachment.Attach, lifetime frozen so builds don't decay); with no ghost, left click sets the cube down. Attached cubes can't be picked up: hold the right button on one (pryHoldSeconds, a ring fills round the crosshair, PixelHold) to pry it loose (PixelAttachment.Detach: all its joints and the ones holding on to it). Throwing and the old stack of three are gone (maxCarried / throwSpeed are unused). Joints are not saved. Details: header of PixelAttachment.cs.

Builds, foundations and prying (follow-up)
- Left click on a cube that is part of a build picks up the WHOLE build (PixelAttachment.Group; joints removed with TakeApart while carried, glued back on release). The wheel / right-hold turn work on the whole build; every carried cube has its own white ghost.
- A held cube or build can also snap flat onto the ground (PixelAttachment.TryGroundSnap, any of the 24 grid orientations relative to the world; the carried cube that must lift the most makes the contact). Clicking pins it with a world joint (AttachToWorld) so it stays as a foundation to build on.
- Prying (hold right on a cube of a build, ring on the crosshair via PixelHold's at argument) works again: FindPickup used to skip cubes whose lifetime is frozen, which every attached cube is.

Neon floor
In first person the normal floor (and its wireframe circle) is hidden (PixelFloor.FloorOff includes PixelFirstPerson.Active; the ground collider stays) and PixelFirstPersonGrid draws a neon grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader) that only shows within a radius of the player, the clicker cube and old pixels lying on the ground. Colour, spacing, radii and brightness are on PixelFirstPerson ("Neon floor").

Throwing
While holding a cube or build: a quick left tap (under 0.25 s) sticks it / sets it down as before; HOLD left to charge a throw - a ghost line (LineRenderer, Sprites/Default) draws the arc to where it lands, longer and orange as power builds (throwSpeed full power, throwChargeSeconds to charge) - and let go to throw along the view.

Hotbar, axe and the Great Oak
- PixelFirstPersonHotbar (added by Enter): 5 slots, wheel (when not carrying) or keys 1-5. Slot 1 = the AXE, drawn in hand (primitives on the camera) with a wind-up / strike / recover swing; the other slots are empty hands. With the axe, left click on a wood old pixel chops it (XP + 150% pay) and on the cube swings and clicks it (bonus XP, +50% pay when a wood block breaks); non-wood things are still picked up. Settings under "Axe" on PixelFirstPerson. Sounds: PixelAxeSynth (axe_swing, axe_chop, nest_fall, nest_catch).
- GREAT OAK: PixelType.GreatOak 77 (see PixelGreatOak.cs header): 5% replacement of a rolled wood block while Woodcutting is in play, 50-100 clicks, shakes bird nests loose that you click for XP before they vanish.

Whetstone
Lumberjack's Pack base block (PixelType.Whetstone 78, 3 clicks, weight 0.35, pack migration packDataVersion 27). Hold it with Block Hold (right button on the cube): while it is held every manual click is followed by 2 extra rapid clicks, like an Overcharge burst (a HoldRule in PixelBlockRules, see docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md). A partly broken block keeps its hits through the hold, so one you only noticed after a click can still be grabbed.
--- docs/systems/PixelFloor.md ---
PixelFloor

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

> LATER CHANGES: Spiral Galaxy and Black Hole were REMOVED (enum values Galaxy / BlackHole stay for saves; defaultsVersion 10). Radar has no circles any more (no range rings, no round dimming, no ping rings; the blips are spread over the whole screen). Liquid Flow (cells 12, detail 3), Circuit Flow (cells 12, thicker traces), Cyber Hex (cells 13) and Watching Eyes (cells 11) are zoomed in (bigger features).
