> UPDATE (big floors, 2026-10): the 8-bit floors (Platformer, Invaders, Brick Breaker, Pong, Snake, Block Stack, Dungeon, Crossing, Road Racer, Fighter) were REMOVED (their enum values stay so saves keep working; defaultsVersion 9 drops them from saved lists). Big non-repeating floors (PixelFloorLayers.cs): Marble, Lava, Deep Space, Ice, Matrix Rain, Radar, Battle Crater, the four Auras, Death Energy, Liquid Flow, Spiral Galaxy, Black Hole, Moon Surface, Red Planet, Circuit Flow, Cyber Hex, Haunted Tiles, Graveyard and Watching Eyes are now ONE huge picture (about 36-40 world units, 512-1024 px) instead of a small repeating tile: FloorStyle.detail multiplies the noise lattices so features keep their size (shadeDetail), static ones use stretchToFloor (one copy, no repeat), scrolling ones use a huge tile (Lava, Space, Matrix, auras, wisps, Liquid). Big textures are drawn a few ms per frame (FillSliced, UsesSlicedFill; a soft blurred version of the picture (FillPreview, sampled on a 96 x 96 grid) shows until the sharp one is done, PixelFloor.Drawing). Menu icons are built small (PreviewTexture clones the style at <= 160 px). Overlay styles (IsOverlayPattern: Radar, Circuit Flow, Watching Eyes): the still picture is drawn once (shadeBase), then OverlayFrame repaints only what moves (radar blips + pings, circuit sparks, blinking eyes) into a cached Color32 copy - far cheaper than redrawing the whole texture. Craters (ShadeCraterField, PrepareCraters): one big crater where the camera looks (SetCraterFocus), several medium ones (cells) and many pits, lit from the upper left; variant 0 battle (scorch + glowing cracks), 1 moon (rays), 2 red planet. Haunted Tiles flickers by dimming the material (UpdateHauntedFlicker). Spiral Galaxy / Black Hole are drawn once at 1024 px (ShadeGalaxyHQ, ShadeBlackHoleHQ) and only pulse. Laser Corridor is scaled up (tile 18). Rain (PixelSkyboxRain.cs) is now one big sheet in front of the camera so it covers the whole view. Animation cost: animated floors are never redrawn faster than animFpsCap (0 = 10 per second) and not at all while the floor is invisible (faded out on the menu / 'Display floor' off; unseen in Update). Anything below that still mentions 8-bit floors or the old small tiles is out of date.

PixelFloor.cs

Floor styles (added by PixelClicker.Awake): restyles the scene floor (floorRenderer, else the collider straight below the cube, else an object named Floor/Ground/Plane) with textures drawn in code at runtime - Inspector list of FloorStyle (name, FloorPattern, three colours, resolution, pixelated, tile size, smoothness, glow + pulse, scroll, cells, seed; defaults added by name via EnsureDefaultStyles): Classic (the floor's own material), Neon Grid, Retro Checker, Pixel Grass, Wood Planks, Cobblestone, Marble, Lava, Deep Space, Ice, Disco (tiles change colour every beatSeconds), Honeycomb, Bricks, Circuit Board, Sand Dunes, Ocean (scrolling caustics), Snow, Tartan, Tread Plate (uses metallic), Hologram, Crystal, Matrix Rain (scrolling code), Hazard Stripes, plus Dragon Ball themed floors (fan-inspired, all drawn in code): Tournament Stage, Radar (was Dragon Radar); Planet Namek, Battle Crater, Gravity Room, Time Chamber; Tournament of Power; the auras Super Aura / Divine Aura / Blue Aura / God Aura (renamed from the Super Saiyan / Ultra Instinct ones, grouped together) and Death Energy (was Destruction Energy); the Dragon Balls and Yu-Gi-Oh Card Back floors were removed (defaultsVersion 4 renames / removes / regroups saved lists and the saved pref name); Laser Corridor (LaserHall, animated: teal metal panels with lit seams and wall-light dashes, red security beams that sway and blink in both directions, white-hot where they cross); animated Neon variants right after Neon Grid: Neon Drift (slides + hue cycle), Neon Merge (every 2nd / 4th line fades so the squares grow bigger and back), Neon Wave (rippling lines), Neon Prism (a rainbow sweeping across) = NeonAnimated, 128 px, animateFps 20, cells divisible by 4 - new patterns TournamentTiles, DragonRadar, DragonBalls, KiAura (one pattern, colours per style), Craters, GravityRoom, PowerArena, EnergyWisps; Namek / Time Chamber reuse PixelGrass / Checker. Second batch (PixelFloorScenes.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from Shade via ShadeExtra; helpers Sstep = shader-style smoothstep (Mathf.SmoothStep is NOT that - it lerps), ScEll/ScBox/ScSeg/ScMix): BattleCrater (reworked Battle Crater), space Galaxy / BlackHole / Hyperspace (+ Moon Surface / Red Planet on Craters), tech DataStream / CircuitFlow / CyberHex / Glitch, trippy Psychedelic / HypnoSpiral / Plasma / OpArt, horror HauntedTiles / Graveyard / WatchingEyes (Bloody Boards and Noir Smoke were removed, defaultsVersion 6), scenes Skyline / GroundSky, mining MineTrack / OreVeins / Strata, farming CropRows / WheatField / Patchwork; Circuit Flow has two bright sparks that random-walk along the traces (PrepareSparks, worked out once per frame like the Fighter) (cells 1-4 picks meadow / desert / arctic / alien twin moons); the scene ones are fitToView and use shadeAspect; styles come from ExtraStyles(); defaultsVersion 5 renames Planet Namek / Time Chamber / Tournament of Power, removes Gravity Room (GravityRoom pattern code stays) and replaces the saved Battle Crater. Third batch (PixelFloorRetro.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeExtra's fall-through to ShadeRetro; styles from RetroStyles(); all animated, fit to view, square virtual pixels; the 8-bit ones use a virtual picture P rows tall, SpriteAt string-bitmap sprites, Tri triangle waves for deterministic motion): retrowave / cyberpunk = SynthSun ('Retro Sunset Grid'), NeonHighway, GridTunnel, RainAlley (tiles), VaporChecker, CityMap (tiles), LightCycles (tiles), ArcadeCarpet (tiles); 8-bit games = Platformer, Invaders, BrickBreaker, PongCourt, SnakeGrid (green LCD), BlockStack, Dungeon, Crossing (names '8-Bit ...'). Also Yu-Gi-Oh Card Back (CardBack, one big card over the whole floor: brown frame + gold line, spiral vortex, dark oval centre; no logos/text), Liquid Flow (animated warped waves), Moving Clouds (CloudSea, scrolls), Kaleidoscope (animated, one big mirrored pattern) and Pac-Man (animated: PacMaze 19x21 filling its tile (FloorStyle.tileAspect = 19/21 so cells are square), tunnels on all four sides and wrapped wall lookups so neighbouring copies join up seamlessly, Pac-Man runs a verified loop PacWaypoints, pellets vanish as he passes and return each lap, four ghosts follow). 8-Bit Road Racer (RoadRacer, animated + fit to view: square virtual pixels via static shadeAspect = the fitted view's width/height (viewAspect, menu previews use 1); scrolling 3-lane road with dashed lines, red/white kerbs, banded grass with trees, a red CarSprite weaving in the middle lane and four traffic cars in the side lanes being overtaken). 8-Bit Fighter (Fighter, animated + fit to view, same square-pixel trick: side-view stage with dithered sunset sky, striped sun, two mountain ranges and wooden planks; two fighters built from body-part segments (FighterPixel, poses Idle/Punch/Kick/Hit/Down/Victory, left one faces right) trade blows every FightBeat s; PrepareFighter replays the exchanges once per frame (deterministic from the seed: attacker, punch/kick, 25% blocked, 9-18 damage) for health bars that drain towards the outside, hit sparks, knock-back, a flashing K.O. (loser lies down, winner raises both arms) and a new round). FloorStyle.stretchToFloor = show ONE copy, Tile World Size across, centred on the floor (clamp wrap; used by the card back); every style's tiling is now centred on the floor's middle (baseOffset); saved style lists get one-off size fixes through defaultsVersion; FloorStyle.fitToView (Card Back, Moving Clouds, Kaleidoscope, Pac-Man) = one copy fitted to the camera's view of the floor between the black bars (FitToView: rays through the view's bottom-left / bottom-right / top-left onto the floor plane, WorldToFloorUv via an affine map from one mesh triangle (BuildUvMap; bounds fallback for unreadable meshes) -> tiling/offset; re-fitted whenever the camera moves, keeps the last fit while the view misses the floor; fitted pictures are clamped (one copy, never repeated over the floor model) and while the camera is away from its normal view the tiling is BLENDED from 'the picture spread once over the whole floor' (title close-up, PixelCameraIntro.Progress 0) to the fitted view (1) (FitBlend, ApplyTiling, TryWholeFloorTiling); animated floors redraw at most 8x a second while the camera swings; while the camera intro moves the camera the fit uses the pose it settles into (PixelCameraIntro.TryGetFinalPose + a hidden fitCam), so the floor doesn't swim / stretch during the zoom at start and when returning to the main menu); animateFps / animSpeed = redraw the texture N times a second (Fill(tex, style, time, reuse), static shadeTime; keep animated textures small, 128-256). New patterns are appended to the end of FloorPattern so saved enum values stay valid. Picked in the pause menu's Settings (Display section, "Floor style" row) or the small picker right of the title screen's Play button (PixelFloor.Instance.Step/SetStyle, PreviewTexture; implements IPixelLookSource). Animations use unscaled time (they move on the title screen) and freeze during Time Stop, saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FloorStyle.

Game-made floor (added later): PixelFloor.InitFloor (Start, then once a second until it works) builds Pixel Ground with BuildOwnFloor: a flat code-made quad (ownFloorSize 0 = 80 world units, UVs 0..1 like a Unity plane) with a thin BoxCollider whose top is groundFallDistance (0 = 10 world units) under the cube's resting spot - the old pixels' falling distance. The scene's floor object is found (FindFloor, which ignores dev shapes) only to be hidden, its collider switched off and its collider material copied (else friction 0.6); tick useSceneFloor to keep using it (and the game still builds its own when the scene has none). Every floor style works on the game-made floor exactly as on a scene floor. 'Display floor' defaults to OFF (only the sky shows, the collider still catches pixels) and PixelFloorModes.cs adds 'When floor is off' (Settings > Display, PixelFloor.OffMode): Invisible / Flat plane (an opaque lit sheet in the style's average colour, rimmed by the edge fade) / Landing pulse (PixelFloorPulse.Landed from OldPixelImpact: a rounded-square ripple on the surface the pixel hit, turned to the contact normal).

World-space ground shader (first slice): Assets/Resources/PixelWorldGround.shader (unlit CG, alpha, works in the built-in pipeline and URP; loaded with Resources.Load so builds include it) draws Lava / Marble / Ice / Deep Space from the WORLD position per pixel (no tiling, no stretching, sharp at any zoom / angle, _Time animation, faded round _Center between _FadeStart and _FadeEnd). A style opts in with FloorStyle.shaderGround (1 lava, 2 marble, 3 ice, 4 space; 0 = painted texture) and shaderScale (world units per pattern cell, 0 = 6); PixelFloor.ApplyShaderGround (PixelFloorModes.cs) builds the material (colours A/B/C, glow, seed) and falls back to the painted texture if the shader is missing; the menus' preview / title pictures stay CPU-painted, the edge-fade ring is off for shader grounds (the shader fades itself, UpdateGroundShader), ApplyVisibility just sets _Fade. The game-made ground is lifted until it is on screen (UpdateGroundHeight, every 0.5 s, normal view via PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera; allowGroundOffScreen turns it off): below the screen's bottom edge PixelViewBounds' bottom plane acts as a false floor and pixels never touch the real ground (no landing pulse, odd falling in the flat view). OldPixelImpact also fires the landing pulse for soft landings (>= 0.6 speed, own 0.08 s gap).

Edge fade update: FadeRadii (PixelFloorFade.cs) gives the fade's start / end radii to the ring, the wireframe AND the ground shader (one rim, smoother-step alpha). A painted one-copy picture (stretchToFloor) is faded out before its own edge (tileWorldSize * 0.5 * 0.94), so its clamped edge pixels never show smeared outwards. Shader grounds keep the digital wireframe rim (their own alpha does the fade, the opaque ring is hidden).

Fourth batch - ten big world floors (PixelFloorWorlds.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeRetro's fall-through to ShadeWorlds; styles from WorldStyles()): Lush Grass, Nebula Field, Still Pond, Graffiti Wall, Leaf Litter, Zen Garden, Persian Rug, Coral Reef, Casino Felt, Stained Glass. Template = the big still floors: 1024 px, stretchToFloor, tileWorldSize 40, drawn once (time-sliced fill), no animation. Unlike older big floors they draw in WORLD units (Wu(s) = tileWorldSize, Per(s, feature) = noise lattice period for a feature of that many world units, hash grids in world units), so features keep their size at any resolution and in the small menu previews. New enum values LushGrass ... StainedGlass are appended after Crossing; saved lists pick the new styles up by name (EnsureDefaultStyles). The Graffiti Wall's bubble-letter tag (PIXEL) is drawn from stroke segments (TagLetters, TagDistance): outline, fill, highlight, drop shadow, overspray, drips.

Moon Surface / Red Planet (PixelFloorPlanets.cs, patterns MoonSurface / MarsSurface, defaultsVersion 13 switches saved styles): their own pictures (Moon: highlands + dark basalt seas, terraced big crater with a central peak, rays, secondary crater chains, a rille, boulders with shadows, rover tracks; Mars: dunes with lee slopes, basalt streaks, dust-filled craters, cracked lakebed, dust devil trails). Both read the shared crater list. FloorStyle.tiltCorrect (Stained Glass, Casino Felt, Nebula Field) narrows a stretched picture by sin(base view pitch) (StyleAspect, PixelOrbitCamera.TryGetBaseTilt) so round things do not look squashed. Threaded drawing: ThreadSafePattern lists the patterns drawn on worker threads (see PixelFloorLayers.cs); a new pattern may join only if its Shade code reads state and never writes it.

Fifth batch - varied grass, see-through lasers, picnic (PixelFloorLive.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeWorlds' fall-through to ShadeLive; styles from LiveStyles()): Wild Meadow, Clover Lawn, Mossy Glade, Prairie Grass and Picnic Blanket are big still world-unit pictures (1024 px, stretchToFloor 40 units, thread-safe, cached) where every grass blade is its own tapered stroke (BladeLayer: a grid turned to the wind with cells lenMax long x laneW wide, a few blades per cell, random lean + gust wave, random overlap order), so nothing repeats or stretches. New FloorStyle fields: hideBase (the floor picture is hidden via HideBaseNow in ApplyVisibility and the edge fade; the collider stays) and overlay (FloorOverlay: None / Lasers / LaserTracker / Ant), handled by PixelFloorOverlay (added by ApplyLive, called from Apply): real 3D objects on the floor plane updated every frame - Neon Lasers (flat LineRenderer beams, core + glow, drifting / turning / pulsing in the style's three colours), Laser Tracker (emitters circling the cube, every beam ends at the mouse's point on the floor, smoothed, with a pulsing ring), and the Picnic Blanket's ant (primitives, no colliders, walks to the mouse point with a tripod gait and wiggling antennae, wanders when the mouse is off the floor). The overlay reads PixelFloor.TryGetLiveFrame (floor height, cube centre, fade radius, visibility) and fades with the floor; the mouse point uses the first person view ray when in first person.
--- docs/systems/PixelGodCubes.md ---
PixelGodCubes

The three Egyptian God Cubes (PixelGodFind.cs, PixelGodSynth.cs, parts of PixelClicker.cs)

Slifer the Sky Dragon, Ra the Winged Dragon, Obelisk the Tormentor (PixelType.GodSlifer 82 / GodRa 83 / GodObelisk 84, PixelClicker.IsGodCube, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes, switch disableGodCubes, weight godCubeSpawnWeight 0 = 0.000001 each, about 1 in 7 million pixels, 100x rarer than a Dragon Cube = the rarest thing in the game). Rare-drop tiers like the Dragon Cubes (rareDrop: spawn without unlocking, show in the inventory only once held, pay 1 of themselves) and kept out of potions, crafting, pets, achievements and the Value upgrades through PixelClicker.IsRareCube (Dragon or God). Only one of each can be held: PixelTier.CanSpawn is false while count >= 1 and AddCurrency ignores a second one (and caps a payout at 1).

Looks (PixelLooks, looksVersion 59): Slifer = a red glass cube held together by storm lightning (the existing lightning look, red / yellow bolts, gold outline); Ra = polished gold with the sun's corona (sunFx, no hover / trickle for it) and a white-gold outline; Obelisk = deep blue stone with a cyan outline that sheds dust (FarmAmbient.Crumbs).

Shows (PixelGodFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos) from CollectInternal, queued, canvas 691 blocks clicks, unscaled time, Escape skips to the backpack, disableShow, showSpeed, darkness, cubeSize; sounds god_slifer / god_ra / god_obelisk from PixelGodSynth, timed to the shows): Slifer = storm clouds + three lightning strikes + a lightning serpent that weaves round the screen and coils into the cube in a blast; Ra = a rising golden sphere bursts into a sun, golden wings (feather fans) beat in time with the sound and fold in with a shockwave; Obelisk = rising rumble and falling rubble, a giant blue fist lowers, cocks back and slams the screen (flash, crack lines, shockwaves, debris) and the cube rises from the crater. Then the cube hops into the backpack.

Tips: god_first on the first find of any. Dev Hub: 'Give next God Cube' (plays the show) and 'Play next God show (preview)'.

Prime Cubes, Gauntlet, Cubelette (PixelInfinity.cs)
- Six stones (PixelType.StoneSpace..StoneSoul 85-90) reuse the God Cube machinery completely: IsGodCube is true for them (and for InfinityCubelette 91), so the one-of-each clamp, CanSpawn, the backpack stash and EnsureGodCubes apply. IsInfinityStone picks them out. Looks: PixelLooks.StoneLook (looksVersion 60).
- Shows: PixelGodFind.DrawStone(stone, ...) (shared timeline, climax at 3.2 s, a different signature effect per stone) and DrawCubelette (six colour streams, hexagon, snap at 3.4 s). Sounds stone_* / stone_cubelette in PixelGodSynth.
- Gauntlet: DeviceKind.Gauntlet 17 (vendorOnly, hold 1, costs in the Device list). The Wizard offers it (PixelWizardMinigame.ModifyWares) when all six stones are held and there is no Cubelette / Gauntlet. Right-click it -> PixelInfinity.Open: place a stone on each face of the flat golden cube, Forge uses up the Gauntlet and the stones, adds the Cubelette and plays its show (PixelGodFind.PlayAtScreen).
- The Cubelette is a trophy for now.
--- docs/systems/PixelHints.md ---
PixelHints

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelHints.cs

First-time tips: Inspector list of Hint (id, enabled, text; defaults added by EnsureDefaultHints), shown once each via PixelNotice (remembered in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Hint.<id>, queued one after another, showDelay, master hintsEnabled). PixelHints.Trigger(id); hooks events itself (potion drunk, device placed device_<kind>, crafted, first combo, minigame spawned minigame_<id>, PixelClicker.UltraGained); PixelShop.TryBuy triggers crafting/grab/timestop/bank, upgrade_interval/clicks/combo/bank, upgrade_ultra (Ultra boost) and pixel_<Type> for packs that unlock pixel types. The event_log intro tip is NOT queued as a box: Enqueue only announces it as a log line (ShortFor text) whose click opens the tip (ShowEventLogTip on tutorial replay does the same). Also owns the event log: a transparent box at the bottom left running from above the bottom black bar up to the top black bar (overlayWidth), newest line at the bottom (PixelHints.Announce(short, fullText)); lines in the upper part fade out towards the top (fadeUpperLines, fadeStartFraction 0.5, fadeTopAlpha; per-row CanvasGroup set in UpdateRowFade, called from ApplyScroll, so it follows scrolling); a new event shows only the newest line (fullView false: RebuildRows builds one row) and the whole list only when the player opens the log (the EventLog key: DisplayOverlay(true); pressing it while only the newest line shows opens the full list, pressing it again hides it); the whole box fades after overlayVisibleSeconds unless the mouse is over it; mouse wheel over it scrolls; left-click a line to show its full tip, Enter shows the box when hidden and fades it away when open, right-click anywhere also fades it away (logWidth, logFontSize); the log (historyMax) is saved with the save file (SaveData.eventTexts/eventFulls, ExportHistory/ImportHistory) and SuppressFor mutes events while a save loads; each Hint has a shortText; also announces new pixel types via onTierUnlocked, the auto clicker and the first black hole; ignored for startupQuietSeconds after start). Every shop purchase / upgrade level / Ultra boost (not Value upgrades: too frequent) also announces itself on the event line (PixelShop.AnnouncePurchase; first-time tips replace it with their own line). The device_Sorter tip is a small box that freezes time until 'Got it' (freezeTime). Extra bank/Time Stop tips: bank_hose/_suck/_spit/_full, timestop_first/_empty. Also runs the new-game intro (after the title screen; marked seen only once both tips were confirmed): once, on a fresh game, opens the Log with a tip, then (after "Got it") the Inventory with a tip, then a third tip on how currency is earned (introEarnText: clicking is the main way, upgrades / minigames / old pixel management open other ways); the tier guide appears after that (showIntro, introLogText, introInventoryText, introEarnText; uses PixelNotice.Show(..., onClosed)). Added by PixelClicker.Awake.

Intro tip 4 (right click): the new-game intro now has a fourth Got-it box right after the one about clicking (introRightClickText): the right mouse button (hold a block, Block Hold + its stake). Showing it marks the stake_intro tip as seen; saves that already finished the intro get stake_intro once after 25 manual clicks instead (PixelBlockHold).

Intro fixes (2026-10-16): the cube cannot be clicked while the new-game intro is pending or running (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock includes PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide), so the tier guide and clicking start after the last box; a game counts as new while fewer than introFreshClicks (60) pixels were collected, so a few early clicks never skip the intro; tip 4 (right click) only shows when Block Hold is unlocked (Standard tree) - otherwise stake_intro stays unseen and shows later via PixelBlockHold.

New game decided at Play (2026-10-16): PixelTitleScreen.NewGameStarted is set in OnPlay (true when nothing was loaded). The intro plays and clicks stay blocked from Play until its last box whenever it is true, whatever the player clicked or which tips were seen in earlier games; the old 'nothing collected' check is only the fallback when there is no title screen.
--- docs/systems/PixelLog.md ---
PixelLog

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelLog.cs

Bottom-left "Log" toggle with two tabs (the old Pixels / Clicks tab was removed; per-pixel click counts are now in Settings > Stats, 'Clicks per pixel', with PixelStats.RecentClicks +N indicators; the code for the old tab is still there but unreachable) - formerly three tabs (the window has ONE fixed size, FixedPanelHeight, on every tab; each tab's list scrolls inside it): Pixels (unlocked tiers with how many times each was clicked (PixelTier.timesCollected, multipliers don't count; overall total; +1 per click indicators; no vacuum deltas)) and Achievements (scrolling list, one badge-style row per achievement (BuildAchievementRow: a panel per achievement with a gap between rows, the pixel picture in a framed box, big title, wide progress bar with its '60 / 100' in the middle; a search box (achievementSearchField, searchPlaceholder) sits between the total bar and the first heading, same width: it filters by title / description / section and shows matches expanded; hovering a row shows a tooltip (UpdateAchievementTip / AchievementTipText) saying explicitly whether it counts CLICKING (clicks only) or COLLECTING (everything earned), with progress and tier; the old click-to-switch grouping is gone, BuildAchievementGroups makes one group per achievement)) and Goals (RefreshGoalsTab: progress bars - the next pixel unlock, a 'Purchase the RGB Pack' goal once the Shop button appears until the pack is bought (PixelShop.ShopAvailable / TryGetBuyGoal(Red, ...): bar = price lines saved up, each capped at its own price; buyRgbGoalFormat/Description), then a 'Purchase the Combo Meter' goal once the RGB Pack is bought (PixelShop.TryGetComboGoal, bar = first-level price saved up) and, once the Combo Meter is bought (ComboMeterBought), a 'Purchase the Auto Clicker' goal (TryGetAutoClickerGoal; both share TryGetPackGoal), plus every PixelMinigame tracker that is running or has progress, e.g. Ghosts Caught, Singularity, Stardust, Meteor Chunks; goalsTabText, goalRowHeight; goal rows are badge-style like the achievements (BuildGoalRow: framed live 3D picture of the goal's item via SkinIcon (Goal.skinTier, GoalSkinTier(minigameId): ghost -> Ghost, blackhole -> Singularity, meteor -> Meteor, stardust -> Solar, next unlock -> that pixel, buy-RGB -> Red; no item = a spinning gold cube), title, wide bar with the count in the middle; the list is as wide as the tab row, the scroll bar sits in the margin; hovering shows the explanation + exact numbers through the shared tooltip, GoalTipText / hoverGoal); tab content groups are built by the shared BuildScrollGroup). Achievement icons show each pixel's real look (SkinIcon: rendered once per type with CreateDisplayPixel in a hidden studio into a cached RenderTexture; PixelAchievements.GetSkinTier; pet achievements too; other achievements keep the spinning PixelCubeIcon); the 'click to switch' mode tag is a single non-wrapping line. Has its own local FormatAmount.
--- docs/systems/PixelLooks.md ---
PixelLooks

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelLooks.cs

Per-pixel-type 3D look, separate from the tier data (the UI keeps the tier colour): PixelLook list on PixelClicker (looks, useLooks; defaults from PixelLooks.CreateDefaults()): colour override + alpha, the basic pixels (White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue) have a subtle drawn surface (PixelLook.basicSurface: Grain / Brushed / Sheen / Mosaic via PixelLooks.BasicTexture, near-white 128 px textures that multiply the colour) (Obsidian and Seed have the same bevel rim, shade taken from their tier colour via outlineShadeOfTier: Obsidian -0.55 (dark, looksVersion 24), Seed -0.4; looksVersion 20) and a thin rim (PixelLook.outlineBevel: a flat rim on every face along its four borders, mitred at the corners, solid at the cube's edge and fading inwards (AddBevelRims; no overlapping boxes, nothing sticks out) - no neon halo / white core - EdgeMesh(..., bevel)) that is a different SHADE of the pixel's own colour (PixelLook.outlineShade: above 0 lightens towards white, below 0 darkens towards black; White -0.3, Gray / Red / Green / Blue +0.4, Black +0.3; thickness ~0.027, strength ~0.5); Black is drawn in a slightly lighter near-black so the sheen shows; looksVersion 21 gives them a material feel (White matte 0.35, Gray brushed metal 0.65 / 0.55, Black glossy 0.85, RGB glossy 0.6 + faint emission 0.12; revertible as one commit) ; looksVersion 17 adds / updates them in saved lists (18 switches the rim to the shade, 19 to the flat bevel) (a Red/Green/Blue look you already edited is left alone), metallic/smoothness, plain emission, neon edge outline (no pixel type uses it by default now; runtime-built mesh of 24 thin boxes with vertex colours, Sprites/Default material), surface texture (streakTexture white streaks on black, damageCracks: cracks that spread with every hit on a tough pixel, drawn by PixelLooks.SurfaceTexture and set per hit via PixelClicker.SetLiveDamage; used by Obsidian; the live cube's property block is cleared in ApplyTierLook), jelly wobble (live cube scale in AnimatePixel; falling copies draw on a child Wobble Visual), old-pixel physics per look (customPhysics: bounce/friction/gravityMultiplier; ignoreViewBounds read by PixelViewBounds via PixelClicker.IgnoresViewBounds; floatAway -> OldPixelFloat: after N ground bounces it stops colliding, drifts up/sideways and is removed off-screen; used by Ghost; a floating ghost is kept above the floor in OldPixelFloat.FixedUpdate so a gravity well can't drag it through the ground), gravity well (gravityWell, wellRadius, wellPullSpeed, wellGrip, wellRingOpacity: OldPixelGravityWell drags other old pixels toward it at a constant speed (overrides floor friction; works while carried with Pixel Grabbing) while it is at full size, plus a very faint camera-facing ring sprite; used by Singularity, whose spawn weight is 0.08; each well holds at most wellMaxPixels (20) pixels at a time: it keeps the nearest unclaimed old pixels in its radius (a pixel is claimed by one well only, OldPixelGravityWell.claims), other Singularity pixels inside the radius count towards the 20 so wells packed close together starve each other and have to be spaced out; the well pixel itself lasts wellLifetime = 300 s (5 min, set in the well's Start via OldPixelDespawn.SetLifetime) and every old pixel inside its pull radius is kept alive by OldPixelDespawn.KeepAlive() each physics step, so it doesn't age while the well works; a flying meteor pixel within wellFlyReach (4) pull radii is steered towards the well (turn rate 5 rad/s, slowed) and, once inside the ring, captured: PixelClicker.LandFlyingPixel + its speed cut to 20%, then it is dragged in like the others), face circles (flat black discs on the 6 faces, used by Vacuum; faceRing + faceRingColor draw each as a vortex: black centre, a glowing violet ring that fades outward (PixelLooks.VortexMesh), plus a violet bevel rim and faint emission, looksVersion 22), dark-matter core (counter-spinning cube with a runtime swirl texture), lightning (Electric: PixelLookLightning builds a dynamic mesh of jagged crossed-ribbon bolts along the 12 edges + a few arcs through the inside, re-rolled ~12x/s on unscaled time, a wide soft glow pass + a thin bright core pass, simpler when more than 6 exist; unlit vertex colours via OverlayMaterial; lightningCoreColor/GlowColor/Jitter), forceTranslucent, and shatter (glass: OldPixelShatter breaks the old pixel into OldPixelShards when it hits the ground; the pixel is removed without payout; sound glass_shatter). Live-cube extras are managed by PixelClicker.UpdateLiveExtras; old pixel copies get them in SpawnFallingCopy.
--- docs/systems/PixelMeteorSolar.md ---
PixelMeteorSolar

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelMeteorSolar.cs

Meteor and Solar rework. Meteor (PixelLook.meteorTexture + damageCracks + cometTail; tier now clicksToCollect 3, pays 45, glow off, packDataVersion 12 migrates saved packs / tiers, looksVersion 35 replaces the saved looks): a dark cratered rock (PixelStarTextures.Meteor(level, max), 64 px, lava cracks that spread / brighten per hit, dull embers at level 0), a few rising embers on the cube (PixelCometFx.Attach), sparks + chips on every non-final hit (PixelCometFx.Hit, disableMeteorFx) and a fiery world-space wake on the old pixel that streaks away (PixelCometFx.AttachWake, added in the fly branch of SpawnFallingCopy); it still launches in a straight tapered trail and FlyAwayLaunched still knocks stars down. Solar (PixelLook.sunTexture + sunFx + custom physics with gravity 0): churning gold with a white-hot face centre (PixelStarTextures.Sun), a turning corona + rays billboard behind the cube (PixelSunFx / PixelSunRays, swells with daylight; also on its old pixels), and the old pixel HOVERS (OldPixelSolarHover: spring to ~1.7 old-pixel widths above the floor with a bob, drifts to a stop) and pays a trickle (TrickleFraction 0.08 of its value every TrickleSeconds 4 via AddCurrency, xPayMultiplier). Daylight (PixelDaylight, added by PixelClicker.Awake, not saved): every Solar click (OnSolarClick, in CollectInternal) adds perClick (0.1), it fades decayPerSecond (0.012); at level L seeds / Seed pets grow faster (GrowthBonus = L x seedGrowthBonus 1.5, added in SolarSeedBoostAt) and hover pay x (1 + L x hoverPayBonus 2); a small sun + bar at the top right (canvas 105) shows while L > 0 and Solar is unlocked (hideBar, disableDaylight). Old Solar pixels last solarLifetimeFactor (0 = 1.6) times as long as other old pixels (LifetimeFactor, in the two normal AddDespawn calls of SpawnFallingCopy); old Solar clusters (5 touching never despawn) show a faint yellow aura: UpdateSolarClusters calls OldPixelSolarAura.Keep() on every member of a complete group and each draws a soft camera-facing glow that fades in / out (overlapping glows read as one aura). Not built: Stardust raising daylight, Water drying faster in daylight.

PixelNotice

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelNotice.cs

PixelNotice.Show(message, seconds, onClosed, compact, small, aboveCombo, nearLog) (nearLog = small box at the bottom left beside the event log, used by the event_log tip shown at the first tier unlock; aboveCombo = a small box at the bottom just above the combo meter, used for the first-combo tip) / Hide() (small = a narrow small-text box, used for the first-time minigame tips minigame_<id> and the first black hole): a one-off tip box (message + "Got it") at the top of the screen under the black bar; built lazily, one at a time; Escape closes it like Got it and drops the queued tips (PixelWindows priority 140, PixelHints.ClearQueue). Used for first-time hints (first black hole, PixelHints); PixelNotice.IsShowing. Typewriter + back: untimed boxes type their text out (TypeCharsPerSecond 80); the first Got it press completes the text, the second closes (OnOk). Optional onBack parameter of Show adds a small back arrow at the top left (hold 0.6 s via PixelHoldButton): it closes the box WITHOUT running onClosed and calls onBack (the shop and skills tutorials pass the previous step). Cubie's pages and the visitor greeting / aside boxes type the same way (visitors: StartType / TickType / FinishType), Cubie's panel and the aside box have a hold-to-go-back arrow (Cubie: one page back, the gift is only handed over once; aside: back to the greeting). nearScreen (a Func<Vector2?> screen point the small box follows, beside it) + freezeTime (Time.timeScale 0 until the box is closed, restored after): used by the first Ghost Hunt tip, which PixelHints shows next to the ghost (PixelGhostMinigame.GhostScreenPosition) with no auto-close. With PixelShop.tipBoxMatchesShop (default on) the big purchase / unlock tip boxes sit to the right of the open shop window (PixelNotice.SideAnchor/SideWidth/SideGap, set by PixelShop.BuildPanel: as tall as the shop, as wide as the currency panel, mirroring it on the left); with the shop closed they fall back to a centred shop-sized box. beside (a window's RectTransform, e.g. PixelLog.WindowRect / PixelUI.WindowRect) puts a 640-wide box just to the right of that window, top- or bottom-aligned (besideBottom); the new-game intro tips use it.

Skip button
PixelNotice.Show(..., onSkip): a small Skip button at the box's top right (hold 0.9 s; like the back arrow). It closes the box without running onClosed and calls onSkip. The Shop, Skills and Toggles tutorials pass an onSkip that jumps to their own end step (marks only that tutorial seen), so skipping one never skips another.
--- docs/systems/PixelOrthoView.md ---
PixelOrthoView

PixelOrthoView.cs

Flat view (added by PixelClicker.Awake; Settings > Display > 'Flat view' = PixelOrthoView.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FlatView, off by default, synced per save in PixelSettingsSync): switches the main camera to orthographic so there is no depth shrinking. The size is depth x tan(fov/2) x sizeFactor at the cube's distance (CubeBasePosition), recomputed every frame while flat, so the cube keeps its on-screen size and the zoom key / orbit camera (which move the camera) still zoom. Blended in and out over blendSeconds (0 = 0.6) by mixing the perspective and orthographic projection matrices (PixelOrthoView.Blend, 0..1). It stays perspective while the title screen shows, the camera intro moves or the camera swings back to the main menu (those lerp the field of view); the blend starts when that ends. [DefaultExecutionOrder(200)] so it runs after PixelZoom / PixelOrbitCamera, which also write the camera in LateUpdate. A camera that is already orthographic in the scene is left alone.

Things that assume a perspective camera and were adapted: PixelViewBounds.EdgePlane (orthographic rays are parallel, so each edge plane is built from the ray origins) and its plane cache (ortho flag / size), PixelFloor.FitToView cache (ortho size), and PixelUIKit.ViewHeightAt(cam, depth) (use it instead of computing the view height from fieldOfView; the Bomb and Ghost minigames and KeepOnScreen use it). Not adapted: the other minigames' depth placement (they still work: positions come from ViewportToWorldPoint), the horizon / fog look (there is no vanishing horizon).
--- docs/systems/PixelOvercharge.md ---
PixelOvercharge

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelOvercharge.cs / PixelElectricLinks.cs / PixelBolts.cs

Electric pixel abilities (both components added by PixelClicker.Awake). Overcharge (PixelOvercharge): a click that harvests an Electric pixel (manual, or the auto clicker's own with triggerOnAutoClicks) gives the auto clicker a split-second burst (burstClicks 8 clicker.AutoCollect() calls over burstSeconds 0.6; needs the Auto Clicker bought - toggled off still counts, needsAutoClicker; the burst's own clicks never start another burst) while PixelOverchargeRing draws jagged bolts circling the cube on tilted orbits (world space, follows the cube, fades) and a flashing, shaking, popping "OVERCHARGE!" rises above it (UI canvas 640; sound overcharge, a code-made PixelSynth.Charge sweep while its clip list is empty; stat overcharge.bursts). Device links (PixelElectricLinks): every scan (linkRange = 3 main-pixel widths, only pixels in the camera view) the Electric old pixels on the floor are linked into a network growing out from the placed devices (PixelPlacedDevice.All): from each device DAISY CHAINS run pixel to pixel (Attach / NearestUnlinked: every pixel links to the nearest unlinked one in reach, then that one to the next; new chains start from the device while pixels remain in reach; pixels keep their old partner via stickiness, maxLinks); Electric pixels out of reach of any device still daisy-chain to each other (a chain grows from both ends), Battery model: pixels linked to a timed device POWER it (PixelPlacedDevice.HoldPower / Powered: its countdown pauses, the timer text shows POWERED; uses-mode devices just get arcs). Every pixel that arcs to another (to a device or only to each other, Register) gets pixelCharge (100) charge and never ages while linked (a free chain's first pixel is tracked in chainSeeds); only device groups drain. The group of a device is the pool and DrainAndHold drains drainPerSecond (25) from ONE pixel at a time, the one FURTHEST along its daisy chain from the device (ChainDepth, ties -> older), so chains burn from the far end and the pixel touching the machine goes last; it flickers / sparks below fizzleFraction and Dissipates (OldPixelDespawn.Begin) at 0; new pixels add to the pool without interrupting; linked pixels never age (KeepAlive) and stay when the device ends. At most maxLinkedTotal (30) pixels are linked in TOTAL across all groups (3 + 10 = 13 used): EvictOldest despawns the oldest beyond that (out with the old, new ones are safe until they are the oldest). The old +1 s per pixel / +10 s chain life / vanish-with-device rules are gone. the Electric pet is a rechargeable battery bank (PixelPets.TryGetActivePetBody: owned, switched on, shown, not shattered; its Rigidbody joins the daisy-chain network as one more node, never despawned, not counted in the 30-cap, never eaten by a Charge Booster): while it is part of a device's group the group drains the pet's own charge (petCapacity 600, petCharge, same drainPerSecond) BEFORE any pixel, and once it is empty the pixels drain as usual; it recharges petRechargePerSecond (2 = about 5 minutes) only while it powers nothing, and a tiny camera-facing meter floats over it (showPetMeter, DrawPetMeter; the charge is not saved, it starts full); the pet stands still while it is linked (PixelElectricLinks.PetConnected); The arcs are one dynamic mesh redrawn every redrawInterval. PixelBolts = shared mesh builder for jagged crossed-ribbon bolts (Jagged, Strokes; glow + core passes; vertex colours for PixelLooks.OverlayMaterial).
--- docs/systems/PixelPadMinigame.md ---
PixelPadMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPadMinigame.cs

Ultra Holes (the reworked Ultra Pad; id pad, pack unlocksMinigame = "pad", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; NOT a takeover minigame): every minInterval..maxInterval s (and AllowAnother) FOUR round holes open in the floor, one near each corner of the screen (CornerSpot: the viewport corner cornerInset inside the black bars, nudged inwards until the whole hole is on screen and AreaFree; at least two are needed; one ClaimArea per hole via claimOwners), each a dark rim + black void + deeper black disc (BuildHole, radius holeRadiusPixels 1.3 main-pixel widths). They stay open roundSeconds (45); a timer line shows at the top of the screen (hudFormat). One hole is the correct one, marked by a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model of the wanted pixel type (random unlocked, floor-lying type) floating in its centre (AnimateModels; the other holes show nothing). An old pixel whose middle comes inside captureFraction (0.85) of a hole's radius falls in (TakePixels: ReleaseOldPixel + OldPixelDespawn.Begin, which makes it a trigger so it tumbles down through the floor while shrinking): the WANTED type in the CORRECT hole pays its normal value (ReleaseOldPixel(body, true)) and has ultraChance (10%) of an Ultra pixel; anything else is simply taken away (no payout, no penalty). The marker jumps to another hole after every correct drop and after swapIdleSeconds (12) without one (NextHole; changeTypeOnSwap also changes the wanted type, off by default). A very gentle pull acts only in a thin band outside each hole's edge (pullAcceleration 5, pullBandFraction 0.5; nothing drawn). An event-log line sums up a round. Sounds pad_spawn/_click/_wrong/_ultra. Ultra pixels are ONE general currency (PixelClicker.TotalUltra held / UltraEarned lifetime, AddUltra(amount), RollUltra(chance), RestoreUltra; saved as SaveData.ultraPixels, older saves add up the old per-pixel TierSave.ultraCount; shown as an animated row at the very top of the Inventory's Currency tab with a line under it - PixelUltraSwatch: rainbow-cycling chip, glow, shine sweep, sparkles) for future upgrades; the minigame's tracker shows Ultra pixels ever earned (PixelClicker.UltraEarned, saved via the tracker count; spending Ultra on boosts does not lower it).  Ultra is now a general minigame reward: PixelClicker.RollUltra(chance) + PixelMinigame.UltraRoll(clicker, chance, ...) (per-round cap ultraRoundCount, event-log line, and PixelUltraFx.Play at the pixel's position: a rising gold "+1 Ultra Pixel" text, a sparkle burst and the sound ultra_gain; the Ultra Pad keeps its own text/sound and only adds the sparkles) are called by Snake (each eaten pixel of a won round + a guaranteed one), Sorting Race (each pixel of a win, chance x (1 + time left fraction)), Breakout (each broken brick) and Black Hole (each swallowed pixel); each has ultraChance, ultraMaxPerRound, disableUltra in the Inspector (0 = the coded default 4% / 6% / 3% / 1%, caps 3 / 3 / 3 / 2). Extends PixelMinigame (id pad); sounds pad_spawn/_click/_wrong/_ultra.
--- docs/systems/PixelPauseMenu.md ---
PixelPauseMenu

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPauseMenu.cs (Settings screen)

The Settings screen is a scrolling list with section headers (Gameplay, Interface, Accessibility, Display, Audio, Controls - the Saving section was folded into Gameplay). Row helpers: AddSettingToggle (re-reads its value whenever the screen opens via settingsRefreshers), AddDropdownRow (every multi-choice setting is a drop-down now; the old cycle-button AddChoiceRow was deleted), AddLookRow (floor / sky picker; the floor's row also holds its on/off tick box), AddEyedropperButton + AddCycleButton (small buttons beside a colour tick box), AddWideButtonRow, AddHeaderRow.
Current layout: Gameplay = replay tutorial, abbreviate numbers, hide purchased, run in background, pause stops game, day / night cycle + length of a day, autosave message + autosave interval, floor (style + tick box), when the floor is off, sky. Interface = tips, event log, popups, block tips, next-up window, drop guide, fancy pause menu, pixel-assemble buttons, camera intro, menu buttons, neon bar glow (+ eyedropper + a small rainbow 'cycle colour' button), custom UI colour (+ eyedropper), custom bar colour (+ eyedropper). Accessibility = cube rotation, pulsing, colour-blind marks, popup size, event log size, UI scale, reset achievement popup position (last). Display = screen filter + strength, VSync, fullscreen, resolution. Audio = mute, UI click sounds (PixelAudio.UiClicks, gates sound id ui_click), the three volumes. Removed: Click effects (row only), Flat view (the View window has Orthographic), Fancy buttons / Fancy windows (always on now), Auto-contrast outline and Cube spotlight (PixelContrast.cs deleted). Accessibility section (collapsible header after Interface): cube rotation (switching it off restores the cube's scene pose, PixelClicker.startRotation), pulsing, colour-blind marks, click effects level, popup size, event log size, UI scale. Interface: first-time tips (PixelHints.TipsEnabled) + Replay tutorial (PixelHints.ReplayTutorial), event log on/off + text size (PixelHints.EventLogEnabled/LogSizeChoice), number popups on/off + size (PixelUI.PopupsEnabled/PopupSizeChoice), UI scale, colour-blind marks. Saving: autosave interval + message (PixelSaveGame.AutoSaveChoice/AutoSaveMessageChoice). Menu search (top right of the main screen, 'Paused' is left-aligned: menuSearchField, OnMenuSearchChanged): hides the button grid and lists matching menu entries plus up to 6 matching settings rows (settingsItems labels); a settings result calls OpenFilteredSettings (settingsFilter: LayoutSettings then shows only matching rows and their headings, folded or not); the search clears each time the menu opens. A Key Bindings screen (BuildKeysPanel, click a key, press the new one, Escape cancels) opens from Controls. Row widths: every row under a section header (toggles, choice rows, sliders, wide buttons) is SettingsRowInset (112) narrower than the list, exactly the Replay tutorial button's width. Per-save settings sync: PixelSettingsSync.Known_ lists each PlayerPrefs key with its TYPE (0 int, 1 float, 2 string) - a key registered with the wrong type is read as 0 and overwritten on load (that wiped the eyedropped glow colour, the hues and the skill pack); an entry stored with another kind than the key's is ignored.

PixelPauseMenu How To Play (tabs)

The pause menu has one Game Guide button (between Settings and Give feedback; it was called How To Play) that opens a screen with three TABS: Controls, How To Play (the written guide) and Database (opens the game database window) - the tabs: Controls (Controls.txt, formerly the pause menu's Controls button) and Game Guide (HowToPlay.txt); each tab is its own GuideScreen panel and the tab buttons switch between the panels (BuildGuideScreen(..., isControlsTab)). Settings ends with a 'Keybinds' header with one 'Change keybinds' button that opens the key-binding screen (titled 'Keybinds').

PixelPauseMenu Game Guide text tabs (Controls and How To Play are two panels; the third tab, Database, opens the database window)

Two more screens that show Assets/Resources/HowToPlay.txt and Assets/Resources/Controls.txt (the user edits these files; GuideScreen, BuildGuideScreen, RefreshGuideScreen, FormatGuideText). File format: lines starting // are hidden notes, # Heading = coloured heading, Key | Action = two columns, anything else is shown as written (TMP rich text allowed). When a keybind or control changes, update Controls.txt; when a major system is added, update HowToPlay.txt.

Deleting a save slot
Hold the RIGHT mouse button on a slot that has a save in the Save / Load window (PixelPauseMenu.UpdateSlotDeleteHold, deleteSlotHoldSeconds 0 = 1.4 s, a red PixelHoldMeter ring on the pointer): when the ring is full PixelSaveGame.DeleteSlot removes the file, its screenshot / meta and the pending name. The hover box shows 'Hold right click to delete'. Works from the main menu's Save / Load window too. The autosave list is not affected.
--- docs/systems/PixelPets.md ---
PixelPets

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPets.cs

Pets (added by PixelClicker.Awake, singleton PixelPets.Instance): every click (PixelCollected, manual or auto at autoClickChanceFactor) rolls chancePerClick (0.0003) for a pet of that pixel type (one pet per PixelType; owned types don't roll). A hit freezes the game (PixelPets.PopupOpen, Time.timeScale = 0, restored on close; PixelTimeStop doesn't undo it) behind a modal popup (canvas 750, spinning PixelCubeIcon, "Awesome!"; Escape closes it via PixelWindows priority 150; popups queue) and the pet then spawns: a rigidbody + box collider the size of the main pixel (PixelClicker.PixelBaseSize) wearing CreateDisplayPixel of its type, bouncy PhysicMaterial, that every hopInterval seconds (only from the ground) hops with a sideways + upward impulse and a rolling torque, heads back to the cube when beyond roamRadius or near the screen edge, is respawned above the cube if it falls out of the world, and is hidden while a takeover minigame runs (TakeoverActive). Mouse: hovering a pet stops it (hoverStopDamping; PixelPets.HoveringPet, sets PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock so the cube ignores the click and PixelGrab skips it), (a pet that is behind the clickable cube from the camera never counts as hovered, so the cube still gets the click: PetUnderPointer compares ray distances) left-click picks it up (drag on the camera-facing plane, followSharpness, collides like Pixel Grabbing) and letting go throws it (throwStrength, maxThrowSpeed). The Ghost pet only hovers (RoamGhost: no gravity, trigger collider, drifts between random spots ghostHoverHeight pixel-widths above the floor with a bob and a slow spin; a thrown ghost's speed fades into the drift). The Glass Pet (MaybeBreak / BreakAndReform) shatters at random while it moves (glassBreakChance per second at glassBreakSpeed, faster = likelier; not while held or standing still): the pet turns invisible but stays solid in place, PetReformShards fly about for glassHoldSeconds, then reverse back into it over glassReformSeconds and it reappears (glassCooldown); sounds glass_shatter / glass_shatter_reform. (Glass old pixels are unchanged: they still shatter on landing and are gone.) The Seed Pet (RoamSeed) is a big sprout (seedPetScale 3x the main pixel, PixelLooks.CreateSproutObject + a tall box collider) that never hops: wherever it lands or is put down it stands up, pushes up a dirt mound, goes kinematic and plants itself (PlantPet, only after a short settle time and when SolidBelow finds ground other than its own collider or the clicker cube; it sprouts up out of the floor at a spot beside the cube (SeedPetSpawnPoint + FindGround: starts hidden below the floor, rises over seedPetRiseSeconds (1.6) with the dirt mound staying at floor level, Pet.rise/groundY, planted at once; if no floor is found below, it drops from the spot as before) and ignores the cube's colliders, so it can't land and plant on top of the cube; picking it up uproots it; picking it up uproots it, Uproot), and while planted it sows a seed every seedPetInterval (10-20) s (SowSeed: a random spot 1.5..seedPetRadius (5) main-pixel widths around it, on screen, PixelClicker.PlantSeedSproutAt = a SeedSprout planted at once that grows a random pixel, capped by seedMaxSprouts; hyper pets sow faster). The Vacuum Pet (SuckAround) sucks up the nearest old pixel within vacuumRadius pixel-widths every vacuumInterval s (ReleaseOldPixel(body, false): no payout, nothing else counts; they fly in and shrink) and a small camera-facing number above it (counterText, LateUpdate) shows the total (stat counter pet.vacuum, PixelStats.Total(key)). The Stats screen has a Pets section (found X / total, every pixel type's pet or ???, the vacuum count). Slower despawn: while a pet is out its pixel type's old pixels age despawnSlowFactor (2) times slower (PixelPets.DespawnRate, read by OldPixelDespawn every 0.5 s). Spawn bonus: while a pet is active (owned and switched on) its pixel type's spawn weight is multiplied by spawnWeightMultiplier (2) in PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier (SpawnWeightOf / PixelPets.SpawnMultiplier; potions and dev spawn selections still override). Toggles window has a fourth Pets tab (tick box per owned pet = SetOn; the Toggles button also shows once a pet exists). Saved as SaveData.pets ("type:off" strings; Export/Import). Stat pets.found, Stats row "Pets found". Dev tools: "Give pet (selected pixel)" button (popup; "All pixels" gives every pet quietly) and a separate "Give all pets" button (all dev gives pass Award(type, popup, startOff: true): the pets arrive switched OFF), and "Give a Dragon Cube" (the lowest-numbered one you still lack).

Which pixels have pets
PixelPets.CanHavePet(type): every pixel type except the seven Dragon Cubes and the hazards (Pest, Gas Pocket, Hornet Nest, Toadstool, Jellyfish, Mold). It used to skip all standalone pixels (Fire, the ores, the skill blocks...); they have pets now. The pet find roll (OnCollected), the 'Pet' achievements (PixelAchievements.RegisterPetAchievements, one per pet type) and the Game Database's Pet entries all use it. The Collect / Click achievements follow the same idea (PixelAchievements.NoPixelAchievements: only Dragon Cubes and hazards are left out).

PixelPetsCustomize

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPetsCustomize.cs

Part of partial class PixelPets: names and outfits. Hovering a pet shows a name tag above it (UpdateTag, EnsureTag: screen-space canvas, the pet's name + a procedurally drawn pencil button; stays tagGraceSeconds after the mouse leaves and while the mouse is over it, and the pet stands still while its tag is up). The pencil opens the customization window (OpenCustomize; freezes the game like the found popup via PopupOpen, Escape closes it through PixelWindows priority 160): a name box (maxNameLength, empty = default "{Pixel} Pet"; Pet.customName), a 3D view (a hidden preview studio far below the scene StudioHeight with a camera rendering into a RenderTexture shown by a RawImage; click-drag turns the model via an EventTrigger, it spins by itself otherwise; no extra light - it uses the scene's directional light) and < > arrows that change the outfit (Pet.outfit, applied to the live pet at once by ApplyOutfit). Outfits (OutfitList) are generated from outfitSeed (outfitCount = 12 + "Plain"), followed by the named special outfits (Specials(): Super Saiyan / Blue / God / Ultra Instinct / Legendary Spikes (Acc.SuperSaiyanHair = a cone-spike mane + Acc.Aura = see-through spheres and flames), Pirate Captain, Viking, Chef, Kitty, Bunny, Santa, Flower Child, Gentleman, Explorer, Punk; new accessories are appended to the Acc enum so saved outfit numbers don't change): 2-3 of four slots (head / face / neck / back) each with one accessory (Acc: Top Hat, Party Hat, Wizard Hat, Crown, Halo, Headphones, Antennae, Bandana, Sunglasses, Mustache, Bow Tie, Scarf, Cape, Wings) in palette colours, built from primitives + a runtime cone mesh in unit-cube space under an "Outfit" child (BuildOutfit, colliders removed) so most of the pet stays visible. Name and outfit are saved in PixelPets.Export (type:off:outfit:escapedName; older saves still load) and used by the Toggles Pets tab and the Stats Pets rows (NameOf).
--- docs/systems/PixelRobotWorker.md ---
PixelRobotWorker

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelRobotWorker.cs

UFO Helper (class and shop pack keep the old name 'Robot Worker' so saves keep working; shop pack unlocksRobot, Upgrades tab, 1M each; PixelShop.robot found/added like the bank; Activate/Deactivate): a small code-built flying saucer (hull, rim, glass dome with a green alien, spinning rim lights; fixed size robotScale) that idly hovers about the map (always higher than the cube - world height at least 1.4 pixel widths over the cube's centre and higher up the screen than it; no text label over it; UserDisabled switch in Toggles > Upgrades, saved as SaveData.robotDisabled; hoverHeight pixel widths above the floor height floorY, PickWander: random spots round the cube between minDistanceFromCube and maxRoamDistance, on screen, and at least minScreenGapFraction of the screen height from the cube ON SCREEN so it is never above it; new spot every wanderMin..MaxSeconds; smooth velocity flySpeed, bob, leans into its flight; stops while hovered so it can be clicked) with a speech bubble showing an alien face floating over it (BuildBubble, clamped to the screen, also a button that opens the window). Click-through: while the mouse is over the saucer or its bubble both fade to hoverAlpha (parts swap to transparent materials, UpdateFade), left clicks pass through (PixelRobotWorker.Hovering stays false, the bubble is not a raycast target) and a RIGHT-click opens the window (BuildWindow, canvas 520, PixelWindows priority 55) listing the placeable devices (PixelConsumables.IsPlaceableKind: Vacuum, Fan, Sorter, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Sprinkler) with the counts you hold. Picking one starts a normal ghost placement (PixelConsumables.BeginRobotPlacement, nothing is used up; the sorter keeps its two-step placing) and the chosen spot becomes the job (SetJob, a persistent see-through ghost via PixelConsumables.CreateGhost). Every checkInterval it looks whether a non-ending placed device of that index stands there (IsOccupied; any sorter counts) and, when not and you hold one, flies to workHeight above the spot (for the Sorter beside the cube, sorterStandOff), switches on its abduction beam (BuildBeam / UpdateBeam: a see-through cone with vertex colours, beamColor, flicker, from the saucer's underside to the spot) for placeDelay s, then PixelConsumables.RobotPlace puts the device down already armed (uses one up; stat robot.placed) and DropDevice slides it down the beam over dropSeconds. Out of stock -> red label, it goes back to wandering. The window (780 x 780, a scroll list of AddRow rows: name left, count right ('Unlimited' for infinite, red when 0), hover highlight; two pages windowPage: devices and, with the Pixel Bank bought, 'Abduct a pixel into the bank' -> a list of unlocked pixel types with how many are banked; footer Back / Stop working). Bank job (bankJob / bankType, UpdateBankJob): it flies over the nearest loose old pixel of that type (FindBankTarget: not kinematic, not held), holds the beam on it for abductSeconds, then PixelBank.StoreOldPixel(body, undersideOfSaucer) / IsFull pulls it into the saucer and banks it; idles (wanders) when none are left or the bank is full (labels 'Bank full' / 'Needs the Pixel Bank'); stat robot.banked. The job is saved as a string (Export/Import, SaveData.robot, device by name). Renamed text fields (ufoTitle, ufoJobSetFormat, ufoBankJobFormat) so old serialized texts are dropped. Screen saver (PixelRobotSaver.cs, same partial class; disableScreenSaver, saverIdleMinutes 0 = 3, saverCheckInMin/Max 0 = 2-5 min, saverRoamSeconds 0 = 20, saverSpeedFactor 0 = 1.6): with no input (PixelInput.AnyInputHeld, pointer move, wheel) and no job / open window for the idle time it flies up out of view above the camera (Leaving -> Away); each check-in does a FlyBy across the top of the screen or a Roam visit (normal wandering) then leaves again; any input drops it back to normal wandering; the speech bubble hides while it is off screen; UserDisabled set (off or on) calls ResetSaver so toggling it off and on respawns it on screen. Ride along (PixelRobotCockpit.cs, same partial class; first row of the UFO window, boardText; PixelRobotWorker.Piloting makes ExternalClickBlock true and blocks Pixel Grabbing / first person): the glass dome holds a small cabin built in BuildUfo (floor + ring, a 16-panel ring console with 32 blinking buttons consoleButtons, a console screen, a round glass WINDOW in the cabin floor (hull / underside are lathe meshes MeshPrim / LatheMesh with a hole of WindowRadius, a dark shaft between, a transparent floor disc) so you can look down through the saucer, a tiny alien at a station, a cabinLamp that is only on while riding). Choosing the row puts you inside as a passenger: the camera (UFO Passenger root object, CopyFrom main camera, main cullingMask 0) sits at bodyT.TransformPoint(walkPos, CabinFloorY + eyeHeight) so it leans and flies with the saucer, which keeps wandering and doing its job on its own (Update runs the AI while Piloting, only hover / click / bubble are skipped). The mouse always looks around (cursor locked while riding, cockpitLookSpeed 0.35), WASD walk (walkSpeed, kept within walkRadius and off the alien); Escape (PixelWindows 125) climbs out (no Leave button: the cursor is locked). The old piloting (flying it yourself, the console screen camera, abducting from the cockpit) was removed.
--- docs/systems/PixelRules.md ---
Pixel rules: value, body and use (design, not built yet)

Status: agreed rule; step 1 built (husks stop paying currency, 2026-10-15). Switch: PixelClicker.husksPayCurrency (off = the rules; on = the old behaviour, for comparing). Still to build: the Recycler, charges from holding utility blocks, the drained look. This is a game-wide rule; every skill, machine and block should follow it. Woodcutting's design (WoodcuttingProgression.md) is the first place it is applied.

The problem

Today one block can pay twice: the click gives currency, and the old pixel it drops can be turned into currency again (the Vacuum and Vacuum Device pay out its stored value, the Pixel Bank stores it, a hovering Solar old pixel pays a trickle). It is not clear which of the two is the "real" reward, and it does not make sense in the game's world: you should get the money or the thing, not both.

The rule

A click splits a block into its VALUE and its BODY.

| What you get | What it is | What it is for |
|---|---|---|
| Currency (from the click) | the block's value | spending in the shop, selling to vendors |
| Old pixel (the body) | the drained, physical leftover - material | machines, crafting, charges, XP; never currency by itself (only the Recycler machine turns husks into currency, at a loss) |
| Charges (from holding a utility block) | the block's use | Pesticide, Whetstone and other tools |

Why it makes sense in the world: extraction

The clicker does not simply "break" blocks: a click extracts the block's essence (its colour, its value) into your counter, and what drops out is the drained husk. That is why you get both: the currency *is* the essence, and the old pixel is what is left once the essence is gone. The husk is still a real thing - wood is still wood - so it is useful as material, but it has no value of its own to cash in.

Idea for the look: old pixels could be slightly duller than the block on the cube (a little desaturated, a faint "drained" shimmer as they drop), so the player can see that the value went into the counter and the husk is what is left.

The three ways to take a block

| Action | You get | You give up |
|---|---|---|
| Left click (break) | its currency + its husk (old pixel) | nothing - this is the staple |
| Hold (Block Hold) on a utility block | its charges (e.g. Pesticide charges) + its husk | its currency |
| Right click on a hazard (clear) | the hazard as currency + its husk | the time the right clicks take |

- Clicking is the reliable way to earn currency, at every stage of the game; going back to farm currency is a real choice.
- Holding a plain block keeps today's Block Hold behaviour (pull it out of the rotation, the stake); holding a utility block converts it to charges.

The rule, word for word

> Husks are never currency by themselves. Only a machine can turn them into something - and the Recycler is the machine that turns them into currency, at a loss.

This keeps the world consistent (a machine is processing husks, like every other machine) while clicking stays the real way to earn currency.

What husks are for

Machines exist to turn husks into something useful; despawning hurts because unused material is lost, not money.

| Machine / system | Turns husks into |
|---|---|
| Recycler (built: PixelRecycler.cs) | currency, at 50% of each husk's value |
| Wood Chipper | skill XP (and Wood Chips) |
| Kiln | Charcoal (fuel / material) |
| Storage Crate | stored material for later |
| Pesticide Sprayer (planned) | used up to clear hives automatically |
| Crafting (planned) | recipe ingredients |
| Conveyor belts, sorters, connectors | moving husks to the right machine |

The Recycler (built 2026-10-15, PixelRecycler.cs)

Built version: an open bin (DeviceKind.Recycler, first on the shop's Utilities card, 50 White; Cubie gives one on his first visit). Drop up to 10 husks in, press the red button on its front: the lid shuts and each husk pays back 50% of its value (it shakes for 2 s first) as currency of its type. The husks stay in the bin as physical pixels until then. The notes below are the original plan; the live numbers are on the component.

- What it does: takes husks in and pays out currency of that block's type at 15% of the block's click value per husk (start value, to be tuned). A Wood husk from a Wood block that pays 3 gives 0.45 Wood.
- Takes time and setup: it processes one husk every so often (proposed: one every 1.5 seconds), so throughput is a real limit; husks are fed by belt, Funnel, Conveyor Connector or by hand (it is a belt acceptor like the Kiln).
- Upgrades through other skills: Electrician power raises its speed; a later tier raises its rate (e.g. to 25%). Another cross-skill link.
- It competes with the other uses: the same husk can go to the Recycler (a little currency), the Wood Chipper (XP), crafting (material) or the Pesticide Sprayer (charges). Choosing where husks go is the builder's puzzle.
- A home for the old systems: the Vacuum can collect husks and hand them to a Recycler instead of paying currency out directly. (The Pixel Bank is storage, not a cash-in: see the table below.)
- Never better than clicking: whatever its upgrades, the rate stays well below 100%, so a block never pays twice in full.

Hands first, machines later (first person)

First person is not a second clicker mode. You can still click the cube high above you, but the mode exists for handling husks by hand: breaking old pixels for XP, picking them up and carrying them, and feeding machines yourself (e.g. carrying wood to the Wood Chipper).

The progression:

1. The cube makes currency and husks.
2. Hands (first person): you save husks from despawning, break them for XP and carry them to machines. Free but slow.
3. Machines take the carrying and processing over (belts, Funnel, connectors, sorters, Chipper, Kiln, Recycler). Faster, but they cost something to buy and place.
4. Machines break (see below), and your hands are needed again. That sends you back to fix the layout properly.

Rules that keep it balanced:

- Hand-breaking a husk never beats clicking. It gives a fraction of the XP the click on that block gave, so clicking stays the main source and camping in first person is never the best play.
- Hands are free but slow, machines are fast but cost something. Otherwise machines lose their point, or hands do.
- Its first moment: before the player owns any machine, first person is the only way to save husks from despawning. Cubie can point at it the first time husks start to vanish.
- Jobs only hands can do keep it alive later: repairing machines (below), grabbing a rare husk before a sorter takes it, carrying a Hive to the Pesticide Sprayer, clearing a jam.

Machine breakdowns and repair (design)

Some machines break, and the player goes into first person to repair them.

Wrong input (the main cause). A machine that gets something it should not take (e.g. a Gas Pocket husk in the Kiln) bangs, smokes and stops.
- This makes sorting matter: a Belt Sorter or Splitter protects the machine, not just tidies.
- The player caused it and can fix it for good with a better layout; it teaches what each machine takes.
- Each machine lists what it accepts (shown in its tooltip / the Database), so a breakdown is never a surprise rule.

Wear over time (optional, gentle). If used at all:
- a worn machine slows down, it does not stop;
- nothing wears while offline;
- upgrades / perks (Electrician) reduce or remove wear.

What a breakdown does. The machine stops and keeps its contents; nothing is lost. It holds things up instead of destroying them, so being busy elsewhere never costs material.

Seeing it. Smoke, sparks and a warning icon over the machine, visible from the normal clicker view, so the player chooses *when* to go down and fix it instead of checking all the time.

Repairing.
- Quick: a few hits or a short hold on the machine in first person (a small bonus for doing it well is optional). The real cost is the walk, not a long minigame.
- Repairs use husks from other skills: metal husks from Mining, Wire from Electrician. This gives Mining a link the connection map says it is missing, and gives those husks another use.

Open questions: which machines can break, which inputs break each one, whether wear exists at all, and which husks repair which machines.

Systems that needed a new job under this rule

Step 1 (built): with husksPayCurrency off none of these pay a husk's value any more. Consumed husks give husk XP instead (PixelClicker.GiveHuskXp -> PixelSkills.AddHuskXp: a share of a manual click's XP, huskXpShare 0 = 0.5; pure husks train Standard, a skill's husks train that skill).

| System | Today | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| Vacuum pixel / Vacuum Device | paid the stored value of every old pixel it sucked up | built: gives husk XP per husk sucked up (no '+N' popup any more). Later it may hand husks to a Recycler / crate instead |
| Pixel Bank (hose) | stores old pixels with their payout, which comes back when they are spat out | decided: husk storage for their EFFECT, never to cash in: keep husks you cannot use yet (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician) and spit them out when a machine wants them. Stored husks carry no payout; capacity is one shared total (already how PixelBank.IsFull works) |
| Solar old pixel | hovered and paid a small currency trickle | built: each trickle tick adds a little daylight instead (PixelDaylight.hoverDaylight, 0 = 0.02 per tick), so a hovering Solar husk keeps the day going |
| PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel(body, credit: true) | re-added the old pixel's reward (black hole option, Ultra Holes right hole) | built: the credit only pays with husksPayCurrency on; Ultra Holes gives husk XP for a correctly sorted husk; the black hole already gives its minigame XP |
| First-person axe | paid 1.5x a block's value for chopping a wood husk | built: Woodcutting XP only (swinging the axe at a wood block on the cube still adds its tool bonus: that is a click) |

Not changed (open question for the developer): the minigames that use husks as play pieces (Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout) still pay currency per husk as their minigame reward. The Pixel Bank's stored payout is kept in the data but nothing pays it any more.

Open questions

- Recycler: is 15% right, how fast does it process, what does it cost, and is there one per save or many?

- Should vendors buy only currency counts (as now), or also husks (e.g. the Lumberjack buys a pile of wood husks for a little)?
- The "drained" look for old pixels: worth doing, and how subtle?
- Which blocks become utility blocks with charges, besides Pesticide (and the Whetstone once it moves to Mining)?
--- docs/systems/PixelSaveGame.md ---
PixelSaveGame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSaveGame.cs

Save/load to JSON in Application.persistentDataPath (tiers, shop packs by name, auto clicker, potions owned). Also saves the running potion (type, combo second type, seconds left; restored after FinishLoad via PixelConsumables.RestoreActive) and the placed devices (PixelConsumables.PlacedState: device name, position, yaw, seconds left, sorter aim/bend/force; GetPlacedDevices / ClearPlacedDevices / RestorePlacedDevice; every PixelPlacedDevice knows its DeviceIndex). Time away does not run their timers down. Loads on start (1 frame delayed), autosaves (shows a small pulsing "Autosaving..." note at the bottom right above the black bar: ShowAutoNote, autoSaveNote* fields; manual saves keep the centred message), saves on quit. Save slots: slotCount slots (slot 1 = the old saveFileName, others ..._slotN.json); CurrentSlot (PlayerPrefs, the slot autosave / quit-save / startup load use; set by SaveToSlot / LoadSlot); SlotHasSave, TryGetSlotInfo. The pause menu's single "Save / Load" entry opens a window (BuildSaveLoadPanel): scrolling slot list of compact rows (name + a Rename button that opens a text box via OpenRename / ConfirmRename; names live in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.SlotName.N, empty = 'Slot N'; date / time played / pixels show in a hover box, UpdateSlotTip), select one, Save / Load buttons, an overwrite warning (BuildOverwriteOverlay) when saving over an existing slot. After a save or load in that window a blue result window the size of the whole menu (BuildResultOverlay / ShowResult, resultColor, texts savedResultFormat etc.) says "Saved to slot N" / "Loaded slot N" (or the failure) with a "Got it" button (Escape closes it too); those calls pass message: false to SaveToSlot / LoadSlot so the centred toast doesn't also show. Restart deletes the current slot's save. Uses PixelClicker.LoadTierState/FinishLoad and PixelShop.SetPackState. Old pixel positions are saved (SaveData.oldPixels = PixelClicker.OldPixelState: tier, payout, position, rotation, velocities, OldPixelDespawn.Remaining; not flying / vanishing ones; skipped while a takeover minigame has moved them) and restored by PixelClicker.RestoreOldPixels after FinishLoad (older saves leave the pixels alone). Settings per save file (SaveData.settings, PixelSettingsSync.cs): Collect() copies a fixed list of PlayerPrefs keys (PixelClicker.Setting.* except display hardware, PixelAudio.*, PixelClicker.Key.*, PixelClicker.Dev.*) into every save, Apply() writes them back on load (keys not in the file are deleted = defaults; an older save without the list changes nothing) and Reload() calls each cache's reload (PixelClicker.ReloadSettingsFromPrefs, PixelShop, PixelKeys, PixelAudio, PixelDisplaySettings, PixelPauseMenu, PixelHints.RefreshLog, PixelDevTools.ReloadFromPrefs). Resolution / fullscreen / VSync / quality / UI scale and the 'seen tip' flags stay global. A new PlayerPrefs setting that should follow the save file must be added to the list in PixelSettingsSync.Known_ (and its cache reloaded in Reload).

Autosave list + screenshots (current). Every TIMER autosave (not manual / quit saves) is also copied into <persistentDataPath>/Autosaves/autosave_<ticks>.json (+ .meta info, + .jpg), at most autosaveHistorySize (100, Inspector; keepAutosaveHistory) - when full the OLDEST are deleted (PixelAutosaves.Add / Trim). The Save / Load window has a small 'Autosaves' button under Save | Load that opens PixelAutosaveWindow (PixelAutosaves.cs): newest first, a Load button per row (PixelSaveGame.LoadFile, the current slot does not change; the game resumes), hover = screenshot + details. EVERY save writes a screenshot next to it (<save file>.jpg, 320 px wide, PixelSaveShots: taken at the end of the frame while the game is visible; a rolling copy every 4 s is used when saving from the pause menu) and the slot rows' hover box shows it above the details (PixelPauseMenu.UpdateSlotTip, SlotThumbPath). Deleting a save deletes its screenshot. With the autosave interval set to Off there are no autosaves to list.

Save rework (current; overrides the slot / autosave wording above). Autosaves (timer, quit, focus loss, Quit > Main menu - Save(bool)) go ONLY into the autosave list (PixelAutosaves.Add, always kept, at least 1); the numbered slots are written ONLY by a manual save (SaveToSlot). Quit / focus-loss saves within 5 s of the last autosave are skipped; no autosave happens on the main menu, and the timer pauses (not resets) on the title screen and while the pause menu is open. TryGetLatest(out slot, out path) = newest file among manual slots and the newest autosave (slot 0); Continue and the startup load use it (ContinueMostRecent). The save's name is SaveData.slotName (GetSlotName / SetSlotName; a name for an empty slot waits in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.SlotName.N until the first save). Settings follow the profile: while the title shows, the newest save's settings are applied (ApplyProfileSettings) and Continue of that same file does not re-apply them; a new game / Restart calls PixelSettingsSync.ResetToDefaults (keys and volumes stay) and sets AutoStartNewGame. Restart deletes the autosaves only (PixelAutosaves.DeleteAll).

Look settings are NOT per save
PixelSettingsSync stores per-save settings in the save file and puts them back on load / start. The look of the interface (UI colour + hue / saturation, button colour, neon bar glow + hue / cycle / picked colour, bar colour, clicker aura + colour / mode) is deliberately left out of its Known_ list: those PlayerPrefs belong to the player's machine. Kept per save they were overwritten on every start by the newest save's copy, so anything changed after the last save was lost.
--- docs/systems/PixelShop.md ---
PixelShop

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelShop.cs / PixelShopUI.cs / PixelShopDefaults.cs

One partial class PixelShop in three files: PixelShop.cs = data types, ALL [SerializeField] settings, lifecycle, buying/requirement logic; PixelShopUI.cs = building and refreshing the UI (button, panel, tabs, scroll list, upgrades window, trackers); PixelShopDefaults.cs = the built-in pack definitions (Create…Pack) plus the BuiltInPacks table (how to recognise each one, which pack it requires, how to build it) - to add a built-in pack: write its Create…Pack and add one line to the table. Keep new serialized fields in PixelShop.cs so the Inspector order stays stable. Shop button (appears once requiredTier = Black is unlocked) + panel.
