(Vacuum spawn weight 0.28 and Electric 0.2 since packDataVersion 11, was 0.2 / 0.15) NOTE: some entries below are outdated - the Seed, Water, Electric, Obsidian, Wizard / Tinkerer / Entrepreneur and Pixel Grabbing packs are no longer separate shop packs (their blocks now come with the Farmer's, Electrician's and Prospector's packs, the vendors are started by Cubie, and Pixel Grabbing is unlocked from the start with a Toggles switch); the descriptions still explain how those blocks and systems behave. ShopPack list: RGB Pack, Auto Clicker, Glass Pixel, Vacuum Pixel, Obsidian Pixel, Luminescent Pixel (Pixels tab), Faster Clicking, Multi-Click, Combo Meter, Old Pixel Capacity (UpgradeEffect.OldPixelCap, 5 levels 75/100/150/200/300 -> PixelClicker.SetOldPixelCap; base cap baseOldPixelCap = 50, the Time Stop stockpile never drops below the cap), Crafting, Pixel Grabbing (Upgrades tab), Ghost Hunt, Black Hole, Bomb Defusal, Ultra Pad, Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout (Minigames tab), Singularity Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by the singularity tracker), Ghost Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by ghosts caught), Solar Pixel (Pixels tab, needs Meteor Pixel + stardust), Time Slow (Upgrades tab, needs Time Stop), Meteor Strike (Minigames tab, needs the Prospector's Pack, which replaced the Obsidian pack), Meteor Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by meteor chunks), Seed Pixel (Pixels tab, listed right BEFORE Electric, needs the RGB pack, 4K each of Red / Green / Blue, PixelType.Seed, spawn weight 0.3; a brown shell you dig open: clicksToCollect 8, pays 15 on the last click, look = white tint with a brown grain texture (PixelLook.shellTexture + damageCracks: dark cracks spread per hit; looksVersion 12-13), packDataVersion 7 moves it before Electric, 8 / 9 update old packs (shell, then 8 clicks / 15). Every hit plays seed_dig (code-synthesized PixelSynth.Dig while its clip list is empty) and SeedDigFx.Play (PixelSeed.cs: two code-built particle systems, dark-brown dirt chunks and pale drifting dust, that fall from the cube's underside; stronger as the shell nears cracking: PixelClicker.SeedDigEffect). The cracked shell does NOT spawn an old pixel: CollectInternal calls PixelClicker.SpawnSeedSprout seedsPerShell (0 = 3) times (all sprouts together capped by seedSproutCap 36; each seed has its OWN despawn timer seedSproutLifetime (0 = 45 s from dropping, SeedSprout.SetLifetime: it withers if its pixel hasn't popped yet) and the pixel it grows is an ordinary old pixel with its own normal timer) -> SeedSprout (PixelSeed.cs): a big green sprout (seedSproutScale 2.2x an old pixel, PixelLooks.CreateSproutObject) tumbles down (it never collides with the live cube and is a trigger for popNoCollisionSeconds after it is born, SeedSprout.SetGhost, so a burst of sprouts doesn't pile up inside the cube), plants itself upright on the first flat contact (dirt puff, a pushed-up dirt mound and a soft green point light make it easy to spot), then (seed_plant: a small code-made crunch, PixelSynth.Crunch, played by every SeedSprout.Plant and when the Seed pet plants itself) over seedGrowSeconds (6) a display model of a RANDOM unlocked pixel type grows from nothing on its tip (CreateDisplayPixel), holds seedHoldSeconds (1) wobbling, and pops off as an ordinary old pixel (SpawnStoredPixel, payout RollOldPixelAmount); the sprout then shrinks away. Rarer types are likelier (RandomSeedPixelTier: chance ~ 1 / spawnWeight^seedRarityBias (1); not rare drops / fly-away / Seed / Singularity), never a Vacuum or Singularity pixel (those only come from bought seeds). Old Solar pixels within seedSolarReach (6 old-pixel widths) speed growth (SolarSeedBoostAt: +seedSolarBoostEach (0.75) each, capped at seedSolarBoostMax (3); the sprout's light turns warm yellow while boosted). Solar clusters: old Solar pixels that touch each other (centres within solarClusterDistance 1.5 old-pixel widths, connected groups) in a group of solarClusterSize (5) or more never despawn (PixelClicker.UpdateSolarClusters, every 0.15 s OldPixelDespawn.KeepAlive(); the old-pixel cap still removes the oldest). Seeds never sit on top of each other: PixelClicker.SeedSpotFree(pos) (spacing seedSpacing, 0 = 0.7 x a sprout's width; SeedSprout.AnyNear, PixelPets.PlanterNear) is checked when you plant (message), when the Seed pet sows (it tries another spot) and PlantSeedSproutAt refuses; a shell sprout that lands too close slides away (SeedSprout.MakeRoom). Sprouts are not saved; stored Seed pixels (Bank, saves) are plain shells), Water Pixel (Pixels tab, listed right after Seed / before Electric, PixelType.Water, TESTING price: 10 White, no requirements so it is available at once - raise it later; pays 3, spawn weight 0.4; look = see-through wobbling blue jelly (wobble, forceTranslucent, looksVersion 15); packDataVersion 10 moves it into place; has a spawn switch in Toggles): old Water pixels are used up by splashing: OldPixelSplash (PixelSeed.cs, added in SpawnFallingCopy when the look has splash) splashes the pixel the first time it lands on the ground (not on other pixels, not while held; or after lying still 1.5 s), whether or not anything needed water: ReleaseOldPixel(body, false) (no payout), WaterSplashFx (blue droplets + mist particles), then WaterSplashPiece.Spawn (PixelSeed.cs): the pixel BREAKS INTO 4 smaller Water cubes (waterPieceCount 0 = 4, waterPieceSize 0.4 of the pixel, waterPieceSpeed 2.2, waterPieceSeconds 1.2; PixelClicker 'Water splash pieces', disableWaterPieces) that are flung out and up and break completely into a small droplet puff the moment they land or time out (WaterSplashFx.PlayMini, ONE particle system from a pool of 24; the pieces are plain rigidbodies, not old pixels, OldPixelImpact ignores them), sound water_splash (PixelSynth.Splash) and PixelClicker.WaterSplashAt gives every growing SeedSprout and planted Seed pet within seedWaterReach (5 old-pixel widths) an INSTANT jump of seedWaterGrowSeconds (1.5 s of growth / sowing time), but each target accepts water only every seedWaterCooldown (0.75 s) so Sorters / auto clickers can't make it instant (a splash flashes the sprout's light blue); Solar pixels make them 'sun soaked': any old Solar pixel near a sprout / pet sets it soaked for seedSunSoakSeconds (8) at the boost it gave, so the speed-up outlasts the Solar pixel. A soft ray of sunlight (SunRayFx in PixelSeed.cs: two crossed vertical ribbons fading upward, unlit vertex colours, shimmering, fades in/out) stands over every sun soaked sprout / planted Seed pet. (a wet sprout's light is blue (looksVersion 16 switches splash on in saved Water looks); a Sorter streaming Water pixels over sprouts makes a farm. Mirror Pixel (Pixels tab, listed before Vacuum, needs the Electrician's Pack (it used to need the Electric pack), 15K each of Red / Green / Blue, pays 4, spawn weight 0.2, PixelType.Mirror, chrome look (PixelLook.chromeTexture: a code-drawn sky / horizon / ground texture so it reads as reflective in any render pipeline, plus emission, and shine: PixelLookShine sweeps a SOFT diagonal band of light across each face in turn (the overlay mesh is an 8x8 grid per face, PixelLooks.ShineGrid, and the vertex alpha follows a moving band so it fades at the edges - the old single quad could only blink as a white rectangle)) added by looksVersion 11 and replaced by 14, moved into place by packDataVersion 6; a click on a Mirror pixel also pays the last other pixel type's last click amount again (PixelClicker.lastReflectTier/Amount in CollectInternal, raises PixelCollected for it too, so Overcharge / pets react)), Electric Pixel (no longer its own pack: it comes with the Electrician's Pack, random 1-15 payout, spawn weight 0.2; older saved lists were migrated by packDataVersion 4-5, MovePack reorders a pack and renumbers requirement indexes). The shop's tracker rows (one per minigame with a goal counter) are off by default (showMinigameTrackersInShop): they moved to the Log's Goals tab. The Upgrades tab has two sub-tabs: Features (the packs; featuresSubTabText, renamed from "Upgrades") and Value (upgradesSubTab 0/1). The old "Pixel" Ultra-boost page was removed (Ultra pixels will get a different use): PixelTier.ultraLevel, UltraMultiplier and PixelShop.TryBuyUltraBoost still exist, nothing in the UI buys them. Value upgrades = the idle-game growth engine: one row per unlocked pixel type, paid in that pixel's own currency, cost valueBaseCost x amountPerClick x valueCostGrowth^level (25, 1.15, no cap by default, valueMaxLevel), Value rows: the orange '!' says 'Each level multiplies this pixel's worth per click by that level.' (valueInfoText) and hovering the Upgrade button shows just 'Shift-click to buy as many as you can.' (valueUpgradeTip); every shop row is boxed (StyleBoxedRow(row, rightInset, hasLevel), called after the picture is added: pack, potion, Value, Operation, Operation-upgrade and search rows): a sunken box beside the orange '!' button holds the name (single line, shrinks to fit), a same-height box to its right the level (row.levelLabel, only on leveled / Value rows; leveled rows put the little level markers at the right end of the cost box via ApplyLevelWidgets), a larger box underneath the cost lines (row.costBox), and the buttons stay on the right (LayoutPackRowLikeCards, the old wide Buy bar along the bottom, is no longer called); Shift-click buys up to valueMaxBulk (PixelInput.ShiftHeld); payout x (1 + level * valueBonusPerLevel) * valueMilestoneMultiplier^(level / valueMilestoneEvery) (PixelClicker.ValueMultiplierAt / ValueMultiplier; settings in PixelClicker "Value Upgrades"). PixelClicker.PayoutMultiplier = Ultra x Value, used for click payouts and offline progress. PixelTier.valueLevel is saved in TierSave. Skill cap (2026-10-15): a pixel type's Value level can't go above the level of the skill that owns it x valueLevelsPerSkillLevel (1) (valueCapBySkill; pure / basic pixels and every type outside a skill pack follow Standard, a pack's types follow that skill: PixelSkills.TryGetOwnerLevel, PixelShop.ValueSkillCapped); a capped row shows 'Needs <Skill> level N' and a 'Locked' button (valueCappedFormat, valueCappedButtonText); levels already above the cap are kept. Every block has a Value row now (skill blocks too, capped by their own skill); only hazards have none. Potion/device prices no longer scale with Value by default (PixelConsumables.scalePricesWithValue, off; renamed from pricesScaleWithValue). The Consumables tab now has two rows of purchase cards (cardRowHeight 322): Potions + Utilities, then Seeds (seedsCardTitle, lists only seed items, same drop-down / count / Buy as the others); card titles are capitals, the cost line starts with 'COST:', and there is no description text: an orange '!' box left of the cost line shows the item's description as a wrapped tooltip (PurchaseCard.tipText). Every shop row (packs, potions, Value rows) works the same way: LayoutRowHeader puts the '!' box (CreateInfoBox) in front of the name, hides the description label (PackRow.descLabel stays as hidden storage for the tooltip text) and gives the freed space to the name / cost bands; the mouse-over sets hoverTip (a Func<string>), drawn by the currency tooltip in UpdateCurrencyAndWindows + a blank spare card (PurchaseCard.blank, for later). The Pixels tab is split into collapsible sections (SectionOf: Base pixels = RGB / Glass / Luminescent packs, Toggleables = single other pixels, Skill packs = packs with 4+ reward pixels; header buttons sectionHeaders, sectionCollapsed). Search box at the window's top left (searchField, searchPlaceholder, searchWidth): while it has text RefreshRows ignores tabs / sections and lists every pack matching MatchesSearch (name, description, reward pixel names); matching consumables (potions, devices, seeds, supplies; RefreshSearchItems, one row each, Buy x1), Value rows and Operation rows are listed too, the cards, sub-tabs and trackers are hidden meanwhile; SelectTab clears it. Four tabs (Pixels / Upgrades / Consumables / Minigames; per-pack tab, Automatic infers it) over a fixed-size scrolling list (ScrollRect + scrollbar). Leveled packs that require another pack (the auto clicker upgrades) are not in a tab: the required pack gets an arrow button opening a second "Upgrades Window". Each pack has a requirements list (PackRequirement: kind Pack = another pack index must be bought, kind MinigameGoal = a minigame id's goal reached; all must be met; ShopPack.ParentIndex = first Pack requirement, used to file upgrade packs under their parent). The Combo Meter is listed right above the Auto Clicker (table order + packDataVersion 16 moves it in saved lists). PixelShopCurrency.cs (partial): the currency panel on the left of the shop window (child of the shop panel, so the shop itself is not moved; hideCurrencyPanel, currencyPanelWidth/Gap/RowHeight/IconSize/FontSize): a scrolling list of unlocked (or held) pixel types, each a spinning PixelCubeIcon in the tier colour + the amount, hover tooltip (PixelHoverTip) with name + full amount. Opening the shop closes the Inventory and Log (UpdateCurrencyAndWindows), and opening the Inventory or Log closes the shop (PixelShop.CloseShopWindow). When the shop closes it brings back the Inventory (or Log) it closed on opening (reopenInventory/reopenLog), unless the player opened another window meanwhile. Every number in the shop uses PixelClicker.FormatNumberShort (always abbreviated from 1K, ignores the setting). Handles buying, requirements, affordability colouring, levels/upgrades, Inspector-ticked purchased. [RequireComponent(typeof(PixelAutoClicker))].

Packs kept out of the shop (ShopPack.hideInShop)

hideInShop leaves a pack out of every shop list (PixelShopUI.HiddenAsPurchased returns true for it) while it keeps existing and working. Set (by the builders and packDataVersion 33, PixelShop.IsDevOnlyPack) on the testing packs: Fire Pixel, Nuclear Pixel, the Farmer's Pack and the Prospector's Pack (packDataVersion 34; so Farming and Mining are reachable only through Dev Tools for now), and on Block Hold (free from the start). Untick it in the Inspector to sell a pack again. Unlock / relock them with the Dev Tools Shop unlocks widget (PixelShop.DevUnlockPack / DevRelockPack / DevUnlockAllPacks / DevRelockAllPacks; relocking also locks the pack's reward pixels with PixelClicker.LockTier).
- Upgrades tab order: the Pixel Bank is shown right above Time Stop (PixelShopUI display sort only; the pack list keeps its indexes because requirements point at them).

RGB is an unlock (2026-10-16): the RGB Pack is hideInShop and given for free by Cubie's second visit (GrantPackRewarding(PixelType.Red)) at 100 Black earned; the Shop button appears with Red (requiredTier Red, packDataVersion 40); the Auto Clicker costs 150 of each pure pixel and is the first Log goal after RGB (the RGB and Combo Meter goals are gone); the Lumberjack's Pack (250 each) follows.
--- docs/systems/PixelSkills.md ---
PixelSkills

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSkills.cs / PixelSkillsWindow.cs

Skill system SHELL (added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelSkills.Instance, disableSkills): skill packs swap the basic cubes (monochrome, RGB, Glass, Luminescent) for another set and you train the skill of the pack in play. SkillPack list (id, name, description, accent, replaces / spawns / xpFrom texts, baseXp + xpGrowth, Perk list with implemented flag; built-ins Standard and Mining are added by id in EnsureDefaults). Mining IS Prospector mode (ActiveId reads PixelProspector.Setting, SetActive writes it; the change applies from the next spawn, the current cube stays), the Skills window is the only place that chooses it; the old Toggles 'Mining mode' switch is gone; a pack is available when its pixels are unlocked (IsAvailable). XP = stat counter skill.<id>.xp (saved generically, Xp, LevelOf(pack, out into, out need)); OnCollected adds 1 XP per manual / autoXpFactor (0.25) per auto click of the active pack's pixels (every pack's baseXp is multiplied by xpCurveScale (2) - BaseXpOf, used by LevelOf, Mastery and the balance report - so Standard's 380 means level 2 after 760 blocks; Standard is different: every collected block gives it a flat standardXpPerBlock 1 XP, manual or auto, whatever skill is trained, no multipliers - AddXpFlat); a level-up posts an event-log line. Window (PixelSkillsWindow.cs): a docked Skills tab on the left edge directly under the Bank + Hose pair (PixelHud.DockInGroup, width PixelBank.DockGroupWidth, appears when SkillsTabUnlocked: a skill pack is owned, or Standard is level 2 and its level-2 popup - which opens the window - is no longer waiting; Cubie no longer introduces skills: the Toggles tutorial lost its skills step and the skills walk-through only auto-starts with autoStartTutorial, off), a window that opens from the left edge like the Bank window (closes / is closed by the Inventory, Log and Bank, CloseWindowIfOpen; PixelWindows priority 14, PixelPop): header (training line + XP bar), pack cards on the left (Standard is a card, locked packs show ???), the selected pack's detail + perk list + Train button on the right; the first availability auto-opens it with a one-off explanation (introText, tip id skills_intro). Pixel packs are generic now: SkillPack.types lists a pack's pixel types (the FIRST one being unlocked = the pack is available), PixelSkills.PackSetting (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillPack, per save; old Prospector = Mining) is the one chosen pack, PixelProspector.Setting just reads / writes it, and PixelSkills.LeftOutOfSpawns(type) (used by PickSpawnTier) keeps a pack's pixels out of the spawns unless the pack is in play OR the type is a guest; the basic pixels step aside while any pack is in play. Guests (cross-breeding): a TreeNode.guestType node, once bought, makes that block a switch in Toggles > Pixels ('Seed (Farming)'; UnlockedGuests, GuestOn, SetGuest, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillGuests) so it also spawns in other rotations. (The Toggles window no longer has '<Pack> mode' switches: the Skills window is the only place that chooses the pack.) Farming (id farming, bought with the Farmer's Pack): see the PixelFarmPixels note below. Electrician (id electrician, PixelSkills.ElectricianId, bought as the shop's Electrician's Pack - a ShopPack with skillId = 'electrician', needs the Auto Clicker, 8K each Red / Green / Blue, listed under Skill packs; PixelShop.IsSkillPackBought; the separate Electric Pixel pack is gone - Electric is the pack's first pixel, no longer a spawn toggle (IsSpecialType lost Electric), and MigrateOldPackData removes a saved Electric pack, upgrades a saved Electrician pack and makes the Mirror pixel need it): its eight pixels are Electric plus the pack's own Resistor (45, 2 clicks, pays 5, weight 0.6, a conductor), Transistor (46, pays 5; its click makes the NEXT collected pixel pay transistorMultiplier (2) x), Fuse (47, pays 5; xfuseBonus (3) if collected within fuseSeconds (3) of appearing, 0.3x after) and Bulb (48, pays 6; x(1 + bulbPerConductor (0.1) x old conductor pixels, max 30, CountConductors)), looksVersion 33, and Wire (PixelType.Wire 42, 1 click, pays 2, weight 1.0, copper), Battery (43, 3 clicks, pays 12, weight 0.4, green) and Capacitor (44, pays 6, weight 0.35, blue) - all IsStandalone; they spawn only while the Electrician pack is in play or as guests (tree node 'Static charge' covers Electric); Electric / Wire / Battery / Resistor old pixels carry the device links (PixelClicker.IsConductorType, PixelElectricLinks.IsConductor), a Battery click sets off an Overcharge too, and a Capacitor pays 1 + stored charge x capacitorPayPerCharge (0.15; every other click stores one charge, cap capacitorChargeCap 40, PixelClicker.CapacitorCharge) then resets; looksVersion 32; CheckFarmBundle also unlocks all of them once the pack is bought, MigrateOldPackData gives an older saved pack its pixels; XP from clicks of any of them; its other perks / nodes (Overcharge+, Wired up, Power bank, Surge) are 'coming soon'. Offline Progression skill (id offline, PixelSkills.OfflineId, available once the Auto Clicker runs): choose it, then choose which pack's pixels to play with (SetPixelPack, buttons in the detail panel); PixelSkills.OfflineMode (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillOffline, per save) makes TrainingId the offline skill and OnCollected gives EVERY click's XP to it only (no XP for the pack in play); ActiveId is still the pixel pack. Its effects are NOT built yet (perks and tree nodes say 'coming soon'). Skill tree per skill (SkillPack.nodes = TreeNode id / name / description / requires / row / cost / implemented, defaults by DefaultNodes for Mining, Offline and Standard (Standard's tree: first point on Block Hold or Power Click, which are locked until then; branches Lucky stake -> High roller and Quick charge -> Aftershock; HasStandardNode), also filled into older serialized packs): one point per level above 1 (PointsAvailable = level - 1 - spent), unlocked nodes are the counters skill.<pack>.node.<id> (NodeUnlocked, CanUnlock, UnlockNode); the detail panel's 'Skill tree' button opens a separate skill tree window (BuildTreeWindow, TreeOpen, PixelWindows 15) to the right of the Skills window that stretches to the right screen edge and is as tall as the Skills window: nodes by depth x row with connector lines (RefreshTree), points label, node text and an Unlock button; it closes with the Skills window. The perk list is collapsible: one header button per level ('+ Level 2  COMING SOON'), the text under it when open (perkOpen, perkHeads/perkBodies, scroll view). A skill's description / replaces / spawns / XP-from text is a tooltip on an orange '!' box at the top left of its card (CreateInfoBox, PackTip, UpdateTip). Not built yet: real effects for perks / nodes, more packs, the XP bar on the main screen, a cycle-skill key, a Stats / Log summary, a HowToPlay paragraph (see Todo.txt).

Perk guests (Woodcutting level 5): Perk.guestType works like TreeNode.guestType: from that level the block is listed in the Toggles window (UnlockedGuests / GuestUnlocked also read perks) and can be switched on to spawn in every rotation (off until the player ticks it). Woodcutting level 5 = Wood ("Lumber stock"), added to saved packs by the Woodcutting perk migration.
--- docs/systems/PixelSkybox.md ---
PixelSkybox

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSkybox.cs

Sky styles (added by PixelClicker.Awake, implements IPixelLookSource): panoramas drawn in code (lat-long 2:1, same mapping as Unity's Skybox/Panoramic shader: u = 0.25 is +z) and set as RenderSettings.skybox (camera clearFlags -> Skybox; Classic restores the scene's skybox + camera background). Inspector list of SkyStyle (colours, sun elevation/azimuth/size, clouds, stars, width, pixelated, exposure, rotateSpeed, flashesPerMinute, seed; defaults by name): Classic, Clear Day, Sunset, Starry Night, Nebula, Aurora, Synthwave, Pixel Clouds, Storm (lightning). Removed skies (Synthwave, Alien World, Blocky Void, Black Hole, Spiral Galaxy, Ringed Planet: sky objects look wrong mirrored; RemovedStyleNames drops them from a saved scene list at Awake, their pattern code is dead). Panoramas and previews are cached per style (first pick of a big sky can hitch briefly). panoramicShader is filled in by OnValidate so builds keep the shader (else falls back to a solid camera colour + warning). updateAmbientLight (off) re-lights the scene from the sky. Everything below the horizon is the sky mirrored (Shade flips dir.y), so every sky fills the whole view (the menu shows no floor). More skies (PixelSkyboxExtra.cs, partial class, ShadeExtra / ExtraStyles, patterns appended to SkyPattern): weather (Rainy Day, Thunderstorm = Rain + flashes, Snowfall, Foggy Morning, Sandstorm, Rainbow Sky), space (Ringed Planet, Spiral Galaxy, Black Hole, Binary Suns) and cube world (Blocky Day / Night / Ember / Void: stepped gradients, square suns / moons, BlockClouds). Watcher (PixelSkyboxEye.cs, SkyPattern.EyeVoid, pitch black): in LateUpdate an eye (three code-textured quads far in front of the camera, so scene objects hide it) opens at a random spot in the upper sky, its slit pupil follows the cursor and it drifts a little towards it for eyeWatchMin..Max (6-14) s, blinks now and then, closes and stays away eyeHiddenMin..Max (10-28) s (eyeSize 0 = 0.16 of the screen height, disableEye; 0 = default). More skies: space variants without objects (6 Nebula colourings: Crimson / Emerald / Cobalt / Violet Haze / Golden Dust / Teal Void; SkyPattern.Starfield = Deep Space / Star Dust / Cold Stars, dense + coloured stars and a dusty band), Violet / Ice / Crimson Aurora, and Underwater / Deep Ocean (SkyPattern.Underwater, UnderwaterColor: caustic web overhead, swaying sun shafts, drifting specks, NOT mirrored; animated: IsAnimated styles are redrawn into their small (384 px) panorama about 12x a second from Update with shadeTime = skyClock, unscaled time, frozen in Time Stop). Rain (PixelSkyboxRain.cs, UpdateRain, disableRain, rainPerSecond 0 = 650 (x2 with lightning)): while a Rain pattern sky (Rainy Day, Thunderstorm) is on, a world-space stretched-billboard particle system falls through the scene, sized from the camera's distance to the cube. The Watcher's eye is fancier (PixelSkyboxEye.cs: heartbeat glow halo, bloodshot veins, fibrous iris with limbal ring / collarette / glints, a separate slit pupil that breathes and narrows when it stares at the cursor, lid line with tapering lashes). Test Grid (SkyPattern.TestGrid, TestGridColor): a NOT-mirrored black sky with a line every 15 degrees (thick every 45 azimuth / 30 elevation), the eight 45-degree azimuths in different colours (0 = +Z green, 90 = +X red), a white horizon, tinted below-horizon half, an orange disc (az 0 / el 30) and a white disc (az 90 / el 15) - for screenshots that diagnose orientation. Sky icons are their own little wide-angle pictures (DrawPreview: a 110-degree view towards the sun, tilted up, sampled through the same Shade; PreviewRect is the whole picture) instead of a crop of the panorama that missed most details. Picked in Settings (Display, "Sky" row) or the picker left of Play; saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.Skybox.
--- docs/systems/PixelSorterDevice.md ---
PixelSorterDevice

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSorterDevice.cs

(extends PixelPlacedDevice) Placed Sorter (device kind = Sorter): a ring (runtime-built mesh) around the cube facing the camera, a bendable pipe (constant-curvature tube mesh rebuilt by Parts.Apply) and three force buttons on the ring (click them; sorterForceMultipliers, selected one is brighter; screen-space hit test in OnTick; sound sorter_button). Placing = right-click in inventory; step 1 preview ring + straight pipe, scroll / Q / E turns it, left-click confirms; step 2 a cone (sorterBendConeDegrees) shows, the pipe end follows the mouse inside it (TryGetBend), left-click inside the cone places, right-click goes back to step 1 (PixelConsumables.UpdateSorterPlacement). While working, PixelClicker.SpawnFallingCopy asks static PixelSorterDevice.Current.TryRoute and spawns each old pixel at the pipe mouth with a velocity along the exit heading x force (slight wobble) instead of the random pop (not for fly-away tiers). Streamed pixels have no collision in the air (OldPixelStream, PixelStream.cs: the collider is a trigger, so the pixels of a stream don't knock each other / the ring / the cube about; each raycasts down, and on reaching the floor is placed on it, nudged away from pixels already lying there, ignores collisions with what it still overlaps, and only then becomes solid; its landing triggers Water splashes / Glass shatters via OldPixelSplash.Land / OldPixelShatter.StreamLand; Bank-spat pixels are unchanged). Settings are in the Device class ("Sorter only" fields); default sorter is auto-added like the fan. Adjustable after switching on: drag the pipe's END to bend it (relative drag, within sorterBendConeDegrees), drag the RING to turn the pipe round the cube (Drag.Bend / Drag.Aim, FindHover, UpdateDrag), and drag the force slider - a thin track beside the pipe with a knob and an 'x1.35' label (Parts.UpdateSlider / ProjectSlider) - to set ANY force between sorterSliderMin (0 = 0.2) and sorterSliderMax (0 = 3); the three buttons still jump to their presets (forceValue, SetForceValue, saved as PlacedState.forceValue). While the mouse is over any handle PixelSorterDevice.PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks.
--- docs/systems/PixelTitleScreen.md ---
PixelTitleScreen

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTitleScreen.cs

Start screen (added by PixelClicker.Awake): freezes the game (Time.timeScale = 0, PixelTitleScreen.Showing; auto clicker and the intro wait) behind a live blurred picture of the scene (each LateUpdate the main camera is rendered into a small RT and blitted down a chain: liveDivisor, lightBlurDivisor, strongBlurDivisor, no post-processing; the cube keeps spinning because PixelClicker.AnimDelta is unscaled while the title shows) with a runtime-drawn pixel-art "PIXEL CLICKER" logo (5x7 bitmap font, bevelled cells + shadow, logoColors, bobs), a black bar across the middle with a Play button and, in ONE row under it (together as wide as Play), three icon buttons drawn in code by PixelMenuIcons (32 px pixel art): Load = a floppy disk, Settings = a cog, Quit = an open door (red); MakeIconButton, hovering one shows its name in the small tip (hoveredText). Load / Settings call PixelPauseMenu.OpenLoadFromTitle/OpenSettingsFromTitle (the pause menu opens its view with openedFromTitle; Back / Escape return to the title; the title canvas drops to sorting 400 and hides its Foreground while the pause menu is open; the pause menu doesn't touch timeScale while the title shows). Play: buttons shrink + logo fades, bar thins while the strong then light blur fade out, then after startDelay time resumes. The component now stays after Play (canvas hidden, textures released): PixelTitleScreen.ReturnToMenu() (pause menu Quit > Main menu) closes open windows, runs PixelCameraIntro.PlayReverse, then Open(true) fades the title back in by running the Play transition (ApplyTransition(t)) backwards. Escape does nothing while it shows (PixelWindows entry). It shows again after Restart (scene reload = fresh launch); showTitleScreen turns it off. The hint system's startup quiet period uses Time.timeSinceLevelLoad (frozen while the title shows), and the intro tips start introDelay seconds after Play. Sound game_start. Bar glow (shared helper PixelBarGlow.cs; the HUD's top / bottom bars get the same glow on their inner edges, fields in PixelHud; player switch Settings > 'Neon bar glow' = PixelHud.BarGlow, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarGlow, synced per save, covers the title bar too) (barGlow, glowSize/InnerSize/Strength/ColorSpeed/EdgeOffset/Saturation: BuildBarGlow / UpdateBarGlow, soft gradient strips outside + inside both bar edges and a bright LED line, hue cycles slowly on unscaled time, fades as the bar thins). Small floor (right of Play) and sky (left of Play) pickers: drop-downs (a PixelUIKit.CreateDropdown listing every look, small caption above, fade in while the mouse is near or the list is open; showLookPickers, lookBoxHeight, lookDropdownWidth, ...; refreshed each frame when the look changes). Settings > Floor style and Sky each have a pop-out button (PixelPauseMenu.OpenLookPopout).

Background crossfade: UpdateBackgroundFade (called first in UpdateBlur) notices when 'Live background' or 'Blur background' is switched, keeps the picture that was showing as a snapshot (fadeTexture, shown by fadeImage above the blur layers) and fades it away over about 1.1 s, so the floor / sky comes in gradually instead of swapping at once. The in-game floor itself already fades with PixelFloor.FloorVisibility. The blur slider toggle ignores the click that comes when you let go of the slider (Toggle.sliderUsed + a pointer-over-slider check).

Logo styles (PixelLogoView.cs)

The logo is no longer a texture: PixelLogoView builds it from ~200 little bevelled cells (body + shadow + glow image each) animated every frame. 20 styles (Classic, Rainbow Wave, Neon, Shatter, Matrix, Inferno, Frost, Glitch, Jelly, Flip, Blocks, Plasma, Synthwave, Gold, Ghost, Storm, Shockwave, Disco, Ocean, Hologram), each a static function plus an intro kind; invisible arrows at the far left / right of the logo fade in on hover and switch style (saved: PixelClicker.Setting.LogoStyle). PixelTitleScreen.BuildLogo only creates the container; it still bobs / scales (localScale) / fades (SetAlpha) it. Add a style = one function + one line in Styles.

Dev Record session button (2026-10-16): in dev builds (PixelDevTools.Available) a 'Record session' button sits at the top left of the start screen (showRecordButton, recordButtonSize, BuildRecordButton), hidden until the mouse comes near like the floor / sky pickers (it rides the same lookPickers fade with no look source). It starts the session recorder, then reads 'Stop & save m:ss'.

Launch intro (2026-10-16, PixelTitleIntro.cs, partial class): at launch the screen starts black, folds down into the middle bar once the blurred background is ready, the bar thumps, then the glow, the dropping logo and the buttons / widgets (popping in one after another) appear; any key or click skips it. playIntro and the timings are in the Inspector under 'Launch intro'. Not played when coming back from the game or when a New game auto-starts.

Menu cube show (2026-10-16, PixelTitleCubeShow.cs + PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs): while the menu shows, the cube becomes a random pixel type every cubeShowSeconds (1 s; look only, PixelClicker.ShowLookPreview, no events). On Play the menu cube pops off as a husk (a mesh / material snapshot thrown out and shrunk away in huskPopSeconds, unscaled time, not a real husk) and the player's block materializes (EndLookPreview), then the camera intro plays. cubeShow off = the real block on the menu.
--- docs/systems/PixelTitleWanderers.md ---
PixelTitleWanderers

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTitleWanderers.cs

Vendors wandering the main menu (component added to the title screen in PixelTitleScreen.Build, layer under foreground, behind the bar / logo / buttons): a small tick-box toggle at the bottom left that is invisible until the mouse is over it (a third, 'Blur background' (while it is ticked its text label is replaced by a blur-strength slider: PixelTitleWanderers.BlurAmount, global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleBlurAmount, 1 = full blur, read by PixelTitleScreen.StrongDivisorForAmount) (the 'Display floor' tick box is no longer on the menu: it is in Settings > Display right under 'Floor style', same global pref; the menu's 'Pets Wander' toggle sits directly above the vendors toggle; the same NPC never walks twice at once, Walker.key) (PixelTitleWanderers.BlurBackground, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleBlurBackground, default ON), stacked above the live one: OFF = no blur - with the live scene the blur layers are simply hidden and the real scene shows sharp behind the title, without it the floor picture is blitted into a full-resolution RT (PixelTitleScreen.UpdateSharp); the wandering vendors and the toggles fade out the moment Play is pressed (PixelTitleWanderers.FadeOut, whatever the settings); a fourth, 'Display floor' (PixelTitleWanderers.DisplayFloor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DisplayFloor, default OFF (the floor is invisible unless ticked), global): OFF = PixelFloor never draws the floor in the game (FloorOff: visibility target 0, no flat base, props stay; the renderer is only forceRenderingOff, so the collider still catches pixels) and only the sky shows; a second one stacked above it, 'Live background' (PixelTitleWanderers.LiveBackground, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleLiveBackground, default ON = the live blurred game scene behind the title as before; OFF = no camera render: PixelTitleScreen.UpdateBlur blits the floor style's picture (PixelFloor.TryGetTitlePicture: the active - animated - texture, tiled 6 across for repeating styles, 1x for fitted / stretched ones, a flat tint for Classic) into the same low-res live RT, so the usual blur chain turns it into a blurred full-screen floor behind the black bar); both appear while the mouse is over either) (PixelTitleWanderers.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleWanderers, global, default off). While on, every arriveEvery seconds (max maxAtOnce at once) a vendor walks in from the left or right edge along the bottom of the screen (feet feetHeight up, walkerHeight 230 tall, walkSpeed), makes stops stops at random spots (standing pauseRange s, sometimes turning to look round, walking bob / sway, mirrored to face his direction) and walks off the nearer-random side; with stayChance (25%) a vendor 'stays for a while' (3x the stops, 2x the pauses). Cast = Cubie (PixelGuideVendor.BuildWalker, which preserves the visit's own pupil / arm / gift references) and every PixelVisitorMinigame (BuildWalker: a holder + 'Art' child drawn by the vendor's own DrawVisitor); the Egg and Bean cats too (whether or not they were met). All of them vanish when the title screen ends. Unscaled time.

Pets & UFO (added later): a fifth stacked toggle 'Pets' (PixelTitleWanderers.PetsEnabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitlePets, default off, global) shows the CURRENT save's pets hopping along the bottom of the menu (UpdatePets: a random maxPets (6) of PixelPets.TitlePets(), rebuilt whenever the owned list / outfits change; each pet is a hidden studio - PixelPets.BuildTitleModel = the real-look pixel + its outfit under a pivot, an orthographic camera into a transparent RenderTexture shown by a RawImage, re-rendered every frame - hopping with squash and stretch every petRest s) The UFO is NOT behind that toggle: if the UFO Helper is bought in the save and not switched off in its own Toggles tab switch (PixelRobotWorker.Active), a block-drawn flying saucer drifts along the top (UpdateUfo, BuildUfoArt). The UFO abducts: every abductEvery (0 = 14-32) s, if a vendor is walking or a pet is hopping on the ground (on screen), it flies over one (UpdateAbduction: Ab.Fly at 1.8x speed, Ab.Beam), switches on a translucent green beam (BuildBeam / UpdateBeam, a child of the saucer) and pulls the victim up over abductSeconds (0 = 2.4) while it shrinks and spins (Walker.abducted / Hopper.abducted make Step / StepHopper skip it); it is then removed (FinishAbduction; abducted pets come back after 20 s via petRespawnAt, vendors just keep arriving). disableAbduction turns it off; clearing the UFO or pressing Play aborts it (AbortAbduction). Everything is removed when the menu ends; the Egg and Bean cats now walk in whether or not they were met.
--- docs/systems/PixelToggles.md ---
PixelToggles

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelToggles.cs

Toggles window: a button docked to the middle of the right edge (PixelHud.Dock with anchor (1, 0.5) = side docking, slides sideways) stays hidden until the Auto Clicker is bought (hideUntilAutoClicker) and opens a window with a group selector (Pixels / Upgrades / Minigames / Vendors / Pets (skill packs are never listed here - the Skills window chooses the pack in play; do not add skill-pack switches (Mining / Farming / Electrician mode) to Toggles) (Vendors = the visitor minigames PixelVisitorMinigame, filtered out of Minigames; tab names are tabNames): a drop-down while docked beside the open shop, where the window copies the currency panel's width and the shop's height (FitBesideShop), tab buttons when it stands alone; besideShop, ApplyGroupControl) over a scrolling list of tick-box rows (no visible scroll bar: PixelScrollBarFade.hideBar; the wheel and bottom fade still work). The tabs are pixel-art icons drawn in code (PixelTabIcons: three cubes in a triangle, a machine with a green up arrow, a joystick + Pac-Man, a market stall with a person, a cat + a dog), used both beside the shop and standing alone (the drop-down is no longer shown); hovering one shows its name in a small tip (UpdateTabTip). Pixels = SetSpawnEnabled for switchable unlocked tiers; Upgrades = PixelAutoClicker.UserDisabled, PixelGrab.UserDisabled, PixelTimeStop.UserDisabled and PixelCombo.UserDisabled (all saved by PixelSaveGame; Working = bought and not switched off); Minigames = PixelMinigame.UserDisabled. Rows are sorted alphabetically (Collect result sorted by label). Every tab with two or more switches starts with a 'Toggle all' row (toggleAllText; ticked while everything is on; skipAll entries are modes rather than on / off items; no skill pack is listed in Toggles any more). This replaced the tick boxes that used to sit on shop rows. Buying the Auto Clicker pack no longer switches it on: PixelShop.TryBuy sets PixelAutoClicker.UserDisabled = true and calls static PixelToggles.ShowHint() (a hint box next to the slid-out tab telling the player to tick Auto Clicker under Upgrades; closes on "Got it" or when the window opens; the tab stays out while it shows). Dev "Skip intro" / "Unlock all" still switch it on. The pause menu adds this component if missing.
--- docs/systems/PixelTreasureMap.md ---
PixelTreasureMap

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTreasureMap.cs / PixelWhaleFight.cs / PixelOceanSynth.cs

Fishing's SPECIAL blocks (a rarer tier beside the base blocks; the same idea is on the to-do for every skill; both are in the Angler's Pack, packDataVersion 23 (24 Driftwood, 25 whale 15-50 clicks), looksVersion 48, PixelType.Bottle 73 / Whale 74, IsFishBlock, BlockSurface.Bottle/Whale drawn by PixelBlockTextures.BottleGlass(level,max) / WhalePixel). Message in a Bottle (4 clicks, pays 8, weight 0.05, cracks spread per hit): the break calls PixelTreasureMap.Begin (sound bottle_break; queued one at a time): glass bursts, a UI scroll (canvas 660) flies from the cube to the middle, unrolls between two rollers over a code-drawn parchment map (MakeMapTexture: stains, burnt edges, island, palms, compass, you-are-here cube, dotted path and a big red X placed from the real screen positions of the cube and the spot), is read for readSeconds (1.8), then shrinks into the spot, where an X (two red planks + dirt patch, Mark) appears on the ground (PickSpot: spotNearest..spotFarthest 3-7 pixel widths from the cube, in view, via PixelBait.FindGround; up to maxMarks 3, fade after markSeconds). Clicking the X (Hot blocks cube clicks via ExternalClickBlock) digs (dig_x, dirt FX, a mound grows, the X shrinks); after digClicks (6) clicks PixelClicker.GiveFishingHaul pays haulPixels (10 +-) good fishing pixels at once (bait most often, then seaweed / shells / fish / pearls) and fountains the same old pixels out for show (dig_done). Right-click the bottle = PixelClicker.DismissBottle (no map, no pay). disableMap. Whale (random 15-50 clicks via randomClicks, pays 20 x RewardMultiplier(need) = clicks x (1 + clicks x rewardGrowth 0.05), weight 0.015): the first MANUAL hit starts PixelWhaleFight (not while a takeover / Dash-type minigame is busy; the auto clicker pauses meanwhile, PixelAutoClicker): the whale stays almost still (drifts at driftSpeed 0.03 screen heights per second) for 2-3 clicks (restClicksMin/Max, the hooking click counts as the first), then dashes (runSpeed 1.3, up to runSeconds 0.9, to a spot at least minRunDistance 0.3 screen heights away, sound whale_yank) and rests again; it no longer flees the mouse or yanks on a timer, a thin fishing line (a PixelTube mesh, unlit vertex colours) is drawn from the cursor (on the whale's depth plane) to the whale with a gentle sag and ripple, fully invisible within lineHiddenWithin (1 pixel width) of the cursor and fading in over lineFadeLength (1.8); fightSeconds (80) without landing it = PixelClicker.WhaleGotAway; landing it plays whale_caught + rings (OnCaught), stats whales.hooked/caught, bottles.broken, treasure.dug. PixelOceanSynth makes all their sounds in code (pixel_bottle, pixel_whale, bottle_break, map_unroll, map_reveal, dig_x, dig_done, whale_hook, whale_yank, whale_caught, whale_escape; PixelAudio.SynthFor asks it first). The block tooltip also shows 'Clicks left'.
--- docs/systems/PixelUI.md ---
PixelUI

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelUI.cs

"Inventory" toggle box (top-left) (rows = swatch + plain light name + right-aligned amount (Currency tab: swatch + number only by default, the name in the hover tip, click a row to switch all rows to name + number: PixelUI.CurrencyShowNames, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryNames, PixelClickable) via SetRow / RowParts, 'Pixels' suffix stripped, ~95% opaque frame, ONE fixed height maxPanelHeight on every tab; infinite resources show an infinity sign via PixelConsumables.OwnedText) with Currency / Consumables tabs (Consumables has a half-width category dropdown with a search box beside it (consumableSearchField, SetCategoryRowActive; searching lists every held consumable whose name matches, ignoring the category); opening it closes the Log via PixelUI.SetInventoryOpen / PixelLog.SetLogOpen - they never show together) (right-click a potion to drink), active-potion timer and a tier guide ("Click X more Y pixels..." / "Y pixel unlocked") at top of screen; Currency tab: per-tier counts, cursor +N popups (manual) / cube popups (auto), vacuum +X total popup, per-entry vacuum +X indicators. Builds in Start() so shop-added tiers are included. The tier guide text ("Click X more...") stays hidden until the new-game intro tips have been closed (PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide). The Inventory has a third Materials tab (RefreshMaterials): one line per PixelMinigame that overrides MaterialName / MaterialAmount / MaterialColor and has some (Stardust, Meteor Chunks, Bomb Parts, Ghost's Ectoplasm - 1-4 per caught ghost, ectoplasmMin/Max, saved via ExtraValue; no use yet, it is on the to-do). Tier lines auto-size down (no wrap) for huge numbers; the Log's rows do the same and FitAmount starts the amount box right after the name's text. Simplified (pinned) Inventory: a small toggle button at the window's top left (four-squares icon, green while on; it pins the tab you are on: PixelUI.CompactMode 1 = Currency, 2 = Consumables, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryCompact, global) drops the title / X / tabs and shows only swatch + number rows (currency, or every consumable you hold as swatch + xN; a plain left click uses it, hover shows the name) in a box as wide as its widest line (FitCompactWidth: swatch + the text at full size, measured with a hidden label; a compactWidth typed in the Inspector overrides it) and as tall as its lines; it ignores Escape (it is not a registered window). It is a movable window now (like the Skills tracker): drag it anywhere (PixelPopoutDrag on the box; the list's ScrollRect is disabled while it doesn't scroll so the drag gets through), two small buttons stacked at its top right - minimise (-/+, only the button row stays) and stick (padlock, can't be dragged); position / minimised / stuck are global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Inventory.CompactX/Y/Small/Stuck (UpdatePinnedPlacement, KeepCompactOnScreen; the spot the player chose is kept as compactDesired and re-applied every frame before the on-screen clamp, so a window that was pushed up to fit when maximised returns to its spot when minimised again - the skill tracker does the same with trackerDesired); un-pinning puts the window back in its normal place. It no longer fades away when other windows open: it stays visible BENEATH them (its canvas sorts below the Shop / Log / Bank / Skills canvases; SetInventoryOpen(false) is ignored unless force; the Inventory dock button and the toggle still close it for good). The Inventory key still cycles currency <-> consumables before closing (InventoryKeyCycle).

Inventory currency groups (PixelUI.Refresh, CurrencyGroup)

The Currency tab lists the pixel rows in GROUPS under foldable headers (click a header, '- PURE' / '+ PURE'; folded keys saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryFolded, global): Pure = White..Blue (the base colours, the shop's money), Composite: <Skill> = a skill pack's goods (PixelSkills.PackOfType; Wood belongs to Woodcutting), one group per skill, Exotic = everything else (Glass, Mirror, Vacuum, Obsidian, Luminescent, Ghost, Meteor, Solar, Singularity, Dragon Cubes...). The Ultra row stays above all of them. The pinned (compact) Inventory shows the same order with no headers. Naming rule: 'Pure' is reserved for the base colours; there is no 'Raw' / 'Refined' ladder (refined results would be crafted items in Materials, not currency tiers).

The Consumables tab opens on its Devices sub-tab by default (PixelUI.consumableSubTab starts at 1).
--- docs/systems/PixelUIKit.md ---
PixelUIKit

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelUIKit.cs

Shared static helpers for code-built UI: EnsureEventSystem, CreateCanvas, CreateText, CreateButton, Stretch, CreateDropdown (arrow = a drawn triangle sprite), CreateInputField (white 3 px blinking caret, placeholder hidden while focused), AddSearchIcon(field) (magnifier inside a search box; used by every search box), Caps(label|button) (UpperCase font style: every window title and tab label is shown in capitals), arrow buttons (UseArrowGlyph(button, pointLeft) swaps a "<" / ">" letter for a drawn triangle: the shop's Back button in the sub window (Pixel Bank / Auto Clicker upgrades), the floor / sky pickers, the pet outfit arrows), UI colour (PixelUIKit.UiColorOn / UiHue / UiSat, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.UiColor/UiHue/UiSat, Settings > Interface, synced per save; there is no slider: an eyedropper button beside the 'Custom UI colour' tick box picks the colour from the screen (SetUiColorFromColor takes its hue and saturation, switches the colour on): Tinted(colour) keeps a window colour's brightness but gives it the chosen hue; StyleWindow remembers every window image so RefreshWindows() recolours them live), bar glow colour (PixelHud.GlowHue / GlowCycle, PixelBarGlow.EdgeColor(baseHue, time, speed, offset, sat)), boxes (PixelUIKit.StyleBox(image, inset): rounded, softly shaded list rows / cards with a thin lit rim, inset = a sunken frame for 3D pictures; used by the Shop rows, currency rows, cards and picture frames), fancy windows (PixelUIKit.StyleWindow(image, colour) gives every window background a code-drawn rounded 9-slice frame with the colour baked in - gradient, bright cyan rim, inward glow; always on (the old flat-window switch and its Settings toggle were removed); new windows should call StyleWindow instead of setting Image.color), fancy buttons (always on, the flat-button switch was removed: CreateButton uses a code-drawn grey-scale 9-sliced ButtonSprite - rounded corners, dark outline, gradient, light top strip - tinted by the button colour; the shop's own button builder calls PixelUIKit.StyleButton too), close buttons (a button labelled "X" draws a cross sprite via UseCloseGlyph; the shop's own CreateButton does the same), TickSprite() (code-drawn check mark used as the fill of every tick box), CreateScrollView/UpdateScrollView (masked scroll list + auto-hiding scroll bar). Every scroll bar uses PixelUIKit.StyleScrollBar(track, handle) (slim ScrollBarWidth 10, rounded dark track + lighter rounded handle): new scroll bars must call it. Bottom fade: while a list scrolls and isn't at its end, PixelScrollBarFade fades the ROWS (direct children of the scroll content, each given its own CanvasGroup: alpha follows the row's centre height above the view's bottom edge, fadeHeight 44 x 1.6) plus a bobbing chevron below the list; nothing is drawn over the window and the mask is untouched (an earlier soft-mask version let rows ghost over the tabs at the top). Not for drop-down templates or views without a RectMask2D. StyleScrollBar also adds PixelScrollBarFade (CanvasGroup alpha: visible while scrolling / hovered / held, holdSeconds 0.45 after the last movement, then fades in fadeSeconds 0.18). UI scripts delegate to it instead of carrying their own copies.

Custom UI button colour
Settings > Interface > 'Custom UI button colour' (tick box + eyedropper, PixelUIKit.ButtonColorOn / SetButtonColorFromColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.UiButtonColor / UiButtonHue / UiButtonSat, per save via PixelSettingsSync). PixelUIKit.StyleButton registers every button image and its base colour; RefreshButtons recolours them all at once (neutral buttons take the picked hue, strongly coloured ones such as red Quit keep theirs; a button whose colour something else changed, like a selected row, is left alone). Replaces the retired 'Pixel-assemble menu buttons' effect (PixelDockFx).
--- docs/systems/PixelVisitorMinigame.md ---
PixelVisitorMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelVisitorMinigame.cs / PixelTinkererMinigame.cs / PixelWizardMinigame.cs / PixelEntrepreneurMinigame.cs / PixelFarmerMinigame.cs

Visitor minigames (no shop packs any more - started by PixelGuideVendor; every minInterval..maxInterval s after firstVisitDelay (+ StartDelayOffset), AllowAnother; dev Spawn/Despawn work). PixelVisitorMinigame (abstract) holds everything: a code-drawn block-built visitor (DrawVisitor(art) in a 300x400 space via Block) waits for a calm moment (CalmForVisit: no Got it box showing or queued (PixelHints.TipsPending), no dragon show / wish / pet popup, not paused / Time Stop; the check runs every frame once the timer is up) and for the shared cooldown (sharedCooldownSeconds 180 after ANY vendor leaves, static nextAnyVisitTime / anyVisiting), visits every visitMinSeconds..visitMaxSeconds (480-900 s; renamed fields so old serialized 300-600 are dropped), then darkens the screen (visitDarkness) and stops time (Freeze, Time.timeScale 0, restored by Unfreeze; left alone if already stopped) from the moment they appear, slides in from the right edge (slideSeconds) and a speech box greets the player with an offer at discountFraction (0.8) off (Wares = items where IsWare(item), PriceOf = ItemCosts x (1 - discount), min 1 per line, paid like the shop incl. minigame currencies). Accept opens the wares window (name, discounted cost coloured by affordability, owned / capacity, Buy x1, Full at the hold limit; RefreshWares), Reject or Escape (PixelWindows priority 150) sends them away; time resumes as they slide out. Texts are Inspector fields (empty = the visitor's Default*). Every vendor is a CUBE character now (Cubie's shape): CubeBody(a, face, top, side, shoe) draws the legs / shoes / arms / shaded cube and CubeEyes the eyes, and each vendor adds their trade on top - Wizard (purple, pointy star hat, long beard, glowing staff), Tinkerer (copper, goggles, cap, gear backpack, wrench), Farmer (leaf green, straw hat, sideburns, wheat stalk, shotgun, seed sack), Entrepreneur (navy suit cube, tie, briefcase, coin cube), the Prospector (orange, hard hat, pickaxe), and the cats Egg (orange cube cat holding the egg) and Bean (grey tabby cube cat with a sun hat and bean pouch). A new visitor = a subclass overriding Id, IsWare, DrawVisitor, DefaultName/Greeting/WaresTitle + EnsureMinigame lines (no shop pack: PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors starts it). Tinkerer sells consumable devices, devicesOffered (3) random ones per visit with stockMin..stockMax (1-3) each; Wizard sells potions (every potion whose pixel is unlocked or owned; combo potions are craft-only) but only potionsOffered (3) random ones per visit with stockMin..stockMax (1-3) bottles each (base hooks OfferedWares / StockMin / StockMax, per-visit stock, 'Sold out' button, 'Stock n' text); Farmer (PixelFarmerMinigame, id farmer, block-drawn: straw hat, overalls, pitchfork, seed sack) sells SEEDS (IsWare = seed items of unlocked pixel types) seedsOffered (5) random ones per visit; stock by rarity via the StockFor(item) hook (spawn weight >= 1: commonStock 10-30, 0.3-1: uncommonStock 4-10, rarer: rareStock 1-3); with dragonSeedChance (5%) per visit he also brings a Dragon Seed (ModifyWares hook, listed first, stock 1, only while the player lacks a Dragon Cube; Highlighted(item) hook -> PixelPulseGlow: the row pulses gold with a glowing outline and yellow name): a Seed device with seedType DragonCube1 as marker (PixelConsumables.IsDragonSeed, typed costs 500K White + 250K Black, hold 3, not in the shop's Seeds card); planting it picks PixelClicker.PickMissingDragonCube() (a Dragon Cube the player doesn't hold; refuses when all seven are held) and the sprout's pop-off gives that cube straight away (AddCurrency, first-find announcement included; the flying pixel is for show, amount 0). He is drawn with black hair, wispy facial hair, a shotgun held upright at his side and a wheat stalk in his mouth; when he brings a Dragon Seed a second speech box (dragonAsideText, base AsideText / asidePanel, 'Next' button) shows after you accept, and the Dev Tools tick box 'Farmer always has dragon seeds' (PixelFarmerMinigame.DevAlwaysDragonSeeds, pref Dev.FarmerSeeds) forces the seed on every visit; Pixel Entrepreneur (PixelEntrepreneurMinigame, id entrepreneur) overrides RollTrades() instead of IsWare: offerCount (3) pixel-for-pixel deals, each asks minPayFraction..maxPayFraction of a held pixel type (>= minHeld) and gives another unlocked type at a rarity-based rate (UnitValue = 1 / (spawnWeight x amountPerClick / clicksToCollect)) times a random luck minLuck..maxLuck; the base builds a trade row (Trade, DoTrade, button 'Trade' then 'Sold') when RollTrades returns a list. Counters <id>.bought, shop.items; sounds <id>_spawn (+ automatic <id>_click).

Peek (2026-10-15)

Every visitor (all PixelVisitorMinigame subclasses: Tinkerer, Wizard, Entrepreneur, Farmer, Lumberjack, Prospector, Egg, Bean; Cubie is separate and keeps his full visits) now PEEKS first (usePeek, on): they slide ~40% in from the right edge, leaning in, with a small speech bubble (peekText, '{0}' = name) and an X, while the game keeps running (no dim, no freeze, not registered with PixelWindows yet). Clicking the visitor or the bubble starts the normal full visit from there (screen darkens, time stops, greeting, wares). The X, or peekSeconds (20) without an answer (time in a menu doesn't count, PixelCalm.InMenu), sends them away: an event-log line (missedLog) and they come back after missedReturnFactor (0.5) of the normal wait (the shared cooldown is shortened the same way). The click catcher (a clear Image + Button on the visitor) is removed as soon as the peek ends.
--- docs/systems/SkillBlocks.md ---
SkillBlocks

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Farming pixels / Wood / Rock

Wood is part of the Woodcutting skill now (see the Woodcutting row; PixelSkills.IsOptIn is false for everything, the old opt-in switch code stays unused). Farmer's Pack (testing price 10 White; replaces the separate Seed and Water packs, which MigrateOldPackData drops; their tiers stay unlocked in old saves) rewards eight farm types (PixelClicker.IsFarm): Dirt (36, one click; old Dirt pixels near a sprout add growth speed, dirtSeedBoost, in SolarSeedBoostAt), Seed and Water (the existing pixels, no longer sold alone), Fertilizer (37; its click calls Fertilize = an instant growth jump for every sprout / Seed pet on the field), Pollen (38; floaty look physics; an old Pollen within pollenReachPixels of a popping sprout doubles its pixel's pay, PollenNear), Wheat (39; payout x (1 + wheatPerSprout x sprouts, max wheatMaxSprouts)), Weevil (40; while it is the pixel ON the cube, UpdateWeevil makes it eat one growing sprout every weevilBiteSeconds, SeedSprout.EatOne, until you click it) and Golden Fruit (41; a sprout grows one with fruitSproutChance 3% once unlocked, pays 40). The eight new types are IsStandalone (no potions / seeds / crafting / pets / achievements / Value upgrades); looksVersion 28 adds their looks; a sprout popping off gives Farming XP (PixelSkills.GrewSprout).

Seed shell rework

Seed block: a glossy ridged seed POD (PixelLook.blockSurface Seed + blob rounded mesh, PixelBlockTextures.SeedShell(level, max): ridges, dimples, a seam; cracks spread per hit with a green light leaking out; RoundedCubeMesh now has UVs per face; looksVersion 41, which also redoes Wood = rough bark on a rounded block and Stone = natural fractured rock with lichen). A cracked shell plants 1-5 seedlings (seedlingsMin/Max, 0 = 1 / 5; replaces seedsPerShell) that grow only plant pixels (PixelClicker.IsPlant = Wheat, Pollen, Golden Fruit, Wood; RandomPlantPixelTier by spawn weight, falls back to any growable type when none is unlocked; SeedSprout.PlantsOnly()). Bought seeds and the Seed pet are unchanged (any pixel).

Woodcutting (PixelSkills.WoodcuttingId, PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock)

Planned rework: randomly arriving trees with designed shapes, level gating, XP curve and cross-skill upgrades are designed in WoodcuttingProgression.md (not built yet).

The first, simple skill (listed right under Standard / Offline): bought as the cheap Lumberjack's Pack (the old Wood Pixel pack renamed by packDataVersion 17; 250 of each pure pixel since packDataVersion 38 (renamed from the Woodcutter's Pack; was 150 White, then 250 White); CreateWoodPixelPack). Blocks (enum 51-56 appended after GasPocket): Wood (34, 2 clicks, pays 3), Bark (51, rough dark jagged wood, 1 click, pays 2), Sap (52, translucent amber blob, pays 4; its old pixel is sticky: bounce 0, high friction), Leaf (53, floaty green flake, pays 1; old pixel drifts down slowly), Heartwood (54, rare, 4 clicks, pays 25, glowing red-brown), Twig (55, the FILLER, pays 1) and the Hornet Nest (56, the HAZARD, 5 clicks, pays 0: when it goes off, hornets (PixelPestSwarm.Scatter) chase off every old pixel within gasBlastWidths of the cube, no payout, and Woodcutting XP drops 10%; right-click defuse works like the other hazards). Looks reuse the Wood bark texture with tints (looksVersion 44), new FarmAmbient kinds Leaves / Sap / Hornets, break bursts in PixelFarmFx.Break, sounds wood_chop + pixel_<type> (PixelSynth.Wood(kind, seed)), Pixel Guide texts, bonus-bank 'good' pixels = every wood block but Twig (PixelClicker.IsPlant no longer includes Wood; the Inventory groups them as 'Composite: Woodcutting'). Perks: level 2 Wood Chipper (implemented: Device.shopSkillId / shopSkillLevel on the chipper open it in the shop and the shop search at Woodcutting 2 even though it is vendorOnly; PixelConsumables.ItemVendorOnly checks the level, ItemSkillGated keeps the Tinkerer from selling it) with a tutorial popup (Perk.noticeText, queued once per skill / level by PixelSkills.QueuePerkNotices, shown by UpdatePerkNotices when calm and tips are on; it points at Consumables and the shop search), level 3 Seasoned wood (old wood pixels last woodcuttingLifespanBonus 0 = 50% longer: PixelSkills.SkillLifetimeFactor read by PixelClicker.LifetimeFactor), level 4 Sap collector and level 6 Heart of the forest (coming soon). Saved packs are migrated (the old 'Sharp axe' level 2 perk is replaced). Skill tree: the first node is axe ('Unlock the axe to get the job done yourself', coming soon) - the axe itself is always available for now; the first person axe will be tied to this node later. baseXp 60, xpGrowth 1.45 (quicker than the others).

Hazard blocks (PixelType.Pest 49 = Farming, GasPocket 50 = Mining; PixelClicker.IsHazard, TriggerHazard, UpdateHazardExpiry, PixelSkills.TryGetHazard / ApplyHazardPenalty / PenaltyFactor)

Negative blocks in the skill packs: a tough block (Pest 6 hits, Gas Pocket 5, pays nothing) that makes up a small share of the spawns (SkillPack.hazardBaseShare 0 = 3% at level 1, hazardPerLevelShare 0.2% per level, hazardMaxShare 8%; weights worked out in PixelClicker.SpecialInfo together with the filler). A few taps are harmless; the hit that would break it sets it off instead of paying (CollectInternal -> TriggerHazard): the Pest kills EVERY sprout (SeedSprout.KillAll), the Gas Pocket explodes (FireBurstFx + old pixels within gasBlastWidths (4) old-pixel widths vanish, no payout). Left alone it leaves after hazardSeconds (7). Right-click defuse: a RIGHT click on a hazard on the cube (PixelClicker.Update -> DefuseHazard, same minClickInterval) counts towards clearing it safely: it needs clicksToCollect + the hits already put in (5 to break, 2 hits in = 7 right clicks; shown as a rising '3/7', DefusePopup); done = no explosion / swarm, no penalty, no payout, the cube rolls a new pixel (hazards.defused). The auto clicker never clicks a hazard (CollectInternal returns for automatic unless the static HazardAutoClear is on - the hook for a future upgrade), so the cube waits on it until you act or it goes away (the auto clicker earns only a quarter of the skill XP, which is the trade-off). XP penalty: each hazard set off takes penaltyStep (10%) off ALL skill XP (PixelSkills.AddXp x PenaltyFactor), cumulative while one is running, the penaltySeconds (60) timer restarting each time (Time.time, so it holds while paused); an event-log line says so. The pinned skill tracker shows the running penalty as a red line under it (trackerPenalty), only for the skill it hit (the penalty is per skill: PenaltyFactor(id), the pack in play when the hazard went off). Part of the packs' pixel lists / reward tiers (packDataVersion 14, looksVersion 42), tip hazard, stat hazards.triggered. Electrician has none yet.

Foraging pixels / Forager's Pack

Foraging (PixelSkills.ForagingId, second base gathering skill after Woodcutting; bought as the Forager's Pack, 150 White, CreateForagerPack, packDataVersion 18): PixelType.Berry 57, Mushroom 58, Herb 59, Truffle 60 (rare, 3 hits, pays 30), Weeds 61 (filler) and Toadstool 62 (hazard; IsForageBlock = Berry..Weeds, IsHazard / IsStandalone cover the Toadstool). A Toadstool that goes off (TriggerHazard) wipes the old pixels within gasBlastWidths in a spore cloud. looksVersion 45; FX in PixelFarmFx (Hit/Break cases, sound forage_pick), sounds PixelSynth.Forage(kind, seed) (pixel_berry..pixel_toadstool); IsGood = forage blocks except Weeds; one shop tip pixel_Berry. Mold replaced the Toadstool in the pack (PixelType.Mold 75, hazard + standalone, 8 clicks, packDataVersion 26 adds it to saved Forager's Packs, the Skills pack lists it and hazardType = "Mold"; the Toadstool type, look and code stay but nothing spawns it): see PixelMold.cs (zone that grows per click, infects fallen pixels, shake held pixels clean, right click only stops the spreading; also the glowing Berry / Mushroom / Herb / Truffle old pixels that glow from half their lifespan). Not built yet: perks' effects, a foraging vendor / machines, the later link to Seed / Farming.

Fishing / ore rework

Fishing (PixelSkills.FishingId, third base gathering skill; bought as the Angler's Pack, 150 White, CreateAnglerPack, packDataVersion 19): PixelType.Fish 66 (2 hits, pays 4), Seaweed 67, Shell 68, Pearl 69 (rare, 4 hits, pays 35), Driftwood 70 (filler), Jellyfish 71 (hazard: stings away the old pixels near the cube like the Toadstool). IsFishBlock = Fish..Driftwood; sounds PixelSynth.Fishing(kind, seed) + fish_splash; looksVersion 46; tip pixel_Fish. Mining ores reworked (same migration 19): Copper, Tin, Iron, Silver 63, Gold 64 and Bedrock 65 (a random 20-30 clicks via randomClicks, pays 150; packDataVersion 20) plus Obsidian, which moved INTO the Prospector's Pack (15 clicks, pays 80; the separate Obsidian Pixel pack and its table entry are gone, packs that needed it now need the Prospector's Pack, saved lists are remapped). Lead / Zinc / Nickel are retired (IsRetiredOre, never spawn, dropped from saved Mining packs; their enum values stay for saves). Obsidian is not IsOre (it keeps its own FX and is not standalone) but counts as a good Mining block and sells to the Prospector vendor; it no longer has a Toggles spawn switch.

Termite Hive, filler family (Woodcutting)
- The Hornet Nest became the Termite Hive (enum PixelType.HornetNest kept for saves; renamed in EnsureTier, packDataVersion 31). Breaking it (PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) calls PixelTermiteSwarm.Release: one termite per old pixel on the floor (not held, capped by TermiteMax), each runs to its pixel and a billboard bar over it counts down (TermiteEatSeconds); a left click on the pixel saves it (stats termites.pixels_saved), otherwise it is eaten (ReleaseOldPixel(rb,false), termites.pixels_eaten). Pesticide Block Hold still clears the hive in one click (clearsHazard). Sound termite_chitter (PixelHazardSynth).
- Woodcutting's filler is a family: Twig (twigs tangle texture), Pinecone (enum 92) and Moss (93) share the filler's spawn share evenly (SkillPack.extraFillerTypes, PixelSkills.GetFillerFamily/IsFillerType, PixelClicker.SwapFiller; extras have spawn weight 0). The Shuffle bonus treats any family member as the filler. looksVersion 61 replaced the Twig / Leaf / Hive looks (new BlockSurface.Twigs/Pinecone/Moss/LeafBlock/Hive, PixelBlockTextures).

2026-10-15 changes

- Foraging is a TRULY RANDOM rotation (SkillPack.uniformRotation: every block of the pack in play, filler and hazards included, equally likely; PixelSkills.TryGetUniformPool, used by PixelClicker.PickSpawnTierBase and the spawn-share readout). Two hazards (hazardType may list several, "Mold,Toadstool", TryParseHazard picks one at random): Mold and Toadstool, both ONE left click to set off and ONE right click to clear (a reaction check). Mold now molds every husk on the field (PixelMold.wholeMap), and its timers are longer (zone hold 30 s, kill 22 s). The Toadstool gives a 12 s dizzy status (PixelToadstoolDaze): the cube jerks away from the cursor after each of your clicks.
- Firemaking (PixelSkills.FiremakingId, dev-only: the Firemaker's Pack, which is the old Fire Pixel test pack, hidden from the shop): Tinder, Kindling, Firewood, Flint, Ember, Fire, Charcoal, filler Ash, hazard Wildfire (5 hits: the fire burns up the husks around the cube; right-click 5 times to put it out). Tag PixelTag.Firemaking / PixelClicker.IsFireBlock. Mechanics still to come. Fire lost its Toggles spawn switch (it is a skill block now).
- Mining: Lead, Zinc and Nickel are back (no longer tagged RetiredOre; PixelShopDefaults.ProspectorOre), and the filler is called Rock everywhere (the enum was always Rock).
- The Prospector's and Farmer's Packs are in the shop with real prices (2,000 White + 1,000 Gray / 1,500 White + 750 Gray). Scene data is updated by packDataVersion 35.
--- docs/systems/WoodcuttingProgression.md ---
Woodcutting progression (design, not built yet)

Status: agreed direction; build step 1 TESTED IN UNITY and feels good (2026-10-16) - only timings and numbers still to tune. Step 1 was built as a TEST (2026-10-15): PixelTreeMode.cs (filler rotation + Oak trees + infested trees + tree bar + Timber), switched on by the hidden Oak Trees (test) shop pack (Dev Tools > Shop unlocks) while Woodcutting is in play. Data (fillers, species slot lists, infested chances) is on the PixelTreeMode component; details in its header. The "Open questions" section lists what is still a proposal.
Today's Woodcutting (blocks, perks, Termite Hive, filler family) is described in SkillBlocks.md; this doc is where it is going.

Goals

- The intro skill. Quick to start and to understand; the first ten levels take about an hour.
- A rhythm, not random noise. Each click should feel random, but the session should have a shape: a beginning, a build-up and a payoff that come round again and again.
- Hazards you can plan around. A Termite Hive should be a known risk, not bad luck.
- Unlocks you can see. Every early level adds one new thing; later levels make your trees visibly richer.
- Better through other skills. Woodcutting stays simple on its own; other skills supply its upgrades (the Whetstone comes from Mining, and so on).

The core idea: random trees, designed shapes

While Woodcutting is in play the cube alternates between two modes:

1. Filler rotation (random). The cube rolls randomly among the basic blocks: Twig, Leaf and plain Wood (later Moss and Sap too). Quick, cheap, the "waiting" part.
2. A tree arrives (random timing). After a random stretch of filler (see "When a tree arrives") a tree of a random species starts.
3. The tree plays out in a fixed shape (designed). Each species is an ordered list of slots: the blocks come in that order, with a few controlled random parts (see "Tree shapes"). The Termite Hive can only appear in a fixed slot, and an infested tree is warned from its first block.
4. "Timber!" When the last slot is chopped the tree falls: a felling show, sound and a small reward (see "Timber reward"). The cube goes back to the filler rotation.

So: when a tree comes is random, what is inside it is designed.

Why it works:

- Keeps the clicker's surprise. You never know when the next tree comes or which species it is.
- An authored rhythm. Inside a tree every click has a place: canopy, trunk, core, payoff. Each species can be tuned like a little level.
- Hazards you can plan around. The Hive only ever sits in the tree's last slot, and an infested tree shows it from the start (see "Infested trees"), so the player can prepare instead of being ambushed.
- The Next Up window becomes a tool. Once you know a species, you know what is coming; the window confirms it.
- Easy to expand. A species is data (a slot list). A new tree is a new list entry, not new code - and the same system fits other skills (see "The same system for other skills").

How the existing mechanics fit in

| Mechanic | With trees |
|---|---|
| Next Up window | Shows the next block: from the tree's slot list while a tree is up, otherwise the pre-rolled filler. |
| Block Hold | Holding pulls the live block out; letting go puts it back as the *next* block (it already works like this through nextInLine). Inside a tree the held block slots back in before the rest of the tree, so the tree's order is kept. |
| Shuffle bonus | Works on filler blocks during the filler rotation, as now. Inside a tree it is off (a tree's order is the point). |
| Hazard defuse / Pesticide | The Hive in an infested tree's last slot: right clicks (10) or a held Pesticide clear it, as now. |
| Great Oak | A rare species (see "Species"); acorns lying on the floor raise its chance when a tree arrives. |
| Auto clicker | Chops filler and trees like a manual click. It never touches a Hive (it waits for you), as now. |
| Focus potion | Lumberjack's Focus (made from collected Termite Hives): no tree is infested while it lasts. |

When a tree arrives

Built (2026-10-16, after testing): the filler run shrinks with the level - 12-30 filler blocks between trees at level 1, down to 3-10 by level 20 (PixelTreeMode minFillerAtLevel1 / maxFillerAtLevel1, minFillerAtTop / maxFillerAtTop, fillerLevelSpan). Trees came far too often at low levels with a fixed 3-10.

- During the filler rotation each click has a growing chance to start a tree: a few filler blocks always come first (minimum 3), then the chance climbs until a tree is guaranteed (by about 10). Inspector data: minFiller, maxFiller (or a chance curve).
- Levels shorten the filler stretch a little (more trees per hour), so the share of junk falls as you level - replacing today's filler fade (75% -> 25% over 20 levels).
- The species is a weighted pick among the unlocked ones; acorns on the floor add weight to the Great Oak.

Tree shapes

A species is an ordered slot list. A slot is one of:

| Slot kind | Example | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed | Leaf | Always that block. |
| Run | Wood x3-6 | That block a random number of times: this is what makes trees different sizes. The range can grow with the level. |
| Wildcard | [Sap 30% / Wood] | A small weighted pick, so details change while the shape stays. |
| Optional | Acorn? (50%) | Sometimes there, sometimes skipped. |
| Hazard | (Hive) | Only filled when the tree is infested; always the last slot before Timber. |

Proposed species

| Species | Unlock | Shape | Character |
|---|---|---|---|
| Oak | 1 | Leaf, Leaf, Wood x3-6, [Sap 30% / Wood], Acorn?, [Heartwood 20% / Wood], (Hive) (Sap from level 2, Acorn from 3, Heartwood from 6; before that those slots are Wood) | The all-rounder; feeds the Great Oak through acorns. |
| Pine | 4 | Leaf, Pinecone, Wood x4-8, Pinecone, Wood x1-2, (Hive) | Tall: a long log run with cones. |
| Birch | 8 | Leaf, Bark, Wood x2-3, Bark, (Hive) | Short and quick: Timber after Timber. |
| Maple | 12 | Leaf, Wood x2-4, Sap, Sap, Wood x1-3, [Heartwood 25% / Sap], (Hive) | Sticky and well paid. |
| Great Oak | 3, rare | Leaf, Leaf, Wood x6-10, Bark, Wood x2-4, Heartwood | Long, guaranteed heartwood core, never infested; its blocks stall the Block Hold meter. 1% when a tree arrives, up to 25% with acorns lying on the floor. |

Infested trees

- Chance per tree: about 25% at level 2 (no Hives before level 2), rising slowly to about 45% at level 20; 0 while the Focus potion lasts; the Great Oak is never infested.
- Warned from the first block: the tree bar shows a Hive icon, the tree's blocks get a darker, bored-through bark look, and a faint termite skitter plays. The player can then:
  - prepare: have a Pesticide ready to hold, or plan a Block Hold;
  - accept it: chop through and clear the Hive with right clicks at the end;
- The Hive keeps today's rules: 4 left clicks set the termites loose, 10 right clicks clear it safely (and collect it).

Dealing with Termite Hives: the toolkit

Pesticide used to be pure luck (a random block you could only use if it showed up at the right moment). Now every play style has its own answer, and preparing is a real choice:

| Approach | How it works | Suits |
|---|---|---|
| Effort | 10 right clicks clear the Hive and collect it (as now). | The clicker: free, but costs time and focus. |
| Preparation | Pesticide charges (see below): one left click on the Hive with charges clears and collects it, using one charge. | The planner: bank charges whenever Pesticide shows up. |
| Automation | A Pesticide Sprayer placed near the cube sprays any Hive that appears, using Pesticide husks fed in by hand or by belt. | The builder: a machine to set up and keep supplied. |
| Avoidance | A Focus potion, or the Infestation dial on Calm (below). | Anyone who wants a quiet stretch. |

Pesticide charges (hold to harvest). Pesticide stays a block in the rotation, but holding it with Block Hold converts it into Pesticide charges instead of paying currency (the game-wide "hold a utility block for its use" rule, see PixelRules.md). Its old pixel (husk) still drops, so it can be fed to a Pesticide Sprayer or used again. Charges are banked: every time Pesticide comes by you can hold it, so when a warned Hive arrives you already have answers stocked. When an infested tree is warned, the tree bar shows your charge count. (Later, Farming can produce charges too - the planned cross-skill link.)

When a Hive goes off anyway (the termites):
- Click threatened old pixels to save them (as now).
- Sap as bait: termites go for Sap old pixels first and get stuck in them, so sap left on the floor near valuable husks is a cheap defence (and gives Sap a real job).
- Husks riding a belt or stored in a Storage Crate are safe from termites: building already protects you.

So a Hive has a full arc: prevent it (Focus, Calm), prepare (charges), automate (Sprayer), respond (right clicks) and recover (sap bait, saving pixels).

Hives are a resource: Focus potions
