- Clearing a Hive safely (10 right clicks, or one click with a held Pesticide) collects it as currency (this already works: right-click clearing collects hazards and drops them as an old pixel). Breaking it with left clicks gives nothing and lets the termites loose.
- Termite Hives are the ingredient for Lumberjack's Focus. The Lumberjack sells it for hives (and a little currency), or it is crafted from them. A Focus potion gives a stretch of trees with no Hives (proposed: the next 10 trees, or 60 seconds as today).
- So a hive costs you something in the moment but pays off later: dealing with it well matters, and avoiding every hive would mean having no Focus potions.

The Infestation dial

Unlocked by a perk or skill node (proposed: the Infestation node, from about level 12). A three-way setting in the Skills window for Woodcutting:

| Setting | Hives | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|
| Calm | about half the normal infested chance | about 10% less Woodcutting XP; for quick, quiet chopping |
| Normal | the normal infested chance | the default (and the only setting before the unlock) |
| Swarm | about double the infested chance | about 15% more XP and many more hives to collect: the way to farm Focus potions |

- A Focus potion still stops Hives completely while it lasts, on any setting (Calm only lowers them and costs XP, so potions keep their value).
- The same dial can later be given to every skill's hazard (Pest in Farming, Gas Pocket in Mining, Mold in Foraging, Jellyfish in Fishing), so each hazard has a reason to exist besides being annoying.

Rejected: skipping infested trees

A free or cheap "skip this tree" was considered and dropped: the warning would turn into a skip prompt and the decision (prepare, accept, clear) would disappear. With hives as a Focus potion ingredient, skipping would also throw away a resource. Players who want fewer hives use Calm or a Focus potion instead.

The same system for other skills

Filler rotation + randomly arriving shapes fit the other skills without new spawn code:

| Skill | Filler | "Trees" (shapes) |
|---|---|---|
| Mining | stone, gravel | ore veins (copper, iron...), a rich vein as the special; a Gas Pocket in a fixed slot |
| Foraging | weeds | bushes (berry bush, mushroom ring, herb patch), a truffle spot as the special; Mold in a fixed slot |
| Fishing | water | spots (shallows, reef), the whale as the special; a Jellyfish in a fixed slot |
| Farming | dirt | crop rows |

Lookahead grows with the skill

How far ahead you can see is itself a reward: early on you react, later you plan.

| Source | What you see |
|---|---|
| Level 1 | The next block (the Next Up window, as now) and, during a tree, the tree bar (Oak 7 / 12, with the Hive icon if infested). |
| Level 7 | Keen eye (perk): the next two blocks. |
| Level 20 | Woodsman's eye (perk): the tree bar shows what the tree's wildcard slots rolled (e.g. whether a heartwood is coming). |
| Skill tree: Read the grain | The next three blocks. |
| Level 30 | Master forester (perk): during the filler rotation a small hint shows the species of the next tree. |

- The Next Up window grows a slot per extra block (the next block on top, then the ones after it, smaller).
- Blocks bumped back by Block Hold show first, then the real next blocks, so the preview is always the true order.

Blocks and their roles

| Block | Where | Hits | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| Twig | filler | 1 | Basic filler. |
| Leaf | filler, tree tops | 1 | Basic filler and the start of most trees; floats down. |
| Wood | filler, tree trunks | 2 | Plain wood in the filler rotation; the log runs of every tree. |
| Moss | filler | 1 | Joins the filler later (level 2+). |
| Sap | filler (2+), oak / maple slots | 1 | Sticky old pixel, pays a bit more. |
| Pinecone | pine trees | 1 | Part of the Pine's shape. |
| Acorn | oak trees (3+) | 1 | Its old pixel on the floor raises the Great Oak chance. |
| Bark | birch, oak, Great Oak | 1 | Cheap trunk block. |
| Heartwood | cores (6+) | 4 | Rare payoff. |
| Termite Hive | last slot of an infested tree | 4 left / 10 right | The hazard. |
| Whetstone | - | - | Leaves Woodcutting: comes from Mining (see "Cross-skill upgrades"). |
| Pesticide | trunk (rare, any tree) | 3 | A utility block: hold it for Pesticide charges (no currency; its husk still drops). |

Level gating

| Level | What changes |
|---|---|
| 1 | Filler: Twig, Leaf, Wood. Trees: Oak only, with plain wood in its special slots. Small trees. |
| 2 | Sap (filler and oak slot), Moss in the filler; trees can be infested. Perk: Wood Chipper (as now). |
| 3 | Oaks drop Acorns; the Great Oak can arrive. Perk: wood pixels last longer (as now). |
| 4 | Pine trees. |
| 5 | Perk: Wood can spawn in any skill (as now). |
| 6 | Heartwood cores can roll. |
| 7 | Perk: Keen eye - see the next two blocks. |
| 8 | Birch trees. First skill point (see "Skill tree"). |
| 10 | Evil Tree minigame can appear (it needs the Lumberjack's Pack today; gate it at 10). |
| 12 | Maple trees. Lumberjack sells Lumberjack's Focus (for Termite Hives); the Infestation skill node can be bought. |
| 15, 20, 25, 30 | Mastery perks (see "Perks"). |

Special trees

- Great Oak. A rare species (above) with a guaranteed heartwood core and a bigger Timber reward.
- Later special trees (the to-do's "special-block tier" for Woodcutting): ideas to pick from - a *Beehive tree* (honey that sticks pixels together), a *Hollow Log* (a creature hides in it), a *Lightning-struck tree* (a quick-click event). Fishing's bottle -> map -> treasure is the model.

Timber reward

When a tree falls:

- A felling show: the cube "falls over" (a quick tilt and drop), chips burst out, a deep timber sound.
- Bonus XP of about 20% of the tree's own XP.
- A chance of an Acorn old pixel dropping (feeds the Great Oak chance).
- Counted in stats (woodcutting.trees_felled), which later achievements and the Lumberjack can use ("Fell 100 trees").

XP and pacing

Today: 60 XP for level 2, each level costs 1.45x the one before, 1 XP per block. That is about 1 hour to level 10 but about 43 hours to 20 and 1,770 hours to 30, so the curve hits a wall around level 15.

Proposed: a curve that flattens - 1.45x per level up to 10, 1.25x from 10 to 20, 1.15x after 20 (new fields on SkillPack, e.g. xpGrowthAfter10 / xpGrowthAfter20, or a small list of level bands).

At about one block per second of manual chopping:

| Level | Total XP | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | 456 | ~8 min |
| 10 | 3,645 | ~1 h |
| 15 | 15,673 | ~4.5 h |
| 20 | 52,379 | ~15 h |
| 30 | 307,337 | ~85 h |

Also proposed: XP per block = its hits (Wood 2, Heartwood 4) instead of 1 per block, so tough blocks are worth their clicks; the Timber bonus comes on top.

Perks

| Level | Perk |
|---|---|
| 2 | Wood Chipper (built) |
| 3 | Seasoned wood: wood pixels last longer (built) |
| 5 | Wood can spawn in any skill (built) |
| 7 | Keen eye: see the next two blocks (see "Lookahead") |
| 15 | Clean cut: Wood sometimes breaks in one hit |
| 20 | Woodsman's eye: the tree bar shows what the tree's wildcard slots rolled |
| 25 | Old growth: Great Oak chance +5% |
| 30 | Master forester: every tree has a heartwood core, and a hint shows the species of the next tree |

The current placeholder perks at 4 ("Sap collector") and 6 ("Heart of the forest") are replaced by the gating unlocks above.

Skill tree

Points: 1 at level 8, then one every 4 levels (12, 16, 20...). Proposed nodes:

| Node | Effect |
|---|---|
| Axe | The first person axe (the existing, not yet built node). |
| Sharp edge | Wood takes 1 hit. |
| Tapper | Sap wildcards roll more often. |
| Acorn hoard | Acorns count double for the Great Oak chance. |
| Big timber | Log runs are 2 blocks longer (more XP per Timber). |
| Read the grain | See the next three blocks. |
| Infestation | Unlocks the Infestation dial (Calm / Normal / Swarm). |
| Chipper tune | The Wood Chipper gives more XP per wood pixel. |

Cross-skill upgrades

Woodcutting stays the simple intro; other skills make it better:

| From | Upgrade |
|---|---|
| Mining | Whetstone - moves to Mining as a block / item. Used in Woodcutting it gives the rapid clicks it gives today (Block Hold), or becomes a permanent "sharpened axe". |
| Farming / Foraging | Pesticide charges made in Farming (on top of holding the Pesticide block), and Saplings that grow better trees (from the existing to-do "Cross-skill links"). |
| Electrician | Chainsaw (idea): a powered tool for the trunk part of a tree. |
| Fishing | Idea: driftwood or a "log raft" that adds a bonus tree. |

Value, body and use (the game-wide rule)

Woodcutting follows PixelRules.md: a click extracts a block's value (currency) and drops its drained husk (the old pixel = material, never currency by itself); holding a utility block (Pesticide) gives its charges instead of currency. Wood husks are material for the Wood Chipper (XP), the Kiln (Charcoal), crates, crafting and the Pesticide Sprayer.

Economy and sinks

Wood goods today only sell to the Lumberjack. Proposed sinks, to decide later: Magic Wood (Evil Tree) and heartwood spent on permanent upgrades (a better first person axe, a stronger chipper), and wood as a crafting material.

Saves and migrations

- Tree state (the species, the position in its slot list and what its runs / wildcards rolled, whether it is infested) is saved with the skill state so a reload does not reroll a half-chopped tree; an old save simply starts in the filler rotation.
- Whetstone leaves the Lumberjack's Pack: a packDataVersion bump removes it from saved packs (the tier stays unlocked so counts are not lost) and adds it to Mining's side.
- The Hive's per-pack shares (hazardBaseShare / hazardPerLevelShare) and the filler fade (fillerStartShare / fillerEndShare) are no longer used while tree mode is on; they stay for the other skills until they move over.

Build plan (in steps)

1. Tree mode (shared system). Data per skill pack: a filler pool, the tree-arrival settings (minFiller / maxFiller) and a list of species (unlock level, weight, slot list with Fixed / Run / Wildcard / Optional / Hazard slots, infested chance). PixelClicker.RollTier serves the filler rotation or the current tree's next slot. Built generic so Mining (veins), Foraging (bushes) and Fishing (spots) can use it later.
2. Tree bar + Timber + infested warning + lookahead. The tree bar (Oak 7 / 12, Hive icon, near the Next Up window), the felling show, sound, Timber reward and stats; the infested look and sound; the Next Up window learns to show 1-3 blocks ahead (PixelClicker.PeekNextTier becomes a peek of N).
3. Woodcutting species + gating. Oak, Pine, Birch, Maple, Great Oak and the filler pool; remove the per-block filler / hazard shares for Woodcutting.
4. Hook the existing mechanics in. Block Hold keeps the tree's order; Shuffle only on filler; Great Oak becomes a species; Focus potion stops infestation; Focus made from collected hives (Lumberjack trade / recipe); holding Pesticide gives charges (and a left click on a Hive with charges clears it); Sap old pixels bait termites.
5. XP curve bands + XP per hit. Then retune with the Balance report (which will need a "per tree" view).
6. Perks and skill tree nodes, including the Infestation dial.
7. Cross-skill moves. Whetstone to Mining (migration); Farming-made Pesticide charges.
8. Pesticide Sprayer (a machine fed with Pesticide husks).
9. Special trees beyond the Great Oak.

Open questions

- Species shapes, run lengths, unlock levels and the infested chance are proposals.
- Filler stretch between trees (3-10 blocks proposed) and how much levels shorten it.
- Focus potion: how many hives per potion, and does it last N trees or N seconds?
- Infestation dial numbers (Calm half / Swarm double, XP -10% / +15%) are proposals.
- Should Pine bring its own block types later (e.g. Pine Log, Resin) or keep sharing Wood / Leaf?
- Lookahead steps: the levels above (7 / 20 / 30 and the tree node) are a proposal.
- Pesticide charges: how many per held block, and is there a cap on how many you can carry?
- Pesticide Sprayer: its cost, range and how many husks per Hive.
- XP per hit vs XP per block.
- Should the auto clicker be able to fell whole trees, or stop at the core so heartwood is always hand-chopped?

=== 11. ROADMAP / OPEN IDEAS (docs/roadmap.md) ===
Roadmap / open notes

Moved from CLAUDE.md.

- Handoff + optimization review written (2026-10-07): see HANDOFF.md in the repo root (working rules recap, unverified items, lessons, and a prioritized optimization list). Done so far: #1-6, 9, 10, 11, 14, 22, 24, 27 (see git log). Remaining items are still open.
- The UI helper (PixelUIKit) and minigame base (PixelMinigame) refactors are verified in Unity by the user.
- Cleanup still worth doing: (PixelShop is now split into three partial-class files; a deeper split into separate logic and UI classes is still possible); (the shop's "add missing defaults" code is now one table-driven loop; potions/devices/achievements keep their own small versions); optionally replace scene searches with one shared game object that owns the references (the version-check duplication is already gone via PixelFind).
- Balance direction (agreed 2026-10-09): idle game played over days, with many more pixels and systems planned (numbers going up). Phase 1 done (long number suffixes up to ~1e306 in FormatNumber; meteor chunkClickCooldown). Phase 2 started: Value upgrades + potion/device prices scale with Value. Built-in pack prices re-laddered for the Value economy (RGB 100 each, Auto Clicker 1K each, then roughly x2-10 per step up to 5M; upgrade levels x5-x10 per level); saved scene prices don't follow code defaults, so PixelShop has an Inspector tick box resetBuiltInPricesNow / context menu "Reset Built-in Pack Prices" (ResetBuiltInPackPrices in PixelShopDefaults.cs: copies costs + level lists from the Create…Pack defaults). Meteor goal field renamed chunkThreshold -> chunkGoal (default 1000) on purpose so old serialized 100s are dropped (the same rename trick works for any other serialized default that must change). Value cost scales by amountPerClick (payout per collect) but deliberately NOT by spawn weight: user wants rarer pixels to be slower to upgrade. Electric is the exception (an early, cheap boost). Rare-currency pack prices are set from earn rate = spawnWeight x amountPerClick relative to White (Glass 0.5, Vacuum 0.2, Obsidian 2, Luminescent 1.5, Ghost 2.4, Meteor 3.75, Singularity 5). (2026-10-09 later: every pixel / minigame pack from Vacuum onward was cut about 30% - Vacuum 17.5K Glass + 35K RGB, Ghost Hunt 35K Black + 17.5K Glass, Obsidian 100K Black + 50K Glass, Luminescent 175K Glass + 700K Obsidian, Ghost Pixel 1.4M Glass + 4M Luminescent, Ultra Pad / Breakout 175K, Sorting Race 70K, Snake / Bomb 35K, Meteor Strike 1.75M Black + 3.5M Obsidian, Meteor Pixel 4M Luminescent + 550K Vacuum, Solar 700K Meteor + 7M Luminescent, Black Hole 350K Black + 70K Vacuum, Singularity 2.8M Obsidian + 280K Vacuum; upgrades / features were left alone.) Earlier ladder: Snake 50K, Sorting Race 100K, Breakout 250K, Time Slow 500K each, Old Pixel Capacity 2.5K-5M each, Solar Pixel 1M Meteor + 10M Luminescent. Potions (agreed 2026-10-09): stay forced (only that pixel spawns) and chainable - the user likes the all-one-type look and chaining. Balanced by price instead: PixelConsumables.potionPriceFromOutput -> price = expected output / potionTargetReturn (3), paid in the potion's own pixel; expected output = duration x (autoClicker rate once bought, even if toggled off, + assumedManualClicksPerSecond 4) x amountPerClick x PayoutMultiplier x ClickMultiplier / clicksToCollect, plus for Vacuum the old pixels re-collected (ExpectedPotionOutput, PotionPriceFromOutput, PotionShopPrice); typed potion costs are ignored while it is on. PixelClicker.potionVacuumEvery (10): while a potion forces the Vacuum tier only every 10th Vacuum click vacuums (VacuumThisClick). Crafting: recipesFollowShopPrices -> ScaleRecipe copies a recipe with the potion's own pixel at craftPotionPriceFraction (0.75) of its shop price (combo potions: the second pixel), other pixel ingredients x Value (Ingredient.amountOverride / Amount, runtime only). Stockpiling is limited by PixelConsumables.maxPotionsHeld (10 per potion type, combo potions too, devices unlimited; ItemRoom, ClampHeld - shop purchase card shows "Full", crafting refuses, loading a save clamps). Still to do: formula-based auto clicker levels, minigame goals measured in hours, and later prestige (wanted) and passive production. Intro tiers (Gray/Black) unlock from lifetime totals at 100 each; shop purchases always spend held currency.
- Possible next steps (not requested yet – confirm before building): more shop packs/tiers, particle polish, filling in sound clips, balancing pass on spawn weights and costs, prestige, prefabs for the runtime-built UI.
- Known gaps: offline progress only covers the auto clicker (no minigames/devices offline); PixelLog keeps a duplicate number formatter (FormatAmount) separate from PixelClicker.FormatNumber – could be unified.
- Fishing rework: Water (the Farming pixel) is Fishing's filler (very common: fillerStartShare 0.75 -> 0.5) and replaces Driftwood (retired, LeftOutOfSpawns); the pack's types are Fish, Seaweed, Shell, Pearl, Water, Jellyfish (packDataVersion 21). A block can belong to several packs (PackForTier / LeftOutOfSpawns prefer the pack in play). Fish is rare (weight 0.15, 1 click, pays 12): its click (manual only, the auto clicker waits) calls PixelWeakspotMinigame.TryStartFishing(done) (a Weak Spots round with the fish still on the cube); PixelClicker.FishingDone(caught) then pays it through a normal click (fishingPass) or says 'The fish got away!' and rolls the next pixel. If the round can't start (minigame switched off, another minigame busy...) the click just catches the fish.
- Fishing, part 2: the Jellyfish is electric now (9 clicks; PixelLook.lightning look, PixelElectricFx Hit / Break cases, sounds pixel_jellyfish / jelly_sting = PixelSynth.Zap / Charge; when it goes off in TriggerHazard it stings jellyfishBlastFactor (1.5) x wider, fires a big FractureFx and fries every bait pellet). Bait (PixelType.Bait 72, in the Angler's Pack, packDataVersion 22, looksVersion 47): collecting one calls PixelBait.Drop (PixelBait.cs: baitPellets 6 small spheres hop out round the cube onto the floor, on screen, max baitMaxPellets 24, rot after baitLifetime 60 s); PixelBait.FishMultiplier (1 + pellets x baitPerPellet 0.35) multiplies a Fish block's spawn weight in SpawnWeightOf; PixelBait.DestroyAll(zap) removes them. Fish without the minigame: when PixelWeakspotMinigame.FishingAvailable is false (switched off in Toggles) a fish takes a random 7-25 clicks (fishNeed / fishReel, manual or auto), the last one pays; with it on, only a manual click starts the round.
- Simple save flow (main menu): nothing loads by itself while the title screen shows (PixelSaveGame.Start skips the startup load when PixelTitleScreen.Showing). The big main button reads Continue when there are saves and none has been loaded / written this run (PixelSaveGame.HasLoadedSave): pressing it loads the most recent file (TryGetMostRecentSlot by write time, ContinueMostRecent, pays the time away) and plays; New Game when there are no saves at all; Play once a save was loaded / written (e.g. after Quit > Main menu: just resumes). The floppy icon opens the slot window: a filled slot loads, and from the title an EMPTY slot turns the Load button into New Game (PixelTitleScreen.StartNewGame(slot): sets the slot (UseSlotForNewGame) and plays; if the game was already played this run (PlayedOnce) it sets PixelSaveGame.AutoStartNewGame, resets tips / achievements and reloads the scene, and the title then presses Play by itself). Autosave / quit-save always write into CurrentSlot (the slot loaded or chosen for the new game).
- Docked button layout: TOP: Inventory (left corner), Shop (right corner); BOTTOM: Log (left corner), Dev Tools (centre, dev builds), Crafting (right corner); Bank + Hose on the middle of the LEFT edge with Skills directly under them (group), Toggles + Pixel Guide (directly under it) on the middle of the RIGHT edge (they slide sideways). PixelHud.Dock(button, anchor, ...) accepts a FRACTION in anchor.x for top / bottom buttons (0 = left corner .. 1 = right corner; pivot = the fraction); a top / bottom group pivots on its left edge. My Window is a square window-icon button on the Toggles tab's inner side (a DockInGroup pair like Bank + Hose: the Toggles tab is ButtonSize.x - ButtonSize.y wide, the square next to it; PixelToggles.BuildMyWindowIcon, shows once PixelMyWindow.Available, hover name box, calls PixelMyWindow.ToggleOpen(), tucks away with the Toggles tab while its window is open). Every docked button has the same colour (PixelHud.SharedButtonColor, applied each frame by PixelDockedButton.ApplyColor; useOwnColor exempts the hose (green while on) and Dev Tools buttons).
- Minigame tip boxes (minigame_<id> hints, PixelHints.Update) now stop time and darken the screen until 'Got it': PixelTutorialDim.Begin(null) (fade + pause + click block) then a small PixelNotice with freezeTime and onClosed: PixelTutorialDim.End; the first ghost tip still sits beside the ghost. PixelWeakspotMinigame.Update waits while PixelTutorialDim.Active so its slow-motion scale isn't re-applied over the freeze.

Profiling (added with optimization step 2)
Profiler markers (Window > Analysis > Profiler, CPU > Timeline / Hierarchy, search "Pixel."): Pixel.FloorRedraw (animated floor texture), Pixel.ElectricLinks.Scan, Pixel.SolarClusters, Pixel.QuantumScan. Reading the code, the old-pixel scans are timer-limited (0.1-0.15 s) and O(n) with early exits, and GetComponent<OldPixelInfo> only appears in timer-driven scans (PixelViewBounds already caches it), so a shared pixel registry / pooling is NOT worth doing until a profile with ~150 old pixels shows these markers (or the per-object Update / FixedUpdate of OldPixelDespawn / ScaledGravity) taking real time.

- Click lag (Editor): per-click click-effect bursts are pooled (PixelBasicFx Burst pool + PixelFx.Configure), the spawn path reuses one MaterialPropertyBlock and caches the live cube's colliders, and Profiler markers Pixel.SpawnFallingCopy, Pixel.Materialize, Pixel.ClickFx show what is left. Still unpooled: the old pixel GameObjects themselves (about a dozen components each), PixelFarmFx / PixelElectricFx bursts, +N popups. Profile before pooling more.
- Pixel rules (value / body / use): a click gives a block's currency and drops its husk (material, never currency); holding a utility block gives charges. Design in docs/systems/PixelRules.md; Vacuum, Pixel Bank and the Solar trickle need a new job under it.

=== 12. OPEN TO-DO ITEMS (Assets/Resources/Todo.txt, unchecked only) ===

Problem to solve: the basic pixels step aside while a skill pack is in play, so skills must be able to produce pure currency. Rule: every skill pixel needs at least one sink in its skill.

Build together with the skill vendors / Sell tab so the numbers are tuned in one place.
